## Rules the Worker and the web UI share (open)

| ID    | Item                            | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ----- | ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SHR-5 | Backfill stored post HTML       | `content_html` is written when a post is published or edited, so posts stored before SHR-2 keep their unlinked HTML (one `<p>`, `<br>` per newline) until they are edited. A migration or a one-off job would re-render them with `formatPostText` from `content_text` and the stored mentions.                                             |
| SHR-9 | Cap on a remote note's hashtags | `noteHashtags` keeps every `Hashtag` object of a remote note, as Mastodon v4.7.5 does (`process_tags` has no limit), so a note with 5000 of them is indexed under, and renders, 5000 tags. Storing them is one statement whatever the count. Decide whether to cap the count, as `MAX_EMOJIS` (50) caps a note's emoji, and at what number. |

By design, and so not open: a mention's domain may carry a port (`@a@127.0.0.1:8794`), as tstodon keeps an actor's `host:port` authority, and a hashtag with no letter before `http://` (`#1http://`) is no link. `scripts/generate-post-text-fixture.mjs` leaves these texts out of the fixture. `normalizeHashtag` lowercases a `Σ` ending a word as `ς`, as `toLowerCase` does (`#ΟΔΟΣ` is `οδος`), where Mastodon's Ruby `downcase` gives `σ`. It is kept (decided with SHR-8): names have been stored that way and the stored name alone does not say whether the post wrote `ς` or `Σ`, so following Ruby would need every tag of a stored post read again from its text.

## Web UI links to Rails pages (open)

Mastodon's web app links to Rails pages that tstodon answers with a "not available on this server" page (`server/routes/unsupported-pages.ts`). Each is a gap a user can reach from the menu.

| ID    | Item                              | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ----- | --------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEB-5 | `/admin/*` beyond the pages built | Software updates, rules, roles, webhooks, relays, server settings, and the `/admin/tags` list reach the notice. Per-post moderation has no admin REST counterpart, so it runs only through the page |
| WEB-6 | `/disputes/*`                     | No appeals: the server has no strikes or moderation warnings to appeal, so the notice stays                                                                                                         |

## Interop with real Mastodon (open)

<!-- constrained-by ../getting-started/e2e-federation.md#a-real-mastodon -->

Scenarios to automate against Mastodon v4.7.5 with the harness in `e2e/mastodon/` ([README](../../e2e/mastodon/README.md)), ranked by risk. `pnpm e2e:mastodon` is the gate. `pnpm e2e:mastodon:probe` sweeps the rows marked "sweep" in both directions and passes; a row leaves this table when the sweep asserts all of it. CI runs both, in three modes (`.github/workflows/e2e-mastodon.yml`).

| ID     | Scenario            | Sweep | Still to cover                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------ | ------------------- | ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| INT-2  | Mentions and DMs    | sweep | Mastodon's `limited` is stored and served (see "Done recently"); two cases stay. (1) `postprocess_audience_and_deliver`: Mastodon processes every delivery of a `Create`, so the same note posted to a second personal inbox, or to one after the note was fetched, gives that inbox's owner a silent mention and turns a direct note limited. tstodon answers a repeated activity id as a duplicate (`inboxClaimKey`) and adds nothing to a note it holds already. Mastodon delivers to the shared inbox of a recipient that has one, as tstodon's actors do, so only a server that posts to personal inboxes meets this. (2) A local status is limited in Mastodon when a client posts `visibility=limited`: the `visibility` enum accepts it and `PostStatusService` does not check it, though no client offers it. tstodon answers 400 (`Visibility.fromMastodon`), and a local status cannot be limited (`RemoteVisibility` is a remote status's only)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| INT-4  | Replies and threads | sweep | A local reply a `replies` page embeds carries an empty `replies` of its own, where Mastodon embeds its first page, and the replies cached from other servers come on pages after the local ones (`remote=true`), where Mastodon lists every other account's in one id order (`server/note-replies.ts`). The reply tree walk does not fetch a cached reply again, where Mastodon's `FetchReplyWorker` refreshes one not read within 15 minutes; it expands a cached reply published within five minutes, which Mastodon leaves out when it had it before the walk (`created_recently`); it walks an opened status younger than five minutes (`FETCH_REPLIES_INITIAL_WAIT_MINUTES`); and it reads at most ten pages of one collection a job while they hold fewer than five replies, where Mastodon reads on up to the tree's 500 pages (`server/remote-thread-backfill.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| INT-5  | Quote posts         | sweep | A quote that waits for a manual approval (`pending`): Mastodon 4.7 has no client API that sets such a policy                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| INT-7  | Blocks and reports  | sweep | A domain block by a user, and by an admin of either server                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| INT-9  | Edits and deletes   | sweep | Delete and redraft. A tstodon `Delete` of a post now carries its Linked Data signature, so Mastodon forwards it to the followers of a Mastodon user who boosted the post (FED-13); with one Mastodon that forward cannot be seen, so the sweep checks only that Mastodon verifies the signature                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| INT-10 | Polls               | sweep | A remote poll that a signed-in client reads (`GET /api/v1/polls/:id`) is fetched again when it may be stale, as Mastodon's `PollsController#refresh_poll` (`server/remote-poll-refresh.ts`), but the fetch is signed by the instance actor, not the reader, so a followers-only poll on a server that checks the signer is not refreshed. The end of a tstodon poll on Mastodon: the shortest poll runs five minutes and the `poll` notification comes from the hourly cron                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| INT-11 | Media               | sweep | tstodon does not download a remote file (`remoteMediaJson`, `remote-status-document.ts`), so it shows what Mastodon would without its copy: `url` is the remote file; `preview_url` is the attachment's `icon` (also `preview_remote_url`), else an image's own file, else null, where Mastodon shows a frame of the video or an audio's cover art read from the file; `meta` holds only the sizes the note declares. A GIF stays an `image` and a video without sound a `video`, where Mastodon makes both a `gifv` from the file. A thumbnail added where the stored attachment had none is not an edit, since rows stored before thumbnails were kept have none. The sweep does not upload a video with a thumbnail                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| INT-14 | Hashtags            | sweep | Home reads the 25 most recently followed hashtags of a reader (`HOME_FOLLOWED_TAG_LIMIT`); Mastodon has no such limit. A post under a followed hashtag from a member of one of the reader's exclusive lists stays off home; Mastodon's hashtag delivery does not check exclusive lists. A remote actor's `featuredTags` collection is read only on a profile backfill (at most once a day), one page; between those, featured hashtags follow the actor's `Add` and `Remove`. A home page reads each followed hashtag at most four pages deep, and clips its other sources there; a hidden flood more than sixteen pages deep (four reads past the horizon) still answers an empty page. The `delete` stream event of a post does not reach a hashtag follower who is not a follower of its author, as in Mastodon (`RemoveStatusService` unpushes it from followers' homes only); a client drops the post on its next read                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| INT-15 | Profile updates     | sweep | A profile field's link is checked as Mastodon's `VerifyAccountLinksWorker` checks it (`server/profile-link-verification.ts`), but a remote actor's fields are checked from the hourly cron after each fetch of its document, where Mastodon queues the check within ten minutes, and the linked page is fetched under the federation host rules, so a limited-federation allow list or a suspended domain refuses it where Mastodon refuses only private addresses. A local field's value links its URLs and hashtags but leaves a mention as text. A verified remote field shows its value as sent, where Mastodon rebuilds the link. The sweep does not check `verified_at`. tstodon reads a remote `featured` collection only when it backfills a profile (a view of the profile, at most once a day); between those, pins follow the actor's `Add` and `Remove`. Mastodon's `Add` has no `id`, so a pin, an unpin and a pin again of one note within one second arrive as two identical requests, and the second `Add` is dropped as a redelivery until the next backfill (`nameCollectionActivity`, `server/activitypub-wire.ts`). The `featured` collection lists URIs where Mastodon embeds the notes. There is no self-service account deletion: only an admin deletes an account. The `Delete` of a deleted account reaches every inbox of its followers and follows, and other known inboxes only up to 1000 in all, and only the actor URI answers 410. The `discoverable` and `indexable` flags |

## Federation (open)

Found by the security and interop audit of November 2026, checked against Mastodon v4.7.5.

| ID     | Item                                  | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------ | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| FED-8  | Key forms, what is left               | An `mldsa-44-pub` Multikey is not read and an `mldsa44-jcs-2024` proof is not checked: no Workers API verifies ML-DSA, and a JavaScript verifier would have to be bundled. A key read from its own document follows redirects on the same origin only (`fetchActivityJson`); Mastodon follows any redirect for a `publicKey` (`fetch_resource_without_id_validation`) and none for a Multikey. Mastodon raises, and refuses the whole actor, on an actor's own `publicKey` without `publicKeyPem` (the `keypairs.public_key` column is `NOT NULL`) and on a key object without an `id`; tstodon skips that key. A new actor refused for a malformed `publicKeyMultibase` leaves no row; Mastodon has saved a bare `Account` (`create_account`) before it raises. A `publicKeyMultibase` longer than 2048 characters is not decoded: it is no key, or refuses the actor when a character is not of its base. A signature under an unknown key id refetches its actor even when the actor was cached recently; Mastodon's `FetchRemoteKeyService` uses an actor that is not `possibly_stale?` as it is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| FED-9  | Smaller federation gaps, what is left | What FED-9 left (see "Done recently"). (1) `linzer` reads a component named by a token (`(date)`) as the field of that name; tstodon refuses such a `Signature-Input` (401), as RFC 9421 names components by string. (2) `@query-param` over a query whose names Rack nests (`a[b]`, `a[]`) is not derived here, where Rack still reads the query's other names, and Rack's `params` adds a form body's parameters (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, or a POST with no `Content-Type`), which tstodon does not read. (3) Behind Puma a string member under `;sf` or `;key` comes out a byte sequence (`a=:eCB5:`), as Puma hands Rack binary strings and `starry` serializes those so; tstodon keeps it a string, as RFC 9421 and `linzer` on UTF-8 strings do. (4) Mastodon's refresh (`ResolveAccountService`) deletes an account whose stored handle's WebFinger answers 410 (`queue_deletion!`); tstodon's refresh leaves it, as purging an actor (`purgeRemoteActorAndAnnounce`) inside the hourly run's D1 budget is not cheap. A handle lookup (a mention, a search) that the handle's domain answers 404 or 410 moves its cached holder to its URI authority here (`settleHandleHolder`); Mastodon leaves it. (5) A first-seen actor whose WebFinger does not answer makes the inbox answer 503 with `Retry-After`; Mastodon answers 401 (`SignatureVerificationError`), which its senders retry as well                                         |
| FED-13 | Forwarding, what is left              | An `mldsa44-jcs-2024` integrity proof is not checked (FED-8). An actor key rotation (OPS-8) does not re-sign what is already stored: an activity queued before it keeps the Linked Data signature made with the old key, which its direct receivers do not check, but a forward of it fails to verify once the receivers hold the new key. A context other than the five Mastodon preloads that tstodon ships (`server/jsonld-contexts.ts`) is never fetched, so a forward that names one is dropped; Mastodon fetches it. One forward reaches the followers of at most 500 local accounts, the replied-to author first (`FORWARD_MAX_ACCOUNTS`). A forward whose body does not read the same as JSON and as compacted JSON-LD is dropped (`AmbiguousJsonLd`); Mastodon processes its compacted form instead, which would need tstodon's handlers to read Mastodon's compacted shapes. So is a forward that uses vocabulary outside Mastodon's full context, even one its own context defines, such as Misskey's `_misskey_*` terms or `isCat`: compacted to Mastodon's context they come back as full IRIs. Mastodon takes it, and forwards it no further. The forward of a quote stamp's `Delete` (`stampDeleteForward`) still leaves out the followers of local quoters, which `ActivityPub::Forwarder` includes. No e2e step: with one Mastodon, every forward leaves out the origin server's shared inbox, so a forward needs a third server to be seen |
| FED-17 | Domain block limits, what is left     | (1) An actor on a host a suspension covers now is hidden on every read path (`AccountPresentation` `Hidden`: `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id` answers 404), where Mastodon v4.7.5 stops only at `domain_not_allowed?` (lookup, resolve, inbox, signed fetches) and renders the account with `suspended: true` elsewhere (`Api::V1::AccountsController#set_account`). So an admin's unsuspend of such an actor lifts nothing: the admin views keep `suspended: true`, where Mastodon's `unsuspend!` clears `suspended_at` and the account renders. (2) Changing a suspension to a silence or a `noop`, or removing it, brings the actors' earlier statuses, boosts and notifications back; Mastodon deleted them when the block was made (`DeleteAccountService` with `reserve_username`), so they never come back. An admin's per-account unsuspend empties them first. (3) An actor first seen after a block's last change is limited by the block only while the block is its host's most specific rule: a narrower `noop` made later exempts it, where Mastodon set its `silenced_at` / `suspended_at` when it created the account (`ProcessAccountService#create_account`) and keeps them; the backfill's `seenBy` cutoff leaves such actors out too                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| FED-18 | Remote custom emoji, what is left     | What INT-13 and FED-18 left (see "Done recently"). (1) A post or profile shows a `:shortcode:` it writes without declaring only when tstodon held the emoji of the author's domain as it stored that post or profile; Mastodon reads `CustomEmoji.from_text` at each render, so an emoji it first learns later shows on the domain's older posts too. (2) The copies stored before migration 0170 keep their image until the emoji is next replaced. (3) An admin cannot disable a remote emoji, as Mastodon's `CustomEmoji.enabled` allows (the `disable` batch action of `Admin::CustomEmojisController`, `Form::CustomEmojiBatch#disable!`; Mastodon has no REST API for it). That needs a remote emoji list in the admin views, a flag on `remote_custom_emojis`, and a pass that drops a disabled emoji from the stored copies (and adds it back when enabled) or a filter where they are rendered                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| FED-19 | Local custom emoji scan               | A local post or profile finds its emoji with `/:([a-zA-Z0-9_]+):/` (`emojisInText` in `server/emoji.ts`), looser than Mastodon's `CustomEmoji::SCAN_RE`: it takes a one-character shortcode and one with a letter, mark, digit or colon right before or after it (`x:wave:`), which Mastodon does not render or tag. The remote side scans with `SCAN_RE` (`undeclaredShortcodes` in `server/remote-emoji-document.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

## REST API gaps (open)

Endpoints Mastodon 4.7 serves that tstodon does not, or serves as a stub.

| ID      | Item                                            | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------- | ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| REST-1  | Collections (4.7)                               | The collections API is not served                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| REST-3  | `annual_reports`                                | Not served                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| REST-4  | Translation                                     | `POST /api/v1/statuses/:id/translate` and `GET /api/v1/instance/translation_languages` are stubs: there is no translation backend                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| REST-7  | `email_subscriptions`                           | Not served                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| REST-8  | `POST /api/v1/accounts` and email confirmations | Sign-up is Cloudflare Access only; the API registration and `/api/v1/emails/confirmations` are not served                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| REST-17 | Admin trend publishers paged by offset          | The rest of REST-17 (see "Done recently"). `GET /api/v1/admin/trends/links/publishers` pages by `offset` over the trending and reviewed domains; Mastodon v4.7.5 pages `PreviewCardProvider` rows by `max_id` with a `Link` header. tstodon keeps no provider rows with ids to page by: closing it needs a provider table written when a domain first trends or is reviewed |

## Done recently (do not re-open)

- FED-18, part: remote and local custom emoji federate closer to Mastodon v4.7.5. (1) A local emoji's `Emoji` tag, in a note and in an actor, now carries `id` (`https://<domain>/emojis/<id>`) and `updated` (its `updated_at` to the second, as Rails' `iso8601`), and its `icon` the image's `mediaType`, as `ActivityPub::EmojiSerializer` writes them. `GET /emojis/:id` serves that document (`EmojisController#show`): `@context` with `toot:Emoji`, `Cache-Control: max-age=180, public`, no signature asked in secure mode (`Vary: Signature` there), 404 for an unknown id (`server/routes/emoji-document.ts`). Cloudflare Access bypasses `/emojis/*` (`infra/cloudflare/access.tf`), and it counts against `federation_fetch` and the edge rate-limit example as `/users/*` does. Mastodon keeps the tag's `id` as the emoji's `uri` (`process_emoji`). An emoji the `CUSTOM_EMOJIS` var configures has no document, so its tag stays as it was (`CustomEmojiSource` in `server/emoji.ts`). (2) A post or profile now also shows each `:shortcode:` it writes without declaring whose emoji tstodon holds for the author's domain, as Mastodon renders `CustomEmoji.from_text(text, account.domain)` (`Status#emojis`: the spoiler, the text and the poll's options; `StatusEdit#emojis`; `Account#emojis` over `emojifiable_text`). The shortcodes are scanned as `CustomEmoji::SCAN_RE` scans them (`undeclaredShortcodes` in `server/remote-emoji-document.ts`; Onigmo's `[[:alnum:]]` is `[\p{L}\p{M}\p{Nd}]`), and matched in their case against both the declared emoji and the stored ones, as `EntityCache#emoji` looks them up, so `:Blob:` is not the declared `blob`. The copy is added to the stored post or profile when it is stored (`recordRemoteEmojiImages`), so the refresh chain keeps it current. (3) An actor document a fetch reads (a mention, a search, a key lookup, the daily refresh), not only the actor's own `Update`, now starts the domain's `RefreshRemoteEmoji` chain at once when it replaces an image (`recordProfileEmojis`, from `cacheActorWithKeys`). The fetch paths carry only the database, so that job goes out on the Worker's own `OUTBOX_PROCESS_QUEUE`, read from `cloudflare:workers`'s global `env`, never a queue a caller injects. `app/queues` and `app/crons` already read that `env`; this is its first use under `server/`. The signed-activity samples now carry an uploaded emoji, and Mastodon verifies them with `id` and `updated`: `scripts/generate-jsonld-reference.mjs` runs again, as `scripts/worker-module-stubs.mjs` stubs `cloudflare:workers` and `virtual:tstodon/web-manifest` for `tsx` (the builders' imports reach both through `body-limit.ts` and `auth-pages.ts`). Tests: `server/note-emoji-tags.test.ts`, `server/remote-emoji-change.test.ts`, `server/remote-actor-profile.test.ts`. The sweep checks that Mastodon stores tstodon's emoji under its document's URL and that the URL serves it (`probe-emoji.ts`). What is left is FED-18.
- FED-16, the activity window: a local follower counts as active as Mastodon v4.7.5's `User.signed_in_recently` decides it, so the `status` notification and the home and list stream fan-out reach the same followers. Before, tstodon counted every follower with an authenticated request on any UTC day from the day a week ago, so a follower last seen up to a day before the cutoff still counted, and the window was fixed at 7 days. (1) Migration 0172 adds `accounts.current_sign_in_at`, Mastodon's `users.current_sign_in_at`, filled from each account's last day in `account_activity_days` at the start of that day, which lies in the 24 hours before that day's last request, as Mastodon's own value does (one primary-key seek per local account). (2) An authenticated request moves it to the request's time when it is unset or more than 24 hours old (`UserTrackingConcern`, `SIGN_IN_UPDATE_FREQUENCY`), and a new Cloudflare Access login moves it to the login's time, as `update_sign_in!` does at a sign-in (`touchAccountSignIn`, `recordAccountActivity` in `server/account-activity.ts`). A streaming connection does not move it, as Mastodon's streaming server runs outside Rails. Each isolate remembers the time it last read, so a request writes only when an update is due. (3) The window is the `USER_ACTIVE_DAYS` var (7 by default, `wrangler.jsonc`, `docs/reference/configuration.md`), and a follower counts while its sign-in time is within that many days of now (`signedInRecentlySince`). (4) The same rule now also limits the home and list stream fan-out (`listHomeFanOutAccounts`) and the followers told of a purged remote actor's removed statuses (`listRemoteActorFollowerAccountIds`), as `FeedManager#push_to_home` and `#push_to_list` push only to a recently signed-in user and `followers_for_local_distribution` and `lists_for_local_distribution` feed the fan-out and `BatchedRemoveStatusService`. The home timeline is read when asked for, so it is unchanged. Difference kept: a `USER_ACTIVE_DAYS` that is not a whole number counts as unset, where Ruby's `to_i` reads a leading number or 0. Worker tests: `server/account-activity.test.ts`, `server/status-notification-audience.test.ts`, `server/remote-status-notification.test.ts`, `server/home-stream.test.ts`.
- FED-17, the admin side: the admin account views show an actor a domain block limits, an admin can lift that limitation per actor, and blocks made before migration 0169 get their rows, as Mastodon v4.7.5. (1) `GET /api/v1/admin/accounts/:id`, the v1 and v2 lists with their `status` filters and the `/admin/accounts/:id` page read `silenced` and `suspended` apart, each the admin's own or a domain block's (`RemoteActorModeration.adminFlags`, `listDomainBlockLimits` in `server/domain-block-limits.ts`), as `REST::Admin::AccountSerializer#silenced` / `#suspended` and `AccountFilter#status_scope` read `silenced_at` / `suspended_at`, which `BlockDomainService` and `ProcessAccountService#create_account` set: an actor can be both, and `status=silenced` lists a suspended one that is also silenced. Before, they read only `remote_actor_moderation`, so an actor on a silenced domain showed `silenced: false`. The notification policy's `for_limited_accounts` holds a domain-silenced sender too, as `NotifyService` reads `silenced?`. (2) An admin's unsilence or unsuspend of a remote actor (API or page) also lifts the silence or suspension each block then holds on it, in one batch with its own moderation, as `Account#unsilence!` / `#unsuspend!` clear the timestamp whoever set it: migration 0171 adds `remote_actor_domain_lifts` (block, actor, severity), and `remote_actor_domain_rules` leaves out a lifted actor for that block while the block keeps that severity, so every SQL reader and `listUnfollowedSilencedAuthorsJson` honour it. Lifting a suspension empties the actor's earlier statuses, boosts, favourites and notifications (`emptyRemoteActorContentSql`), which `DeleteAccountService` deleted when the block was made. A suspension that covers the actor's host now is not lifted (FED-17, narrowed). Saving the block again, whatever changed, forgets its lifts on the first page of its `Apply` chain, as each `DomainBlockWorker` run re-limits the accounts `without_silenced` / `without_suspended`; removing the block or the actor's own account `Delete` deletes them. (3) An actor a suspension suspended when made, on a host a narrower `noop` exempts now, is found by `/api/v1/accounts/lookup` and rendered `suspended: true` (`AccountPresentation` `Emptied`), as Mastodon's `ResolveAccountService` passes `domain_not_allowed?` there; its statuses stay unserved, boosts included. Before, it was hidden (404). (4) Migration 0171 lists each silence and suspension in `domain_block_limit_backfills` with its `updated_at`, and the hourly `ResumeLiftedDomainBlockLimits` sweep claims each and queues a `Backfill` chain that records only the actors first seen by the block's last change, when Mastodon's `BlockDomainService` last ran; each page stamps the claim, so the sweep starts no second chain while one runs, and the last page drops the entry. An actor pruned and fetched again counts as first seen when fetched again. The Mastodon sweep's moderation area checks a domain silence, its per-account lift and the silence again on a save, both ways. What is left is the narrowed row.
- INT-13, the rest: a custom emoji whose image changes shows the new image as Mastodon v4.7.5 shows it, in both directions. (1) Mastodon keeps one `CustomEmoji` per shortcode and domain and renders every post and profile of the domain with it (`CustomEmoji.from_text`). It fetches the image again when a tag names another URL than the stored one (`process_emoji` in `ActivityPub::Activity::Create`, `ProcessStatusUpdateService#update_emojis!` and `ProcessAccountService`), and keeps the stored file when the new one does not download. tstodon kept the image URL with each note, so a new image from a Mastodon admin showed only on later posts. `remote_custom_emojis` (migration 0170) now holds the image last read for each emoji of an account domain, dated by the tag's `updated` (else the note's date, or the actor's fetch). A note, an edit or any fetch of an actor that names another image replaces it, unless it is dated before the stored one, so a late or retried delivery does not bring back a replaced image. Nothing is recorded for a domain whose media is rejected (`skip_download?`). Each replacement moves the domain to a new generation, and one self-chaining `RefreshRemoteEmoji` chain per generation (`server/remote-emoji-refresh.ts`) brings the copies in the domain's posts and their earlier versions (200 posts a page) and its actors' profiles (200 a page) to the stored images; a chain that a newer generation overtakes stops. A note or an actor's `Update` starts the chain, and the hourly cron starts one that could not be queued or stalled. (2) When a tstodon admin gave an emoji a new image, tstodon stored it under the same key, so its URL did not change. Mastodon fetches a remote emoji's image again only for a new URL, so it kept the old image. The new image now goes under a new key, and the old object is deleted. The sweep checks both directions (`probe-emoji.ts`). What is left is FED-18.
- INT-8: the sweep (`e2e/mastodon/probe-move.ts`) now asserts the rest of a move in both directions. The Worker already did it as Mastodon v4.7.5 does, so only the sweep changed. (1) Blocks and mutes are carried over as `MoveWorker#carry_blocks_over!` and `#carry_mutes_over!` carry them. Each local account that blocked or muted the old account blocks or mutes the new one, unless it already blocks, mutes or follows it. A mute keeps its `hide_notifications` choice and does not expire. Each new block or mute leaves an account note naming the old account, unless the account has a note there (`add_account_note_if_needed!`; `server/move-carry.ts`, `server/move-carry.test.ts`). The sweep checks this for a blocker and a muter on each server. (2) A cancelled redirect clears `movedTo` and moves no follower back, as `Settings::Migration::RedirectsController#destroy` does: it clears `moved_to_account` and sends an actor `Update`. When an actor `Update` has no `movedTo`, the moved account is cleared, as `ActivityPub::ProcessAccountService` does (`server/remote-update.ts`). The sweep cancels on each server, and the other server stops rendering `moved`. A follower that blocks the new account keeps the follow of the old one on both servers (`UnfollowFollowWorker` rescues `NotPermittedError`), so that follower's server still receives the cancel `Update`.
- FED-17: making an instance silence or suspension limits every actor cached at or under its domain then, a narrower rule notwithstanding, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `BlockDomainService` sets `silenced_at` / `suspended_at` over `Account.by_domain_and_subdomains` with the block's `created_at`. Before, tstodon read only the rules on each request, so under a suspension of `example.com` an actor already known on a `noop`-exempted `open.example.com` lost its local follows but stayed visible. The queued, self-chaining `RecordDomainBlockLimits` job (`server/domain-block-limits.ts`, started by every create, change and removal of a block, and by `ApplyDomainBlockEffects`) writes a `remote_actor_domain_limits` row per actor and block (migration 0169) in pages of 500, read in the order of the account domain's and then the URI host's key index (a `(key, rowid)` cursor), and only while the block still silences or suspends when the page's statement runs. `remote_actor_domain_rules` yields each row at the block's current severity, so every SQL reader of the rules (timelines, search, known inboxes, replies, pinned statuses, follow request approval, notification groups) honours it, and `listUnfollowedSilencedAuthorsJson` reads a silence's rows. The Worker's per-actor checks read a suspension's rows (`listDomainSuspendedActors`): account presentation hides the actor, the inbox refuses its activities except its own account `Delete`, and its signed fetches are refused, as for an actor an admin suspended. An actor first seen after the block follows its most specific rule alone, as `ActivityPub::ProcessAccountService#create_account` reads `DomainBlock.rule_for`. Changing the block to a `noop` or removing it lifts the limitation at once (the view joins the block row), as `process_retroactive_updates!` and `UnblockDomainService`; changing a suspension to a silence silences the same actors; the job then deletes a lifted block's rows, and the hourly `ResumeLiftedDomainBlockLimits` sweep queues that deletion again for a block whose chain stopped. `AsciiDomainNames` deletes the rows of a block it merges away, and an actor's own account `Delete` deletes its rows, as Mastodon destroys the account; pruning an unreferenced actor keeps them, as Mastodon keeps the account row. Blocks made before this change have no rows until they are edited or made again: recording them now would also limit actors first seen after the block, which Mastodon does not. Deviation: Mastodon keeps the first silence or suspension an account got (`without_silenced`, `without_suspended`) and an unblock clears only the block's own timestamp, so of two blocks both limiting one account, removing the first lifts it; tstodon keeps a row per block, so the other block still limits it. The admin account views do not read the rows yet (FED-17, narrowed).
- REST-17, the rest: four lists page as Mastodon v4.7.5 pages them. (1) An account's pinned statuses (`pinned=true`) are paged as its other statuses (`AccountStatusesFilter#pinned_scope` under `to_a_paginated_by_id`): newest pin first, bounded by status id, `limit` 20 (at most 40), with the `Link` of a full page keeping `pinned`; the other `AccountStatusesFilter` filters now apply to pins too, a remote actor's included (`exclude_replies`, `only_media`, `tagged` in `listRemoteActorPinnedStatuses`). Before, every pin was answered with no `Link`. A local account's pins are read from `status_pins` by key, then filtered by the same SQL as its other statuses; a remote actor's pins keep the `featured` collection's order and are paged in memory (`pageInOrder` in `server/id-cursor.ts`). (2) `GET /api/v2/notifications/unread_count` counts the groups of the first `limit` (100, at most 1000) past the read marker, as `paginate_groups_by_min_id(limit, min_id: last_read_id)`, under the list's `types`, `exclude_types`, `include_filtered` and `grouped_types`. Before, it grouped the 200 newest notifications, so it stopped at 200, and it ignored `types` and `exclude_types`. (3) `max_id` holds together with `since_id` or `min_id` on every list paged by id, as `Paginable#paginate_by_max_id` and `#paginate_by_min_id`: `idCursorBounds` and `idCursorFragment` gave `since_id` or `min_id` alone, which every list paged through them inherited (scheduled statuses, notification requests, follow requests, followers, the timelines, the admin lists), and v1 notifications, the link timeline and the admin tags carried their own copy of the rule. A home page's followed hashtags read both bounds (`c(lo, hi)` in `server/followed-feed.ts`). (4) Favourites and bookmarks are paged by the `Favourite` and `Bookmark` row id, newest first, and the `Link` names row ids: a status favourited long after it was posted is first in the list, not placed by its age. Migration 0168 adds the `(account_id, id)` indexes the pages walk; every row already has an id shaped like `newEntityId()` (0051, 0092), so it backfills nothing. What is left is REST-17 (the admin trend publishers). Tests: `server/pagination-links.test.ts`, `server/grouped-notifications.test.ts` (the grouped count past 200 and past the marker), `server/featured-collection.test.ts` (a remote actor's pins), and `server/pagination-plans.test.ts` (the favourites and bookmarks pages).
- FED-9: RFC 9421 component parameters are derived, and WebFinger failures store nothing, as Mastodon v4.7.5 does. (1) A message signature covering a component with parameters never verified: tstodon derived none. `messageSignatureBase` (`server/http-message-signature.ts`) now derives them as `linzer` 0.8.0 does on Mastodon's Rack request (`Adapter::Abstract#retrieve`): `;sf` reads the field as a dictionary and reserializes it (`serializeSfDictionary` in `server/structured-field.ts`; an empty or unparseable field, or one that is no dictionary, has no value), `;key` takes the member a string key names, `;bs` wraps the value as one byte sequence (a field sent on two lines is joined first, as Puma and `Headers` join it, where RFC 9421 wraps each line), and `@query-param;name` reads the query as Rack's `parse_nested_query` (split at `&`, form-decoded, the last of a repeated name, an empty value for a name without `=`) and percent-encodes the value as `URI.encode_uri_component` (a space as `%20`). Refused, as `linzer` refuses them: `;req` (a response's, so a replayed response signature never verifies), `;tr`, a parameter RFC 9421 does not register, `name` off `@query-param`, `;sf` or `;key` with `;bs`, a key that is a token, and a component repeated with its parameters in another order. `;sf=?0` reads as no `;sf`, as Ruby reads `false`. `scripts/generate-rfc9421-components-reference.mjs` runs `linzer` itself (`scripts/rfc9421-components-reference.rb`, in the Mastodon image or with the gems on `LINZER_RUBYLIB`) over 54 requests, RFC 9421's sections 2.1.1 to 2.1.3 and 2.2.8 and B.2.2 among them, and records each base, signed with RFC 9421's test-key-rsa, in `server/rfc9421-components.fixture.ts`; `server/rfc9421-components.test.ts` holds tstodon to it, verifies B.2.2's `rsa-pss-sha512` signature over the derived base, and checks that a tampered member, parameter or query value, a renamed component and `;req` do not verify. (2) A `Signature-Input` that does not parse, or names two signatures, stays a 401 (`MalformedSignatureInput`, `MultipleSignatures`). Mastodon passes `Linzer::Signature.build` no label, so it raises `Linzer::Error` ("Multiple signatures found but none was selected.", recorded in the fixture), which `SignatureVerification` does not rescue: a 500, not copied here. Mastodon picks none of two signatures, so there is no selection rule to match; `;bs` on a field the request lacks also raises unrescued in `linzer` (`NoMethodError`) and is refused here. (3) `check_webfinger!` raises on any lookup that does not confirm the handle, so Mastodon stores no account and leaves a cached one as it was. `WebfingerConfirmation.handleWrite` (`packages/domain/src/webfinger-confirmation.ts`) now does the same: a first-seen actor whose lookup does not answer (a timeout, a 5xx, a 4xx but 404 and 410) is not stored, and the fetch fails as an unanswered one (`VerificationUnavailable`), so the inbox answers 503 with `Retry-After` (`KeyUnavailable`) and the sender delivers again, a `resolve=true` search finds nothing and an account search answers 503, a mention is not made, and the next request asks WebFinger again. Before, the actor was cached under its URI authority and the delivery accepted. Every actor fetch but an actor's own `Update` runs `check_webfinger!` in Mastodon, so a cached actor whose round trip does not confirm a handle is left as it was, document and keys included, where it moved to its URI authority or was written under its stored handle. Its handle is then settled as Mastodon's refresh resolves the stored handle: `refreshRemoteActor` asks that handle's own WebFinger once the refetch failed, and an answer naming another account or linking another actor moves the former holder to its URI authority (`invalidate_username!`), so an actor cannot keep a handle its domain now gives to another by still declaring it or by failing its own WebFinger; a 404, a 410 or no answer leaves it. A refresh now costs at most seventeen subrequests per actor. `server/remote-actor-webfinger.test.ts` covers the inbox, search, mention and account search, the refresh, and a handle its domain hands to another actor.
- FED-8: an actor's keys are read as Mastodon v4.7.5's `ProcessAccountService#public_keys` reads them, also when its document names a key only by its id. A key given in full under the actor's URI or a fragment of it is taken as given. Any other entry, an id alone or a key object whose id is elsewhere (GoToSocial's `/main-key`), is fetched from its own URL when the actor is cached, and only when that URL is on the actor's host (`non_matching_uri_hosts?`, the port aside); a key id on another host is never fetched. An object counts only when its `owner` (`controller` for a Multikey) is the actor, and a fetched key only when its own document names the actor as its `owner` (the first of its `publicKey` for an actor stub, as GoToSocial serves; its `id` is not read) or, for a Multikey, has the key id as its `id` and the actor as its `controller`. At most ten entries of each list are read, so at most twenty keys are fetched, six at a time as a Worker opens six connections and a queued fetch's deadline already runs (`publishedKeys` and `resolvePublishedKeys` in `server/actor-keys.ts`). A refetch reads them again: a key whose URL answers anything but such a key is dropped, as `set_fetchable_key!` deletes it, and a fetch that gets no answer at all stops the refetch, as the error Mastodon's HTTP client raises does, so the actor keeps its keys; for an actor `Update` the inbox then answers 503 with `Retry-After` and releases the activity (`KeyUnavailable`), so the sender delivers it again, as Mastodon's job is retried. A signature under an unknown key id takes its key only from that set, as Mastodon looks the key up among those `ProcessAccountService` stored, and the document already fetched at the key id is not fetched again. A key id another actor holds now moves to the actor whose set holds it, as `upsert_all(..., unique_by: :uri)` moves it (`prisma/sql/upsertRemoteActorKeys.sql`); the set holds only keys the actor's host vouches for, a host that serves both actors. A `publicKeyMultibase` that is not of its base (a character base58 lacks; base64url that Ruby's `strict_decode64` refuses, such as bits left after the last byte) refuses the whole actor, embedded or fetched, as the `ArgumentError` Mastodon does not rescue; `Multibase::Error` (an unknown codec or base) still only skips the key (`multibaseKey` in `server/multibase.ts`). `scripts/generate-key-forms-reference.mjs` gained those values; their fixture entries were made by running Mastodon v4.7.5's `app/lib/multibase.rb` with `base58` 0.2.3. Tests: `server/key-forms.test.ts` (each value against Mastodon's reading), `server/actor-key-forms.test.ts` (a key named by id or at `/main-key` cached with its actor and kept on a refetch, kept while its host does not answer, dropped on a 404; a Multikey served under another id refused; key ids on another host never fetched; a key whose document names another owner, or that the actor's document gives to another, not taken; a key moved only when its own document names the new actor, not by another actor of the host or one on another host; an actor with a malformed `publicKeyMultibase` refused, and a cached one left as it was; twenty keys fetched six at a time; an actor `Update` answered 503 while a key it names does not answer, and applied on the retry).
- FED-16: a local note's `status` notification is sent from the queue, a page of notifying followers at a time, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `FanOutOnWriteService#deliver_to_all_followers!` reads them with `find_in_batches` in `DistributionWorker` and queues a `FeedInsertWorker`, then a `LocalNotificationWorker`, for each. Before, the publish request read them in one query and notified each inline: 60 notifying followers cost 2,141 D1 statements (2 cost 111), so past about 27 the invocation passed D1's 1,000 and the later notifications lost their stream event and push, and a scheduled post for 53 cost 1,896 in one cron run. Now the publish reads the first 50 (`NOTIFYING_FOLLOWER_PAGE_SIZE`, in follow id order, `listNotifyingFollowers` with the same 7-day activity rule and block and mute exclusions) and queues a `NotifyLocalNoteFollowers` job with a `Follower` step for each follower the note reaches and a `Page` step for the next page (`server/local-note-follower-notifications.ts`), so it costs 41 statements whatever the number of followers, scheduled posts included. A `Page` job reads the stored note's audience back (visibility, language, mentions, whom it answers); a `Follower` job stores the notification once (`notifyAccountOnce`; `insertNotificationOnce` now matches the sender's account id, so it serves local senders), so a redelivered job notifies nobody twice. A reply to someone else reads no follower, and a direct note reads only the followers it names (`listNotifyingFollowersAmong`, sought by follower), as `deliver_to_mentioned_followers!`. A queue send that fails is logged (`StatusNotificationsNotQueued`) and the publish still succeeds, as the note is stored. Scheduled posts are published 5 a run, chained while a full run publishes any, where one run of 20 made 822 statements. A queue invocation of 5 jobs makes at most 187 statements, its notifications' stream renders and pushes included. Differences kept: Mastodon's `FeedInsertWorker` reads the follow again when it runs; a queued `Follower` job does not, so an unfollow or `notify=false` in the seconds between the page and the job still notifies. A redelivered `Page` job starts a second chain of the later pages; its notifications are stored once, but the pages are read twice, as for `NotifyRemoteNoteBoosters` and `NotifyRemoteNoteFollowers`. No migration. Worker tests: `server/local-note-follower-notifications.test.ts`, `server/d1-budget.test.ts`, `server/notifying-followers-plan.test.ts`.
- FED-16, fetched notes: a note this server fetches and stores first is streamed and resolves its parent as Mastodon v4.7.5 does for every status it stores. (1) `Create#distribute` runs `DistributionWorker` for a status `FetchRemoteStatusService` stores within the six-hour real-time window, and `FanOutOnWriteService` treats it as a delivered one: the home and list feeds of the author's followers, the feeds of the hashtags' followers, and for a public status the hashtag and public streams. tstodon sent such a note's notifications but no stream event. Now a fetched note that is distributed (a boosted, quoted, replied-to, pinned, searched or thread-fetched one) is told to the `user` and `list` streams of the author's followers (`startHomeFanOut`) and, when public, to the public and hashtag streams (`startPublicFanOut`) as `update`, as an inbox `Create` is (`streamEvent` in `server/remote-status-persist.ts`). Only the run that holds the note's `Created` marker streams it, and only while it was published within the window of now, so a step that failed once and is resumed later does not show an old note as new. (2) `Create#resolve_thread` runs for every status Mastodon stores, whenever it was published. A reply tstodon fetches on its own (boosted, searched, pinned or a thread job's parent) whose parent it does not hold now queues its `ResolveRemoteThread` job, as an inbox `Create` or a `QuoteRequest`'s instrument did already. A note an outbox backfill stores queues none, as Mastodon reads no outbox. Loops and job storms: Mastodon bounds every fetch a request sets off, quotes and replies included, with one counter (`status_discovery_per_request`, `DISCOVERIES_PER_REQUEST` 1000). tstodon's jobs carry only a hop, which bounds a chain's depth, so a chain is kept to one line of parents (`thread` in `PersistOptions`, `ThreadResolve`). A note a thread job fetches queues its own job one hop further up the chain, so a chain stops at 1000 statuses (`THREAD_RESOLVE_HOP_LIMIT`). A quoted note, a reply a thread fetch finds listed, and a parent the thread fetch walks to itself queue none (`Skip`): where each could start its own chain, one forged activity could set off a tree of them. A reply whose chain reached the limit keeps its claim for good (`THREAD_RESOLVE_EXHAUSTED`), so no `Create`, job or context read starts the chain above it again. The walk of an opened status's parents (`backfillAncestors`, five at most) stops below such a reply (`findRemoteThreadResolveExhausted`, a seek by object URI, pinned in `server/remote-thread-resolve-plan.test.ts`), and when it stops at its own limit it queues the topmost note's job at the hop past the notes it stored. A cycle ends because the claim queues a job only while the parent is not stored, and a reply still gets one job. So one activity or one context read starts at most one chain, of at most 1000 parents, each of which may fetch up to three quoted notes (`MAX_QUOTE_DEPTH`). Mastodon also resolves the parents of quoted notes and listed replies within its 1000; tstodon does not, so their parents come from a context read. A walk that starts below a chain it did not finish, as on a note whose own job is lost, counts its hops from 1 again: a chain that is not exhausted can be walked again once a context read's refresh expires (15 minutes). Notes stored by a background job that stores many (a thread job, the ancestors walk and the listed replies of a context read) queue their stream fan-outs (`JobStart`, `startJob` in `server/inline-job.ts`) instead of running each first page in the job's own invocation, where twenty fresh replies could pass D1's 1000 statements; a fan-out the queue refuses is logged (`JobNotQueued`). An inbox delivery and a single fetch keep the first page after the response. The five-minute initial wait for a context fetch stays off: the replies walk would wait for it too (INT-4), and the parents of a backfilled note, a quoted note or a listed reply come only from there. No migration. Tests: `server/home-stream.test.ts`, `server/public-streams.test.ts`, `server/remote-thread-resolve.test.ts`, `server/remote-thread-backfill.test.ts`. What is left is in the FED-16 row.

- REST-18: grouped notifications are keyed as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Notification::Groups#set_group_key!` keys them. A notification of a groupable type (`GROUPABLE_NOTIFICATION_TYPES`: `favourite`, `reblog`, `follow`, `admin.sign_up`) stores its key when it is recorded, `{type prefix}-{hour bucket}`: the prefix is `favourite-{status id}` or `reblog-{status id}`, and `follow` or `admin.sign_up` alone, so every follower of one window shares one group; before, each follower was a group of their own (`follow-{sender id}`), and no group had a time bucket. The bucket is the hour the notification is recorded in (Unix seconds / 3600), or the bucket of the prefix's previous notification while that one was recorded less than `MAXIMUM_GROUP_SPAN_HOURS` (12) ago and the bucket began less than 12 hours before. Mastodon keeps that previous bucket in Redis for 12 hours after its last use; tstodon reads it back from the prefix's newest stored key (`findNotificationGroupBucket.sql`, two seeks of `idx_notifications_account_group`), one indexed read per groupable notification. The rule is `NotificationGroup.groupKey` (`packages/domain/src/notification-group.ts`), and `deliverNotification` (`server/notify.ts`) applies it before the insert. A notification the policy holds gets no key, as Mastodon gives a `filtered?` one none, and stays `ungrouped-{id}` after its request is accepted; before, it joined its group once accepted. Every reader uses the stored key: v1's `group_key` (`{key}` or `ungrouped-{id}`, as `NotificationSerializer`), the v2 page walk (`pageNotificationGroupsOlder` / `Newer`), the group summaries (`summarizeNotificationGroups`), `unread_count`, `GET /api/v2/notifications/:group_key`, its `/accounts` and `/dismiss` (`dismissNotificationGroup`). A group's members are sought by key through `idx_notifications_account_group` (migration 0167, Mastodon's `index_notifications_on_account_id_and_group_key`), not by status or sender. Without `grouped_types`, v2 groups all four groupable types, as `paginate_groups`; before, `admin.sign_up` was left ungrouped. `/api/v2/notifications/ungrouped-{id}/accounts` answers an empty list, as Mastodon's `where(group_key:)` matches no row; it listed the one sender before. Migration 0167 adds `notifications.group_key` and keys each existing groupable row that is not held by the hour it was recorded in, in one `UPDATE`. Remaining differences. (1) The rows written before 0167 are bucketed by their own hour, without the 12-hour span, so a group that ran across hours before the migration splits by hour. (2) Mastodon buckets by the activity's `created_at`; tstodon by the time it records the notification, which differs only for a remote boost, whose `Announce` Mastodon dates by its `published`. (3) Mastodon's Redis entry outlives a deleted notification; tstodon's previous bucket is read from the stored rows, so when the newest notification of a prefix is deleted (a remote actor's undone favourite or boost), the next one reads the bucket before it. (4) Bucket numbers compare as strings, which holds while they have six digits, until 2084. The sample of three accounts where Mastodon takes eight is recorded in the #75 and #76 entry. Tests: `packages/domain/src/notification-group.test.ts` (the prefixes, the bucket span and the Redis expiry), `server/deliver-notification.test.ts` (a key for an accepted notification, none for a held one), `server/grouped-notifications.test.ts` (two followers in one `follow-{bucket}` group in v1, v2 and `unread_count`; a favourite's key in both versions; a held favourite kept apart after its request is accepted), `server/notification-group-plans.test.ts` (the statements' query plans).

- REST-17: the lists Mastodon v4.7.5 pages now page as it does. Timelines (home, public, tag, list, link) send a `Link` after any page that is not empty, as `Api::V1::Timelines::BaseController` (`unless: -> { @statuses.empty? }`): `max_id` of the last status as `rel="next"`, `min_id` of the first as `rel="prev"`, keeping each controller's `PERMITTED_PARAMS` (`local` on home; `local`, `remote` and `only_media` on public; `local` and `only_media` on a tag, not `remote`, `any`, `all` or `none`; `url` on a link). v1 notifications offer `rel="next"` after any page that is not empty too. An account's statuses (keeping `AccountStatusesFilter::KEYS`), scheduled statuses and notification requests send `rel="next"` after a full page (`records_continue?`) and `min_id` as `rel="prev"`; follow requests, domain blocks, followed tags and a list's accounts send `since_id` as `rel="prev"`. Follow requests are one list of local and remote requests by follow row id (`listFollowRequests`), newest first, as Mastodon's `paginate_by_max_id`; before, remote requests were not paged and the list was sorted by date. `GET /api/v1/domain_blocks` pages by `BLOCK_LIMIT` (100, at most 200) and `GET /api/v1/followed_tags` by `TAGS_LIMIT` (100, at most 200), each by its row id: migration 0166 gives `account_domain_blocks` and `followed_tags` an `id` shaped like `newEntityId()` from `created_at` and the rowid, and a trigger gives every row inserted without one the same, so no insert path can leave a row out of the pages. `GET /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts` pages 40 (at most 80) members by account id, a remote member by its outbound follow id, and answers every member with no `Link` for exactly `limit=0` (`unlimited?`), as the web UI asks. `unread_count` counts at most `limit` notifications (100 by default, at most 1000, `paginate_by_min_id(limit, ...).count`), and v2's at most `limit` groups. A `min_id` page of scheduled statuses or notification requests is answered newest first, as `to_a_paginated_by_id` reverses it, so its `rel="prev"` is its newest id. A bare `?limit` with no `=` is Rack's `nil` and takes the default (`rackQueryParam` in `server/http.ts`); before, it read as empty, an empty page. The web UI already followed these `Link` headers: a timeline that got no `rel="next"` stopped loading more after its first page, and now scrolls on. A `min_id` page is answered newest first everywhere Mastodon's `to_a_paginated_by_id` (or a feed's `Status` default scope) does: the timelines, v1 notifications, an account's statuses, favourites, bookmarks, scheduled statuses and notification requests, so `rel="prev"` names the page's newest id and a client catching up moves a page at a time. A page merged from several queries (home, lists, public, tag, link, a remote profile) ends at the earliest last row of any query that read a full `limit` (`timelinePage` in `server/timeline-documents.ts`): before, an entry of that query that did not render (a boost of a note from a domain no longer federated) was padded with another query's older row, and `max_id` of that row skipped the first query's unread rows. A page that cut leaves short links `rel="next"` to the cut. A remote profile's `Link` follows the rows read, not the rendered statuses. A domain or tag rename keeps the row's `id`, so its place in the pages does not move; the `(account_id, id)` indexes are not unique, as the renamed row is written before the old one is deleted. What is left is REST-17. Tests: `server/pagination-links.test.ts`, `server/pagination-plans.test.ts` (the new statements' query plans), and `server/http.test.ts` for `rackQueryParam`.
- DM-11: a quote silently mentions the quoted author, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Quote#ensure_quoted_access`, so that author passes `StatusPolicy#show?` on a followers-only or direct quote. Migration 0165 adds `silent` to `status_mentions` and `status_remote_mentions`, as 0113 did for `remote_status_mentions`. A local note that quotes writes the mention when it is posted (`publishLocalNote`, so the scheduled publisher too), whatever the quote's state, as `PostStatusService#attach_quote!` does before `accept!`: a local quoted author in `status_mentions`, a remote one in `status_remote_mentions`. A remote quote of a local note already got one when its `QuoteRequest` is accepted (`ensureQuotedAccess`); Mastodon's `Create` never accepts such a quote, so no other path writes one. A mention the note already makes stays a real one. A silent mention is no mention to show: `listStatusMentionsByIds` and `listStatusRemoteMentions` read `silent = 0`, so the REST `mentions`, the note's `Mention` tags and `to`/`cc`, conversation participants, the notification policy's private-mention check, and the mention checks of `listNotifyingFollowers` and the home feed's `mentionsBlockedOrMutedSql` (`active_mentions`) leave it out, and so do a local account's direct timeline and conversations, for local and cached remote notes alike (`listDirectStatusesForAccount`, `listRemoteDirectStatusesForAccount`, and `listShownRemoteStatusMentions` for participants), as Mastodon's `participants_from_status` reads `active_mentions` and only a mention notification pushes a conversation. An accepted direct `QuoteRequest` no longer publishes a conversation update to the quoted author. It grants access where Mastodon's `mentions` do: `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id` (`listViewerStatusAccessJson`), a followed author's direct note on the home timeline (`deliver_to_mentioned_followers!`), account statuses and the outbox (`AccountStatusesFilter`), status search, the reach of a stamp `Delete`, a signed fetch of a followers-only or direct note (`noteAddresses` in `server/routes/activitypub.ts` now reads `status_remote_mentions`, `hasStatusRemoteMention`), and a remote vote in its poll (`canVote`). No mention notification is sent, and nothing new federates: the quoted author was already notified with `quote`, and a remote one was already addressed and delivered to. An edit that drops a mention now leaves it silent instead of deleting it (`ProcessMentionsService#assign_mentions!`), for local accounts and remote actors (`replaceStatusMentions`, `replaceStatusRemoteMentions`), so whoever was mentioned keeps access, and the quoted author's silent mention survives an edit that mentioned and then dropped it; a mention made again is promoted. `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id/quotes` now skips `StatusFilter` for the quoted author, as `QuotesController` does, and renders a suspended author's quote for that reader (`StatusAuthors.existing`), its account stripped. Migration 0165 backfills the quotes stored before it: each local quoting note's quoted author, local or a cached remote actor, and the local quoted author of a remote quote a `QuoteRequest` accepted (or revoked since) before `ensureQuotedAccess` existed. Each of the three statements walks `status_quotes` once by a unique index and seeks the rest by key, so the backfill reads each quote three times. Differences kept: the remote quoted author is also named in the quote note's `cc` (`NoteQuote.addressAuthor`), where Mastodon only delivers to it; a receiving Mastodon turns that into the same silent mention. A quote by an author on a domain the instance suspends or keeps off its allow list (`Hidden`) is still left out for the quoted author. The backfill skips a quote whose quoted note is gone, as nothing names its author, where Mastodon keeps `quoted_account_id`. Tests: `server/quoted-author-access.test.ts` (a followers-only local quote opens for the quoted author with no mention, no `mention` notification and no `Mention` tag; an edit that drops mentions keeps them silent and a mention made again is shown; a remote quoted author and a remote actor an edit dropped still fetch the note; the quoted author lists a suspended author's quote; the backfill and its plan), `server/status-quotes-list.test.ts` (the quoted author lists followers-only and direct quotes, local and remote, and opens each), `server/remote-poll-vote.test.ts` (a silently mentioned actor votes in a followers-only poll), `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts` (a direct quote opens for the quoted author with no conversation, stream event or direct timeline entry).
- REST-16: every paged REST list reads `limit` as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Api::BaseController#limit_param`: the default only when there is no `limit`, else `[params[:limit].to_i.abs, max].min`. Before, any value below 1 took the default. Now `limit=-5` asks for 5, and `limit=0`, `limit=abc` or `limit=` give an empty page. `rubyToI` reads the value as Ruby's `String#to_i` does: `5abc` is 5, ` 5` and `+5` are 5, `1_0` is 10, and `0x10` is 0. One parser serves every list, `queryLimit(c, PageSize.<family>)` in `server/http.ts`, and it replaces `queryLimit`, `queryAccountsLimit`, `queryNotificationsLimit`, `queryTrendsLimit` and `queryAdminLimit`. It reads the raw query string, because the params cast leaves out a number that does not parse. `PageSize` holds Mastodon's controller constants: statuses 20 (at most 40, also conversations and search), accounts 40 (80, also notifications), trends 10 (20), admin lists 100 (200), and admin domain blocks and domain allows 100 (500, `MAX_LIMIT`).
