Three sizes were wrong before: search defaulted to 5, not `RESULTS_LIMIT` 20, the admin domain rules capped at 200, and the admin trend publishers used the trends size 10, not `PreviewCardProvidersController::LIMIT` 100 (at most 200). `GET /api/v1/endorsements` answers every endorsement, with no `Link`, for a `limit` of exactly `0`, as `EndorsementsController#unlimited?` does; `00` is an empty page, and an account's endorsements have no such rule. Mastodon's other `unlimited?`, on a list's accounts, already holds, as tstodon answers every member (REST-17). Search answers 400 for a negative `limit` or `offset` (`require_valid_pagination_options!`). Search and account search answer nothing for `limit=0`, as `SearchService` and `AccountSearchService` do, an exact match included. A conversation page with `min_id` and `limit=0` returned every conversation (`slice(-0)`), and is now empty. The `Link` header keeps `limit` and the filters the controller permits, as `Api::Pagination#pagination_params` does, plus the cursor. That is `limit` alone on most lists, the notification filters on v1 and v2 notifications, and `FILTER_PARAMS` on the admin accounts (v1, v2) and reports lists. Before, it kept every query parameter but the cursors, `offset` and unknown ones included. Two differences are deliberate. Mastodon answers 500 for `limit=0` on most lists paged by `max_id`, because `records_continue?` holds for an empty page and `pagination_max_id` reads the id of a missing record. tstodon answers an empty page with no `Link`, as Mastodon's timelines do. On the trends, which page by `offset`, Mastodon answers `limit=0` with a `rel="next"` (and past the first page a `rel="prev"`) that points at the same page; tstodon sends no `Link` there either. Scope versus 404 order is not part of this change. What is left is REST-17. Tests: `server/limit-param.test.ts` (an account's statuses, blocks, conversations, admin domain allows, search), `server/pagination-link-params.test.ts`, `server/status-quotes-list.test.ts`, and `server/http.test.ts` for `rubyToI`, `limitParam` and the `PageSize` constants.
- DM-10: `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id/quotes` and status search filter as Mastodon v4.7.5 does. The quotes list follows `Api::V1::Statuses::QuotesController#index`. It needs a token with `read` or `read:statuses`, and an app token with no user is enough (`doorkeeper_authorize!`). The status is sought first, because `set_status` runs before that check: a status the reader may not see answers 404 with or without a token, and one it may see answers 401 without one. The page holds the accepted quotes whose quoting status exists. In SQL, before `LIMIT`, it leaves out quotes by a local or remote author that the reader blocks, mutes, or is blocked by (`not_excluded_by_account`, `listQuotePage` in `server/quote-store.ts`). The rest go through `StatusFilter` (`permittedStatusIds`). Mastodon skips the filter for the reader who wrote the quoted status, whom each quote silently mentions (`Quote#ensure_quoted_access`); tstodon records no such mention (DM-11), so that reader keeps the visibility check (`viewableStatusIds`) and skips the rest: it sees a silenced author's quote and a quote from a domain it blocks, but not a followers-only or direct quote it may not open. The list was filtered by `StatusPolicy#show?` alone, after the limit, and answered an anonymous reader. Quotes come newest quoting status first and are paged by quote id, as `paginate_by_max_id` on `quotes`: `max_id` and `since_id` apply together, and `min_id` is not read. The `Link` cursors are quote ids, not status ids: `next` comes when the page before the filter is full, and `prev` (`since_id`) comes from that page's first quote unless the filtered page is empty. Status search (`searchStatuses`) follows the `StatusFilter` run of `StatusesSearchService`. A silenced local account's status is left out only for a reader who neither is that account nor follows it (accepted), and a suspended account's status is left out for every reader. Before, both were left out for everyone. The filter stays in SQL before `OFFSET`, so a page skips only what its reader sees. Mastodon filters after its Elasticsearch page, so its page can come back short; tstodon keeps the full page, as since #72. Each statement seeks the quotes, the quoting statuses, the author's moderation row, and the follow by key (`server/status-filter-sql-plan.test.ts`). Two differences remain: a `limit` below 1 takes the default, as on every paged list (REST-16), and the quoted author has no silent mention, so it sees fewer quotes than on Mastodon (DM-11). Tests: `server/status-quotes-list.test.ts`, `server/status-search.test.ts`, `server/status-filter-sql-plan.test.ts`.

- FED-17: creating a domain block checks the rule already covering its domain, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Api::V1::Admin::DomainBlocksController#create`: `DomainBlock.rule_for(domain)` (`findDomainBlockRule` in `server/admin-domain-block-store.ts`, the block on the host or its nearest parent) refuses the new block when it names the same domain or the new block is not `stricter_than?` it (`DomainBlockPolicy.stricterThan` in `packages/domain`). `POST /api/v1/admin/domain_blocks` then answers 422 with `ExistingDomainBlockErrorSerializer`'s `{ error, existing_domain_block }` (`ApiAdminExistingDomainBlockError`), the error "You have already imposed stricter limits on <domain>."; before, only an exact duplicate was refused, with a plain validation error. A suspension under any rule, or a silence that keeps a parent silence's `reject_media` and `reject_reports`, is still created. `/admin/instances/:domain` refuses the same block with Mastodon's "you need to unblock it first" and links the covering block; an existing block on the domain itself is changed there, as Mastodon's edit form, and `PUT` never compares rules, as `#update`. `Hostname.fromInput` stores an internationalized domain in its ASCII form (`TagManager#normalize_domain`), for user domain blocks, allows, email domain blocks and imports too; a name with URL syntax in it (`@`, `?`, `#`, `\`) is left as typed, as an ASCII one, so `DomainValidator` (`Hostname.isValidDomain`) refuses it. Sign-up matches an email domain block as `EmailDomainBlock::Matcher`: the address's host in ASCII and each domain above it (`domain_variants`), so a block on `bücher.de` refuses `a@mail.bücher.de`. Rows stored in Unicode before are renamed by the hourly `AsciiDomainNames` job from migration 0164's cursor (`server/domain-name-ascii.ts`): SQLite has no punycode, and lookups that matched either form could not reach the admin instance page, whose path is redirected to the ASCII name. Where the ASCII name is stored already the rows merge: a domain block keeps the stricter severity and every rejection of the two, the other tables the earlier `created_at`; a renamed block's `domain_key` follows by migration 0163's trigger. A Unicode block applied to no actor (remote hosts are ASCII), so once a page commits the job queues what making each moved block does: `ApplyDomainBlockEffects` severs the follows of a suspension and clears the media of `reject_media` from the block's row as it stands, and `SeverBlockedDomainFollows` severs a user's follows under their own block; both find nothing they already did. Create checks the covering rule first and then answers Mastodon's validations together, in the model's order: `Validation failed: Severity is not included in the list, Domain can't be blank` (or `Domain is invalid`); an unknown severity is compared by its rejections only, as `stricter_than?` reads one; update answers the severity message too (before, 422 "domain is required" on create and 400 on update). The admin page of a remote account links "View domain block" when a rule applies to its domain, else "View summary for domain" and "Add new domain block" (the instance page's block form, `#new-domain-block`, which carries the domain), as `Admin::AccountsController#show` (`rule_for(account.domain)`); Mastodon's REST `Admin::Account` has no domain rule, so the API is unchanged. Per-account limitation at block time stays in FED-17.
- REST-15: the public and local timelines, their streams, and `exclude_replies` on an account's statuses keep a reply only when it answers its own author, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Status.without_replies` (`reply = false`, or `in_reply_to_account_id = account_id`) in `PublicFeed#without_replies_scope`, `FanOutOnWriteService#broadcast_to_public_streams!` and `AccountStatusesFilter#no_replies_scope`. Before, the public timelines showed every reply, and `exclude_replies` dropped every reply, Alice's thread of her own posts included. The filters read the columns REST-14 stores (`WITHOUT_REPLIES_SQL` in `server/status-timeline-store.ts`, `REMOTE_WITHOUT_REPLIES_SQL` in `server/remote-status-timeline-store.ts`): a local status is a reply when it names a parent or whom it answers, so a reply whose parent was deleted stays one, as Mastodon's `reply` flag stays; a remote note is a reply when it has an `inReplyTo`, so a reply to a note not held, which answers no one, stays out, as in Mastodon. The public streams render each entry through the public timeline query (`server/public-fan-out.ts`), so they follow without a change of their own. The hashtag and link timelines keep replies, as Mastodon's `TagFeed` and `LinkFeed` apply no reply scope. The predicates read each row's own columns, so each statement keeps its plan (the same `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` before and after) and no statement is added. Difference kept: a local reply whose parent was deleted before migration 0162 lost both its parent and whom it answered, so it now counts as no reply and shows on the public timelines; nothing stored tells it apart, so there is nothing to backfill. Mastodon's other callers of `without_replies`, the account RSS feed and email subscriptions (REST-7), are not served. `account-status-filters.test.ts` and `remote-account-statuses.test.ts` expected `exclude_replies` to drop a reply to the author's own status; they now expect it kept. `api-params-contract.test.ts` now replies to another account's status, and `blocked-by-author-lists.test.ts` no longer expects a remote reply to the viewer on the public timeline. Tests: `server/without-replies.test.ts` (both public timelines, `public:local`, a local and a remote account under `exclude_replies`), `server/without-replies-plan.test.ts` (each statement still seeks its index; a public page walks `(visibility, id)` without a sort).
- FED-17: an instance domain rule (`silence`, `suspend`, `reject_media`, `reject_reports`) covers the subdomains of the domain it names, and each host follows its most specific rule, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `DomainBlock.rule_for` (`domain_variants`, ordered `by_domain_length`): under a suspension of `example.com`, a `noop` on `open.example.com` exempts that host. `suspendedDomains`, `mediaRejectedDomains` and the new `reportsRejectedDomains` (`server/admin-domain-block-store.ts`) return `CoveredHosts` (`packages/domain`), so `RemoteActor.onAnyHost`, `checkFederatedHost`'s blocked hosts, delivery, inbox refusal and `Flag` handling all match parent domains. SQL reads the view `remote_actor_domain_rules` (migration 0163): triggers store each host's labels in reverse order (`remote_actors.host_key`, `uri_host_key`, `domain_blocks.domain_key`, spelled by the one view `host_keys`, which reads a name as `Hostname.ofAuthority` does), so the actors under a rule are one index range, never a `LIKE` scan. It replaces the exact `domain_blocks` subqueries in `DOMAIN_RESTRICTED_ACTORS_SQL`, `DOMAIN_SUSPENDED_ACTORS_SQL`, the public timeline, `listKnownRemoteInboxes`, `listRemoteNoteReplies`, `listRemoteActorPinnedStatuses`, follow request approval, account search and the notification group statements, whose suspended-hosts JSON parameter is gone. `listUnfollowedSilencedAuthorsJson` expands each listed actor's hosts into their parent domains with a recursive CTE and seeks `domain_blocks.domain`. A suspension severs the follows of the subdomains' actors and a `reject_media` clears their media, as `BlockDomainService` and `ClearDomainMediaService` act on `Account.by_domain_and_subdomains`. Three surfaces stay exact, as in Mastodon: a user's domain block (`domain_blocking?`), the allow list (`DomainAllow.rule_for` is `find_by(domain:)`), and peer search (`Instance.searchable`). Mastodon also stores a block's silence or suspension on each account when the block is made (`silenced_at`, `suspended_at` over `by_domain_and_subdomains`, a narrower rule notwithstanding), so an account known before a narrower `noop` stays limited there; tstodon reads the rules on each request, so the narrower rule always wins. Creating a block under a parent rule is left in FED-17.
- REST-14: a reply's `in_reply_to_account_id` follows Mastodon v4.7.5's `carried_over_reply_to_account_id`: a reply to its author's own reply answers whom that reply answers, any other reply its parent's author, so Alice's reply to her own reply to Bob names Bob. Before, the REST entity, home's and lists' reply filter (`feedRepliesSql`, `feedLocalRepliesSql`) and the context's self-reply order (`promoteSelfReplies`) read the parent's author, so such a reply stayed on the home of a follower of Alice who does not follow Bob and moved up a context. Mastodon decides this when it saves the reply with its parent (`Status#set_conversation`) and never again, so tstodon now stores it then: migration 0162 adds `in_reply_to_account_id` (a local account) and `in_reply_to_actor_uri` (a remote actor) to `statuses` and `remote_statuses`. A local note sets them in the statement that inserts it (`insertLocalNote.sql`, `setStatusReplyUri.sql`), a remote note in its insert (`insertRemoteStatus.sql`, with the local parent `parseLocalStatusId` finds), and a remote note stored after its replies links each reply that answers no one yet (the `remote_statuses_link_replies` trigger, Mastodon's `ThreadResolveWorker`). A status that arrives above an already linked reply changes nothing below it, as in Mastodon: Alice's reply to her own reply to a note not yet held answers no one, also once the note arrives. The readers now read the columns, so a timeline page costs the same statements and fewer subqueries (the reply filter seeked the parent per row; `d1-budget` unchanged), and the reply's own write gains no statement. The notification path reads them too, and the walk of `server/own-ancestry.ts` (one recursive query, up to 1000 steps) is deleted with its two SQL files. The REST field is kept when the parent is deleted, as Mastodon nullifies only `in_reply_to_id` and its `reply` flag stays: a parent's stored account counts as a reply's, so the author's reply under a reply whose own parent was deleted still carries it over. The migration fills in the statuses stored before in one statement a table that walks each thread down once from where an author's run of their own statuses starts, so each row is read and written once through the reply indexes (the walk up from every reply was quadratic in a long self-thread, a risk for D1's 30 s statement limit on a large cache). A reply whose parent was deleted before the migration answers no one, as its parent's author went with `in_reply_to_id`. It matches a remote reply to a local status by the `/users/<username>/statuses/<id>` ending of its URI as migration 0138 does, since the origin is configuration. Differences kept: Mastodon computes it again from the parent's current value when the reply itself is edited (`set_conversation` runs on every save), tstodon keeps the first; a deleted local account is not nulled out of replies that answer it (Mastodon's foreign key nullifies); and the public timelines and `exclude_replies` did not read it yet (REST-15, since done). `server/thread-context-limits.test.ts` promoted a reply to its author's own reply to someone else; its fixture now stores whom each reply answers, and the case is a reply that answers its own author under a parent that does not load. Tests: `server/reply-account.test.ts` (REST local and remote, the late parent, home, the context), `server/reply-account-backfill.test.ts` (values, and each reply's children sought by key), `server/reply-account-plan.test.ts` (each parent and the trigger's replies are sought by key); domain: `status-notification-audience.test.ts`.
- FED-16, the late parent: a remote reply whose parent tstodon does not hold is linked when the parent arrives, and a self-reply then sends its `status` notification, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `ThreadResolveWorker`. Before, a self-reply whose parent came late counted as a reply to someone else and notified nobody, also once the parent was stored, and the parent came only when someone read the reply's `/context`. Now a note an inbox `Create` or a `QuoteRequest`'s instrument stores queues a `ResolveRemoteThread` job, from its late steps, when it replies to an http(s) URL off this server that names no stored status (`Create#resolve_thread`, `Request.valid_url?`; `server/remote-thread-resolve.ts`). The job (`server/remote-thread-resolve-job.ts`) fetches the parent from its URI and stores it under an author on that host (`FetchRemoteStatusService`), which links the reply, since tstodon links a reply by its `inReplyTo`; a parent published within the real-time window is distributed as any fetched note is. When the reply was published within the six-hour real-time window (`within_realtime_window?`), the job distributes it again as `DistributionWorker` with `skip_notifications` does: no mention notification, but the `status` notification to each notifying follower `FeedInsertWorker#notify?` would reach (the followers it names for a limited or direct reply), paged as a delivered note's are, and the home and public stream events (`redistributeRemoteNote` in `server/remote-status-persist.ts`). A parent that is itself a reply to a status not held queues its own job, as the parent's `Create` does in Mastodon, so the chain above fills in one job a status. The jobs run on their own queue, `tstodon-pull` (`PULL_QUEUE`, `app/queues/tstodon-pull.ts`: batches of 1, `max_concurrency` 2, `max_retries` 3, dead-lettering to `tstodon-outbox-dlq`), as Mastodon runs the worker on its `pull` queue: a forged chain of 1000 slow parents never holds up deliveries on the single outbox consumer. Terraform creates and attaches it (`pull_queue_name`, `pull_consumer_*`), and the deploy, launch and runbook pages list it. Bounds: one job a reply, and at most 1000 statuses a chain (`hop`, Mastodon's `DISCOVERIES_PER_REQUEST`, the per-request-id counter the chain shares). Migration 0161 adds `remote_statuses.thread_resolve_queued_at`, a claim taken by the statement that checks the parent, so a redelivered `Create`, a retried job or a second route to the same reply queues nothing more; a claim whose job could not be sent is given back and the delivery or job retried, and one older than an hour counts as lost and can be taken again. The job reads what is stored at each step: a stored parent is not fetched again, and a notification is stored once (`notifyFromRemoteOnce`), so a retried or redelivered job fetches nothing twice and notifies once. A parent server that cannot be reached or does not answer in time retries the job (`retry: 3`); any answer that is no note, a 5xx included, ends it, as `FetchRemoteStatusService` reads any HTTP error as no status (a 5xx now carries its `status` on `VerificationUnavailable`). A self-reply is now decided as Mastodon's `carried_over_reply_to_account_id` decides `in_reply_to_account_id`, for remote and local notes: a reply to its author's own reply takes that reply's answer, so Alice's reply to her own reply to Bob, or to her own reply whose parent is not linked, notifies nobody (`NoteThread.of` in `packages/domain`; `server/own-ancestry.ts` climbs the author's own statuses in one recursive query, at most 1000). REST, home and the context's self-reply order still read the parent's author (REST-14). Differences kept: Mastodon's job returns at once when an earlier run linked the reply; tstodon cannot tell that from a parent that arrived on its own, so it distributes again, which notifies nobody twice but tells the streams again (a client drops a status it holds). Mastodon's discovery counter also counts the other fetches under the request id and expires after five idle minutes. Mastodon stores the carried account when it links a reply, tstodon reads the statuses as they are now. A direct reply's conversation stream is not told again. The rest is in the FED-16 row. Mastodon's five-minute initial wait for a context fetch (`FETCH_REPLIES_INITIAL_WAIT_MINUTES`) stays off, for the replies walk too (INT-4): a note found by search or through a boost gets no thread job, so its parents still come from the context fetch, and the interop sweep's steps where tstodon reads a Mastodon thread's replies (`repliesOfRoot`, `repliesOfReplies` and the five-deep thread in `e2e/mastodon/probe-threads.ts`) resolve a root seconds old and wait for its replies; the sweep dates only Mastodon's copy back (`ageOnMastodon`), so restoring the wait would hold those steps for five minutes. Worker tests: `server/remote-thread-resolve.test.ts`, `server/status-notification-audience.test.ts`; domain: `status-notification-audience.test.ts`.
- DM-10: `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id/context` and `GET /api/v1/statuses?id[]=` filter statuses as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Status.permitted_statuses_from_ids` does with `StatusFilter#filtered?` (`permittedStatusIds` in `server/status-filter.ts`). The context hid every status from a silenced or suspended domain (`restrictedDomains`), even from a reader who follows the author, and did not hide a silenced account at all: a local account the admin silenced, or a remote actor silenced on its own (`remote_actor_moderation`). Now a status by a silenced author, silenced on its own or by its domain's `silence` block, is left out only for a reader who does not follow the author (an accepted `follows` or `outbound_follows` row), and an anonymous reader never sees it, as `silenced_account?`. The reader's own statuses are always kept. The other conditions stay as they were: `StatusPolicy#show?` (`viewableStatusIds`), blocks either way and active mutes (`listSuppressedAuthors`), and the reader's domain blocks. A suspended author's status, on its own or by its domain, is left out by the renderer (`AccountPresentation.available`), as `StatusPolicy#show?` refuses an `unavailable?` author. The batch lookup ran only `StatusPolicy#show?`, so it also showed statuses by authors the reader blocks, mutes or domain-blocks; it now runs the same filter. `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id` still answers to `StatusPolicy#show?` alone, as Mastodon's `show`. The silence check is one statement, `prisma/sql/listUnfollowedSilencedAuthorsJson.sql`, which replaces the read of the restricted domains (deleted with `listRestrictedDomains.sql`), so the context runs as many statements as before, and the batch lookup at most five more, however many ids it names; each listed author, and each silencing domain block, is sought by key (`server/status-filter-plan.test.ts`; migration 0160 folds any domain block stored in another case to lower case). A follow request not yet accepted does not count. A failed lookup keeps only the reader's own statuses. The quotes list and status search now filter too (the DM-10 entry above). Tests: `server/thread-context.test.ts`, `server/visibility-batch-queries.test.ts`, `server/status-filter-plan.test.ts`.
- FED-16: the `status` notification ("notify me when this account posts") goes where Mastodon v4.7.5's `FanOutOnWriteService` and `FeedInsertWorker#notify?` send it, in four more cases. (1) A note that actively mentions an account or actor the follower blocks or mutes, a mute that keeps notifications included, notifies that follower no more, local or remote, as `FeedManager#filter_from_home` filters it (`crutches[:active_mentions]`). The notifying-follower queries (`listNotifyingFollowers`, `listNotifyingRemoteFollowers`) leave such a follower out, and a queued `Status` step reads it again when it runs (`findNotifyingRemoteFollow`), as `FeedInsertWorker` filters when it runs. A silent mention is not an active one. A unary `+` on each block and mute target column keeps SQLite seeking them by the follower, not by every account that blocks or mutes the mentioned one, as D1 keeps no `sqlite_stat1` (2.15 s to 12 ms for 5,000 followers of an account 3,000 others block and mute; `server/notifying-followers-plan.test.ts` pins the plans). (2) Only followers who signed in recently are notified, as `followers_for_local_distribution` keeps the users `User.signed_in_recently` (`current_sign_in_at` within `User::ACTIVE_DURATION`, 7 days). tstodon already records each UTC day an account made an authenticated request, web app or API token (`account_activity_days`, migration 0124), which is what Mastodon's `UserTrackingConcern` moves `current_sign_in_at` on, at most once a day; a follower counts when it has a day in the last week (`signedInRecentlySinceDay`). No migration. (3) A note this server fetches and stores first is distributed when it was published within the real-time window (6 hours), as Mastodon's `Create#distribute` distributes a status `FetchRemoteStatusService` stored only then (`override_timestamps` is set for inbox deliveries only): a boosted note, a quoted one, a reply's parent or a reply a thread fetch stores, a pinned one (`Add`, or the featured collection, as `FetchFeaturedCollectionService`), a search result. Its notifying followers get the `status` notification and the local accounts it mentions their `mention`, which a fetched note sent none of before. A note an outbox backfill stores (`delivery: "Backfilled"`) notifies nobody, as Mastodon reads no outbox, and nor does a quoted note fetched below one. A `QuoteRequest`'s instrument published before the window now notifies its mentions no more either. A distributed fetched note writes the `Created` marker an inbox delivery writes (`remote_status_owed`), so a step that fails once is finished by the next delivery or fetch that brings it (`resumeCreated`, and `resumeFetched` for a boost of it), and whether it is within the window is decided against the instant the marker records, as Mastodon decides once, when it creates the status. Home was already read-time, so a fetched note was on it before. (4) A remote note's notifying followers are read 50 at a time in follow id order (`NOTIFYING_FOLLOWER_PAGE_SIZE`): the inbox request reads the first page, and a `NotifyRemoteNoteFollowers` job each later one, queuing a `FinishRemoteNoteRecipient` job for each follower the note reaches and the next page, as Mastodon's `find_in_batches`. Worker tests: `server/remote-status-notification.test.ts`, `server/status-notification-audience.test.ts`. What is left is in the FED-16 row.
- DM-8: `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id/context` limits and orders a thread as Mastodon v4.7.5's `ContextsController` and `Status::ThreadingConcern`. Each level is still limited before the visibility and block filters, because Mastodon does the same (`ancestor_ids(limit)` and `descendant_ids(limit, depth)`, then `permitted_statuses_from_ids`), so a filtered thread can come back short there too. Three differences are fixed. An anonymous reader gets up to 40 ancestors (`ANCESTORS_LIMIT`), not 20; descendants stay 60 at depth 20, and a signed-in reader gets 4096 of each. Descendants come in Mastodon's `ORDER BY path` order, depth first with siblings by id, and the limit is taken in that order: `prisma/sql/listThreadDescendantIds.sql` orders SQLite's recursive queue by depth descending, then id, which pops rows in exactly that pre-order without building a path, and stops after `limit + 1` rows. The walk is linear: a 4096-reply chain takes about 25 ms (a path column with an `instr` loop check took about 1 s). The depth bound and the limit end a forged loop, and the opened status cannot recur; Mastodon's `NOT id = ANY(path)` is not needed, since a status names one parent. After the filter, replies to their author's own status move to the top, each group in order (`promote_by!`, `promoteSelfReplies` in `server/thread-context.ts`); the query reads the parent's author from its row, as Mastodon's `in_reply_to_account_id`, so a parent that does not load still counts. The `Mastodon-Async-Refresh` header follows `show` too: only a signed-in reader queues a thread fetch, any reader is told of a running one whatever the status's age. Mastodon's five-minute initial wait (`FETCH_REPLIES_INITIAL_WAIT_MINUTES`) is not applied: Mastodon fetches a new reply's ancestors when the reply arrives (`ThreadResolveWorker`), tstodon only through this fetch, so the wait left a fresh reply's parent missing in the e2e sweep (INT-4 keeps the difference). The filter itself still differs (DM-10). Tests: `server/status-context.test.ts`, `server/thread-context-limits.test.ts`, `server/remote-thread-backfill.test.ts`.
- REST-6: `GET /api/v1/peers/search` is served as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Api::V1::Peers::SearchController` without Elasticsearch. It lists up to 10 known peer hostnames that start with `q`, leaving out domains the instance blocks (`Instance.searchable.domain_starts_with`), sorted by name. The sort is deliberate: Mastodon without Elasticsearch names no order, and with it ranks by account count, which tstodon does not keep per domain; a name order is stable and seeks the `host` index by range. `q` is normalized as `TagManager#normalize_domain` (stripped of NUL and ASCII whitespace as Ruby's `strip`, one trailing `/` dropped, lower-cased, IDNA ASCII, one trailing dot dropped); a `q` Addressable refuses as a host gives `[]`, and a blank `q` gives `null`, as Mastodon renders its unset `@domains`. Anyone may call it. Under limited federation an anonymous call answers 401 and a signed-in one 404, the order Mastodon's callbacks run in. Mastodon's `peers_api_enabled` setting has no counterpart, so the endpoint is always on, as `instance/peers` is. Differences: the domains are hostnames without a port, as `instance/peers` lists them, where Mastodon's `instances` view holds `accounts.domain` with its port. The response schema is `ApiPeerSearch` in `@tstodon/api`. Tests: `server/peers-search.test.ts`.

- Home and lists drop what `FeedManager` filters. Mastodon v4.7.5 runs every status bound for a home through `FeedManager#filter_from_home`, for a list through `filter_from_list?` and then `filter_from_home(:list)`, and a followed hashtag's post through `filter_from_tags?`. tstodon's home and lists are queries, and two of those rules were missing from them. (1) A status whose active mentions name an account or actor the viewer blocks or mutes is dropped, a mute without notifications included, and so is a boost whose boosted status does (`crutches[:active_mentions]` against `blocking` and `muting`). The viewer's own statuses stay. This covers followed local authors' notes and boosts, followed-hashtag notes, followed remote actors' notes, remote boosts, and lists (`mentionsBlockedOrMutedSql` in `server/followed-feed.ts`). Each check seeks a mention table (`status_mentions`, `status_remote_mentions`, `remote_status_mentions` without silent rows, `remote_status_remote_mentions`) by the status id and checks its rows against the viewer's blocks and mutes, gathered once (a unary `+` keeps SQLite from seeking once per mute: 9.9 s to 16 ms over 30,000 statuses and 2,000 mutes; `server/feed-mentions-plan.test.ts` pins the plan). It adds no statement and needs no index. (2) Home drops a followed author's reply unless it answers its own author, the viewer, or an account or actor the viewer follows, and drops a reply whose parent this instance does not hold (no `in_reply_to_account_id`). That is the rule a list with `replies_policy: followed` already applied, so home shares `feedLocalRepliesSql` and `feedRepliesSql`. (3) A followed hashtag brings what `filter_from_tags?` keeps, which reads no exclusive lists, no reply rule and no follow languages: `FeedInsertWorker` pushes to home when either path passes. So the exclusive-list, reply and language rules apply to the follow branch of `listHomeStatuses` only, the local hashtag side keeps exclusive-list members, and the remote hashtag query keeps followed actors, home listing a note both queries return once. (4) A muted conversation stays on home and lists, as Mastodon reads `ConversationMute` only in `NotifyService`; notifications and the direct timeline still leave it out. This supersedes the "muted conversations" in the home entries below. The home stream asks the same query per entry, so it follows all of these. The other rules were already applied: the author's own status, follow `reblogs`, blocks either way of the author and of a boost's original author, a domain block of a boost's original author, and a mute that keeps notifications. Left as is. Mastodon decides at fan-out and tstodon at read time. A later block or mute clears the same statuses on both (`clear_from_home` checks mentions too), but an unblock or unmute brings them back here and not in Mastodon, a follow made later shows earlier replies to that account, and a reply whose parent is deleted or evicted later leaves home (a local parent's deletion nulls `in_reply_to_id`, so that reply stays). Worker tests: `server/home-feed-filter.test.ts`, `server/feed-mentions-plan.test.ts`.
- Followers collection synchronization (FEP-8fcf) works both ways as in Mastodon v4.7.5 (FED-9). Sending. The `Create`, `Update` or `Announce` of a followers-only status, from an account with fewer than 25,000 followers, is stored with `outbound_activities.synchronize_followers` (migration 0158; `DistributionWorker#options`, and the owed activities of FED-14 too). Each POST of it carries `Collection-Synchronization: collectionId="…/followers", digest="…", url="…/followers_synchronization"`, the digest being the XOR of the SHA-256 of the account's accepted remote follower URIs under the inbox URL's scheme and authority, read at each attempt (`DeliveryWorker#synchronization_header`, `remote_followers_hash`; `server/followers-synchronization.ts`, the model in `packages/domain/src/followers-synchronization.ts`). Mastodon caches that digest per host until a follow changes; tstodon reads it at each delivery step, once per host whatever the number of inboxes there, one statement over an index on `(target_account_id, remote_actor_uri)`: at 24,000 followers on one host that is 24,001 rows read and about 130 ms in workerd, 88 ms of it hashing (`server/followers-synchronization-cost.test.ts`). `GET /users/:username/followers_synchronization` serves the account's followers under the signer's scheme and authority, as `FollowersSynchronizationsController`: the account checks first (404, 410, 403), then a signature from an actor that federates here (401, 403, 503, `requireSignedFetch`, secure mode or not), `max-age=0, private`, an `OrderedCollection` without a count (`ApFollowersSynchronizationCollection`). Receiving. A verified inbox POST whose header parses as Mastodon's `SignatureParser` reads it, names the signer's followers collection, a URL on the signer's host and a digest other than that of the local accounts following the signer queues a `SynchronizeFollowers` job (`PrepareFollowersSynchronizationService`; `server/remote-followers-synchronization.ts`). A lock row (`followers_synchronization_locks`) keeps the same actor, URL and digest from being queued again until the job has run (`lock: :until_executed`). The job reads the URL (same host as the actor), its `first` page (same host) and up to ten pages in all, signed as the instance actor (`SynchronizeFollowersService`). For each local account a page lists, once a job however often it is listed (its accounts read in one statement a page): a follow stays, a request is accepted, and an account with neither sends the actor an `Undo` of a `Follow` id it never sent (`<actor>#follows//undo`, as Mastodon's). A page is at most 1 MiB, so a job hashes at most about 250,000 items (about 1 s); every item counts in the digest, repeats included, as in Mastodon. When every page was read and the list adds up to the header's digest, or the header named none or a blank one (`present?`), each local follow of the actor that was not listed is ended with its `Undo`, as `UnfollowService`; Mastodon never adds a follow. `DISABLE_FOLLOWERS_SYNCHRONIZATION=true` turns off both directions. Tests: `packages/domain/src/followers-synchronization.test.ts` (digest vectors and header parses checked against Mastodon's own `SignatureParser`), `server/followers-synchronization-endpoint.test.ts`, `server/followers-synchronization-delivery.test.ts`, `server/followers-synchronization-receive.test.ts`, `server/followers-synchronization-cost.test.ts`; the real-Mastodon sweep (`follows` area) drops a stale follow each way. Differences left: the header is not covered by tstodon's draft-cavage signature, which signs `(request-target) host date digest` where Mastodon signs every header it sends; a header with a duplicate key is ignored, where Mastodon's `SignatureVerificationError` goes unrescued (a 500); a page whose items are not all strings ends the job without removing anything, where Mastodon's job fails and is retried; the lock lapses after an hour and a job is tried three times, where Sidekiq holds it through 25 retries; a listed local account is recognised by its `/users/:username` URI only, the one form tstodon serves.
- DM-9: a direct note is on the home timeline as Mastodon v4.7.5 puts it there. `FanOutOnWriteService` delivers a direct note, as a limited one, to its local author's home (`deliver_to_self!`) and to the home of each local account it mentions, silently or not, that follows the author (`deliver_to_mentioned_followers!`), through `FeedManager#filter_from_home`, and to no list, hashtag follower, or public stream. tstodon's home is a query, so `listHomeStatuses` keeps a followed author's direct note when `status_mentions` names the viewer (a point lookup on its primary key, run for direct rows only, so no index and no new statement), and the viewer's own; `listFollowedRemoteStatuses` keeps a followed actor's direct note that `remote_status_mentions` names the viewer in, as INT-2 did for limited ones. Everything home applies stays: mutes, blocks, exclusive lists, followed languages, muted conversations, filters, and the id cursor's paging. A notification request that holds the mention does not keep the note out of home, as Mastodon's `NotifyService` filters the notification only. The home streaming fan-out asks the home query for each entry, so a mentioned follower's `user` stream now hears the note, and the author's already did. A deleted note leaves home with the query. Left as is: Mastodon fans out at write time, so a follow made after the note never brings it, while tstodon shows a followed author's earlier direct note that mentions the viewer (Mastodon's `merge_into_home` and `populate_home` read `list_eligible_visibility`, without direct notes, except the account's own). Mastodon also drops from home a note that mentions an account the viewer blocks or mutes (`crutches[:active_mentions]`), and home now does so too, for every visibility (see "Home and lists drop what `FeedManager` filters" above). A purged remote actor's limited and direct notes are announced as gone to the followers they address only, and the purge's broadcast to every follower lists its public, unlisted and followers-only notes and boosts, as Mastodon's `unpush_from_home` sends a `delete` only to a feed it removed the status from (`listRemoteActorAddressedHomeEntries`). The review of DM-9 also found that a local note sent its `status` notification ("notify me when this account posts") to every notifying follower, so a follower a direct note does not mention got a notification rendering it. It now goes where Mastodon's `FeedInsertWorker#notify?` sends it: to the followers the note reaches, so a direct note's mentioned ones, and to none for a reply to someone else. Migration 0159 deletes the stored ones a direct note sent to an account it does not mention. Remote notes send none at all (FED-15). Worker tests: `server/direct-home.test.ts`, `server/status-notification-audience.test.ts`.
- FED-15: a followed remote actor's note sends the `status` notification ("notify me when this account posts") as Mastodon v4.7.5's `FanOutOnWriteService` and `FeedInsertWorker#notify?` send it for a remote status. `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/follow` with `notify=true` already stored `outbound_follows.notify` and the relationship reported `notifying`; now a note an inbox `Create` stores notifies each accepted local follower that asked: every one for a public, unlisted or followers-only note, the ones it names (mentioned or silently addressed) for a limited or direct note, none for a reply to someone else or to a parent tstodon does not hold (Mastodon leaves `in_reply_to_account_id` unset), none whose follow keeps other `languages`, and none who mutes the author, whether or not the mute hides notifications (`filter_from_home`, `crutches[:muting]`; local notes too). A boost, an edit, a redelivery and a fetched note notify nobody, and a `QuoteRequest`'s instrument notifies only when published within the real-time window, as Mastodon fetches it and distributes only a recent status. A queued recipient's job reads its follow again when it runs (`findNotifyingRemoteFollow`), as `FeedInsertWorker#notify?` does, so an unfollow, `notify=false` or a mute in between stops it. The sender's blocks, domain blocks and muted conversations are screened as for any notification, and `status` bypasses the notification policy (`filterable: false`). The rule is `StatusNotificationAudience` in `packages/domain`, which the local publish now shares, so a local note also stops notifying a follower whose follow keeps other languages. The inbox request notifies the first eight recipients of a note (mentions, conversations and status notifications together) and queues a `FinishRemoteNoteRecipient` job with a `Status` step for each other one, so its D1 statements stay flat with the number of notifying followers; a note with none costs one indexed read (`listNotifyingRemoteFollowers`). No migration. What is left is FED-16. Worker tests: `server/remote-status-notification.test.ts`, `server/status-notification-audience.test.ts`; domain: `status-notification-audience.test.ts`. The sweep's `follows` area follows with `notify` both ways and waits for the `status` notification.
- DM-9: a direct note is on the home timeline as Mastodon v4.7.5 puts it there. `FanOutOnWriteService` delivers a direct note, as a limited one, to its local author's home (`deliver_to_self!`) and to the home of each local account it mentions, silently or not, that follows the author (`deliver_to_mentioned_followers!`), through `FeedManager#filter_from_home`, and to no list, hashtag follower, or public stream. tstodon's home is a query, so `listHomeStatuses` keeps a followed author's direct note when `status_mentions` names the viewer (a point lookup on its primary key, run for direct rows only, so no index and no new statement), and the viewer's own; `listFollowedRemoteStatuses` keeps a followed actor's direct note that `remote_status_mentions` names the viewer in, as INT-2 did for limited ones. Everything home applies stays: mutes, blocks, exclusive lists, followed languages, muted conversations, filters, and the id cursor's paging. A notification request that holds the mention does not keep the note out of home, as Mastodon's `NotifyService` filters the notification only. The home streaming fan-out asks the home query for each entry, so a mentioned follower's `user` stream now hears the note, and the author's already did. A deleted note leaves home with the query. Left as is: Mastodon fans out at write time, so a follow made after the note never brings it, while tstodon shows a followed author's earlier direct note that mentions the viewer (Mastodon's `merge_into_home` and `populate_home` read `list_eligible_visibility`, without direct notes, except the account's own). Mastodon also drops from home a note that mentions an account the viewer blocks or mutes (`crutches[:active_mentions]`), which tstodon's home does for no visibility. A purged remote actor's limited and direct notes are announced as gone to the followers they address only, and the purge's broadcast to every follower lists its public, unlisted and followers-only notes and boosts, as Mastodon's `unpush_from_home` sends a `delete` only to a feed it removed the status from (`listRemoteActorAddressedHomeEntries`). The review of DM-9 also found that a local note sent its `status` notification ("notify me when this account posts") to every notifying follower, so a follower a direct note does not mention got a notification rendering it. It now goes where Mastodon's `FeedInsertWorker#notify?` sends it: to the followers the note reaches, so a direct note's mentioned ones, and to none for a reply to someone else. Migration 0159 deletes the stored ones a direct note sent to an account it does not mention. Remote notes send none at all (since sent, FED-15). Worker tests: `server/direct-home.test.ts`, `server/status-notification-audience.test.ts`.
- RFC 9421 HTTP Message Signatures are accepted and sent as Mastodon v4.7.5 does them (FED-9, and FED-8's Ed25519 note). Accepting. A request that carries `Signature-Input` (and `Signature`) is read as an RFC 9421 message signature, as `SignedRequest` picks `HttpMessageSignature` (`parseRequestSignature` in `server/http-signature.ts`, the scheme in `server/http-message-signature.ts`, RFC 8941 structured fields in `server/structured-field.ts`, parsed to a `Result`). As with `linzer` 0.8.0: one signature only, a string `keyid` and an integer `created` required (`REQUIRED_PARAMETERS`), the signature base built from the components as sent and `@signature-params` re-serialized, and the algorithm taken from the key, `rsa-v1_5-sha256` for an RSA key and `ed25519` for an Ed25519 Multikey (`Keypair#linzer_public_key`; `alg` is not read). Before any key lookup, as `verify_signature_strength!` and `matches_time_window?`: `@method` and `@target-uri` must be covered (`TargetNotSigned`), and `content-digest` on a POST (`DigestNotSigned`); a signature is valid 5 minutes after `created`, or until `expires` but at most 12 hours, with an hour of clock skew either side, and an `expires` that has passed fails at once, as `linzer`'s own check (`SignatureExpired`). `Content-Digest` (RFC 9530, `sha-256` only) replaces `Digest`: a mismatch is `DigestMismatch` (401), and one that is not a structured-field dictionary is `MalformedContentDigest` (400), Mastodon's `MalformedHeaderError`. `@target-uri` is the request URL with `X-Forwarded-Proto`'s scheme, as Rack's `url` behind a TLS-terminating proxy. The inbox, the signed-fetch verdict (secure mode, private objects) and the body-limit refusal all read the signature this way, so key resolution and its refusals (blocked and not-allowed hosts before any fetch, WebFinger, admin suspensions) are the draft-cavage ones. An Ed25519 key still verifies no draft-cavage signature. Sending. Mastodon has no setting for RFC 9421: `Request#perform` signs with draft-cavage and, when that is answered 400 or 401, sends the request again with `sig1=("@method" "@target-uri" "content-digest");created=…;keyid="…"` (no `content-digest` on a GET), the "double-knock". Deliveries (`server/delivery.ts`) and signed ActivityPub GETs, each redirect hop (`server/federated-fetch.ts`), now do the same (`fetchDoubleKnocking`). Tests: `server/http-message-signature.test.ts` checks RFC 9421's ed25519 vectors (B.2.6, and B.4's transformed requests, which still verify or no longer do), its `rsa-v1_5-sha256` proxy signature (section 4.3: refused as one of two signatures, verified alone), and three requests made and verified with Mastodon's `linzer` (`test/rfc9421-vectors.rb`, RFC 9421's test keys), which tstodon's own signing reproduces byte for byte; `server/structured-field.test.ts`; `server/inbox-message-signature.test.ts` (accepted, and each refusal); `server/actor-key-forms.test.ts` (an Ed25519 Multikey's RFC 9421 delivery); `server/authorized-fetch.test.ts` (a secure-mode GET); `server/delivery-double-knock.test.ts` and `server/federated-fetch-signing.test.ts` (the second knock on 400 and 401 only). The Mastodon e2e sweep (`signatures`) runs Mastodon's own `Request#perform_rfc9421_signed_request` against tstodon's inbox: no setting makes Mastodon double-knock to a server that takes its draft-cavage signature.
- INT-2's `limited` follows Mastodon v4.7.5. A remote note its audience makes direct is stored `limited` when a local account or a cached remote actor is in its `to`/`cc` without a `Mention` tag, or when it came to the personal inbox of an account it does not mention (`Create#process_audience`, `delivered_to_account_id`). The inbox passes the personal inbox a request came to on to the `Create` handler, and that inbox's owner gets a silent mention. `RemoteVisibility` (`packages/domain/src/visibility.ts`) adds `Limited` to a remote status's visibilities only, so a local status cannot be limited, and every reader of a remote status's visibility takes the wider type. The REST API renders it `private` (`REST::StatusSerializer#visibility`). Only the accounts it addresses see it (`StatusPolicy#requires_mention?`), and none of them may boost it (`reblog?`). It joins no conversation and no direct timeline, and a mention on it skips the private-mention notification policy, as `FanOutOnWriteService` and `NotifyService` act on `direct` only. It reaches the home timeline of the followers it addresses and no list (`deliver_to_mentioned_followers!`). Its tags stay out of search, its quote policy is not denied as a direct note's is (`quote_policy_for_account`), an `Update` keeps it limited (Mastodon sets a visibility once), and the retention prune keeps it as it keeps a direct note. No migration: `remote_statuses.visibility` is free text with no check. Rows stored `direct` before stay so: a silent mention on one may come from an `Update` that dropped a tag, which leaves a Mastodon status direct, and an audience-only remote actor left no row to tell. `statuses_count` changes nothing, as tstodon reports 0 for a remote account. Worker tests: `server/remote-limited-visibility.test.ts`. The sweep's `direct` area has Mastodon's `ActivityPub::DeliveryWorker` deliver two such notes, to the shared inbox and to a personal one. What stays is in the INT-2 row; DM-9 has since brought direct notes to home the same way.
- INT-4 and FED-9 follow Mastodon v4.7.5 more closely, and INT-2's `limited` is written down. (1) INT-4, the `replies` collection. A served page of a note's replies embeds each local reply as its note and lists a remote one by its URI, and the collection embeds the page its query names as `first` (`?page=true`), as `RepliesController` does (`ApRepliesServedPage`). A followers-only or direct note's collection is served as the note is, to a signer it addresses (`StatusPolicy#show?`), with its public and unlisted replies, and the note embeds its author's replies whatever its visibility, as `NoteSerializer`; it was a 404 and an empty page. `loadLocalNoteDocuments` takes each note with its author, so a page by several accounts renders in a fixed number of statements. (2) INT-4, the reply tree walk (`server/remote-thread-backfill.ts`). Each `replies` collection is read until its pages hold five replies, and up to 500 are kept, as `FetchAllRepliesService` (its `call` is `FetchRepliesService`'s, so `MAX_REPLIES` resolves to the parent's 5 for the page loop, and to 500 in `filter_replies`); it read three pages and 20. Every listed reply is stored, 20 fetches a job, and a job that stops inside a list records its offset (`Level.offset`). Migration 0157 adds `remote_statuses.replies_fetched_at`: the walk records when it read each status's replies, skips a cached reply read within 15 minutes, and does not walk an opened status read so, as `fetched_replies_at` and `should_fetch_replies?`; opening a status queues its thread again after 15 minutes, not an hour. (3) FED-9. A first-seen actor whose WebFinger round trip ends in a mismatch (an answer for another actor, a subject that does not loop back, a 404 or 410) is not cached, and its signed requests fail (401 at the inbox and in secure mode), as `check_webfinger!` raises (`WebfingerMismatch`); it was cached under its URI authority. A test that signed as a Mastodon instance actor now has its WebFinger answer, as Mastodon's does (`Account.representative`). (4) INT-2. Mastodon's `limited` is a direct note with an audience-only recipient; its row lists what Mastodon does with one and what tstodon would need.
- Three interop gaps follow Mastodon v4.7.5 (INT-11, INT-10, INT-15). (1) INT-11. A remote attachment keeps the thumbnail its `icon` names (`MediaAttachmentParser#thumbnail_remote_url`), served as `preview_url` and `preview_remote_url`; a video or an audio without one has a null preview instead of its own file. The type follows Mastodon's accepted media types (`video/x-ms-asf` is audio, another type is `unknown`), `text_url` is null, and a changed thumbnail makes an `Update` an edit. (2) INT-10. `GET /api/v1/polls/:id` by a signed-in reader fetches a stale remote poll again and applies it as an `Update`, as `PollsController#refresh_poll` (`Poll#possibly_stale?`: never fetched since its `Create`, or more than a minute ago and before it ended; `remote_statuses.poll_fetched_at`, migration 0156). A poll option whose count is null, which Mastodon sends for a poll that hides its totals, no longer drops the note. (3) INT-15. A `VerifyProfileLinks` job checks an account's field links as `VerifyLinkService`: an `https` link written as it normalizes, the page read after up to three redirects when it answers 200, up to 1 MB, and a `rel="me"` link back to the profile (ignoring case) or a first `rel="me"` link that redirects to it exactly. A local account's fields are checked when it saves them and keep their verification while their value stays (`fields_attributes=`); a remote actor's are checked after each fetch of its document, which stores them unverified (`remote_actors.links_checked_at`). A local field's URL is a `rel="me"` link in the REST account and the actor document, so Mastodon can verify it too.
- Four interop gaps follow Mastodon v4.7.5 (INT-18, INT-2's first half, INT-1, FED-9's WebFinger part). (1) INT-18. An edit through `PUT /api/v1/statuses/:id` that changes nothing stores no revision and sends no `Update`, and answers the status as it is, as `UpdateStatusService` raises `NoChangesSubmittedError` when `significant_changes?` is false: the text, content warning, sensitive flag, language and ordered media ids (`StatusRevision.changesNote`), the quote policy against the stored one, a media description or focus (`mediaAttributeChanges`), and the poll (a request that names one always moves its end, as `expires_in=` does). The text and warning are compared as sent, before they are trimmed, so a change of surrounding whitespace is an edit, as Mastodon asks before `prepare_contents` strips them. A new status with a content warning is now stored sensitive, as `PostStatusService`, and migration 0155 marks the stored ones and scheduled ones the same way. (2) INT-2. A held mention of a direct status records it in `withheld_conversation_statuses` (migration 0154), which the conversation list, the direct stream and the direct timeline leave out, as `NotifyService` adds a direct message to the conversations only when its notification is not filtered. Accepting the sender releases its `held` rows and streams the conversations they now end (`publishConversationsJoined`), as `UnfilterNotificationsWorker`. Dismissing clears `held`, so a later accept does not release them, as `FilteredNotificationCleanupWorker` deletes the notifications the accept would read. A mention the policy drops (`for_private_mentions: drop`) or that lands in a muted conversation writes a row that is never released, as `NotifyService` returns on `drop?` before `push_to_conversation!`. Tests whose recipients took strangers' direct messages set `for_private_mentions: accept` (`test/notification-policy-fixture.ts`); the e2e Private mentions scenarios follow the sender first, and `probe-direct.ts` checks the held message has no conversation on tstodon too. (3) INT-1. `fetchActivityResource` (`server/federated-fetch.ts`) asks with `text/html;q=0.1`, as `FetchResourceService`; an answer that is not an ActivityPub media type leads, through its `Link` header's `rel="alternate"` link or else an HTML page's `<link rel="alternate">` (`server/activity-alternate.ts`), to the document, one hop, under the same host, DNS and size guards, and the document must carry that URL as its id. Unlike Mastodon, that hop still follows same-origin redirects, as every tstodon ActivityPub GET does, and an answer of another media type that names no alternate is still read as JSON, where Mastodon reads nothing. (4) FED-9. A WebFinger `subject` whose username `Account::USERNAME_ONLY_RE` does not match ends the round trip `UnsupportedUsername`: the actor is not cached and its signed requests fail, as `check_webfinger!` raises. Other definite WebFinger mismatches still cache the actor under its URI authority, where Mastodon refuses it too.
- REST gaps REST-2, REST-5, REST-9 to REST-13, served as Mastodon v4.7.5's controllers serve them, each with a contract test against its `@tstodon/api` schema. REST-2: `PUT /api/v1/statuses/:id/interaction_policy` (`server/routes/statuses/interaction-policy.ts`) sets a published status's quote policy; a change reaches the note's streams and an `Update` reaches its followers, sent as `announceNoteUpdate`'s `Unedited` revision so the note keeps the `updated` it had and a receiver records no edit, as Mastodon's `StatusUpdateDistributionWorker` after a policy change. REST-5: `GET /api/v1/domain_blocks/preview` counts the accepted follows each way with the domain's actors, matched as the domain block matches them (account domain or URI host), under `write:blocks`; without a `domain` it counts the follows with local accounts, as Mastodon's `where(domain: nil)`. REST-9: `GET` / `POST /oauth/userinfo` answers `OAuthUserinfoSerializer`'s claims to a token with the `profile` scope alone, is `userinfo_endpoint` in the authorization server metadata, and takes the open CORS policy. REST-10: `POST /api/v2/notifications/clear` routes to the v1 action, which Mastodon's v2 controller repeats. REST-11: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/pin` / `unpin` route to endorse / unendorse. REST-12: `GET`, `PUT` and `PATCH /api/v1/notifications/policy` read and write the v2 policy as `filter_*` booleans. REST-13: `POST /api/v1/conversations/:id/unread` forgets the conversation's last-read status and tells the `direct` stream. Known differences: a form value of `filter_*` is cast as tstodon casts every boolean (`true` / `1`), where Mastodon's setter takes any present string as true; an app-only token answers 401, as on every user-only endpoint, where Mastodon answers 422.
- CI gaps CI-1 to CI-4. CI-1: the four tstodon-to-tstodon scenarios that forged B's user's `Follow` from the host (follow-create, interactions, delete-shared-inbox, accept) now have B's user follow through B's client API (`followRemote` in `e2e/lib.ts`), so B holds the `Follow` it sent and both Workers do every fetch and delivery; `e2e/federation-ci.sh` runs all ten scenarios, and the host-side signing and seeding helpers are gone. CI-2: the thread backfill step was the probe racing Mastodon's ancestor cache, not tstodon. Mastodon v4.7.5 links a fetched reply to its parent in a `ThreadResolveWorker` job, one hop per job, and caches a status's ancestor ids for ten minutes the first time its context is read (`Status#ancestor_ids`, never invalidated). A context read while the chain was half linked kept answering 1 or 2 of 3 ancestors. Reading the leaf's context at once after resolving it froze the answer in 6 of 6 rounds while every `in_reply_to_id` was linked, and in none of 4 without the early read; the proxy log of a full tstodon-secure run and 20 more runs of the threads area shows no 401, 429 or 503 to Mastodon's signed fetches (the e2e Worker has no `RATE_LIMIT_COUNTER`, so it rate-limits nothing). The probe (`e2e/mastodon/probe-threads.ts`) now waits for Mastodon's copy to link every ancestor through `in_reply_to_id` before it reads the context. CI-3: every action in `.github/workflows/` is pinned by commit SHA with its version as a comment, which Dependabot's github-actions updates keep. CI-4: `ci.yml` runs `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high` (`vp pm audit --production --level high`). Overrides in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` clear the transitive advisories (deepmerge-ts, mysql2, js-yaml 4, undici, follow-redirects, fast-uri, postcss-selector-parser), the web UI's `axios` and `js-yaml` pins move to 1.20.0 and 5.4.1 (recorded in `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md`), and `auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` lists the one advisory left, esbuild's dev server under drizzle-kit, with its reason.
- Remote-note edits, the outbox queue and the outbox page stay well inside D1's 1000 statements per invocation (PERF-5 to PERF-7). (1) PERF-5. The inbox `Update` of a remote note notified each local booster in one loop on the request: 93 statements with 2 boosters, 1946 with 60. As Mastodon's `notify_about_update!`, which batches them into `LocalNotificationWorker`s, the request now lists the first 50 boosters by account id and queues one `NotifyRemoteNoteBoosters` job for each (`Booster`) and one for the next page (`Page`), which does the same (`server/remote-note-booster-updates.ts`). Each job stores the `update` notification with `notifyFromRemoteOnce` since the edit, so every booster is notified once, also after a redelivered `Update` or a retried page. The request now makes 27 statements with 2 boosters and 26 with 60. (2) PERF-6. A queue invocation of 10 `FinishRemoteNoteRecipient` jobs whose recipients have their `direct` stream open made 833 statements. The outbox queue's `maxBatchSize` is now 5 (`app/queues/tstodon-outbox-process.ts`, and Terraform's `queue_consumer_batch_size` default): the same jobs make 418 an invocation, and 10 `NotifyRemoteNoteBoosters` jobs go from 308 to 143. `remote-note-recipients.test.ts` and `remote-note-booster-updates.test.ts` hold an invocation to half the limit, with the batch size read from the built `wrangler.json` (`test/queue-hold.ts`). (3) PERF-7. `getUsersByUsernameOutbox` rendered each note with `loadLocalNoteDocument`, about six statements a note: 134 for a page of 20 varied statuses. `loadLocalNoteDocuments` reads the media, mentions, parents, polls, quotes and quote policies of the whole page at once, and `statusTargetRefs` its boosted statuses: 27 for the same page. `outbox-page-render.test.ts` compares the page's items byte for byte with the per-note renderer, which the note route keeps.
- tstodon Linked-Data-signs its own activities as Mastodon v4.7.5 does (the signing part of FED-13), so another server can forward them: Mastodon forwards a reply in a tstodon post's thread to the author's followers, and a tstodon `Delete` to the followers of the post's boosters. As Mastodon's `Payloadable#serialize_payload` (`sign?`, `always_sign`, `signing_enabled?`), `ldSigningOf` (`server/own-activity-signature.ts`) signs a public or unlisted status's `Create`, `Update` and `Announce`, the actor's `Update`, a featured hashtag's `Add` and `Remove` and a `QuoteRequest` outside secure mode (`AUTHORIZED_FETCH`, or limited federation, as Mastodon's `authorized_fetch_mode?`), and the `Delete` of a public or unlisted status, the `Undo` of such a boost, the `Delete` of the actor and of a quote stamp in any mode; a `Like`, a `Follow`, a vote, a pin, a `Move` and the `Create`, `Update`, `Announce`, `Delete` or `Undo` of a followers-only or direct status are not signed. A `QuoteRequest` is signed whatever the quoting status's visibility, with the status inlined as its `instrument`, as Mastodon does (`Quote#sign?`). An activity is signed once, with its actor's RSA key (`<actor>#main-key`), when it is stored for delivery (`deliveredActivity`, called by `enqueueLocalActivity`, `enqueueTargetedActivity` and `enqueueActivityToInboxes` in `server/delivery.ts`, which take the sender's `LocalAccount`, and by `readDepartingActor` for the actor's `Delete`), and every inbox gets that stored body. An activity for named inboxes is signed only once an inbox is admitted, and a status's `Delete`, which goes to the followers and to the post's wider reach as two stored activities, is signed once for both. `ApActivityDocument` (`@tstodon/api/activitypub`) carries the optional `signature` (`ApLdSignature`). A signature that cannot be made is logged (`ActivityNotLdSigned`) and the activity goes unsigned. Signing costs about 1.3 to 1.7 ms warm per activity (p50 over 100 runs in Node, the same V8, with 4 CPUs at load average 3.5: 1.7 ms for a 3 KB `Create` with media, a quote and tags, 1.3 ms for an `Announce`); of that, the RSA signature is 0.3 ms and the two URDNA2015 canonicalizations the rest. An isolate's first signature of a rich note takes 11 to 19 ms. Mastodon reads a signed activity compacted to its own context, so the builders were audited against Mastodon's own `compact`, `safe_for_forwarding?` and `verify_actor!`: `scripts/own-activity-documents.ts` builds one of each signed activity with tstodon's builders (`server/own-activity-samples.ts`: a reply with two attachments, focal points, alt text, a blurhash, a mention, a hashtag, an emoji, a content warning, `sensitive` and an accepted quote; polls; an edit; boosts and their `Undo`; a `Delete`; the actor's `Update` with fields, emoji, avatar, header, `featured`, `discoverable`, `indexable`, `alsoKnownAs`, `movedTo`, and suspended; the actor's `Delete`; featured hashtags; a `QuoteRequest`; a quote stamp's `Delete`) and signs each, and `scripts/generate-jsonld-reference.mjs` runs Mastodon's code on them into `server/own-activity-signature.fixture.ts`. The audit found four things, all fixed. (1) A note did not define `sensitive`, so Mastodon read every signed note as not sensitive (it came back as `_:sensitive`); the note's context now defines it as `as:sensitive`. (2) The quote terms `quote`, `quote_uri` and `_misskey_quote` all named FEP-044f's `quote`, so Mastodon read one `quote` holding the URI three times; they are now `quote`, Fedibird's `quoteUri` and Misskey's `_misskey_quote`, each as Mastodon's context defines it. tstodon sends `quoteUri` where Mastodon sends `quote_uri`, a key Mastodon's own context does not define, which a signature would not cover; Mastodon, Misskey and Fedibird read `quoteUri`. (3) A status's `Delete` used `atomUri` with no context defining it; it now names `ostatus:atomUri`, as Mastodon's `DeleteNoteSerializer`. (4) A `Create` and an `Update` of a note carried the note's `@context` nested inside the object; Mastodon reads that, but `safe_for_forwarding?` refuses it, and so does tstodon's own forward check (`readsAsSigned`). The note's terms now go on the activity, as Mastodon sends them (`noteCreateActivity`, `noteUpdateActivity` in `server/activitypub.ts`, used for posts, edits and poll counts). The actor's `Update` and the other activities needed no change. tstodon does not send `contentMap`, `memorial`, `attributionDomains` or the profile settings, so they were not audited. Tests: `server/own-activity-signature.test.ts` holds the builders to the fixture, and checks that for each sample Mastodon's compacted reading equals its plain reading, `safe_for_forwarding?` holds, Mastodon's `verify_actor!` accepts tstodon's signature, and tstodon's own `readLdSignature` / `ldSignedBytes` gate verifies it (before the fixes, 18 of its 61 checks failed, for six samples: the two `Create`s, the two note `Update`s, the `Delete` and the `QuoteRequest`); `server/own-activity-signing.test.ts` checks which activities are signed, that a public post's stored `Create` carries one signature that verifies as a forward, that a followers-only `Create` is unsigned, and that in secure mode a `Delete` is signed and a `Create` is not (without the hook, 2 of its 19 checks fail). The interop sweep's `signatures` area (`e2e/mastodon/probe-signatures.ts`) checks that a sensitive public post reads on Mastodon as sent and runs Mastodon's `verify_actor!` on the `Delete` tstodon delivered, which fetches the key from tstodon. The sweep passes 229 of 229 steps with both servers open (2 skipped) and 231 of 231 with both in secure mode. The retry of a post's owed `Create` or `Update` and its `QuoteRequest` (FED-14, `server/pending-status-federation.ts`) builds them with the same builders and signs them the same way (`deliveredActivity`).
- A local post's `Create`, or an edit's `Update`, that could not be built when the post was stored (its media or quote did not read back) is now sent later (FED-14), as Mastodon's Sidekiq retries the worker that distributes it. It was logged (`StatusNotFederated`, `StatusUpdateNotFederated` with a `cause`) and never sent, so the post stayed local and a quote of a remote post stayed `pending` without its `QuoteRequest`. The status now records the activity it owes (`pending_status_federations`, migration 0153: one row per status, `Create` or `Update`, a `revision`, the time of the edit an `Update` stands for, the tries so far and the next one; the row goes with the status), and a `FederatePendingStatuses` job sends it: queued once per run, after the shortest wait its failed tries left, and by the hourly cron while a row is due, which covers a queue message that was lost. The job builds the activity from the status as it is then (`readNoteObject` in `server/local-note-object.ts`, which the post and edit paths now share) and stores it in the batch that deletes the row, only while the row still has the revision it read (`storeOwedActivity`): a deleted post has no row and sends nothing, two runs of the same row store one activity, and a run that built a version changed since stores nothing. A retried `Update` carries the time of its edit as `updated`. A post whose author is suspended or disabled by then is not sent, and its row is dropped. A retried `Create` stores its `QuoteRequest`, with its target, in that same batch while the quote is pending, so a crash after the store loses neither; the stuck-delivery sweep starts the `QuoteRequest`'s delivery if the job did not. Every change of a post that still owes an activity moves the row to a new revision and queues the job at once, and sends nothing of its own, so the post's current version goes out, as a `Create` when the `Create` was never sent: an edit, a moderator's sensitive mark, a quote refresh or revoke and an edit that does not federate (a direct post without remote mentions) included, and a poll's count `Update` is not sent before the `Create` either. An edit that cannot tell whether a `Create` is owed (the row's `UPDATE` failed) sends no `Update` then and owes one instead (`cause` `pending`), which an owed `Create` outranks. Tries back off as Sidekiq's `ExponentialBackoff` without its random part, at most 12 hours apart (`PendingStatusFederation.afterFailure` in `packages/domain`); the 12th failed try, about two and a half days after the first, gives the activity up and logs `StatusFederationAbandoned`. A try that throws counts as a failed one; a row whose own write failed is tried again a minute later rather than at once. A sent one logs `StatusFederated`. Costs on the normal path: every post now reads `status_quotes` for its quote, also a post without one, and a failure of that read now defers the post's `Create` where it was ignored; every change of a post runs one `UPDATE` of `pending_status_federations`, which changes nothing unless an activity is owed. Tests: `server/pending-status-federation.test.ts` (a quote post sent once its quote reads, once however often the job runs; its remote quoted author asked once with a `QuoteRequest`, the quote left pending; a deleted post never sent; a post edited meanwhile sent as the `Create` of the edit, with no `Update`; the give-up logged after the retries, and nothing sent after it; an edit's `Update` sent later with the post's current text and quote; an edit or a delete between a retry's read and its store, which stores nothing, the edit then sent; a post whose media does not read back sent later with it; a post whose author was disabled never sent, even once enabled again; an edit whose check of the row fails sends its `Update` later; a poll's counts not sent before its owed `Create`), `packages/domain/src/pending-status-federation.test.ts`, and `server/note-quote-read.test.ts`, whose edits moved to a post that owes no `Create`. Deviations: Mastodon's Sidekiq default retries 25 times over about three weeks; tstodon tries 12 times. Mastodon sends the `Create` it could not build before a later edit's `Update`; tstodon sends one `Create` of the edited post. The first retry comes at least 25 seconds later. A `Create` or `Update` a retry stored is not expanded to the followers when the queue refuses its `ExpandFollowers` message, as on the post path.
- A remote actor already cached under a username that Mastodon's `Account` would refuse is refreshed again (FED-8, nit 7). Mastodon validates `preferredUsername` only when it changes (`will_save_change_to_username?`); tstodon refused such an actor on the daily refresh and on its own actor `Update`, so it was never refreshed again. Both paths, and the outbox backfill's refetch, now compare with the stored username first, as the key refetch did: a refetch that reads the document as refused reads it again with the username the actor is cached under (`readCachedActorDocument` in `server/remote-actor-fetch.ts`, one D1 read only then), and an actor `Update` passes the signer's. tstodon stores usernames lowercased, so the document's username is compared lowercased: one in another case (`Bob.` cached as `bob.`) is the same username, as the stored value cannot tell the cases apart. Mastodon compares the stored case exactly. A cached actor whose document changes its username to one Mastodon refuses is still refused. Tests: `server/remote-actor-webfinger.test.ts` (an actor cached as `bob.` refreshed by the cron and by its `Update`; one renamed to `carol.` not taken), `server/remote-actor-webfinger.test.ts` also refreshes one cached as `bob_.` whose document says `Bob_.`, and `server/remote-actor-document.test.ts` (the comparison).
- Remote actor keys are kept and verified as Mastodon v4.7.5 keeps its `Keypair` records (FED-8), and a forward whose actor vouches for it with a FEP-8b32 integrity proof is accepted (the proof part of FED-13). A remote actor had one key, its single `publicKey`, stored on its `remote_actors` row, and a document whose `publicKey` was a list was refused. Each key an actor document publishes is now a `remote_actor_keys` row (migration 0152): every `publicKey` whose `owner` is the actor and every FEP-521a `assertionMethod` Multikey whose `controller` is the actor (an `ed25519-pub` or `rsa-pub` `publicKeyMultibase`, read as Mastodon's `Multibase.decode_key_to_pem` reads it), at most ten of each, with its id on the actor's host (`actorKeys` in `server/actor-keys.ts`). A refetch replaces the set, as `set_fetchable_key!` does, and a key id another actor held moves to the new one. A signature finds its key by id (`findRemoteActorKey`, Mastodon's `Keypair.from_keyid`); an unknown key id is fetched, either as the actor's own document, whose keys must include it, or as a key document whose `owner` (`controller` for a Multikey) lists it (`fetchKeyOwner`, Mastodon's `FetchRemoteKeyService`). The aliases tstodon accepted for a key id (the actor's URI, `<actor>#main-key`) are gone, as Mastodon has none; the callers that used them to fetch an actor now call `resolveRemoteActor` with its URI. An HTTP signature (draft-cavage) and a Linked Data signature verify only with an RSA key, as in Mastodon. A forwarded activity whose Linked Data signature is missing or does not verify is checked for an `eddsa-jcs-2022` proof (`server/integrity-proof.ts`, Mastodon's `ObjectIntegrityProof`): a `DataIntegrityProof` for `assertionMethod`, not expired, over the body without its `signature`, made with an Ed25519 key of the activity's actor on a host that federates here. JCS is computed as Mastodon's `json-canonicalization` gem computes it (`server/json-canonical.ts`), including its 16-digit numbers, which differ from RFC 8785 for numbers such as `0.30000000000000004`. A proof whose options name an `@context` covers the document with only those first entries of its context, as in Mastodon; tstodon then processes that covered document, without `proof` or the failed `signature`, so the proof covers exactly what is processed and the activity is not forwarded further, as Mastodon does not forward it. A fetched actor whose `preferredUsername` does not match Mastodon's `Account::USERNAME_ONLY_RE` (or is longer than 2048 characters) is refused, as Mastodon's `Account` validation refuses it (from FED-9). `scripts/generate-key-forms-reference.mjs` runs Mastodon's own `Multibase`, `to_json_c14n`, `ObjectIntegrityProof.verify_eddsa_jcs_2022` and `USERNAME_ONLY_RE` in the Mastodon image, and `server/key-forms.test.ts` matches tstodon against that fixture. The Worker grew by 21 KiB (3.6 KiB gzipped). Tests: `server/key-forms.test.ts`, `server/actor-key-forms.test.ts` (a delivery signed with the second key of a `publicKey` list; an HTTP signature under an Ed25519 Multikey's id refused; a key served as its own document taken only when its owner lists it; a dropped key forgotten on refetch; a proved forward applied, and dropped when changed, proved by another actor's key or under an RSA key's id, or expired; a proof taken when the Linked Data signature fails, that signature dropped), `server/remote-actor-document.test.ts` (remote usernames).
- The inbox-side gaps of FED-9 follow Mastodon v4.7.5. An `Undo` of a `Like`, an `Announce` or a `Block` that is handled before the activity it undoes now drops that activity, as `delete_arrived_first?`: the `Undo` is remembered in `undone_before_arrival` (`rememberUndone.sql`) before it deletes what it ends, and the statements that store a `Like` (`upsertRemoteFavourite.sql`), an `Announce` (`insertRemoteBoost.sql` and `upsertRemoteAnnounce.sql`) or a `Block` (`insertRemoteBlock.sql`) check that table, so an activity stored at the same time is either deleted or dropped. An `Announce` also checks it before it fetches the boosted status, and a dropped `Block` still ends the follows, as Mastodon's. An `Undo` that names its activity by id only, or embeds it without a `type` (`handle_reference`), ends the `Announce`, `Follow` or `Block` with that id and is remembered; a `Like` is only remembered, as `try_undo_like`. `remote_blocks` keeps the `Block`'s id for this (`activity_uri`, migration 0151). An `Undo(Accept)` turns the local account's follow back into a request (`Follow#revoke_request!`, `revokeOutboundFollow.sql`). A `Create` served at the URI an `Announce` names stores its object (`FetchRemoteStatusService#expected_activity_type?`, `storeNoteDocument`), so a Lemmy community's share of a post boosts the post; the `Create` and its object must sit on its actor's host, an embedded `Create` is fetched by its id, and only one `Create` is read per document.
