The first attachment with an `href` (a Lemmy link post) is the URL the preview card unfurls (`noteCardUrl`, Mastodon's `@links.first`) and is no media. A note's `attachment` may be one object (`as_array`) and an attachment's `url` a `Link` (`url_to_href`, `url_to_media_type`); an entry that does not read is left out rather than rejecting the note. An object `type` given as a list is read as the converted type it lists, else the note type it lists (`equals_or_includes_any?`), in a `Create` and an `Update`. A remote edit dated later than the stored one takes the note's language, even when its text is unchanged (`update_immediate_attributes!`). A `Delete` that arrives before its `Create` was already covered by the tombstone of PR #155; `server/remote-delete.test.ts` now shows it. The tombstone is now checked in the batch that stores a status (`refuseBuriedRemoteStatus.sql` in `upsertRemoteStatus`) rather than read before it, so a `Delete` handled while its `Create` is stored either finds the status or buries it first, as Mastodon's `create:<uri>` lock does, and no path stores a deleted status again: an `Announce`, a search and the thread backfill included. A `Create` reached through an `Announce` shares the status already stored, if any, as `find_existing_status`, so an old `Create` does not roll back a later edit. Tests: `server/undo-before-activity.test.ts`, `server/remote-lenient-objects.test.ts`, and the language case in `server/remote-update.test.ts`. Deviations: an `Undo` of a `Like`, an `Announce`, a `Block` or one named by id is remembered whether or not it found the activity, where Mastodon remembers only an `Undo` that found nothing. A redelivery of the same activity id within the six hours is a duplicate to the inbox claim either way, so only an activity stored while the `Undo` runs is treated differently, and it is dropped. One consequence: a server that reuses the id of an undone activity for a new one within the six hours has it dropped, where Mastodon keeps it when its `Undo` found the first. An `Undo(Accept)` finds the follow by its `Follow` id only, as Mastodon's `undo_accept` does, not also by the account as an `Accept` does. A block recorded before migration 0151 has no `Block` id, so an `Undo` that names it by id only does not lift it. The status of a `Create` read this way is stored under its `attributedTo`, which must sit on the `Create` actor's host; Mastodon stores it under the `Create`'s actor.

- Forwarded activities are accepted and sent as Mastodon v4.7.5 does (FED-2, and the forwarding part of INT-9). An inbox request signed by another actor than the activity's was refused as `ActorMismatch`. As Mastodon's `ProcessActivityService` (`different_actor?`), it is now a forward: the request is checked with the forwarder's key (`resolveKeyOwner` reads the key's owner, cached or fetched), and the activity counts only when its actor's own `RsaSignature2017` Linked Data signature verifies (`verifyForwardedActivity` in `server/inbox-forward.ts`): not expired, within Mastodon's JSON-LD limits (no `@graph`, `@included` or `@reverse`, at most 1000 values), made with a key of that actor on a host that federates here, over the body as received. A signature covers the JSON-LD graph and tstodon reads the JSON, so the body must also read the same both ways (`readsAsSigned`): compacted to Mastodon's `full_context` it must give back the same JSON (only a list of one, `as:Public` and a `null` may differ, as Mastodon's `patch_for_forwarding!` allows), with no nested `@context`, and every member must reach the graph (no term that no context defines, no relative IRI); the signature's options are held to the identity context the same way. Otherwise a forwarder could keep the signature while a nested `{"content": null}` context, `as:content` or `@id` for `id` changed what tstodon reads (a note's content, the target of a `Delete`, an actor's key, the signature's `expires` written as `sec:expiration`). Mastodon instead processes the compacted form; tstodon refuses what does not already read as it (`AmbiguousJsonLd`), which also drops a forward with terms no context defines that Mastodon would take. `ld-signature.test.ts` checks that the copies Mastodon itself forwards (its `compact` and `patch_for_forwarding!` output, from the fixture: a note with an attachment and its `focalPoint`, an edit, a `Delete`, a poll) still verify. A forwarded `Update` of an actor is applied from the actor's own document, fetched, not from the one it carries, and a forwarded `Delete` of an account purges it only when its server answers 404 or 410 for it, without a redirect (`answersGone`); any other failure keeps it. A key document fetched to verify anything is taken only for an actor on the key's own host. Otherwise the forwarder gets 202 and nothing is applied, as Mastodon drops it; the refusal is logged as `UnverifiedForward` with the reason. v4.7.5 does not fall back to fetching the object from its origin: an activity without a valid signature (or FEP-8b32 proof, FED-13) is dropped. Domain and suspension checks apply to the forwarder and the actor; the rate limit and reachability count the forwarder. Signatures are checked with jsonld.js 9 (URDNA2015, `server/ld-signature.ts`) over the contexts Mastodon preloads, shipped in `server/jsonld-contexts.ts` and never fetched; jsonld is patched (`patches/jsonld@9.0.0.patch`) so its Node.js HTTP loader is not bundled. `scripts/generate-jsonld-reference.mjs` dumps those contexts from the Mastodon image with a fixture of Mastodon's canonical N-Quads and its own signatures, which `ld-signature.test.ts` matches byte for byte. The Worker grew by 299 KiB (63 KiB gzipped); a check of a 3.4 KB `Create` (the compaction and expansion that `readsAsSigned` needs, two canonicalizations and an RSA verify) costs about 2 ms of CPU warm and 30 to 50 ms on an isolate's first one (Node, same V8). Outbound, an activity that carries its actor's signature is forwarded as received (`forwardRemoteActivity` in `server/activity-forward.ts`): a new public or unlisted reply to a local status to the replied-to author's remote followers (`Create#forward_for_reply`), and a newer edit or the `Delete` of a remote status to the remote followers of the local accounts that boosted or quoted it and of the replied-to local author (`ActivityPub::Forwarder`), without the actor's own inbox, signed (HTTP) by the replied-to author or else the first sharer. The replied-to author comes first among the at most 500 accounts a forward reaches. An inbound activity is forwarded once however often its handling is retried (`outbound_activities.forward_of`, migration 0150); a retry queues the expansion again while the stored forward's fan-out is still `Queued`, so a lost queue send is recovered. The plan is `remoteActivityForward` (`server/status-reach.ts`); the body is stored as an outbound activity and an `ExpandForward` job lists the followers in one query and delivers through the Workflow. A new note or a newer edit forwards from its owed late steps, so a note the server already had is not forwarded; a `Delete` forwards before the status and its boosts go. A `Create` whose object is on another host than its signer's is now ignored, as Mastodon's `non_matching_uri_hosts?`; it was stored. A `Delete` records a tombstone for its object's URI when that URI is on the deleting actor's host, whether or not the status was stored, as Mastodon does (`remote_status_tombstones`, migration 0150, kept as Mastodon keeps them), and a later `Create`, or an `Update` handled as one, of that URI stores nothing (`tombstone_exists?`), so a signed `Create` replayed after the delete does not bring the status back. Tests: `server/ld-signature.test.ts` (Mastodon's canonical forms and signatures; a changed body, actor or option, another key, an expired signature, unsupported JSON-LD), `server/inbox-forward.test.ts` (a forwarded `Create`, `Update` and `Delete` applied, also from the actor's own server; a forward without a signature, with another actor's key, with a changed body, with an expired signature, replayed, of an object on another host, or from a suspended domain is not; nor a note, `Delete`, actor key or expiry that a nested context, a compact IRI or `@id` rewrites; a forwarded actor `Update` and `Delete`; an actor not cached yet; a key document served on another host; a status deleted and created again), `server/activity-forward.test.ts` (a reply forwarded to the author's followers but not to the replier, once however often its handling runs, the replied-to author kept among 500 sharers; no forward when unsigned, followers-only, a reply to a remote status or a known note; an `Update` and a `Delete` of a boosted remote status forwarded to the booster's followers, and not when unsigned or nobody shared it), `server/inbox-refusal.test.ts`.

- A local status's and a local account's `url` is its page in the web app, `/@name/id` and `/@name`, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `ActivityPub::TagManager#url_for` (INT-17). The REST status and account, the ActivityPub Note (which had no `url`) and the actor all named the ActivityPub URI, so a client that opened a post's link, such as the standalone composer of `/share`, landed on JSON. `InstanceIdentity.profileUrl` and `statusUrl` build both, and every rendering of a local status (timelines, notifications, streaming, outbox items, the embed) goes through them. As Mastodon's `redirect_with_vary`, `/users/:name`, its `followers`, `following` and `/statuses/:id` asked for as a page (no `Accept`, a browser's, `text/html` or `*/*`) answer 301 to the page with `Vary: Origin, Accept` (`webAppMiddleware` in `server/web-app.ts`); the reverse redirect of `/@name` for an ActivityPub client reads `Accept` the same way, so the two never loop. Worker tests that read ActivityPub documents now send `Accept` (`test/activity-json.ts`). The Mastodon sweep's first step checks that Mastodon's copy of a tstodon post has the `/@name/id` page as its `url`. A mention's link in a post's HTML and its REST `mentions[].url` are the account's profile page too (`RemoteActor.profileUrl` for a remote account), so the web UIs match the link to the mention and open the profile in the app; the ActivityPub `Mention` tag still names the actor. A post stored before this change keeps its old mention links in its HTML. Access bypasses `/@*` (`infra/cloudflare/access.tf`) so another server can resolve a pasted post link; Access sends no assertion there, so the web app reads the browser's `CF_Authorization` cookie for its viewer and sends a browser without a valid one to `/auth/sign_in`. A status page's oEmbed discovery and link previews still need an Access login, as before. A local boost still carries its original's `uri` and `url`, where Mastodon's is the boost's `/activity` URI.
- A suspended or deleted local account's ActivityPub documents are refused as Mastodon v4.7.5's `AccountOwnedConcern#check_account_suspension` (FED-12): its outbox, followers, following, `featured` and `tags` collections, its notes and their `replies`, and its quote stamps answer 403 while it is suspended and 410 once it is deleted, cached 3 minutes (`documentOwner` in `server/routes/activitypub.ts`). The actor document is still served while suspended, with `suspended: true`, as Mastodon's `skip_temporary_suspension_response?` for JSON. `noteQuote` returns a `Result`: a note or outbox page whose quote could not be read answers 500, uncached, instead of a document without its `quote`. A `Create` or an edit's `Update` whose quote cannot be read is not sent, as one whose media cannot be read, since a receiver drops the quote of an explicit `Update` that has none; the log line names the cause (`StatusNotFederated` or `StatusUpdateNotFederated` with `cause` `quote` or `media`), and FED-14 tracks sending it later. A poll's `Update` of its counts still goes out without the quote it could not read: Mastodon handles it as an implicit update, which keeps the quote it has. In secure mode the 403 and 410 are `max-age=180, private`. The suspension check still runs after the authorized-fetch check, so in secure mode an unsigned fetch gets 401 where Mastodon answers 403.
- `Article`, `Page`, `Image`, `Video`, `Audio` and `Event` objects are stored as statuses, as Mastodon v4.7.5 converts them (FED-4, `ActivityPub::Activity::CONVERTED_TYPES`). The inbound note schema (`NoteDocumentSchema` in `server/activitypub-wire.ts`) took only `Note` and `Question`, so posts from WriteFreely, Lemmy, PeerTube, Funkwhale and Mobilizon were dropped. Every inbound path parses through that schema, so a `Create`, an `Update` (and the FED-3 `Update` of an unknown object), an `Announce`, a search that resolves a URL, the thread backfill and the outbox backfill all take them. As `StatusParser#processed_text`, a converted object's text is its name as an `<h2>`, its summary, and its `url` (or else its id) as a link (`linkify` in `server/html.ts`); its content is dropped, an `Article`'s too, and its spoiler text is empty, so a summary is text and not a content warning. `url` is read as `url_to_href(url, 'text/html')`: the first string, or the first `Link` whose `mimeType` is `text/html` or absent (PeerTube's links carry `mediaType`, so its watch page, listed first, is taken; Funkwhale's carry `mimeType`); only an `http(s)` URL is kept, for a `Note` too. `attributedTo` is read as its first entry, as Mastodon does, which PeerTube's account-and-channel list needs. The language falls back from `contentMap` to `nameMap` and `summaryMap`. Attachments, mentions, hashtags, the reply, the visibility and `sensitive` go through the `Note` path; a `Question` is unchanged. Remote HTML's `h1` to `h6` now render as a paragraph of strong text, as Mastodon's `MASTODON_STRICT` (`UNSUPPORTED_ELEMENTS_TRANSFORMER`), since every converted text starts with a heading. Mastodon builds no attachment from an `Image`, `Video` or `Audio` object's own `url`: it reads only `attachment` (`Create#process_attachments`), so a PeerTube video or a Funkwhale track has no media there either, and tstodon matches it. As in Mastodon, the text's link is unfurled into a preview card. Tests: `server/remote-converted-objects.test.ts` (for a WriteFreely `Article`, a Lemmy `Page`, a PeerTube `Video`, a Funkwhale `Audio`, a Mobilizon `Event` and an `Image` with a Pixelfed-shaped attachment: the `Create` stores the text and the attachments and reaches a follower's home, an `Update` edits it, an `Update` before the `Create` stores it, a `Delete` or a `Tombstone` removes it, a resolving search finds it, each REST answer checked with `expectContract`; an `Article`'s content is dropped and its language read; a reply is threaded and its mention notified), `server/remote-thread-backfill.test.ts` (a `Page` ancestor and a `Video` reply are fetched), `server/remote-outbox-backfill.test.ts` (an embedded `Video` and a `Page` by URI are stored), `server/sanitize-html.test.ts` (headings). The real-Mastodon harness (`e2e/mastodon/`) cannot cover this: Mastodon never sends these types. Gaps left in FED-9: an `Announce` of a `Create` (Lemmy communities), a `Link` attachment as the preview card's URL, a single attachment object or a `Link` attachment `url`, and a list `type`.
- WebFinger and remote interaction follow Mastodon v4.7.5 (FED-7, and the WebFinger part of FED-9). `/.well-known/webfinger` (`getWellKnownWebfinger` in `server/routes/discovery.ts`, the resource forms in `server/webfinger-resource.ts`) resolves every form `WebfingerResource` does: `acct:user@domain`, a bare `user@domain`, the actor URL and the profile URL (`/users/:username`, `/@:username`), and the instance actor as `acct:<domain>@<domain>`, the bare domain, the site URL or `/actor`; the account domain and the web domain (`INSTANCE_PUBLIC_ORIGIN`'s host) both count as local. The answer is `WebfingerSerializer`'s: `subject` is the account's `acct:` whatever form was asked, `aliases` are the profile URL and the actor (the actor alone for the instance actor), and `links` are `profile-page` (the instance actor's is `/about/more?instance_actor=true`, which now redirects to `/about`), `self`, the OStatus `subscribe` template (`/authorize_interaction?uri={uri}`), the FEP-3b86 `Create` (`/share?text={content}`) and `Object` (`/authorize_interaction?uri={object}`) templates, and the avatar with its media type when the account has one, the server is not in limited federation and the account is not suspended. It is `application/jrd+json`, cached for 3 days; a missing resource or one that is neither a URL nor a handle answers an empty 400, one that names no local account 404, a deleted account 410, each cached for 3 minutes (the old JSON error bodies are gone). `GET /authorize_interaction` (`server/routes/authorize-interaction.ts`, `AuthorizeInteractionsController`) sends a signed-out browser to sign in and back; for a signed-in one it resolves `uri` (or `acct`) as search does: a URL through `resolveSearchUrl`, a handle as `ResolveAccountService` (a bare username or this domain is local, any other is looked up over WebFinger), and redirects to `/@acct` or `/@acct/:id` in the web app; what does not resolve, and a status the viewer may not see, gets the not-found page, as does an account search would not list (pending approval or unavailable, `searchableLocalAccounts` / `searchableRemoteActors`). Each username in the redirect is URI-encoded, and a signed-in request counts against the account's `api_account` budget (`RateLimit.accountCheck`, `limitAccount` in `server/rate-limit.ts`), since a browser session has no token for the rate-limit middleware to count. `/authorize_follow` and `/.well-known/proxy` redirect there with their query, and `/intent` turns `web+mastodon://follow?uri=…` into `/authorize_interaction` and `web+mastodon://share?text=…` into `/share`. `/share` (`getShare` in `server/web-app.ts`, `SharesController`) renders the standalone composer (the `share` pack, now in the web manifest) with `title`, `text` and `url` joined into `compose.text`. The fetch options a resolve uses on a viewer's behalf are `resolveFetchOptions` (`server/search-url.ts`), which search shares. So another server's "interact from your server" works for a tstodon user: its helper asks tstodon's WebFinger for `https://<domain>` and opens the template; before, that answered 404 and its fallback `/authorize_interaction` did too. Deviations: a URL on another host names no account here (Mastodon matches the path alone); the redirects go straight to `/@…` (Mastodon's `web_url` goes through `/web/@…`); `/share` has no signed-in header and ignores `visibility`. Tests: `server/webfinger.test.ts` (every resource form and link, 400 / 404 / 410), `server/authorize-interaction.test.ts` (signed in and out, local and remote accounts and statuses, suspended accounts, an encoded username, the account budget, the redirects and `/intent`), `server/share-page.test.ts`; all 17 failed before the change. `e2e/web/share.spec.ts` shares text from `/share`, posts it and opens the post through `/authorize_interaction`. The interop sweep's `search` area checks that Mastodon's WebFinger of a tstodon user reads the `subscribe` template, and that following it on tstodon with a Mastodon post's URL lands on that post.

- Privacy settings and admin suspensions federate as Mastodon v4.7.5 does (FED-5, FED-6). Saving `/settings/privacy` sends the actor `Update`, as `Settings::PrivacyController` queues `UpdateDistributionWorker` (`federateActorChange` in `server/local-actor.ts`, also used by `update_credentials` and the profile image routes). Unlocking an account, from the privacy page or `update_credentials` (`changeAccountLocked` in `server/follow-request-approval.ts`), accepts its pending follow requests, local and remote, as `UpdateAccountService#authorize_all_follow_requests`; a remote requester gets the `Accept(Follow)` through `acceptRemoteFollowRequest`, the path `POST /api/v1/follow_requests/:id/authorize` uses. Requesters that are silenced or suspended (the account, the actor, or its domain) stay pending, as Mastodon skips silenced ones. The request accepts the first 20 local and 20 remote requests and `AuthorizeFollowRequests` queue jobs the rest; a page of 20 remote requests measures 264 D1 statements. Suspending or unsuspending a local account (`moderateLocalAccount` in `server/admin-actions.ts`, from the admin API, the admin pages and report actions) sends the actor `Update`, with `suspended: true` and the profile stripped, or with the profile back, as `SuspendAccountService` and `UnsuspendAccountService` (`distribute_update_actor!`). The old row said Mastodon sends a `Delete` of the actor on suspension: v4.7.5 sends this `Update`, and the `Delete` only when the account is deleted (`DeleteAccountService#delete_actor!`). An actor `Update` now goes to the inboxes of Mastodon's `AccountReachFinder`, not only the followers': the remote reporters of the account and the actors it mentioned (in its latest 200 statuses), followed or asked to follow in the last two days (`listAccountReachInboxes`, read in `ExpandFollowers` for an `Update` of its own actor; one more statement). Relays are not served, so none are reached. Suspending a remote actor sends it a `Reject(Follow)` for each of its follows of local accounts, as `SuspendAccountService#reject_remote_follows!`, and an `Undo(Follow)` for each local follow of it. Deviation: tstodon already severed both directions at suspension (recorded as `account_suspension`), where Mastodon keeps local accounts' follows of the suspended actor and sends their `Undo(Follow)` only on deletion (`undo_follows!`); the `Undo` tells the remote server what tstodon already dropped. Pending requests from the actor also get a `Reject`, where Mastodon leaves them. The severance runs in pages of 10 local accounts (`server/suspended-actor-severance.ts`, `severActorPage` in `server/severed-relationship-store.ts`), the first in the request and the rest in `SeverSuspendedActorFollows` queue jobs; a page with follows both ways measures about 360 D1 statements, and the instance-wide domain suspension still severs in one batch. While pages remain, the actor gets no post: the follower inbox list (`listAcceptedRemoteFollowerInboxes`) leaves out suspended actors, as its sibling reach queries do. A severance whose next page could not be queued, or whose job gave up, is restarted by the hourly `ResumeSuspendedActorSeverances` job (up to 5 actors suspended over an hour ago that still have follows, each under a new event). Saving an account as unlocked when it already is also accepts the requests a lost unlock page left pending. Unsuspending a remote actor restores no follow, as in Mastodon; Mastodon also refetches the actor then (`refresh_remote_account!`), which tstodon does not. Tests: `server/account-unlock.test.ts`, `server/suspension-federation.test.ts`; interop sweep steps in `e2e/mastodon/probe-follows.ts` and `e2e/mastodon/probe-moderation.ts`.

- A `Follow` whose `Undo` was handled first is dropped, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `delete_arrived_first?`. The interop step "follow, then unfollow at once: Mastodon → tstodon" failed now and then (`the target's follower count is back`). Mastodon sends the `Follow` and the `Undo` as two `DeliveryWorker` jobs that Sidekiq runs at once. In the failed run of PR #151 the proxy saw the `Follow` 15 ms before the `Undo`, both were handled at the same time, the `Undo` answered 31 ms before the `Follow`, and its delete ran before the `Follow`'s insert: the follow stayed. An `Undo(Follow)` that ends no follow is remembered by the actor and the `Follow`'s id for six hours (`undone_before_arrival`, migration 0149; `rememberUndoneFollow.sql` in one batch with the delete), and `upsertRemoteFollow.sql` records the `Follow` only when no such row is younger than six hours, in the same statement. A `Follow` dropped this way gets no notification and no `Accept`. The activity vacuum deletes the rows past six hours (`undone` in `ActivityVacuumPass` and `activity.vacuum`). `server/undo-before-follow.test.ts` sends the `Undo` and then the `Follow`; it failed before the fix with a follower and an `Accept`. `Undo` of a `Like`, an `Announce` or a `Block` stays in FED-9.

- An `Update` of a note this server never stored is handled as its `Create`, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `Update#update_status` (FED-3). It was dropped, and `server/remote-update.ts` said Mastodon dropped it too. As Mastodon, the `Update` is ignored when the note was published more than a day ago (`OBJECT_AGE_THRESHOLD`, read from `published`, not `updated`; a `published` that does not parse is not too old), and when the note's id is on another host than the signer's (`non_matching_uri_hosts?`, now checked for a cached note too). Otherwise it runs the `Create` path, `applyRemoteCreate` (`server/inbox/create.ts`, the `Create` handler's body): a vote on a local poll, the `Created` owed marker, mention rows and notifications, the direct conversation, the quote, the link card and the `update` stream event. A note stored from any document whose `updated` differs from its `published` (a `Create`, an `Update` handled as one, a fetch) is stored as edited then, as Mastodon's `Create#process_status_params` (`noteFromUnknown`), so a late, older `Update` does not roll it back (the `isNewer` guard). A redelivered `Update` that edits nothing, with no edit pending, for a note whose `Created` marker is still owed, stores the counts it carries (a `Question`'s votes) and then finishes those steps as a redelivered `Create` does (`persistRemoteObject` with the row just written), so a mention is notified once. `resumeCreated` runs them only from the version the row holds (`sameVersion`: both unedited, or edited at the same instant), so a `Create` of the original that arrives after an `Update` stored a newer version never runs the original's mentions; before, it checked only that the row was unedited. One deviation remains: Mastodon processes an `Update` from a suspended author (`activity_allowed_while_suspended?`) and drops only the unknown note; tstodon's inbox refuses every activity of a suspended actor with 403, so nothing changes for one. Tests: `server/remote-update.test.ts` (a note published 23 hours ago is stored and is on a follower's home, one published 25 hours ago is not, a suspended author's `Update` is refused with 403, a forged note and an off-host note are not stored, an off-host cached note is not edited, a note whose `updated` differs from `published` is stored as edited, an older `Update` after the newer one stored as a `Create` changes nothing, a replayed `Update` does not notify again), `server/inbox-retry.test.ts` (an `Update` that failed before its mention rows or after every late step is redelivered: one notification, the stream event once, nothing owed; a `Create` of the original after a failed newer `Update` notifies none of its mentions; a count-only `Update` that finishes owed steps stores its counts; a failed `Create` of an edited note resumes on redelivery). The interop sweep's edits area has a step where a Mastodon user no tstodon user follows edits a post to mention a tstodon user.
- `statuses_count` leaves direct statuses out, as Mastodon's `Status#increment_counter_caches` (FED-11). The 0138 triggers counted every `statuses` row, so the REST `statuses_count` and the ActivityPub outbox's `totalItems` revealed how many direct messages an account sent. Migration 0148 replaces `statuses_insert_stats` and `statuses_delete_stats` with ones that skip `visibility = 'direct'`, adds `statuses_update_visibility_stats` for a row whose visibility moves into or out of direct (no request does today: an edit keeps the visibility, as Mastodon), and recounts `account_stats.statuses_count` for every account. Tests: `server/counters.test.ts` (its reference count and the backfill replay leave direct out, and a visibility flip stays counted), `server/actor-collections.test.ts` (REST and AP counts with a direct status).
- The followers, following and outbox collections are paged and counted as Mastodon v4.7.5 serves them (FED-1). They were one page whose `totalItems` was that page's length (20 by default, 40 at most), and Mastodon reads a remote account's statuses, followers and following counts from those `totalItems` (`ActivityPub::ProcessAccountService`), so no tstodon account showed more than 20 to 40 of each there; `hide_collections` was ignored. Without `page`, `followers` and `following` (`followCollection` in `server/routes/activitypub.ts`) answer an `OrderedCollection` with the account's count from `account_stats` and `first` (`?page=1`), and the outbox its `statuses_count`, `first` (`?page=true`) and `last` (`?min_id=0&page=true`), each a link, as Mastodon. A page is an `OrderedCollectionPage` with `partOf`, `next` and `prev`: 12 follows newest first, with the count, or 20 activities with none (`CollectionPage` in `packages/domain`). An account that hides its collections serves the count without `first`, which Mastodon reads as hidden, and refuses every follows page with 403, the owner and the instance actor included, as Mastodon's `protect_hidden_collections`. An outbox page lists the account's statuses and boosts (`listOutboxStatusIds` in `server/actor-outbox-store.ts`), rendered as they are now: a `Create` of the note as its URI serves it (`loadLocalNoteDocument`, which the status route shares), or an `Announce`; so a deleted status, an edit's old version and an undone boost never show, and the pages reach every status; the activity vacuum no longer keeps each account's 40 newest public activities for the outbox (`pruneOutboundActivities`). As `AccountStatusesFilter`, an unsigned fetch reads public and unlisted statuses, a signed one also those that mention its actor, an accepted follower the followers-only ones too, and a fetcher the account blocks (or whose domain it blocks) nothing. A suspended account's outbox, root and pages alike, answers 403 cached for 3 minutes, as Mastodon's `check_account_suspension`, so neither its count nor its followers-only posts are served. A page whose boosted status could not be read answers 500, uncached; only a boosted status that is gone leaves its boost out (`statusTargetRef` in `server/quote-uri.ts` returns a `Result`). Each visibility is a range of `idx_statuses_account_visibility_id` (migration 0148, created before its recount), merged in id order, so a page does not read the statuses it skips. As Mastodon, a root is `Cache-Control: max-age=180, public` (`private` in secure mode) and a page `max-age=60`, `public` for an unsigned fetch and `private` otherwise, with `Vary: Signature`. One deviation: Mastodon numbers the follows pages (`?page=2`), and tstodon links them by follow id (`?max_id=…&page=true`, `?min_id=…&page=true`), so a page reads a bounded index range (a deep `OFFSET` would read every follow before it) and a walk neither skips nor repeats a follow made meanwhile; consumers follow `next`, so the URL shape is theirs to ignore. Rows read do not grow with more follows or statuses, nor with the direct and followers-only statuses an outbox page passes over, unsigned or signed (`server/d1-budget.test.ts`). A signed page also reads the statuses that mention its signer, starting from `idx_status_remote_mentions_actor`: the branch joins with `CROSS JOIN`, because D1 keeps no `sqlite_stat1` and SQLite otherwise drove the join from `idx_statuses_account_id` to skip the merge's sort, reading every status of the account for a signer mentioned nowhere (12000 more rows after 12000 more statuses). A root costs 3 statements and 4 rows, a follows page 5 statements and about 41 rows, an outbox page of 20 notes about 125 statements and 340 rows unsigned and 133 statements and 350 rows signed, as each note's media, mentions, parent, poll, quote and quote policy are read for it. The outbox and follows `?limit` parameter is gone, as Mastodon has none. Contract schemas `ApActorCollection`, `ApActorCollectionPage`, `ApOutbox` and `ApOutboxPage` (`@tstodon/api/activitypub`); `server/actor-collections.test.ts` walks 45 follows and 45 statuses page by page and checks deleted, edited and unboosted statuses; the interop sweep's `profile` area checks that Mastodon shows a tstodon account's three counts and its hidden collections.
- Request-path performance and CI coverage (PERF-1 to PERF-4, CI-1, CI-2 of the November 2026 audit).
  - A remote note may name any number of local accounts, as in Mastodon. Every one can read it and finds it in its conversations: the mention, silent-mention and remote-mention rows are written set-based with `json_each`, in one batch whatever the count. The inbox request notifies the first 8 recipients and publishes their conversation updates (`INLINE_NOTE_RECIPIENTS`); one `FinishRemoteNoteRecipient` job per further recipient on the outbox queue does the rest. A job re-screens the recipient, skips one an `Update` made silent, retries when the note's mentions cannot be read, and settles one whose account was deleted. Measured with every recipient's `direct` stream open: about 650 D1 statements per inbox request for 30 or 60 recipients, 833 per 10-job queue invocation (`remote-note-recipients.test.ts`).
  - `familiar_followers` reads its accounts in one batch (9 statements for 1 or 20 ids) and answers the first 40 distinct ids.
  - Bulk and single accept and dismiss of notification requests run one set-based batch, and skip request groups whose held notifications are gone, as the single routes do. Not capped, as in Mastodon.
  - The quotes list checks visibility with `viewableStatusIds`.
  - HTTP behaviour tests cover bulk notification requests, status unmute, admin link-trend approve, header blobs, `/auth/sign_up` and the v1 search alias.
  - `e2e-mastodon` runs all four authorized-fetch modes. `e2e-federation.yml` runs six tstodon-to-tstodon scenarios (`e2e/federation-ci.sh`, no process-compose) on pull requests and nightly.
- Security hardening from the November 2026 audit (SEC-1 to SEC-6). SEC-1, delivery SSRF: an inbox URL comes from a remote actor document, yet delivery POSTed to it with no host check and followed redirects. `prepareInboxDelivery` (`server/delivery.ts`) now accepts only `http(s)` URLs, runs `checkFederatedHost` at each attempt (private and reserved addresses, IP literals, `localhost` / `.internal` / `.local`, a name resolving to one; `FEDERATION_ALLOW_HOSTS` still exempts a host), settles a refused inbox without contacting it (`OutboxInboxRefused`), retries a failed DNS check as a local failure, and sends with `redirect: "manual"`: a 3xx is retried like any other non-2xx answer that is not unsalvageable, as Mastodon's `DeliveryWorker` raises on it. An inbox on another host than its actor is still accepted, as Mastodon accepts it (split-domain servers and some software serve the inbox elsewhere). SEC-2, dependencies: zod 4.6.7, hono 4.13.19 (one hono serves Void's router and ours), better-auth 1.7.25, and `.github/dependabot.yml` (npm grouped weekly, Void majors ignored; github-actions weekly); see CI-4 for the audit step. SEC-3, body size: one request-wide cap (`app/middleware/03.body-limit.ts`, `server/body-limit.ts`), 1 MB (Mastodon's `MAX_JSON_SIZE`) for any body without a file and the upload routes' own limits plus form overhead, answered 413 on a declared `Content-Length` before the route runs and, for a chunked body, as soon as the counter passes the cap, without buffering; the inbox reads at most 1 MB (`InboxRefused` reason `PayloadTooLarge`), and fetched ActivityPub and WebFinger documents are read up to 1 MB (Mastodon's `body_with_limit`). Custom emoji uploads are now held to Mastodon's 256 KB. SEC-4, tokens in logs: `observability.redact_query_string` keeps `?access_token=` out of Workers Logs and traces; the query parameter stays supported for third-party streaming clients, and the web UI already sent its token as the WebSocket subprotocol. SEC-5, security headers: every HTML page carries Mastodon's content security policy (`server/security-headers.ts`, `app/middleware/00.security-headers.ts`: `default-src 'none'`, `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, a per-response style nonce on the web app shell, which the hashtag column's react-select reads from `meta[name=style-nonce]`, and the embed code's style hashes for the "Get embed code" preview; remote images and media over `https:`; no policy on scripts, as `/sw.js`'s would stop the service worker fetching remote avatars), `Referrer-Policy` and `X-Content-Type-Options` on every answer, `frame-ancestors 'none'` with `X-Frame-Options: DENY` (except a status's embed page, which any site may frame); the sign-in page's sign-out script and the import page's reload moved to `public/sign-out.js` and `public/page-refresh.js`; HSTS is left to Cloudflare's edge. SEC-6, signature window: a signed `Date` is accepted for 65 minutes (5 minutes plus the 1 hour skew, Mastodon's `matches_time_window?`) instead of 12 hours. Tests: `server/delivery-host-guard.test.ts`, `server/middleware-coverage.test.ts`, `server/body-limit.test.ts`, `server/security-headers.test.ts`, `server/http-signature.test.ts`; `e2e/web` scenarios fail on any content security policy refusal.

- The interop sweep's last four known gaps are closed (INT-4, INT-9, INT-14): it passes 221 of 221 steps with both servers open and 223 of 223 with both in secure mode, with no known gap. Each change has a Worker test. (1) Replies of replies. The thread backfill of an opened remote status walks its reply tree, as Mastodon's `FetchAllRepliesWorker`: after the first pass, `BackfillRemoteThread` jobs (`from: Level`) expand the cached public and unlisted replies one level at a time (`listRemoteThreadLevel`, a recursive CTE over `in_reply_to_uri`), five a job or fewer once the replies a job expanded listed 20, and queue the next slice or level, until a level is empty or the tree listed 1000 replies or read 500 pages (`MAX_REPLIES`, `MAX_PAGES`). No one vouches for a reply or an ancestor, as in Mastodon's `FetchReplyWorker`: each is fetched from its URI and stored only under an author on that host, and an embedded reply is read as it comes only when its URI is on the origin that listed it. A reply below the status whose list does not answer is skipped, as `FetchAllRepliesWorker` skips a failed fetch; a first pass that cannot read the status's list whole, or a job that cannot queue the next, is retried and leaves the fetch running; only the last job closes it. A `replies` collection on another origin is not read (`fetch_collection_page` with `reference_uri`) (`server/remote-thread-backfill.test.ts`). (2) Delete of a boosted post. `deleteLocalStatus` reads, before the delete, the post's reach as Mastodon's `StatusReachFinder` with `unsafe: true` (remote accounts that mentioned, boosted, favourited or replied to it) and, for a public or unlisted post, the remote followers of the local accounts that boosted it, and sends the `Delete` there as a second activity (`statusDeleteReach`, `server/status-reach.ts`). Mastodon v4.7.5 sends nothing to those followers for a local post (`RemoveStatusService#remove_reblogs` passes `original_removed: true`, which skips `remove_from_remote_reach`) and reaches them for a remote post with `ActivityPub::Forwarder`; tstodon sends the author's `Delete` instead (`server/status-delete-federation.test.ts`). (3) Followed hashtags. Home holds the public notes, local and remote, under the reader's followed hashtags, posted or received since the follow, as Mastodon's `deliver_to_hashtag_followers` and `FeedManager#filter_from_tags?`: no boosts, none of the reader's own, none from an author it blocks or mutes, that blocks it, on a domain it blocks, or that the instance limits. tstodon builds home at query time, so the cost is bounded per page: an id sorts by its instant, so the follow is an id floor; the viewer's hidden authors are left out before anything is ranked; and a page reads each followed hashtag's index at most four pages deep below its cursor (`followedTagHorizonSql`), with every home source clipped to that horizon, so a hidden flood shortens a page instead of hiding a post. A page the horizon leaves empty is read again past it, up to four times (`HOME_HORIZON_STEPS`, `server/timeline-documents.ts`). A D1 budget test reads 417 more rows for a page after 6000 hidden posts under a followed hashtag, and 1746 when the flood leaves home empty. A followed actor's notes stay on the followed-actor query. The streaming fan-out asks the followers of the entry's hashtags too (`listHomeFanOutAccounts`) (`server/followed-tag-home.test.ts`, `server/d1-budget.test.ts`). (4) Inbound featured hashtags. Migration 0147 adds `remote_featured_tags` and `remote_actors.featured_tags_uri`. An `Add` or `Remove` of a `Hashtag` to the signer's `featured` collection features or unfeatures it (`Add#add_featured_tags`), ten at most; the profile backfill reads the `featuredTags` collection, or the hashtags of `featured` for an actor that names none, and keeps the hashtags among its first ten items (`FetchFeaturedTagsCollectionService`, `max_items: FeaturedTag::LIMIT`). `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/featured_tags` lists them as `REST::FeaturedTagSerializer` does: the actor's order, the count and date of its public and unlisted posts held here, `/@user@domain/tagged/tag`, and none for an unavailable account. `last_status_at` is a date, for a local account too (`server/remote-featured-tags.test.ts`).
- A vote, an edit and a remote sender's notification follow Mastodon 4.7.5. A vote `VoteValidator` refuses (one's own poll, an option the poll lacks) answers 422 with its message (`PollBallot`). `PUT /api/v1/statuses/:id` is the whole revision (INT-18): a field the request leaves out is removed, and a poll without `expires_in` is refused. The notification policy applies to remote senders, and `for_private_mentions` defaults to `filter` (INT-2, migration 0146). The interop sweep's INT-2 and INT-18 steps are plain steps, and with PR #133's federation fixes it passes 217 of 217 steps with both servers open, with 4 known gaps (INT-4, INT-9, INT-14).
- Pins, account deletion, the `replies` collection and outbound featured hashtags federate with Mastodon (INT-15, INT-4, INT-14). The interop sweep passes 213 of 213 steps with both servers open, 214 of 214 with tstodon's `AUTHORIZED_FETCH` on, and 215 of 215 with both servers in secure mode; six more steps are known gaps, which the INT-2, INT-4, INT-9, INT-14 and INT-18 rows keep. Each change has a Worker test. (1) Pins. A local actor names a `featured` collection, served at `/users/:name/collections/featured` (`getUsersByUsernameCollectionsFeatured`, `server/routes/activitypub.ts`): the pinned public and unlisted notes, newest pin first, as URIs. A pin of such a note sends an `Add` and an unpin a `Remove` to the followers; a followers-only pin is neither listed nor announced (`featuredActivity`, `server/activitypub.ts`). The inbox handles a remote actor's `Add` and `Remove` on its own `featured` collection (`server/inbox/featured.ts`): the note must be the actor's and on its host, it is fetched by its URI when it is not cached (one fetch), and an actor keeps at most 20 pins. Mastodon sends both without an `id`, which the inbox refused with 400; such an activity is now named after the signed request's `Date` and `Digest` (`nameCollectionActivity`, `server/activitypub-wire.ts`), so a redelivery is a duplicate and a later pin of the same note is not (`server/featured-collection.test.ts`). (2) Account deletion. `purgeAccount` (`server/admin-actions.ts`) reads what the `Delete` of the actor needs before it removes anything, stores the `Delete`, its targets and the key that signs it in the D1 batch that deletes the account's rows, and starts its delivery once that batch has committed, as Mastodon's `DeleteAccountService` sends after the deletion (`server/actor-delete.ts`): a deletion that fails part way sends nothing and can be tried again, and one that commits always has its `Delete` stored. A delivery that does not start is started by the stuck-delivery sweep within the hour. It goes to the inboxes of the account's remote followers and of the remote accounts it follows, then to every other inbox this server knows, most recently fetched first, up to 1000 inboxes in all (the followers and follows are never cut); an account with no remote follower and no remote follow sends nothing. Its id is one per deletion (`<actor>#deletes/<key>`). The activity is stored without an account, and migration 0142 adds `outbound_departed_signers`, which keeps the name and the private key that sign it. A trigger (migration 0145) deletes that row when the last of the activity's targets leaves `Queued`, whatever settles it: a delivery, the stuck-delivery sweep, or the purge of a remote actor that fails its deliveries. The actor URI of a deleted account answers 410 (migration 0144, `deleted_account_names`); its other paths and WebFinger answer 404 (`server/account-delete-federation.test.ts`). An activity sent to named inboxes used to keep a `Queued` fan-out row for good, which kept it from the activity vacuum: such a row is `Expanded` from the start now, and migration 0143 settles the old ones (`server/targeted-activity-retention.test.ts`). An inbound `Delete` of a remote actor was handled before (`server/remote-actor-delete.test.ts`). (3) Replies. A note document carries `replies` in the shape of Mastodon's `NoteSerializer`, and `/users/:name/statuses/:id/replies` serves the collection and its pages (`server/note-replies.ts`): the author's own replies, then other local accounts' replies, then the replies cached from other servers (`RepliesPage`, `packages/domain/src/replies-page.ts`), public and unlisted only, 60 URIs a page, one D1 query a page. A note that not everyone may read answers 404 (`server/note-replies.test.ts`). (4) Featured hashtags, outbound. A local actor names a `featuredTags` collection, served at `/users/:name/collections/tags`, and featuring or unfeaturing a hashtag sends an `Add` or a `Remove` of a `Hashtag` to the followers (`server/featured-tags-federation.test.ts`). All three collections need a signature in secure mode, and the two actor collections are empty for a signer the account blocks.

- The interop sweep's known gaps for remote media metadata (INT-11), a remote account's custom emoji (INT-13) and a remote account's profile fields (INT-15) are closed. It passes 206 of 206 steps with both servers open; 13 more steps are known gaps, which the INT-2, INT-4, INT-9, INT-14, INT-15 and INT-18 rows keep. Nothing is migrated: the new values are optional keys of `remote_statuses.media_json` and `remote_actors.profile_json`, and a row written before reads without them. (1) An `Update` reads as an edit of the media only when a file's URL, a description or the order changes (`sameMedia`, `server/remote-status-edit.ts`), as Mastodon's `ProcessStatusUpdateService` and `MediaAttachmentParser#significantly_changes?`; before, any stored property counted, so a property kept later would have marked every stored note as edited on its next `Update`. Mastodon also compares the thumbnail's URL, which tstodon does not keep. (2) A remote attachment keeps its `blurhash`, `width` and `height`, and `focalPoint` (`remoteAttachmentMetadata`, `server/remote-attachment.ts`): a blurhash of at most five components on each axis whose length is the one it declares, a size of two positive integers, and a focal point given as `[x, y]` or `"x,y"`, each axis held to -1..1. One that does not read is left out and the note is kept. The REST API shows `blurhash`, `meta.original`, `meta.focus`, and for an image `meta.small`, the size Mastodon's `small` style would have (`server/remote-media-metadata.test.ts`). (3) A remote actor's `Emoji` tags that its display name, bio or fields write are kept on its profile and listed as the account's `emojis` (`actorEmojis`, `server/remote-emoji-document.ts`). A shortcode matches as written, case and all, as Mastodon's `CustomEmoji.from_text`. (4) A remote actor's `PropertyValue` attachments are the account's `fields` (`actorFields`, `server/remote-actor-fields.ts`): the first fifty, each name and value trimmed and cut at 2047 characters, as Mastodon's `ProcessAccountService#property_values` and `Account::Field`; the value is sanitised as remote HTML when rendered, and `verified_at` is null (`server/remote-actor-profile.test.ts`).
- The interop sweep covers INT-5 and INT-7 to INT-15 in both directions, and the five bugs those areas found are fixed (`e2e/mastodon/`, one file per area: `probe-edits.ts`, `probe-reactions.ts`, `probe-polls.ts`, `probe-media.ts`, `probe-emoji.ts`, `probe-hashtags.ts`, `probe-profile.ts`, `probe-moderation.ts`, `probe-quotes.ts`, `probe-move.ts`). It passes 202 of 202 steps with both servers open, 203 of 203 with tstodon's `AUTHORIZED_FETCH` on, and 204 of 204 with both servers in secure mode. Thirteen more steps are known gaps, which the INT-9, INT-11, INT-13, INT-14, INT-15 and INT-18 rows keep. INT-12 is done: a stranger's favourite and boost, a favourite of a followers-only post, an author's boost of their own followers-only post, `favourited_by` and `reblogged_by`, and the notification withdrawn with an undo all pass. The quote area (the `quote` notification, the `nobody` and `followers` policies, a revoked quote, a deleted quoted post, a policy closed by an edit) and the move area (an account moved from each server to the other, with its followers on both) pass with no fix. A forwarded report reaches the other server's moderators in both directions, with the post and without the reporter's name. The fixes, each with a Worker test. (1) An `Announce` of a followers-only status by its own author is kept, as Mastodon's `announceable?`: `RemoteBoost.fromAnnounce` (`packages/domain/src/remote-boost.ts`) takes the target's author, the boost is followers-only whatever its audience says, and the home and profile queries show it (`BOOSTABLE_TARGET_SQL`, `server/remote-boost-store.ts`); another actor's boost of the post is still dropped. (2) A vote counts from the vote rows: `recountPollOptionIfActive` (`prisma/sql`) sets an option's tally to the number of its local and remote vote rows in the batch that writes the vote, and replaces `updatePollOptionsIfActive`, which wrote the options one request had read plus one. Mastodon delivers the choices of a multiple-choice ballot at once, and the second was lost; two local or remote voters at the same moment lost one the same way. (3) `GET /emoji/:id` serves a custom emoji's image when the Worker serves the media (`getEmojiById`, `server/routes/media.ts`), for an hour in the client's cache and not in the Worker's, because an admin replaces the image under the same key; before, every emoji URL answered 404 in that mode. (4) A local note names the custom emoji its text, content warning and poll options use as `Emoji` tags (`noteDocument`, `server/activitypub.ts`), in the document at its URI and in each `Create` and `Update`; before, only the actor document did. (5) `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/featured_tags` answers an empty list for a remote account this server knows, and 404 for one it does not; before, 404 for both. A review of these fixes found five more things, also fixed and covered. The emoji route no longer takes the static twemoji paths (`/emoji/1f600.svg`): an emoji's key is `emoji/{id}`, digits only, and any other path goes to the assets with its cache headers. A boost is shown without a follow only when its row and its target are both public or unlisted (`OPEN_TARGET_SQL`), so a post narrowed to followers after a public boost of it no longer shows on the booster's profile to everyone. An emoji image is a PNG, GIF or WebP file (and APNG), an older one of another type is served as `application/octet-stream`, and every object the Worker serves from the bucket carries `nosniff`. A custom emoji shortcode matches in the case it is written (`emojisInText`), as Mastodon's `CustomEmoji.from_text`, so an `Emoji` tag's name is always in the text. A local vote follows Mastodon's `VoteValidator` (`votePoll`, `ballotRefusal`): a single-choice poll takes one option once, a multiple-choice poll each option once, else 422. The harness changes: Mastodon's `web` and `sidekiq` share the `public/system` volume, without which no remote file Mastodon downloaded could be read back; `provision.rb` makes the Owner `moderator`, and the sweep calls it again for the Mastodon users a scenario uses up; `mastodonRails` runs Ruby in Mastodon for a migration, an alias, an account deletion and a poll's end; `tstodonAdmin` makes a tstodon admin with an admin-scoped token; a `knownGap` step waits eight seconds where a step waits twenty. Also recorded in the harness README: Mastodon applies an `Update` as an edit only when it is dated after the one it holds, to the second; its `PUT /api/v1/statuses/:id` empties a field the request leaves out; it does not send a `Delete` to the followers of a local account that boosted the post.
- CI runs the interop harness against a real Mastodon (INT-16). `e2e/mastodon/ci.sh` is one whole run: it removes the state of an earlier run, runs `bootstrap.sh`, starts `start-tstodon.sh` in the background, waits for the Worker, runs `pnpm e2e:mastodon` and `pnpm e2e:mastodon:probe`, keeps the logs of both servers in `.cache/e2e-mastodon/`, and removes the stack and the tstodon database again. The workflow `.github/workflows/e2e-mastodon.yml` calls that script and nothing else, so a run on a developer's machine is the run CI does. It has three legs: both servers open, tstodon in secure mode, and both in secure mode. It runs on a pull request that changes the Worker's server code, `packages/api`, `packages/domain`, `packages/core`, the harness or the lockfile, every night, and on `workflow_dispatch`; it is a workflow of its own because `ci.yml` has no path filter. A failed leg prints the end of the Worker's and Mastodon's logs and uploads all of them as the artifact `e2e-mastodon-logs-<mode>`. The workflow needs no repository secret: the script generates the certificates, Mastodon's keys and `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`. One leg takes about thirteen minutes with the images already pulled, since the sweep grew to INT-5 and INT-7 to INT-15.
- The interop sweep against a real Mastodon covers INT-1, INT-2, INT-3, INT-4 and INT-6, and the five bugs it found are fixed (`e2e/mastodon/`, one file per area: `probe-search.ts`, `probe-direct.ts`, `probe-threads.ts`, `probe-follows.ts`, `probe-authorized-fetch.ts`). It passes 84 of 84 steps with both servers open, 85 of 85 with tstodon's `AUTHORIZED_FETCH` on, and 86 of 86 with both servers in secure mode; four more steps are known gaps, which the INT-2 and INT-4 rows keep. INT-3 and INT-6 are done. INT-3: with `AUTHORIZED_FETCH=true` (`TSTODON_AUTHORIZED_FETCH=true e2e/mastodon/start-tstodon.sh`) Mastodon still fetches a tstodon actor with its profile, its `outbox`, `followers` and `following` counts and its notes, signed by its instance actor, and an unsigned fetch gets the restricted actor document and 401 for a note and each collection. INT-6: a locked account on each side authorizes and rejects follow requests, a follow after a `Reject` is a new request, removing a follower ends the follow on the other server, and a follow with an unfollow at once leaves no follow. The fixes, each with a Worker test. (1) A URL lookup fetches the URL once and follows the id it names (`resolveSearchUrl`, `server/search-url.ts`), as Mastodon's `FetchResourceService`: what is stored under the URL is used, else the URL is fetched once and stored as the note or the actor it is; a document that names another id (Mastodon's page URLs `https://host/@user` and `https://host/@user/123` serve the document whose id is `/ap/users/...`) is looked up under that id, stored or fetched once, and must then name itself. Before, a profile page URL and a post's page URL resolved to nothing, and an actor URI with no `type` was fetched twice. The lookup runs once whatever `type` asks for, and a resource of another type is left out. A profile URL of this instance (`/users/name`, `/@name`) answers the local account. Not followed: an HTML page's `<link rel="alternate">` and a `Link` header (INT-1). (2) `user@this-domain` names the local account in `/api/v2/search`, first, with or without `resolve`, and nothing is fetched (`localHandleUsername`); an account pending approval is left out of every exact local match, as of the text search (`searchableLocalAccounts`); before, with the instance's own host in `FEDERATION_ALLOW_HOSTS` it stored and answered a remote copy of its own account, and otherwise nothing. (3) The reply backfill of an opened remote status reads up to three pages of `replies` (`THREAD_BACKFILL_REPLY_PAGES`, `server/remote-thread-backfill.ts`), still at most 20 replies, and follows `next` only on the status's origin: Mastodon's first page holds the author's own replies only. A status document or a later page that does not answer retries the job and leaves the fetch running, so an outage does not close a thread cut short for the hour the claim lasts. (4) An `Undo` that embeds what it undoes is claimed in the inbox under both ids (`inboxClaimKey`, `server/inbox-store.ts`): Mastodon's `Undo(Follow)` id is `<actor>#follows/<n>/undo`, with `n` from the follow requests' sequence while the follow is pending and from the follows' once accepted, so two `Undo`s of one actor can carry one id and the second was answered as a duplicate. (5) A conversation's participants include the remote actors a remote message mentions (`listRemoteStatusMentions`, `server/mention-store.ts`). Also recorded in the harness README: Mastodon keeps a filtered private mention out of the conversations until its notification request is accepted, and leaves a reply out of a home feed unless the reader follows the account replied to.
- Three bugs the first sweep against a real Mastodon found are fixed (the harness is `e2e/mastodon/`). (1) Search accepts a long query and looks up a post URL. `GET /api/v2/search` answered 500 (`LIKE or GLOB pattern too complex`) for any `q` over 48 bytes: D1 refuses a LIKE pattern over 50 bytes. The text matches of account search, remote actor search, status search, the admin account filters and the admin metrics domain filter now use `instr(lower(column), lower(?))` (`containsFolded`, `server/d1.ts`), which takes no pattern. Case folds as before, ASCII letters only; `%` and `_` in the text now match themselves. A URL with `resolve=true` is looked up as Mastodon's `ResolveURLService` does (`resolveStatusUrl`, `server/search-url.ts`): a local post by its URI or page URL when the URL names its author, a remote post from the cache or fetched from its URL and stored (`persistFetchedObject`), each only when the viewer may see it. The answer is that one status or account and nothing else, as Mastodon's `SearchService`: no text search runs beside it. A document whose id is not the URL, an author on another host, a suspended domain and a note the viewer may not see resolve to nothing (`server/search-url-resolve.test.ts`). (2) A remote status renders its mentions. The REST `mentions` of a cached remote status was always `[]`; it is now read per page from `remote_status_mentions` (without the silent mentions that only grant access) and `remote_status_remote_mentions`, in stored order, through the builder local statuses use (`mentionEntities`, `server/mention-documents.ts`; `server/remote-status-mentions.test.ts`). The order is the local accounts, then the remote actors, each in stored order, where Mastodon sorts all of them by mention id; a mentioned remote actor that is not cached is left out, as for a local status. Only the note's `Mention` tags are mentions, as Mastodon's `Create#process_mention`: a local account that is only in `to`/`cc` gets a silent row (`process_audience`), so it can read the note, is not listed and is not notified. A mention an `Update` drops becomes silent and keeps its access (`ProcessStatusUpdateService#update_mentions!`). Rows stored before this keep `silent = 0` whether a tag or the audience made them: nothing stored tells the two apart (the mention notification was sent for both), so such an account stays listed until the note's next `Update`, which sorts its rows again. Not followed: Mastodon turns a direct note with a silent mention into a `limited` one, which has no conversation; here it stays `direct` and its conversation lists it. (3) A 404 stays a 404 for a request that names `text/html`. Void's SPA fallback answered every Worker 404 with `public/index.html` and status 200 when `Accept` contained `text/html`, which Mastodon's fetches do (`text/html;q=0.1`), so a note Mastodon may not read answered a page and Mastodon did not fall back to its stored copy. `routing.notFound` is `none` in `packages/worker/void.json` (`server/activitypub-negotiation.test.ts`). A browser gets the same 404 on a path nothing answers, including the web app's client routes outside `WEB_APP_PATHS` (`/accounts/:id`, `/timelines/...`), as upstream Rails answers them.
- The limits the SHR-12 review found are fixed, in seven steps (SHR-12 follow-up). (1) A remote reply's local parent (`findThreadParent`, `server/thread-context.ts`) and the local note an `Announce` names (`announcedTarget`, `server/remote-boost.ts`) are read through `readStatusById` and `readableRow`, as the other inbox reads of a local note: a row that does not read is logged (`InvalidStoredRowSkipped`) and counts as none. The inbox answered 202 for both already, as the parent read behind a mention was tolerated and the `Announce` handler reads its target first; a value fault was a `RepositoryError` inside. (2) A fetch heals a remote row that does not read. `cachedRemoteStatus` (`server/remote-status-persist.ts`) hands the row to its caller, and the paths that fetch notes (`persistAnnouncedObject` for an `Announce` and the thread and outbox backfills, the quote context of `remoteQuoteContext`) fetch the row's URI and replace the row (`healFromFetch`) when the document names that URI as its id, sits on its author's host and is attributed to the author the row names, the proof a fetched note needs to be stored (`fetchedAsItself`). The heal is `settleInvalidRemoteStatus` with a `Fetched` document: it keeps the stored visibility, publication date and language as an `Update` does, is dated by the document's `updated` only, and writes no marker. A fetch that fails or proves nothing leaves the row and the activity is settled with 202 as before; `persistRemoteObject` (a `QuoteRequest`'s instrument, an outbox note) still leaves such a row. (3) A heal no longer overwrites a row another delivery replaced meanwhile. `overwriteRemoteStatus` (`server/remote-status-store.ts`) starts its batch with the guard of `refuseReplacedRemoteStatus.sql` (a history row with a `NULL` `status_id`, which the `NOT NULL` constraint refuses), keyed on every column a rewrite replaces: `salvageRemoteStatus` reads them with the row as one ASCII text, each column's storage class and its bytes in hex (`ROW_VERSION_SQL`), so a column that holds a blob, or text that is not UTF-8, still compares equal. The `edited_at` of the ordinary guard is not enough, as an unreadable row may hold none that reads and two rewrites of it may leave the same one. The inbox answers 500 and the redelivery reads the row again. (4) An `edited_at` that does not read is unknown, not never (`SalvagedRemoteStatus.edit`). A document that changes the content of such a row replaces it only when it carries an `updated` later than the row's `updated_at`, the last write of the row, which no edit it holds was received after: a replayed older `Update` and the `Create` of the note are dropped (`NotNewer`), and so is a document without `updated`, an `Update` included, as dating it now would date its replay now too (`NotDated`). No document replaces the content when `updated_at` does not read either. A document that changes nothing still replaces the row, and its `updated` becomes the row's edit date. `updated_at` is an instant on every write of the row: `setRemoteStatusCard.sql` wrote SQLite's `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` ('YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS') and now writes one too, and the salvage reads the older form, in UTC, from rows that still hold it. The cost: a real edit dated before the row was last written (made before a count refresh or a link card that was written first, or dated by a clock that runs behind) is dropped, and the row stays until a later edit. (5) When `poll_json` was the column that did not read, the salvaged poll is the document's, so the tally compared as unchanged and the settle was skipped. The salvage says so (`pollFilled`), and the heal's batch then settles the local votes whenever the status has a poll. (6) One delivery at a time runs the late steps a marker stands for. `remote_status_owed` has a lease, `claimed_until` (migration 0141): the delivery that writes the marker holds it from that batch, another takes it in one `UPDATE` only when it is `NULL` or past (`claimOwed`, `server/remote-status-owed.ts`), and one that does not get it goes without the steps and is answered 202, as it has nothing of its own left to do: every step is the holder's, whose sender delivers again when it fails. `finishRemoteNote` gives the lease back when a step fails, so the redelivery resumes at once; a delivery that was cut off leaves it to expire after 60 seconds (`OWED_LEASE_SECONDS`). An `Update` that is no edit runs the steps of its own document and leaves a pending edit to its holder. A `QuoteRequest` that misses the lease syncs the quote of its note itself (`syncRemoteQuote`, in `resumeCreated`), outside the lease, as it answers that quote and is not sent again: without it the request found no quote row, was answered 202 with no `Accept` or `Reject`, and the quote stayed pending. The sync is safe beside the holder's: both read the same document, a quote of the same status keeps the state it has, and of two that insert the row at once one fails on the unique quoting status, is answered 5xx and finds the row on redelivery. `notifyFromRemoteOnce` stays the backstop for a lease that expires under a slow holder. (7) A `QuoteRequest` streams the note it stores, as Mastodon v4.7.5 does: its `QuoteRequest` imports the instrument through `FetchRemoteStatusService` (`import_instrument`), which performs it as a `Create` and distributes the note inside the real-time window, and the `Create` that follows finds the status and distributes nothing. `streamEvent` no longer asks for a `Create`: an inbox activity that holds a `Created` marker streams the note as `update` under the same window, and deletes the marker when its steps finish, whichever activity it is. Before, a `Create` that arrived after the `QuoteRequest` that stored the note found nothing owed, and the note was never streamed. An accepted quote is announced with `status.update` already (`announceRemoteQuoteChange`). The note document readers (`noteFromUnknown`, `noteHashtags`, `applyMediaPolicy`) moved to `server/remote-note-document.ts`, so the heal no longer imports the module that calls it. Limits, not fixed: a heal that races the author's `Delete` can bring the note back, as a `Create` or `Update` on the ordinary path can; a lease can expire under a holder that is still running past 60 seconds, and the delivery that takes it over then sends the stream event a second time; a late step that throws, where the others return an error, leaves the lease to expire, so the redelivery waits up to 60 seconds; an `Announce` that embeds its own note does not heal from it, only from a fetch, so a followers-only note, which the fetch cannot read, is never healed by an `Announce`; when the holder of a lease fails and its sender stops retrying, the steps stay undone and the sweep logs the marker after 7 days (`RemoteStatusOwedAbandoned`); `persistRemoteObject` does not heal an unreadable row from a `QuoteRequest`'s instrument or an outbox item. Tests: `server/inbox-unreadable-row.test.ts` (a remote reply to a local note whose row does not read gets 202, and `findThreadParent` and `recordRemoteBoost` read the note as none; an `Announce` of an unreadable remote note whose author's host serves the document replaces the row and stores the boost, for a value and a shape fault; a `Create` that quotes it replaces the row and resolves the quote; a document attributed to another actor, and a note that is not on its author's host, leave the row; a replayed older `Update`, the `Create` and an undated `Update` leave a row whose edit date does not read, an `Update` dated now replaces it, also when `updated_at` is in SQLite's form, and a document that changes nothing gives it its edit date; a stored link card dates the row as an instant; a row that also holds a real and a blob, text that is not UTF-8, or text with a NUL is replaced), `server/inbox-retry.test.ts` (a heal whose batch runs after another delivery replaced the row fails, and its redelivery is dropped as older; a `Create` that arrives in the first late step of the `QuoteRequest` that stored the note leaves one `update` on the public stream and one mention notification; two deliveries that resume one failed `Create` at once start the fan-out once; a `Create`, and an `Update` that is no edit, leave the steps to a lease that stands and finish them once it is past; a `QuoteRequest` that arrives while a `Create` holds the lease and has not stored the quote accepts it and queues its `Accept`; a note a `QuoteRequest` stores is heard once on the public stream as `update`, and not again when its `Create` arrives; a `QuoteRequest` and a `Create` sent together leave the quote accepted with one `Accept`, as a smoke test), `server/remote-poll.test.ts` (a tally that replaces a row whose `poll_json` does not read settles the local vote).
- A stored row that will never read no longer makes the inbox answer 500 to every redelivery, beyond the note's own `Create` and `Update` (SHR-12). A failed read still answers 500, so the sender retries; a row that does not read is settled with a logged 202, as Mastodon drops a `RecordInvalid`. (a) The other inbox readers of a remote status row now tell the two apart through one settle, `readableRow` (`server/d1.ts`): the row is logged (`InvalidStoredRowSkipped`) and counts as none. An `Announce` of such a note is dropped; a `Create` or `Update` that quotes it stores the quote unresolved, without fetching the note over the row; a `QuoteRequest` whose quoting note it is gets no answer, and one the policy refuses still gets its `Reject`; the quoted author's `Accept`, `Reject` or stamp `Delete` for a quote of it is dropped; a reply's parent is unknown (`findThreadParent`, `loadNoteReply`). `cachedRemoteStatus` (`server/remote-status-persist.ts`) is the one read of the persist paths and keeps the row apart from an absent one (`Unreadable`), so nothing is fetched or stored over it. A row whose shape fails `RemoteStatusRowSchema` (an empty `published_at`) was left out by `queryTyped` and read as absent, a third answer for the same fault: the readers the inbox settles with (`findDeliveredRemoteStatus`, `findDeliveredRemoteStatusById`) now parse the raw row, so it is a `StoredRowInvalid` too (`fault: "Shape"`, where a value that does not read is `"Values"`), and its author's `Create` replaces it where it answered 500. The general finders (`findRemoteStatusByObjectUri`, `findRemoteStatusById`, `findStatusById`, `findPollByStatusId`, `findPollById`) answer through `foundRow` and keep what they gave before: none for a shape fault, a `RepositoryError` for a value fault, so the REST API still answers 404 and a job still skips. (b) `StoredRowInvalid` is now the error of any stored row that does not read (`server/d1.ts`; the remote status one adds its author, `InvalidRemoteStatusRow`). `readStatusById` (`server/status-store.ts`) and `readPollByStatusId` (`server/poll-store.ts`) return it, also for a row whose shape does not parse, and `handleRemotePollVote` drops a vote on such a poll with a logged 202 (`PollVoteDroppedForInvalidRow`), neither stored as a status nor counted. The other inbox reads of a local note settle through `readableRow` too: an `Announce` of a note whose row does not read is dropped, a `Create` or `Update` that quotes it is stored without the quote, a `QuoteRequest` on it gets no answer, and the answer to a quote such a note makes is dropped. A value fault answered 500 there before; a shape fault answered 202, as the row read as none. (c) The heal (`settleInvalidRemoteStatus`, now `server/remote-status-heal.ts`) starts from what the row still holds: `salvageRemoteStatus` reads each column that reads on its own as stored and takes only the others from the document, and the delivery is then applied as to a stored note. A document that changes the content of a note edited since is dropped unless it is dated later, the rule of an ordinary `Update` (`contentChanged`, `isNewer`, `server/remote-status-edit.ts`): a replayed older `Update`, or the `Create` after an edit, no longer rolls the note back, and a document that changes nothing still replaces the row. An `Update` keeps the stored visibility, publication date and language and dates its edit. A new tally settles the local votes in the batch that replaces the row. A row that does not read even with those columns filled has its fault in a column the upsert keeps (the id, the author, the object URI) and is logged and left (`InvalidRemoteStatusRowKept`, `RowNotRewritable`), not replaced on every delivery. The other reasons logged are `NotTheAuthorsNote`, `DocumentNotANote` and `NotNewer`. (d) `noteFromUnknown` typed its failure as a possible `RepositoryError`, which callers returned as a failure to retry. It only fails with `InvalidObject` (a document that does not parse, or one `RemoteStatus.fromFetched` refuses, such as a `url` that is no URL), and is typed so; the note is dropped with 202. This also settles the `RepositoryError` the heal dropped: there is none. (e) `upsertRemoteStatus` writes a note whose URI was read as free with a plain `INSERT` (`prisma/sql/insertRemoteStatus.sql`). When another delivery stored the URI since, the batch fails on the unique `object_uri` and the write gives `undefined`, where the upsert updated that row and the tag and marker statements, keyed on the new id, failed on the foreign key (or, with neither, committed and returned an id no row holds). The delivery that lost is one that found the note cached (`storedMeanwhile`): it leaves the row, its tags and its guard (`refuseReplacedRemoteStatus`) to the other, writes no marker for a row it did not create, and finishes the other's `Created` marker as a redelivery does (`resumeCreated`). By design, not limits: a drop is logged once per delivery, as nothing records that a row was already reported; the readers outside the inbox (the REST API, the thread context's `listThreadAncestors`, the queue jobs) fail with a `RepositoryError` on a row whose values do not read, and read one whose shape does not parse as none; the list readers leave such rows out. The other limits this entry listed are fixed (SHR-12 follow-up, above). Tests: `server/inbox-unreadable-row.test.ts` (for a row whose values and for one whose shape does not read: an `Announce`, a quoting `Create`, a `QuoteRequest`, a refused `QuoteRequest`, a reply with a mention and a `Reject` of a local quote each get 202; the author's `Create` replaces a row whose shape does not parse; an older `Update` and the `Create` leave a row edited since, and a newer `Update` replaces it; an `Update` that changes nothing replaces it and keeps the date of the edit; an `Update` keeps a readable visibility and publication date; for a local note whose values or whose shape do not read, an `Announce`, a quoting `Create`, a `QuoteRequest` and a `Reject` of its quote each get 202; a local and a remote row whose shape does not parse answer 404 on `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id` and its `/context`), `server/remote-poll.test.ts` (a new tally that replaces an unreadable row settles the local vote), `server/remote-poll-vote.test.ts` (a vote on a poll whose status row or poll row does not read gets 202 and is neither stored nor counted; a vote whose read fails gets 500 and is counted on redelivery), `server/inbox-retry.test.ts` (a `Create` and an `Update` whose note carries a `url` that is no URL get 202 and store nothing; a `Create` whose first batch runs after a `QuoteRequest` stored the note returns that row, with one row, its tags, no marker left and one mention notification; a `QuoteRequest` and a `Create` of one new note sent together both get 202, as a smoke test: the test runtime does not interleave them, so it passes without the fix). Not tested: a reply to an unreadable note was answered 202 before (the parent read behind a mention was already tolerated), so its test pins the answer; a row left as `RowNotRewritable` cannot be reached through the inbox, as the row is found by its object URI and matched by its author, which the document proves to be URLs; a failed read inside the heal; the fan-out of the delivery that lost a race.
- A remote `Create` or `Update` that failed after its row was stored is finished when the sender delivers it again (SHR-11 phase 2, items (a) and (c)). The steps that run after the batch of `upsertRemoteStatus` (the mention rows and notifications, the direct conversation, the quote, the link card, the boosters' `update` notifications and the fan-out) answered 500 on a failure, but the redelivery did not resume them: a `Create` found the note cached, and an `Update` read the content already stored, so it was not an edit. A marker now says the work is owed (`remote_status_owed`, migration 0140; `server/remote-status-owed.ts`): `Created` for a note an inbox activity stores (a `Create`, or the note of a `QuoteRequest`), `Edited` for an `Update` that changes the content. It is a statement of the batch that writes the row, built from the id the row is stored under (the `after` statements of `upsertRemoteStatus`), and the redelivery brings the document, so the only payload is the local accounts an edit newly mentions (`mentions_json` on an `Edited` marker), which a redelivery cannot work out again once the failed delivery wrote their mention rows. `finishRemoteNote` (`server/remote-status-persist.ts`) runs the late steps for both paths and deletes the marker last, by the token of the delivery that wrote it, so it does not delete one a later edit has replaced. A `QuoteRequest` that resumes a `Created` marker runs the steps but leaves the marker, as a `Create` may have written it and still owes the fan-out. `persistRemoteObject` finishes a cached note that has a `Created` marker when its author delivers it again, and `applyNoteUpdate` finishes a pending `Edited` marker although the content it carries is stored already. Every step gives the same result the second time. The writes replace what they wrote. A notification goes through `notifyFromRemoteOnce` (`server/notify.ts`), which skips one the account already holds for the same kind, status and sender: a mention at any time, a booster's `update` since the marker was written, so a later edit notifies again. The check and the insert are one statement (`insertNotificationOnce`), as a marker also stands while the delivery that wrote it is still running, and another activity that carries the note (a `Create` beside a `QuoteRequest`, a poll refresh during an edit) then runs the same steps at the same moment; only the delivery that inserted holds, publishes and pushes the notification. The fan-out comes last, and a client drops a status it already holds.
