It is started with `startHomeFanOut` and `startPublicFanOut`, which run it once the response is ready and send a failed page to the queue, so it cannot fail the delivery: the marker stands for its start, and is deleted without waiting for it. A resumed `Create` counts as stored now (the delivery that wrote the marker never streamed it), so it is fanned out when it is still inside the real-time window. A resumed edit notifies the accounts its marker names, so a mention the note already had is not notified again; a newer edit that replaces the marker takes those accounts over. A failed notification is now an error too, where it was dropped. The six-hourly cron deletes the markers older than seven days and logs `RemoteStatusOwedAbandoned`. Limits, not fixed: a sender that gives up leaves the work undone, and the marker shows it until the sweep; a note fetched for an `Announce`, a quote or a backfill writes no marker, as no delivery of its document comes again; a `Create` redelivered after an `Update` edited the note is not resumed, as it carries an older document: its `Created` marker is stranded until the sweep, and the note is never streamed as new; a `QuoteRequest` that failed and whose note no `Create` delivers leaves its marker to the sweep too, although its redelivery ran the steps; a notification that was inserted but whose stream event or push then failed is not sent again, as the redelivery finds the row; and an `Update` that changes no content writes no marker. Tests: `server/inbox-retry.test.ts` (a `Create` whose remote mention write D1 refuses gets 500 and leaves a marker, and its redelivery writes the mention, notifies the mentioned account once, stores the quote and deletes the marker; an `Update` refused the same way notifies its booster once on redelivery, and a second edit notifies again; an `Update` whose marker D1 refuses to delete notifies its booster once across both deliveries; an `Update` redelivered after the failure is heard once on the public stream as `status.update`; a `Create` and an `Update` whose local mention row D1 refuses notify the account on redelivery; a resumed `Update` does not notify a mention the note already had; a `Create` that arrives during the late steps of the `QuoteRequest` that stored the note notifies the mention once; a `QuoteRequest` that resumes a failed `Create` leaves the marker, and the redelivered `Create` is heard once on the public stream as `update`), `server/notify-once.test.ts` (also three requests at once), `server/deliver-notification.test.ts` (a notification already recorded is neither held nor published), `server/remote-status-owed.test.ts` (the sweep).
- A remote `Create` or `Update` no longer loses work, or answers 500 for good, in four more cases (SHR-11 phase 1; (a) and (c) are phase 2, above). (b) The settle of the local votes on a remote poll ran after the batch of `upsertRemoteStatus` (`server/remote-status-store.ts`) committed: when it failed, the retry read the new tally as stored and skipped it, so the local votes stayed added on top of counts that already held them. It is now a statement of that batch (`settleRemotePollVotesStatement`, `server/remote-poll-vote-store.ts`). (d) A stored row that does not read as a status failed every `Create` or `Update` of its note with a `RepositoryError`, answered 500 until the sender gave up. `remoteStatusFromRow` now fails with `StoredRowInvalid` (the row's id and author), which `findDeliveredRemoteStatus` keeps apart from a failed read. `settleInvalidRemoteStatus` (`server/remote-status-persist.ts`) answers such a delivery with 202: the author's replaces the row under its id (`overwriteRemoteStatus`), with no history version, notification or stream event, and any other is logged and dropped, as Mastodon drops a `RecordInvalid`. A `Create` that names its note by URI only is dropped too. Other readers of the row still fail with a `RepositoryError`. (e) Two `Update`s of one note applied at once both read the same version and both kept it in the history, and the later one replaced the other's version unrecorded. An `Update` that changes no content (a poll count refresh) did the same without a version: it put back the content it had read, under the newer `edited_at`, and a redelivery of the edit was then dropped as not newer. `upsertRemoteStatus` now runs a guard (`prisma/sql/refuseReplacedRemoteStatus.sql`) first in the batch of every write to a row that exists: it names the `edited_at` of the version that was read and, when the row holds another, inserts a history row with a `NULL` `status_id`, so the `NOT NULL` constraint rolls back the batch. The inbox answers 500 and the redelivery reads the row again. A new row has no guard, and an empty `edited_at` reads as none, as the row parser reads it. (f) `handleRemotePollVote` (`server/remote-poll-vote.ts`) swallowed storage errors on a vote `Create`: a failed read of the poll's status stored the vote as a remote status, and a failed write dropped the vote with 202. It now returns the error, `receiveRemoteCreate` passes it on, and a vote found already counted queues the poll's `Update` when no `DistributePollUpdate` job has started since it was counted (`polls.update_distributed_at`, migration 0137, set by `startPollUpdate`), so a redelivery finishes the work. A vote sent again after its `Update` went out queues nothing: the inbox dedupes on the activity id and a vote on its actor and option, so the same vote under new `Create` ids would otherwise federate the poll to every follower once per debounce. Tests: `server/inbox-retry.test.ts` (two `Update`s computed from one version keep the original and each edit once, the stale one failing until redelivered; an `Update` with the replaced content, applied while an edit is stored, fails and leaves the edit and its history; the author's `Create` and `Update` of a note whose row is unreadable get 202 and replace it, another actor's `Update` gets 202 and leaves it), `server/remote-poll.test.ts` (an `Update` whose settle D1 refuses gets 500 with the tally unchanged, and settles on redelivery), `server/remote-poll-vote.test.ts` (a vote whose write D1 refuses gets 500 and is counted once on redelivery; a vote on a poll whose status is unreadable gets 500 and is not stored as a status; a vote counted with no update queued is distributed when sent again, and one sent again after its update went out queues none).
- A remote `Create` or `Update` whose write failed is retried (SHR-10). `receiveRemoteCreate` (`server/inbox/create.ts`) answered `ok` whatever `persistRemoteObject` returned, and `dispatchRemote` (`server/inbox/dispatch.ts`) dropped `applyRemoteUpdate`'s result, so a failed write was answered 202, the activity's claim completed, and a redelivery was not applied: the note or the edit was lost. Both now return the error, so the inbox route releases the claim and answers 500, and the sender redelivers the same activity id. A redelivery is applied in full when the failure was in the write of the row and its tags, which is one batch: nothing was stored, so it runs as a first delivery. Only a storage failure is an error there: a fetch that fails, a note that does not parse, another actor's note and a refused domain are still `ok`, and answered 202. A failed `Update` also left the history version written before the upsert, so a later success showed the replaced version twice: the version is now a statement (`remoteRevisionStatement`, `server/remote-status-edit.ts`) that `upsertRemoteStatus` runs in the batch that writes the row and its tags, so it is kept only when the row is replaced. An `Update` notifies the local accounts it newly mentions as soon as their mention rows are recorded, no longer after the remote mentions and the quote sync: a failure there left the rows, so the redelivery found nobody new to notify. Known limit, not fixed (SHR-11): a failure after the row is stored is answered 500 too, but its redelivery does not finish the work. A `Create` finds the note cached and does nothing more. An `Update` writes its mentions and quote again, but reads the content already stored, so it is not an edit, and the boosters' `update` notifications and the `status.update` fan-out are skipped. Tests: `server/inbox-retry.test.ts` (a `Create` and an `Update` whose tag write D1 refuses get 500 and are applied on redelivery, the edit with `edited_at`; the history is the original and the edit, once each; an `Update` whose remote mention write D1 refuses notifies its new local mention once, on redelivery).
- Remote statuses carry their tags, read as Mastodon reads them (SHR-8). Three changes. (1) A remote status rendered `tags: []` whatever was stored: `mastodonRemoteStatuses` now reads the tags of a page of statuses in one query (`listRemoteStatusTags`, in stored order) and renders them as `REST::StatusSerializer::TagSerializer` (`name`, `url`). A status edit has no `tags` in Mastodon, so the history is unchanged. (2) The tags of a note that arrives are the names of the `Hashtag` objects of its `tag`, as Mastodon v4.7.5's `ActivityPub::Activity::Create#process_hashtag` and `ProcessStatusUpdateService#update_tags!`, no longer the hashtags its content HTML reads as: `noteHashtags` (`server/remote-status-persist.ts`) takes `tag` as an array or one object and `type` as a string or a list, normalizes each name with `normalizeHashtag`, keeps the ones `isHashtagName` accepts, once, in the note's order. A Create and an Update replace the stored set, so an Update that drops a tag removes it. Behaviour change: a `#x` in a remote note's content with no `Hashtag` object for it is no longer indexed, found by hashtag search, or rendered in `tags`; a `Hashtag` object whose name is not in the content is. A failed tag write now fails the upsert (the delivery is retried) rather than leaving the row to the backfill. (3) Statuses stored before SHR-1 were indexed under their ASCII tags only. Migration 0136 marks the ones whose HTML has a non-ASCII character and a `#` or `＃` (`tags_indexed = 0`), and the `BackfillRemoteTags` job indexes them again, 100 a pass in one batch of four statements, now chaining while a pass is full and indexed something. The note's `tag` is not kept, so these statuses are indexed from their stored HTML (`htmlHashtags`), as before: a status stored before this change keeps content-read tags until its author updates it. Each statement of a pass skips a status a delivery has indexed since the pass read it. Local posts need nothing: `BackfillLocalNoteTags` (migration 0132) read every local note again with the SHR-1 rule. Migration cost and what to do on a large database: [runbook](../operations/runbook.md#remote-tag-reindex-migration-0136). The final-`Σ` difference stays, by design (above). Tests: `packages/worker/server/remote-status-hashtags.test.ts` (signed Create and Update through the inbox; all 3 fail on the commit before the change: content `#rshinhtml` was the tag, the `Hashtag` objects were not), `remote-tag-reindex.test.ts` (251 statuses sent back by the migration's statement, drained in 3 passes; an ASCII-only status is left alone), `tag-remote-timeline.test.ts` (stored tags in `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id` and a tag timeline, through `expectContract`).
- Hashtags fold accents to ASCII, as Mastodon's `HashtagNormalizer` (SHR-3). `normalizeHashtag` (`@tstodon/core/post-text`) lacked the `ASCIIFolding` step, so `#café` and `#cafe` were two tags here and one in Mastodon. It now folds with the table of Mastodon v4.7.5's `app/lib/ascii_folding.rb` (181 Latin-1 and Latin Extended-A letters, after NFKC and lowercase) (a final `Σ` still lowercases to `ς`, unlike Ruby; see SHR-8). A hashtag stream's identity (`StreamChannel.key`) is the stored name too, so a socket on `Café` gets the posts its `cafe` row is found by, and a trend review is recorded under the stored name. Stored names move with the rule: migration 0135 seeds a `tag_name_fold` cursor, and the `FoldTagNames` job (`server/tag-name-fold.ts`, queued by the hourly cron, chaining while pages are full) renames each stored name that holds a non-ASCII character to what `normalizeHashtag` gives now, 200 names a page in one batch of 15 statements, in `tags`, `followed_tags`, `featured_tags`, `local_status_tags`, `remote_status_tags`, `trend_reviews` (kind `Tag`) and `stream_subscriptions` (hashtag streams). Where the folded name is already stored the rows merge: follows, features, status tags and subscriptions are kept as a union (a follow or feature keeps the earlier `created_at`); a `tags` row takes the more restrictive `usable` and `listable`, the folded row's `display_name` (else the old row's) and the earlier `created_at`; a `Rejected` trend review wins over `Approved`. The renames and the cursor are one batch, so a repeated page writes nothing. The rename cannot be undone: after a merge nothing records which rows came from `café`. Until the job has run (up to an hour after the deploy, then a few seconds per page), a lookup of `café` reads `cafe` and so misses the rows still stored as `café`: their tag timeline, `tagged` filter, followed and featured tags, moderation and hashtag streams. Unfollowing or unfeaturing `cafe` in that window removes a `café` row too, so the job does not bring it back. Once the cursor is complete the job does not scan again: if a build without the fold writes names after it (a rollback past this change, then a deploy forward), reset the cursor as the runbook says. New posts are stored under the folded name from the deploy on. The migration must be applied before the code is deployed, as `pnpm run deploy` does. The web UI's `hasHashtag` and `isHashtagName` answer as before (a folded name is a tag exactly when the name was). Checked against Ruby 3.3 running Mastodon v4.7.5's `HashtagNormalizer`, `ASCIIFolding` and `Tag::HASHTAG_INVALID_CHARS_RE` (a one-off run, not in the repository): every code point but the surrogates (1,112,064), alone and in six contexts (between ASCII letters, before a combining acute, after `A` and a combining ring, after `É`, before a combining cedilla and `X`, doubled after a decomposed `é`). On the 1,102,292 code points where the two runtimes' Unicode data agree, 3 differ, each in one context: a doubled `U+0F73`, `U+0F75` or `U+0F81`, which Ruby's `unicode_normalize` does not reorder canonically. The other 9,772 are Unicode versions (Ruby 3.3 has 15.0, Node 22 has 17.0: `Alphabetic`, NFKC and lowercase of newer characters), which depend on the Ruby a Mastodon server runs. Tests: `packages/core/src/post-text.test.ts` (20 names pinned to Ruby's output), `packages/domain/src/stream-channel.test.ts` and `packages/worker/server/tag-name-fold.test.ts` (16 tests fail on the previous commit's `normalizeHashtag`: the tag timeline of `acfcafe` listed one of the two posts).
- A URL's host passes IDNA2003, as Mastodon's (SHR-7). twitter-text's `is_valid_domain` asks libidn for the host in ASCII (`IDN::Idna.toASCII`) and drops the URL when libidn raises, and counts what punycode adds to the host against the 4096-character limit. tstodon checked neither, so it linked `https://👍.com/` and counted it as 23 where Mastodon leaves it as text and counts 14. `packages/core/src/idna.ts` (`toAscii`, no dependency) is libidn's `idna_to_ascii_8z` with its default flags: per label, nameprep on Unicode 3.2 (map to nothing, case folding, NFKC, prohibited characters, the two-direction rule, unassigned code points), then punycode and the 63-octet limit; an ASCII label is taken as written. `urlEntities` applies it to the URLs Mastodon counts as 23 and not to `xmpp:` and `magnet:` URIs or mentions, as Ruby. The stringprep tables are packed from RFC 3454's text (`packages/core/src/idna-tables.rfc3454.txt`) by `scripts/generate-idna-tables.mjs` into `idna-tables.ts`; `idna.test.ts` fails when that file is stale. NFKC is the browser's, with what libidn's older one does differently: unassigned code points are refused before it, five ideographs keep the decompositions Unicode later corrected, and a starter composes across marks (`ᄀ`, a mark, `ᅡ`). Ruby's case-insensitive matching also reads `ﬁ` as `fi` and `ß` as `ss` in a scheme or a top-level domain (`https://example.ﬁ`, `ßb://`), which JavaScript's does not; `post-text-url-pattern.compose.ts` now writes those spellings into the patterns. Evidence, all against libidn 1.42 through `idn-ruby` and Mastodon v4.7.5's Ruby: `toAscii` matches on 3,336,189 hosts (every code point alone, between two ASCII letters, and between two Hebrew letters) and on 2,400,000 seeded random labels, run once; `scripts/generate-post-text-fixture.mjs` now keeps the texts it left out for libidn and adds an IDN corpus (emoji, right-to-left and mixed-direction hosts, labels near 63 octets, zero-width joiners, the soft hyphen, `。` and full-width dots, upper case, `xn--` labels, each top-level domain spelled with `ﬁ`, `ß` and the like, 3,000 seeded random hosts), and the module matches all 9,545 texts in entities, countable text and length and all 3,793 hosts of `idna.fixture.json`; the previous commit's module fails 860 of those texts. Matching stays linear: a URL over 4096 characters is dropped before its host is read, and on 16 adversarial texts of 50,000 characters (long hosts of `é`, soft hyphens, Hangul with marks, 715 URLs with 63-octet labels) the slowest takes 55 ms, and four times the text takes four to five times as long. The web UI's `render` chunk grows by 4,509 bytes gzipped (70,248 to 74,757). Tests: `packages/core/src/idna.test.ts`, `post-text.fixture.test.ts`, `post-text.test.ts`, `server/status-limits.test.ts` (a post with `https://👍.com/` padded to 500 is accepted and not linked; fails on the previous commit with 422), `features/compose/components/__tests__/character_counter-test.tsx`.
- URLs follow Mastodon's server rule on both sides (SHR-6). `@tstodon/core/post-text` found URLs with twitter-text's own pattern, as Mastodon's web UI counts them, but Mastodon's server finds them with the pattern `config/initializers/twitter_regex.rb` overrides it with, so its two sides disagree; tstodon now has the server's for the Worker and the web UI. A path takes any character but a space and `<>()?` (`https://例え.jp/パス` is one URL and counts 23, where it counted 25 and linked without its path), `dat`, `dweb`, `ipfs`, `ipns`, `ssb`, `gopher` and `gemini` URLs are links and count 23, and `xmpp:` and `magnet:` URIs are links at their own length (`Extractor.extract_extra_uris_with_indices`). As in twitter-text's extractor, a match without a scheme is no URL and neither is a URL inside it (`example.com/https://example.org`), a `t.co` link ends after its slug, a URL over 4096 characters is none, and text of spaces only counts as nothing. A host under a top-level domain twitter-text does not list (`.example`) is still no link, in Mastodon too. The patterns are composed in `packages/core/src/post-text-url-pattern.compose.ts` from twitter-text's lists and the gem's definitions, and frozen by `scripts/generate-post-text-url-pattern.mjs`. A differential test holds the module to Mastodon's Ruby: `scripts/generate-post-text-fixture.mjs` ran `Extractor.extract_entities_with_indices` and `StatusLengthValidator` (Mastodon v4.7.5, twitter-text 3.1.0, libidn) over 5,707 texts (the texts `post-text.test.ts` pins, 126 edge cases and 6,000 seeded random texts, without repeats) and wrote `packages/core/src/post-text.fixture.json`; all 5,704 it kept match in entities, countable text and length, and the 3 it left out are SHR-7's. Of the 24 pinned lengths one changed (`https://例え.jp/パス`, 25 to 23). Matching is linear: twitter-text's pattern matches from a domain with no scheme, so the plain scan walks a run of labels once per label (17.8 s on 25,000 characters of `👍.`); `urlAttempts` finds where a domain starts in one pass from the right and the pattern runs only there (under 20 ms on each of 30 adversarial 50,000-character texts). `valid_extended_uri` runs in three parts, because V8 interprets a pattern that holds the top-level domains twice (716 ms on 50,000 characters of `xmpp:a.a.a.…`, now 5 ms). Tests: `packages/core/src/post-text.fixture.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/post-text.test.ts` (30,000 random texts against the plain scan, the timings), `server/html.test.ts`, `server/post-format.test.ts` and `server/status-limits.test.ts` (fail on the previous commit), `features/compose/components/__tests__/character_counter-test.tsx`.
- Local tag matching reads an index written from `extractHashtags` (the index half of SHR-3). The tag timeline (with `any[]`, `all[]`, `none[]`), an account's `tagged` filter, a featured tag's count, the hashtag streams and the admin tag measures matched `content_text LIKE '%#name%'`, a second rule beside the shared one: `#category` listed under `cat`, `(#cat)` (no tag) listed, and a full-width `＃ＣＡＴ` (tag `cat`) did not. `local_status_tags` (migration 0132, as `remote_status_tags`: `(status_id, tag)`, cascade on the status's delete, index on `(tag, status_id)`) holds a local note's tags, of every visibility; the readers keep their visibility checks. Publishing writes the rows in the note's insert batch, and an edit replaces them in its update batch (`indexLocalStatusTags` in `server/hashtags.ts`, only for the note's owner). Stored notes are indexed by the `BackfillLocalNoteTags` job, now on a `local_status_tags` cursor of its own (the `local_note_tags` one is complete on a running instance): 200 notes a page in one `json_each` insert (a page stays three statements, under `D1_BATCH_CHUNK_SIZE`), which indexes a note only while its text is still the text its tags were read from, so an edit racing the page keeps its own tags. It is queued by the hourly cron and chains while pages are full. Until it completes (up to an hour after the deploy, then a few seconds per 200 notes), stored notes are missing from tag timelines, `tagged`, featured tag counts and the admin tag measures. The migration must be applied before the code is deployed, as `pnpm run deploy` does. No query matches a tag with `LIKE` now; status text search still does. Tests: `server/local-status-tags.test.ts` and `server/local-tag-backfill.test.ts` (5 of the 6 tests fail on the previous commit: `#lstcategory` and `(#lstcat)` listed under `lstcat`, `＃ＬＳＴＣＡＴ` did not, and the `lststream` subscriber got the `#lststreamer` post).
- A local Note's `tag` lists its hashtags (SHR-4), as Mastodon's `NoteSerializer`: after the `Mention`s, a `Hashtag` per tag with the tag page as `href` and `#name` as `name`, and `Hashtag: "as:Hashtag"` in the `@context`. A remote Mastodon indexes a post under its tags from these objects, not from the links in `content`, so tstodon's posts reached no remote tag timeline. The tags are `extractHashtags` of the text, the same list the REST `tags` render. Test: `server/post-format.test.ts` (the Note had only its `Mention` before).
- Local posts link their URLs, hashtags and mentions, as Mastodon's `TextFormatter` (SHR-2). `textToHtml` only escaped the text, so a local post had no link in the web UI, in other clients or in the Note other servers got. `@tstodon/core/post-text` now has `extractEntities` (URLs, hashtags and mentions with their positions, overlaps removed as twitter-text does), and the length count and the hashtag list are built on it. Mentions follow `Account::MENTION_RE` and the extractor's end check: on 30,000 random texts the port found the same 1,924 mentions at the same positions as the Ruby regex (run once against Ruby 3, not kept as a test). `server/html.ts` `formatPostText` writes Mastodon's HTML: a `<p>` per blank-line block and `<br />` per newline, a URL with its scheme and what is past 30 characters in `invisible` spans, a hashtag as `class="mention hashtag"`, and a mention as an `h-card` link whose `href` is the REST `mentions[].url`, which is how the web UI finds the account. A mention that names no account stays text. Publishing and editing resolve the mentions before they render. A post with no text has empty `content`, as in Mastodon (it was `<p></p>`). The server's mention pattern is gone: `mentionUsernames` and `remoteMentionHandles` read the same entities, so a mention inside a URL is no longer one, and `@a@b` (no dot) now is. Account notes still use `textToHtml`. Tests: `packages/core/src/post-text.test.ts` (entity table, the 24 pinned counts unchanged), `server/html.test.ts`, `server/post-format.test.ts` (post, edit and the ActivityPub Note; fails on the previous commit).

- Hashtags follow one rule on both sides, Mastodon's `Tag::HASHTAG_RE` and `HashtagNormalizer`, in `@tstodon/core/post-text` (SHR-1). The Worker took ASCII names only, so `#日本語` was shown as a tag in the compose form but was never stored, listed or followable (`isValidTag` refused it). Its route lookup only lowercased, while search used NFKC. Now `extractHashtags`, `normalizeHashtag` and `isHashtagName` serve the status store, the tag routes, the tag timeline and search, and the web UI's hashtag warning and search quick action. A tag counts only at a line start or after a space, as in Mastodon, so `(#tag)` is no longer one. Local statuses now render their `tags` (they were always `[]`), with the name percent-encoded in the URL, as Rails' `tag_url`. The match is a linear scan equal to the regex, which backtracked quadratically: a 20,000-character `#1` plus ZWNJ text took 7 s, and a differential test checks the scan against the regex on 20,000 random texts. Tag timelines (with `any[]`, `all[]` and `none[]`), an account's `tagged` filter and hashtag streams use the normalized name too. Unfollow and unfeature still take a name an older rule stored (`2024`). Tests: `packages/core/src/post-text.test.ts`, `server/unicode-hashtags.test.ts` (fails on the previous commit), `server/status-extract.test.ts`.
- The Worker and the web UI count a post's length with one rule, `@tstodon/core/post-text`. They disagreed on 8 of 22 sample posts, so a post the counter allowed could get a 422 (or the reverse). The Worker had its own URL pattern and counted code points over the content warning and the body together. Now both use twitter-text's URL pattern, as the web UI did, generated into `packages/core/src/post-text-url-pattern.ts` by `scripts/generate-post-text-url-pattern.mjs`. Both count grapheme clusters over the content warning as written plus the countable body, as Mastodon's `StatusLengthValidator` does: `👍🏽` is one character. The profile note uses the same count. The module imports no Zod, and a test checks that. Tests: `packages/core/src/post-text.test.ts` (pinned counts from upstream's web UI), `server/status-limits.test.ts` (each divergent case at 500 and 501), and `features/compose/components/__tests__/character_counter-test.tsx`. Hashtags still differ: the server's `#` pattern is ASCII-only, the web UI's is Unicode.
- Streaming needs a user's token, as in Mastodon 4.2 and later (mastodon/mastodon#23989). A WebSocket upgrade or SSE request to `/api/v1/streaming` without a token (no `Authorization: Bearer`, `access_token`, or `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol`) is `401 {"error": "Missing access token", "status": 401}` before its stream is read; `/api/v1/streaming/health` still answers `OK` to anyone. Public and hashtag streams also need `read` or `read:statuses`, as every stream but `user:notification` does in Mastodon 4.5 and later. The anonymous `StreamHub` (`"anonymous"`) and the `Anonymous` stream viewer are gone: a socket's state holds its account id, which names its hub, so the public fan-out renders every hub's documents for its account. A hibernated socket whose attachment no longer parses (one left from before the change) is closed with `1008 Missing access token` the next time its hub reads it, and the next fan-out drops the old hub's `stream_subscriptions` rows. Mastodon's web UI (`src/mastodon`, unchanged) opens no stream when signed out: the user stream (`containers/mastodon.jsx`) and the public, community, firehose, and hashtag columns connect only when `signedIn`, and the list and direct columns are for signed-in users.
- A test run that passed every test but exited 1 with `EnvironmentTeardownError: [vitest-worker]: Closing rpc while "onUserConsoleLog" was pending`, naming `server/tag-remote-timeline.test.ts`, no longer happens. The cause: storing a status queues a `ProcessLinkCard` job for its first link, and the queue consumer runs in the test file's isolate after the request has answered. That file's link-fragment search test stored three remote notes linking to `https://app.example/...`. The unfurl's DNS lookup failed (the workerd `failed to lookup address information` lines), and `resolveLinkCard` counts a network error as transient, so each job was retried three times, then logged `OutboxJobExhausted` and, through the dead-letter queue, `OutboxJobDeadLettered`. The tests after it are short, so those logs come as Vitest tears the file down; under load one is still in flight when the file's RPC closes, and Vitest reports it as an unhandled error. A trace of the queue (each job, attempt and fetch logged, with the file held open in `afterAll`) showed the nine `app.example` attempts and the late logs. With the file unchanged, 4 of 32 runs (four running at once) failed this way; with the fix, 0 of 64 did, and none looked up DNS. The tests now answer those link-preview fetches with a 404 and wait for them (`answerLinkPreviews` in `packages/worker/test/link-card-fixture.ts`): a missing page settles the job on its first attempt and stores no card, as the failed lookup did, and nothing reaches DNS or logs after the test. The same helper covers the other tests whose statuses queued previews of unresolvable links: `suggestions-trends`, `timeline-filtered`, `admin-api-contract` (where it was the file's last test), `admin-content-pages` and `index`. Other background work in tests still reaches unresolvable hosts and was left as is: delivery Workflows in `index.test.ts`, a remote outbox backfill in `admin-content-pages.test.ts`, push delivery in `push-delivery.test.ts`, and the queued unfurl in `link-card.test.ts`'s refresh race test.
- Requests no longer hang when they arrive together while the IP block list is being refreshed. `currentIpBlocks` (`server/ip-access.ts`, run on every request that carries `CF-Connecting-IP`: always on Cloudflare, and miniflare adds it locally) shared the D1 read in flight between requests. The read belongs to the request that started it, so when that request's client went away, workerd cancelled the read, and every request that joined it waited on a promise that never settled. Those requests hung until the runtime cancelled them ("your Worker's code had hung"), or failed with "Cannot perform I/O on behalf of a different request". These were the intermittent 500s in the e2e streaming scenario: `custom_emojis`, `trends/tags`, `followed_tags`, `lists`, `follow_requests` and even `badge.png` on a page's first load. They came in bursts exactly 60 s apart, the cache's TTL. Now only the compiled list is shared: a request that finds the copy stale reads the list itself, and a read that started before `forgetIpBlocks` is not kept. Regression test: `server/ip-access.test.ts`. The Access JWT check had the same shape: `server/access-jwt.ts` kept one `jose` `createRemoteJWKSet` per isolate for `CF_ACCESS_JWKS_URL` and the team certs, and that set shares its reload in flight between callers (jose 6.2.12 stops sharing it only when it detects Workers from `WebSocketPair` or `navigator.userAgent`). It now keeps only the parsed key set per isolate: a request that finds it missing or older than 10 minutes fetches the certs itself (5 s timeout), a token whose `kid` is not in the set refetches once, at most once per 30 s per URL, and a fetch that started before a newer one cannot replace its keys. A failed, non-200 or unusable certs fetch is now `VerificationUnavailable` everywhere (it was `InvalidToken` unless it timed out). Regression test: `server/access-jwt.test.ts`, whose first case hangs against the old code once jose's Workers detection is hidden. The e2e suite uses inline JWKS, which does not fetch. Some e2e failures had a second cause: another checkout ran `pnpm e2e:web` at the same time on the same fixed ports, its servers failed to bind, and Playwright's readiness checks passed against the other checkout's servers. Readiness URLs now carry a checkout id (`e2e/web/checkout.mjs`) that only that checkout's edge and remote server answer, and `serve.mjs` exits when its edge cannot listen, so a clashing run fails to start.
- `GET /api/v1/conversations` and the trends endpoints page as Mastodon's do. Conversations page by their last status id, as `AccountConversation#last_status_id`: `max_id`, `since_id` and `min_id` (which wins over `since_id` and takes the oldest `limit` above it) filter on it, `limit` is 20 by default and at most 40, and the response sends `Link` `next` (`max_id` of the last conversation's last status, on a full page) and `prev` (`min_id` of the first's), through `setPaginationLinks` (`server/http.ts`). tstodon stores no conversation rows (they are grouped from the viewer's direct statuses by reply root), so `listConversationsForAccount` (`server/conversation-store.ts`) reads the direct statuses newest first in batches of `limit × 10` (at least 40) until the page is full; the first status seen of a conversation is its last, so a conversation with a status above `max_id` never shows below it. A page deep in the list reads every direct status above it, up to 1,000 per call (the list ends there). No migration: the batches use the direct timeline's existing queries. `GET /api/v1/trends/tags`, `/statuses` and `/links` (and `/api/v1/trends`) send `Link` `next` with `offset + limit` on a full page and `prev` with `offset - limit` (at least 0) past the first, through the new `setOffsetPaginationLinks`, which shares `setLinkHeader`'s link builder. The params schemas were already right (`ApiGetConversationsParams` is the shared cursor schema, `ApiGetTrendsParams` has `offset`). The web UI needed no change: Private mentions and Explore → Posts load their second page, which `e2e/web/paging.spec.ts` checks; trending tags and links are one request in the UI, as upstream. Tests: `server/conversations-trends-paging.test.ts`.
- The web UI's statuses slice is typed per field: `Status` (`src/mastodon/models/status.ts`) is `ImmutableMapOf<StatusFields>`, each field the importer's `NormalizedStatus` field through `ImmutableOf` plus the reducer's `isLoading`, `collapsed` and `isSavingQuotePolicy`, so `status.get('reblog')`, `get('account')`, `get('media_attachments')`, `get('quote')` and the rest have their types; the runtime value is the same `fromJS` Map. `makeGetStatus`'s statuses are `ExpandedStatus` (declared by `selectors/index.d.ts` beside the JavaScript selectors) and a reader of any form of a status takes `AnyStatus` (`ImmutableMapReaderOf`); `immutableMapWith` retypes a field set to another value, and `ImmutableOf` merges a union of objects into one Map type. 40 TypeScript consumers lost their casts or were narrowed (see `src/UPSTREAM.md`, "JS reducers moved to TypeScript"). Fixes found by the types: a saved quote policy was stored as a plain object and lost on the next streamed update; re-importing a status with unchanged content skipped the http(s) check of `url` and the attachments' `remote_url`; the link timeline's title read `.title` off a Map and was always empty. Left loose: upstream's plain `StatusShape` casts, `detailed_status.tsx`'s `any`, attachment `meta`, and the fetch stub (`{ isLoading: true }`).
- The web UI's sixteen remaining JavaScript reducers are TypeScript (`src/mastodon/reducers/{compose,history,media_attachments,statuses,timelines,status_lists,conversations,notifications,trends,settings,user_lists,announcements,filters,push_notifications,meta,height_cache}.ts`, moved with `git mv`), so every slice of `RootState` is typed. The state keeps its runtime Immutable structure (the same `Map`s, `List`s and `OrderedSet`s, keys and values; no `Record`s), and only its types changed: a fixed-field `Map` is an `ImmutableMapOf<Fields>` (`src/mastodon/utils/immutable_map_of.ts`, mirroring Immutable 5's `MapOf`), read with `.get('field')` directly, and plain-object actions are typed from their creators (type constants `as const`) and matched by type with RTK's `addCase` or `reducers/match_action.ts`'s `matchAction`. The TypeScript consumers lost their casts of these slices. Fixes the types brought out: a failed history fetch never stopped loading (`fetchHistoryFail` had no `statusId`); redrafting a status whose mention has no link in its content threw; the visibility button and modal threw on a `null` `privacy`; hashtag suggestions from the tag history were typed as full API hashtags; the quotes list never paged (it read a `next` the `fetchQuotes` payload does not have, replaced the list on a next page, and set `statusId` on the lists' map instead of on `quotes`); blocking or muting removed nothing from the reply trees (`reducers/contexts.ts` passed `[key, id]` entries as ids); a translation with an attachment the status lacks overwrote its last attachment's, and one arriving for a deleted status threw (as did undoing one); a status streamed to a timeline not loaded yet threw, as did `timelineDeleteStatus` on a missing timeline; a deleted reblog stayed in `account:<reblogged status id>` timelines; the account media gallery threw on a status no longer in the store; streamed collection notifications ignored the `collections` show / alert / sound settings; the push toggles of the collections settings group never showed (it read keys the state never had); a streamed announcement update appended its reactions, mentions, tags and emojis again (Immutable 4's `mergeDeep` concatenates lists), and dismissing an announcement not in the list added a bare one; a list fetch failing without a response threw in the settings reducer, and the column actions threw before the store was hydrated; the per-account user lists started with a list's fields among their ids, and expanding one not loaded threw; `apiGetFeaturedTags` was typed as hashtags. Still loosely typed: a status's own fields (`Status`, `models/status.ts`, `unknown` per field as upstream has it: typing them needs a `Record` or a cast, and touches every status reader), so compose and the statuses reducer read a status through guards; a trending hashtag or link (`TrendItem`), a notification's `report` / `event` / `moderation_warning`, and a history version's poll, attachments and emojis stay `ImmutableMap<string, unknown>` through `fromJS`; `getIn` / `setIn` stay `unknown` everywhere. Reducer tests (`src/mastodon/reducers/*.test.ts`) pin the state transitions (compose, history and media attachments written against the JavaScript reducers first). See `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md` ("JS reducers moved to TypeScript").
- The web UI registers for Web Push: `POST /api/web/push_subscriptions` and `PUT /api/web/push_subscriptions/:id` (Mastodon's `Api::Web::PushSubscriptionsController`, `app/routes/api/web/push_subscriptions/`, handlers in `server/routes/web-push-subscriptions.ts`) answer the registerer with `ApiWebPushSubscription` instead of 404, which had made it clear the subscription. They accept only the web app's token (`web:<session id>`, `server/web-token.ts`; another app's token gets 403 and uses `/api/v1/push/subscription`), and share the endpoint/key/policy validation and token-keyed storage with the REST handlers (`createPushSubscription` / `updatePushSubscription` in `server/routes/push.ts`). `PUT` needs `data` and an `:id` naming the session's subscription (404 otherwise); alerts left out turn off, as in Mastodon. The initial state's `push_subscription` is now the session's subscription (it was always `null`, so the UI would subscribe again on every page load). Tests: `server/web-push-subscriptions.test.ts`.
- The web UI's account and interaction actions are TypeScript (`src/mastodon/actions/{accounts,interactions,blocks,mutes,pin_statuses,bookmarks,favourites,domain_blocks,reports}.ts`, converted in place with the same exports and action shapes) and send every request through typed wrappers in `src/mastodon/api/` (`accounts.ts`, `interactions.ts`, `domain_blocks.ts`, new `reports.ts`). New params schemas in `@tstodon/api`: `ApiLookupAccountParams`, `ApiGetRelationshipsParams`, `ApiMuteAccountParams`, `ApiGetAccountStatusesParams` (`params/account-actions.ts`), `ApiDomainBlockParams` (`params/domain-blocks.ts`) and `ApiCreateReportParams` (`params/reports.ts`); account lookup, relationships, mute, an account's statuses, domain block and unblock and reports parse with them (`server/request-params.ts`), and the handlers' local `MuteBodySchema`, `DomainBodySchema` and `ReportBodySchema` are gone. Corrections, server: a form-encoded report's `status_ids[]`, `rule_ids[]` and `forward_to_domains[]` were dropped (the body was not folded), so the report kept no posts; `GET /api/v1/mutes` sent no `Link` header, so the UI never loaded past the first 40 mutes; a mute's form `notifications=1` and an account statuses `pinned=1` now read as true (`true` only before), and a mute without a body no longer fails on an empty JSON body. UI: featuring a profile posted to `accounts/:id/pin`, which has no route (Mastodon 4.4's `endorse` / `unendorse` now); the optimistic unfollow never showed (`unfollowAccountRequest` got the bare id); block, mute and their undo failures showed a generic alert instead of the server's message; a domain block's `accounts` were ids typed as `Account[]`, so the suggestions and grouped notifications reducers removed nothing; the mute modal sent its duration as a string. The changed files are listed under "JS actions moved to TypeScript" in `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md`. Not done: the paged lists (followers, following, blocks, mutes, follow requests, reblogged / favourited by, bookmarks, favourites) still read `limit` and the cursor with `queryCursor`, not a params schema (the UI sends none; it follows the `Link` header); `GET /api/v1/domain_blocks` is still unpaged; `with_suspended` changes nothing. Tests: `server/api-params-contract.test.ts` ("REST request params of the account and interaction actions": lookup, relationships, mute with options and defaults, the mutes `Link` header, pinned and filtered account statuses, domain block and unblock, reports, each as JSON and form or bracketed and plain query).
- The web UI's status and compose actions are TypeScript and send through the shared contract: `actions/compose.js`, `statuses.js`, `history.js`, `importer/index.js` and `importer/normalizer.js` became `.ts` (exports, action types and action shapes unchanged, so the JavaScript reducers read them as before), and their requests go through typed wrappers (`api/statuses.ts`: `apiGetStatus`, `apiGetStatusSource`, `apiGetStatusHistory`, `apiSubmitStatus`, `apiDeleteStatus`, `apiMuteStatus`, `apiUnmuteStatus`, `apiTranslateStatus`; `api/compose.ts`: `apiUploadMedia`, `apiGetMedia`, `apiUploadMediaThumbnail`; `api/search.ts`: `apiSearchAccounts`). New params in `@tstodon/api` (`packages/api/src/params/`): `ApiCreateStatusParams` / `ApiUpdateStatusParams` (with `ApiStatusMediaAttributeParams` and `ApiStatusPollParams`; the fields the web UI sends as `null` are nullable), `ApiDeleteStatusParams`, `ApiUploadMediaParams` (and `thumbnail` on `ApiUpdateMediaParams`), `ApiSearchAccountsParams`, and `exclude_unreviewed` on `ApiSearchParams`. The status create and edit, delete, media upload and account search handlers parse with them through `server/request-params.ts`; `CreateStatusBodySchema`, `MediaAttributeSchema`, `mediaAttributesFromForm` and the delete handler's `DeleteStatusBodySchema` are gone, and `request-params.ts` now reads an object keyed by position (`media_attributes[0][id]` in a form) as an array, as Rails does. Corrections: a form-encoded status create or edit dropped `media_ids[]` and the whole `poll` (`poll[options][]`, `poll[expires_in]`, ...), because the handlers read only unbracketed keys, so the post went out without its media or poll; an edit from the web UI of a post with an attachment without alt text failed with 400, because the UI sends `"description": null` in `media_attributes` and the schema only took a string (now `null` clears the description, as Mastodon's). Edge cases that now answer differently: a poll `expires_in` that is not a number (`"soon"`) takes the default (on an edit, keeps the poll's end) instead of a 422, a string one is read with `parseInt`, and a form `media_attributes` entry without an `id` is a validation error (JSON already was) instead of being skipped. UI: `apiGetEndorsedAccounts` is typed as the array it returns, the "Post published" alert lost a `dismissAfter` nothing read, `upload_form.tsx` passes dnd-kit's ids as strings, and three components no longer dispatch status actions with an `undefined` id; the files are listed in `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md` ("JS actions moved to TypeScript"). Not done: tstodon still keeps no custom thumbnails (`thumbnail` is read and ignored), and `hooks/useSearchAccounts.ts` still calls `api()` itself. Tests: `server/api-params-contract.test.ts` ("statuses, media and compose search": create with media or a poll and edit with `media_attributes` as JSON and as a form, a `null` description, `delete_media` in the query and the body, v1 / v2 upload fields and a thumbnail, account and hashtag search with string booleans).
- The web UI's timeline, notification, conversation, trend, announcement, filter, list, settings, push subscription and streaming actions are TypeScript (`src/mastodon/actions/{timelines,notifications,conversations,trends,announcements,filters,lists,streaming,settings,store,columns,height_cache,emojis,onboarding}.ts`, `actions/push_notifications/*.ts`, `src/mastodon/stream.ts`, moved with `git mv`; every export, action type and action shape kept). Their requests go through typed wrappers (`src/mastodon/api/{timelines,conversations,trends,announcements,filters,web_settings,push_subscriptions}.ts`, `apiGetList` / `apiDeleteList` in `api/lists.ts`) with new `@tstodon/api` params schemas (`packages/api/src/params/{timelines,conversations,trends,filters,web}.ts`: the home, public, tag, list and link timelines, account statuses, conversations, trends, the v2 filter and filter status, web settings and the web push subscription), which the handlers now parse with through `server/request-params.ts` (their local `FilterV2BodySchema`, `FilterStatusBodySchema`, `WebSettingsBodySchema`, `queryFlag` and `c.req.query` / `queries("x[]")` reading are gone). Server corrections: a form-encoded v2 filter lost its `context[]` (stored as `home` only) and its `keywords_attributes[0][...]`, since that handler read neither `key[]` nor nested keys; `server/request-params.ts` now also reads an object keyed by position (`key[0][a]`) as an array, as Rails' nested attributes do. `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/statuses?pinned=1` listed all statuses (only `pinned=true` was read; its other flags took `1` already). A form-encoded `PUT /api/web/settings` (`data[...]`) is read instead of refused. UI corrections: `fillTimelineGaps` sent `maxId` (not a param) and walked the first two items rather than the gaps; `submitMarkers()` was dispatched without its argument and rejected; a streamed notification without a title message (`severed_relationships`, `annual_report`) threw before reaching the grouped list; the filter modal sent `action` instead of `filter_action`; Web Push registration threw without a VAPID key in the shell and its settings save threw before a subscription existed; the SSE fallback never sent `only_media` for media streams; two columns could be moved with an `undefined` id and the media and link timelines requested `undefined`. The details are in `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md` ("JS actions moved to TypeScript"). Not done: `POST` / `PUT /api/web/push_subscriptions` still have no route (the wrappers are typed; Web Push from the web UI does not register); `GET /api/v1/conversations` reads `limit` but not the cursor, and neither it nor the trends send a `Link` header, so the UI never loads a second page of conversations or trending statuses; `expandTimeline` takes the path as a string (as `timelines_typed.ts` passes it), so the params are checked per endpoint where each `expand*` builds them, not against the path. Tests: `server/api-params-contract.test.ts` ("REST request params of the actions moved to TypeScript": each timeline, conversations, trends, a v2 filter and filter status as JSON and as a form, web settings) and `server/request-params.test.ts`.
- The web UI's initial state and the streaming messages are in the `@tstodon/api` contract. Initial state: `ApiInitialState` (`packages/api/src/initial-state.ts`, Mastodon's `InitialStateSerializer`, with `ApiInitialStateMeta`, `ApiInitialStateCompose`, `ApiInitialStateRole`, `ApiWrapstodon`, `ApiWebPushSubscription` and `ApiWebSettings`) is what `initialState` in `server/web-app.ts` returns (no `Record<string, unknown>` left, and the credential account's `source` is read from its type instead of being parsed again), and `src/mastodon/initial_state.ts` aliases it in place of upstream's hand-written interfaces. Streaming: `ApiStreamingEvent` (`packages/api/src/streaming.ts`) is a discriminated union on Mastodon's `event` with each payload decoded (`update` / `status.update` a Status, `notification` a Notification, `conversation` a Conversation, `delete` an id, `notifications_merged` `"1"`, and Mastodon's `announcement`, `announcement.reaction`, `announcement.delete` and `filters_changed`, which tstodon does not send; `encrypted_message` is gone from Mastodon and not listed), with the wire forms `ApiStreamingWireEventJSON` / `ApiStreamingMessageJSON` (an entity payload as a JSON string), `encodeStreamingEvent`, and `ApiStreamingWireEvent` / `ApiStreamingMessage`, which parse a sent SSE frame or WebSocket message back to the decoded event. The event names are `STREAMING_EVENTS` in `@tstodon/api/values`. The domain's `StreamEvent` payloads carry the entity types (`entity<ApiStatusJSON>()`: the hub checks only that a payload is an object, so a delivery is not parsed twice), the hub builds its WebSocket messages and SSE frames through `streamingEvent` and `encodeStreamingEvent`, and the public fan-out's payload is an `ApiStatusJSON`. The UI's `stream.js` types its events with the wire type and `actions/streaming.js` decodes entity payloads with their contract types (JSDoc in the JS files; type-only, the client bundle has no Zod). Corrections: the initial state's `meta.admin` is the site contact account as in Mastodon, so `null` (tstodon has no contact account, as `/api/v2/instance` says); it was the viewer's own id when the viewer was an admin. A viewer whose account moved gets `meta.moved_to_account_id` and the target account in `accounts`, as Mastodon's, so the UI shows its moved banner (it was never sent). On the UI side the shared types follow Mastodon's serializer where upstream's interfaces did not: `access_token`, `admin`, `sso_redirect` and `status_page_url` may be `null`, `show_trends` (signed in only) and `compose.text` (share links only) are optional, and `role.collection_limit` is optional: Mastodon 4.6 sends it, tstodon has no collections, and the UI reads a missing limit as 0. A conversation the documents builder left out was streamed as a `conversation` event without a payload, which the UI's `JSON.parse` throws on; it is now not sent. A streamed `move` notification (tstodon's extension) reached the UI's legacy notification code, which has no message for it, and the grouped list as an empty group; the UI now drops notification types that are not Mastodon's, as its REST requests leave them out. Tests: `server/stream-contract.test.ts` parses every event tstodon sends (`update`, `status.update`, `delete`, `notification`, `conversation`, `notifications_merged`) over WebSocket and SSE with the contract; the stream fixtures (`test/stream-fixture.ts`, `test/sse-fixture.ts`) parse every event any stream test reads with `ApiStreamingMessage` / `ApiStreamingWireEvent`; `server/web-app.test.ts` parses the initial state with `ApiInitialState` (its local schema is gone), signed out, signed in and moved; `packages/api/src/schemas.test.ts` covers encoding, decoding and refusals. Not done: tstodon sends no `announcement*` or `filters_changed` events (Mastodon publishes an announcement and filter changes to every user stream; the UI falls back to fetching), `push_subscription` is always `null` and the UI's `POST /api/web/push_subscriptions` has no route, so Web Push from the web UI does not register; `languages` repeats the English name where Mastodon has the native one; `media_attachments.accept_content_types` lists MIME types only (Mastodon adds file extensions); and the SSE `KNOWN_EVENT_TYPES` in upstream's `stream.js` still lacks `status.update` and `notifications_merged` (upstream's list; the web UI uses the WebSocket).
- The REST request side is shared too: `@tstodon/api` has a params schema per request the web UI's TypeScript wrappers (`src/mastodon/api/*.ts`) send (`packages/api/src/params/`: lists create / update / members, account note, follow options, featured tag create, `GET /api/v1/accounts` and `familiar_followers`, `update_credentials` / `PATCH /api/v1/profile`, media update, boost, poll vote, notification policy, notifications v1 / v2 and requests (list, accept, dismiss), v2 search, directory, suggestions, followed tags and list members), under Mastodon's names (`ApiCreateListParams`, `ApiUpdateCredentialsParams`, ...). The schemas are the JSON shape; the UI types its wrappers with their input types (type-only or `satisfies`, so still no Zod in the bundle), and the handlers parse with them through `server/request-params.ts` (`readParams`, `readOptionalParams`, `readQueryParams`, `readMergedParams`), which folds a form or query string into that shape first: `key[]` into `key`, bracketed form keys nested, and each field cast to the boolean (`true` / `1`, as `isTruthy` read them), number (`parseInt`; one that does not parse is left out, so the default applies) or array its schema declares. The handlers' local body schemas and their `booleanish` / `stringList` / `queries("x[]")` reading are gone. Where a handler reads an enum leniently (policy `for_*`, directory `order`, search `type`, boost `visibility`) the field stays a string so its answers do not change. Corrections: `PUT /api/v2/notifications/policy` had no route (the UI's policy controls use `PUT`; only `PATCH` was served); `DELETE /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts` read only the body, but the UI sends the ids in the query, so removing an account from a list failed with 400; a form's `key[]` arrays were not read from bodies, so a form-encoded poll vote (`choices[]`) and list member add (`account_ids[]`) failed and a follow's `languages[]` was dropped; `PATCH /api/v1/profile` answered with the display name and locked flag from before the change (it rendered the request's cached session account); `ApiAccountJSON` is written out around the recursive `moved`, which TypeScript could otherwise resolve to `{}` depending on file order. UI: `apiGetFamiliarFollowers` sends `id[]` (it sent a single `id`), list update no longer sends the list's `id` in the body, `apiFetchNotificationGroups` lost a `url` param that would have gone out as a query param, and the policy update no longer accepts `summary`; the changed files are listed in `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md`. Not done: the profile's `avatar_description`, `header_description`, `show_media`, `show_media_replies`, `show_featured` and `attribution_domains` are parsed but not stored; `GET /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts` and `/api/v1/followed_tags` answer everything in one page (`limit` is not read); the directory lists local accounts only (`local` changes nothing); the collections, email subscription, quote policy and annual report wrappers have no route, and the JavaScript actions (status create / edit, timelines, `update_credentials`, media upload, reports, filters, ...) and `api()` callers outside `src/mastodon/api` are not typed. Tests: `server/api-params-contract.test.ts` (each covered endpoint with its JSON body and the form-encoded or plain-key query equivalent, accepted the same way) and `server/request-params.test.ts`.
- Outgoing mail will not be built (MAIL-1, closed). Sign-in is Cloudflare Access, so there is no confirmation or password-reset mail to send; in-app notifications and Web Push cover what Mastodon's `NotificationMailer` sends, and admins get report and pending-approval notifications in the app (`notifyAdmins`). Not covered: `UserMailer`'s moderation warnings, appeals, archive-ready and new sign-in notices, and `AdminMailer`'s digests. Bringing mail back would need a `send_email` binding (or an external sender) and a sender address; OPS-7 removed both.
- Signed requests cannot be replayed after the fact (SIG-1). Inbox deliveries and signed GETs verified the signature over `date` but never looked at the `Date`, so a captured delivery or authorized fetch could be sent again at any later time. `checkSignatureStrength` (`server/http-signature.ts`), run by `verifyInboxRequest` and `verifySignedFetch` and, before any key lookup or fetch, by the inbox (`routes/activitypub.ts`) and the signed-fetch verdict (`server/signed-fetch.ts`), refuses a signature that does not cover `date`, one on a request with a body that does not cover `digest`, and a `Date` that does not parse, is more than 65 minutes old or more than 1 hour ahead, as Mastodon's `SignedRequest#matches_time_window?` (`app/lib/signed_request.rb` in v4.7.5, checked against the source): a signature without `expires` is valid for 5 minutes after it was created (its `Date`, or hs2019's `created`), and `CLOCK_SKEW_MARGIN` (1 hour) is allowed on both sides. `EXPIRATION_WINDOW_LIMIT` (12 hours) only caps an explicit `expires`; an earlier version of this check used it as the window, which let a captured request be replayed for 12 hours. The inbox logs these as `InboxRefused` reasons `DateNotSigned`, `DigestNotSigned` and `SignatureExpired` (`detail` `past`, `future` or `invalid`), all 401; a signed GET with one is `Invalid` (401, or the restricted actor document in secure mode). Deviations: hs2019's `(created)` / `(expires)` are not accepted in place of `date` (the signing string has no `(created)`), and an `expires` signature parameter does not stretch the window, because nothing signs it (Mastodon honours it up to 12 hours). Tests: `server/http-signature.test.ts` (fresh, 64 minutes old and 59 minutes ahead accepted; 66 minutes old, 61 minutes ahead, unsigned `date`, unsigned `digest` on a POST and an unparseable `Date` refused, including signatures that otherwise verify), `server/inbox-refusal.test.ts` (each reason logged before a key lookup), `server/authorized-fetch.test.ts` (a replayed signed GET is 401 without a key fetch).
- Secure mode for this server's own objects (OPS-11), Mastodon's `AUTHORIZED_FETCH`: off by default, on with `AUTHORIZED_FETCH=true` (`server/authorized-fetch.ts`, [Authorized fetch](../reference/configuration.md#authorized-fetch-secure-mode)). A GET of a local note, quote stamp, outbox, followers or following collection then needs a valid signature from a remote actor whose domain federates here: unsigned or unverifiable is `401`, a key server that does not answer `503`, a signer on a suspended or not-allowed domain (checked before its key is fetched) or one an admin suspended `403`. An actor GET without a usable signature gets Mastodon's restricted document (`id`, `type`, `preferredUsername`, `inbox`, `outbox`, `publicKey`, `endpoints`), so remotes can still read the key; signed, the whole document. `/actor`, WebFinger, NodeInfo, the inboxes, the API and the web UI stay public. Every guarded response in secure mode is `Cache-Control: private` with `Vary: Signature`; the configuration reference warns against a Cloudflare Cache Rule that caches `/users/*` regardless of the origin. The signature is verified once per request (`signedFetchVerdict`, `server/signed-fetch.ts`, shared with the DM-6 audience check and the quote stamp's block check), and an admin-suspended signer is now refused there too (it could read a followers-only note it followed before). Deviations: `FEDERATION_ALLOW_HOSTS` does not imply secure mode as Mastodon's `LIMITED_FEDERATION_MODE` does; `Date` freshness is not checked (SIG-1). Tests: `server/authorized-fetch.test.ts` (off: unchanged; on: unsigned 401 for each object and the restricted actor, signed from an allowed domain 200 and private, suspended domain 403 cached or not and without a fetch, allow list and suspended signer 403, forged 401, key server down 503, a Mastodon-style instance actor signer fetched on first use, `/actor`, WebFinger and NodeInfo public).
- Inbox refusals are logged (OPS-5). Every refusal of a delivery to `/inbox`, `/actor/inbox` or `/users/*/inbox` writes an `InboxRefused` warn event (`server/inbox-refusal.ts`) with `reason`, `status`, `path`, `keyIdHost`, the claimed `actor`, the verified `signer` once known and a `detail` (URIs clipped to 200 characters; no body, header value or key), and an `inbox.refuse` metric point (doubles `[1, loggedFlag]`, blobs `[reason, status]`). Reasons: `UnparseableBody`, `InvalidActivityId`, `ActivityRejected` (400); `MissingSignature`, `DigestMismatch`, `UnknownLocalActor`, `ActorMismatch`, `KeyRejected`, `SignatureMismatch` (401); `KeyUnavailable` (503); `DomainSuspended`, `DomainNotAllowed`, `ActorSuspended` (403); `RateLimitedDomain` (`inbox_domain`, after verification) and `RateLimitedAddress` (`inbox_ip`, from the rate-limit middleware) (429). Floods do not spam the logs: each isolate logs at most 5 lines per reason per minute, counts the rest in the metric (`loggedFlag` 0), and reports them in one `InboxRefusalsSuppressed` line when that reason's next window opens. Two behaviour changes came with it: a `Digest` that does not match the body is refused before any key lookup or fetch (it could never verify), and a delivery whose `keyId` or `actor` host is suspended or outside the allow list is refused with 403 before anything is fetched (an actor not cached yet used to get 401 after a refused fetch; a cached one already got 403). The 401 reasons come from the boundary, without changing `resolveRemoteActor`: whether the signature check ran and failed, and, when it did not run, whether the key is cached for another actor or served from another host than the actor's. A transient delivery failure's HTTP status is now stored on its `outbox_deliveries` row (`http_status` of a `Queued` target, and of a `RetryExhausted` one, is the last failed attempt's status; NULL for a network, timeout or local failure, and cleared on success); `persistOutboxTarget` and the run's `persist` take it, no migration. Runbook: the reason table under "Inbox refusals", the `inbox.refuse` metric, a query, an alert and the inbox flood playbook. Tests: `server/inbox-refusal.test.ts` (one event and point per reason with its fields, nothing from the body or signature in the line, the 403s fetch nothing, five lines per reason per minute with the rest counted, another reason not crowded out, the next window and `InboxRefusalsSuppressed`), `server/outbox-delivery.test.ts` (503, then 429, then a network failure on the row while it waits; cleared on delivery; `RetryExhausted` keeps 502).
- Unused bindings removed (OPS-7). `server/mailer.ts` (`sendEmail`) had no caller since sign-in moved to Cloudflare Access (#82): nothing in the server sent mail (no notification, report, admin or sign-up mail). It is deleted with the `EMAIL` `send_email` binding, the `EMAIL_FROM` and `EMAIL_TRANSPORT` vars, and the `APP_CACHE` KV namespace, which only ever held mail captured by `EMAIL_TRANSPORT=capture`. `REMOTE_DNS_CACHE` was bound and provisioned but read by nothing, and goes too, so the Worker has no KV namespace. Removed from `packages/worker/wrangler.jsonc`, the five `e2e/*.wrangler.jsonc`, the test bindings in the root `vite.config.ts` and `e2e/web/serve.mjs` (`X_LOCAL_EXPLORER`, used only to read captured mail), `infra/cloudflare` (both `cloudflare_workers_kv_namespace` resources, `create_kv`, the title variables and the `kv_*` outputs; an existing state destroys the two empty namespaces on the next apply, see the infra README), and the docs (configuration, deploy checklist, launch checklist, runbook, architecture). `void.json` keeps Void's KV inference off, so `void deploy` adds no namespace back; `send_email` is not inferred. Mail is not planned (MAIL-1, closed above). Test: `packages/core/src/removed-bindings.test.ts` (no config declares a KV namespace, `send_email` or the mail vars; no source names them; KV inference off).
- Scheduled, encrypted D1 backups off the production account (OPS-4). Recovery was Time Travel only, inside the same account and its window. The `D1 backup` workflow (`.github/workflows/d1-backup.yml`, daily and on demand) runs `wrangler d1 export --remote` with a D1-Edit-only token through a throwaway config that names the database by id, checks the dump, gzips and age-encrypts it to the public keys in `D1_BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENTS` (the plaintext never leaves the runner), uploads `d1/tstodon-<stamp>.sql.gz.age` with its SHA-256 to an S3-compatible bucket meant for a second Cloudflare account or another provider (and optionally as a workflow artifact), and prunes by `scripts/d1-backup.mjs` (keep 35 days, the first backup of each of 12 months, and always the newest 7; unknown keys are never touched). It does nothing until configured; a half-configured or manual run fails and names what is missing. `scripts/d1-restore-check.sh` decrypts a backup into a scratch local D1 database and reports counts and missing migrations; a local export, encrypt, decrypt and import round trip re-exported byte-identical. A Worker cron on D1's export API was rejected: it would put a D1 token and the backup bucket's credentials into the Worker being backed up. The runbook ([Scheduled D1 backups](../operations/runbook.md#scheduled-d1-backups): settings, token scopes, bucket lock, key custody and rotation, manual export, restore), the deploy checklist (step 14), the launch checklist and the secrets inventory describe it.
- Analytics Engine writes stay under the per-invocation cap (OPS-10). `stream.render` wrote one point per recipient, so a queue batch of fan-out pages (ten `FanOutRemovedStatuses` jobs alone could write 400) passed Analytics Engine's 250 data points per Worker invocation, and the platform dropped the rest without an error. Now `streamRenderTally` (`server/metrics.ts`) writes one point per fan-out pass and target (`FanOutHome`, `FanOutPublic` per page, `FanOutRemovedStatuses` per job): doubles `[renders, delivered, errors, totalMs, maxMs, p50Ms, p95Ms]`, blobs `[home or list or public, ok or error]`. Each request (middleware `00.analytics-budget.ts`), queue batch (both consumers), cron run and delivery Workflow run writes under `withMetricsBudget`: past 249 points `writeMetric` counts instead of writing, and the invocation ends with one `metrics.dropped` point (`[droppedPoints, writtenPoints]`, `[source, indexThatLostMost]`) and a `MetricsDropped` log; a point dropped after the invocation settled (a `waitUntil`) is logged. The metric reference, runbook table and SQL describe the new shape.
- Actor keys can be rotated (OPS-8). `server/actor-key-rotation.ts` replaces a local account's or the instance actor's RSA key pair in place under the same key id (`<actor>#main-key`, as Mastodon's; the old key is dropped, no grace period) and sends a rotated account's followers an actor `Update` with the new key (none for a suspended account, nor for the instance actor, which nobody follows). Deliveries and fetches read the key when they sign, so queued deliveries go out with the new one. Admins rotate one account (its admin page, `POST /api/v1/admin/accounts/:id/rotate_key`), the instance actor, or every key (`/admin/actor_keys`, `POST /api/v1/admin/actor_keys/instance_actor` and `/rotate_all`); the bulk `RotateActorKeys` job does 20 accounts a page with 20 s between pages and skips accounts the run already rotated (migration 0129: `accounts.key_rotated_at`, `instance_actor_keys.rotated_at`). Remote rotation: when a signature fails against a cached remote key, `resolveRemoteActor` (inbox and signed GETs) fetches the actor again, once per actor per 5 minutes (`remote_actors.key_refetched_at`), and verifies with the new key. Runbook: [Rotating actor keys](../operations/runbook.md#rotating-actor-keys).
- The inbox no longer fetches from admin-suspended domains when it handles `Create`, `Update` or `QuoteRequest`. Only `Announce` and `Move` passed the receipt's `blockedHosts` (suspended domains, `server/routes/activitypub.ts`) to their fetch options, so an allowed actor's `Create` of a note quoting a note on a suspended domain fetched that note, its author and its stamp and stored them; an `Update` naming its object by a URI there, an edit that newly quotes a note there, or an actor `Update` whose `movedTo` points there fetched it; and a `QuoteRequest` whose `instrument` is a URI there fetched it. `server/inbox/create.ts`, `server/inbox/dispatch.ts` (`Update`) and `server/inbox/quote-request.ts` now pass `blockedHosts`, which `persistRemoteObject`, `applyRemoteUpdate` and the quote context carry to every nested fetch (quoted notes, their authors, stamps, move targets). The queued jobs that fetch (thread and outbox backfill, the quote retry jobs, link cards, actor refresh) already read the suspended domains themselves. Tests: `server/inbox-blocked-hosts.test.ts` (each case asserts no request reaches the suspended domain and nothing from it is stored; all five failed before the fix).
- Outgoing ActivityPub GETs are HTTP-signed (OPS-9). `fetchActivityJson` (`server/federated-fetch.ts`), which every fetch of a remote ActivityPub document goes through (actor and key fetch, refresh and handle resolution; notes for the inbox, announces, quotes and quoted notes, replies and thread backfill; collections and outbox backfill; quote stamps for verification and the quote retry jobs; `Update` refetches; `Move` targets and aliases; search and lookup with `resolve`), now signs each GET with draft-cavage HTTP Signatures over `(request-target) host date accept`, `rsa-sha256`, as Mastodon's `Request` signs with `on_behalf_of` (it signs every header it sends except `User-Agent` and `Accept-Encoding`). `Accept` is Mastodon's `application/activity+json, application/ld+json; profile="https://www.w3.org/ns/activitystreams"`. The key is the instance actor's (`keyId` `https://<domain>/actor#main-key`), stored in `instance_actor_keys` on first use and never regenerated, read once per request or job (`instanceActorFetchSigner`, `server/instance-actor.ts`); if D1 cannot give it the GET goes unsigned and `FetchNotSigned` is logged. `FederatedFetchOptions.fetchSigner` is required, so a new caller cannot forget it; `unsignedFetch` exists for tests. Every fetch is signed as the instance actor, never as a local user: no caller has the requesting user's key at hand, and the followers-only objects a user-signed fetch would unlock are delivered to us rather than fetched. Mastodon signs some fetches as the user (a `resolve` search of a status URL, for example), so a remote's followers-only post opened by URL does not resolve here. WebFinger stays unsigned, as Mastodon's `Webfinger` (no `on_behalf_of`); tstodon fetches no NodeInfo or host-meta. Redirects: up to three are followed when they stay on the request's origin (scheme, host and port), each hop signed again for its own path and query; a redirect to another origin, to plain HTTP, a fourth redirect or a loop fails the fetch as a 4xx did before, and one 5 s deadline covers the chain (before, every redirect failed). The host guard, the scheme check and the timeout are unchanged. `GET /actor` answers unsigned (secure-mode servers verify our signatures by fetching it) and must be bypassed in Access (launch checklist). tstodon itself still answers unsigned GETs for public objects (OPS-11). Tests: `server/federated-fetch-signing.test.ts` (signature verified with `verifySignedFetch` against the stored instance actor key, keyId, `Date`, `Accept`; a mock secure-mode server that answers 401 to unsigned GETs serves an actor by handle and by key, a status, a collection and a quote stamp; WebFinger unsigned; same-origin redirects re-signed, cross-origin, downgrade, loop and fourth redirect refused; `/actor` serves the same key across requests).

- Outbound delivery retries for days and tracks unreachable domains, as Mastodon (OPS-2, OPS-6). The delivery Workflow tried 5 times over about 81 minutes; a remote down longer missed everything sent meanwhile, no domain was tracked, and a target whose Workflow failed to start or errored in a step stayed `Queued` with nothing retrying it. Now `OutboxDeliveryWorkflow` (body in `runOutboxDelivery`, `server/outbox-delivery-run.ts`) makes 17 attempts, Mastodon's `ActivityPub::DeliveryWorker` `retry: 16`, sleeping Sidekiq's default backoff `count⁴ + 15 + rand(10) × (count + 1)` seconds between them (`OutboxDelivery.backoffMs` / `nextDelayMs`, `packages/domain/src/outbox-delivery.ts`): 15 seconds first, about 14 hours last, about 2.1 days in all. A 429 or 503 with a readable `Retry-After` (seconds or an HTTP-date) waits at least that long, capped at 12 hours. Classification follows Mastodon's `response_error_unsalvageable?`: a 4xx other than 401, 408 and 429, or a 501, is `Permanent` at once (401 was permanent before, 501 transient); `DeliveryAttemptOutcome.TransientFailure` now carries its cause (`HttpStatus`, `Throttled`, `Network`, or `Local` for this server's own failure). The loop is driven by the row's attempt count with stable step names; the row stores `next_attempt_at` while it waits (migration `0126_delivery_domain_health.sql`). Steps: D1 reads and writes retry three times, the POST never (its attempt is counted instead). Workflows limits relied on: 17 × 3 steps (cap 1,024 Free, 10,000 Paid; sleeps do not count), sleeps under the 365-day cap, no wall-clock limit per instance, sleeping instances outside the concurrency limit. Per-domain failure tracking: `delivery_domain_health` (same migration; `DeliveryDomainHealth` in `packages/domain/src/delivery-domain-health.ts`) counts distinct UTC days with a failed attempt (an HTTP failure or no answer; a `Local` failure or an activity settled without asking the remote counts nothing); on the seventh day with no success between, the domain is `Unavailable` (Mastodon's `DeliveryFailureTracker::FAILURE_DAYS_THRESHOLD` and `UnavailableDomain`, kept per domain rather than per inbox URL). Fan-out (`ExpandFollowers`) and targeted enqueue record inboxes on an unavailable domain as `Skipped` / `DomainUnavailable` targets (`OutboxDeliverySkipped`, `outbox.skip`), reading the unavailable list from D1 once per activity; a Workflow already retrying skips its next attempt. Any answer from the remote (a success, or a permanent refusal), any verified inbound activity from the signing actor's host (`server/routes/activitypub.ts`, Mastodon's `track_inverse_success!`), or the admin's "Mark as available" deletes the record; delivery and inbox paths check an isolate copy of the table (read again each minute), so they cost a D1 write only when the domain is tracked. `/admin/instances` lists the failing and unavailable domains under "Delivery failures", and `/admin/instances/:domain` shows the domain's record with "Mark as available" (Mastodon's `restart_delivery`); there is no admin REST endpoint for it, as Mastodon has none. An hourly sweep (`sweepStuckDeliveries`, `server/stuck-delivery-sweep.ts`; `StuckDelivery` in `packages/domain/src/stuck-delivery.ts`) looks at up to 50 `Queued` targets whose next attempt is over an hour late (index `idx_outbox_deliveries_due`): it creates the Workflow a target never got, restarts one that errored, terminated or completed without settling the row, waits for an active one, and gives up (`RetryExhausted`) a target over 10 days old. Observability: `outbox.deliver` per attempt (`[latencyMs, failedHttpStatus, attemptNumber]`, `[outcome, host]`), `outbox.domain`, `outbox.sweep`, a fourth `outbox.expand` double (skipped targets); log events `OutboxDeliveryGivenUp` (`RetryExhausted`, `Permanent` or `Stuck`), `DeliveryDomainUnavailable`, `DeliveryDomainAvailable`, `OutboxDeliverySkipped`, `OutboxDeliverySweep`, `OutboxWorkflowRestartFailed`. Deviations: Mastodon's per-inbox Stoplight circuit breaker is not ported; Workflow instances already sleeping on the old schedule restart their steps under the new step names when the new version runs them (one extra attempt at most). Tests: `packages/domain/src/outbox-delivery.test.ts`, `delivery-attempt-outcome.test.ts`, `delivery-domain-health.test.ts`, `stuck-delivery.test.ts`, `packages/worker/server/outbox-delivery.test.ts`, `admin-pages.test.ts`.
- Outbox dead-letter queue and retention (OPS-1, OPS-3): `tstodon-outbox-process` dead-letters to `tstodon-outbox-dlq` (provisioned in `infra/cloudflare`, written as the consumer's `dead_letter_queue` by `queueConsumerOptions` in `void-plugins.ts` from the queue file's `deadLetterQueue` export). The handler sends a job that returned retry on its third attempt there instead of dropping it; Queues moves what throws or does not parse after `max_retries`. The dead-letter consumer stores each job in `outbox_dead_jobs` (migration 0127) and the operator-sent `RequeueDeadOutboxJobs` job sends them back (runbook: [Dead-lettered jobs](../operations/runbook.md#dead-lettered-jobs)). The six-hourly `VacuumActivities` job prunes finished `outbox_deliveries`, `outbound_activities` no delivery or outbox page still reads, and `inbox_activities` past a dedupe floor of 7 days, 500 rows per table per pass over the indexes of migration 0128 (`*_RETENTION_DAYS`, default 30; [Activity retention](../reference/configuration.md#activity-retention)).
- Production operations docs: an [operations runbook](../operations/runbook.md) (D1 Time Travel and exports, R2 media copies, what KV and the Durable Objects hold, a secrets inventory that treats a D1 export as a key backup; Workers Logs, `wrangler tail` and Logpush; every structured log event the Worker emits with its meaning; the Analytics Engine metrics with SQL; delivery health from `outbox_deliveries` and the delivery Workflow; queue, cron, D1, Durable Object and R2 checks; suggested alerts; playbooks for a remote down, inbox floods, spam waves, D1 limits, media growth, Access lockout, secret rotation, rollback and the media backfills; upgrades) and a [launch checklist](../operations/launch-checklist.md) (bindings, secrets, Access protect and bypass paths checked with `curl`, first admin, media metadata stripping checked with `exiftool`, Web Push, rate-limit headers, an IP block, crons, backups, and a 16-step federation smoke test against Mastodon 4.5+ and Misskey). The deploy checklist now bypasses `/actor` in Access (remote servers fetch the instance actor to verify what it signs) and no longer says the server sends mail. The queue handler logs `OutboxJobDropped` (`jobKind`, `attempts`) when it gives up on a job, which before left only the `outbox.job` `failed` metric. The metric reference in `d1-heavy-scenarios.md` gains `push.deliver` and the full list of paged jobs. The gaps found were OPS-1 to OPS-10 above (OPS-9 is done, see below).
