- Rate limits, after Mastodon's Rack::Attack throttles and `RateLimiter` families. `RateLimit` / `RateLimitPolicy` in `packages/domain/src/rate-limit.ts` (with the `HitRateLimitCounters` contract) decide which buckets a request counts against: `api_ip` (300 / 5 min per address, unauthenticated `/api/*`), `api_token` (300 / 5 min) and `api_account` (1500 / 5 min), the per-account families `media` (30 / 30 min), `statuses` and `reblogs` (300 / 3 h), `follows` and `reports` (400 / 24 h), and per address `sign_in`, `app_registration`, `oauth_token`, `federation_fetch` and `inbox_ip`; `inbox_domain` counts a delivery per sending server only once its signature verified (`server/routes/activitypub.ts`). `app/middleware/02.rate-limit.ts` (`server/rate-limit.ts`; the later middleware moved up one number) answers Mastodon's 429 (`{"error":"Too many requests"}`, `Retry-After`, `X-RateLimit-*`) and sends `X-RateLimit-*` on API responses. Counters live in the `RateLimitCounter` Durable Object (`RATE_LIMIT_COUNTER`, migration tag `v2`), one per subject, with an alarm that deletes it after its last window; each isolate remembers counters already over. The address is `CF-Connecting-IP` (IPv6 per `/64`). `RATE_LIMITS_ENABLED` and `RATE_LIMITS` (JSON overrides) configure it; the test Worker runs with the limits off and `server/rate-limit.test.ts` turns them on. `authenticate` is memoized per request, so the middleware and the handler read the token once. See "Rate limits" in `docs/reference/configuration.md` and the edge rule in `docs/operations/cloudflare-deploy.md`.
- Browser e2e runs and passes again (20 of 20). It could not start a browser where only Chromium revision 1243 is installed: `@playwright/test` 1.56.1 wants headless shell 1194, so it is pinned to 1.63.0, the release that uses 1243 (1.65.0 is current and uses 1254). Six scenarios had failed since sign-in moved to Access (#82) and images gained dimensions (#86); none was a product bug. The admin scenarios (three), "Privacy and reach" and the CSV export lost the login: `e2e/web/fixtures.ts` set `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` with `page.setExtraHTTPHeaders`, which windows the web app opens do not inherit, and the export's `<a download>` link is fetched by Chromium outside the page, past context-wide headers and `route` handlers too. Since the Worker keeps no session cookie, every request needs the assertion, which Access supplies at its edge from the `CF_Authorization` cookie. The suite now does the same: the fixtures set that cookie and `e2e/web/access-edge.mjs`, on the public port 8793 in front of Void's dev server (now on 8795), turns it into the header on every request, WebSocket upgrades included. The image scenario expected `naturalWidth` 64 and got 566: since #86 the attachment's `meta` has `original` and `small` widths, so Mastodon's media gallery renders a `w` srcset, and `naturalWidth` is then the laid-out width. It now checks the srcset widths and decodes the chosen copy (64 × 48). CI gained an `e2e-web` job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) so the suite does not go unrun again. See `docs/getting-started/e2e-web.md`.
- Stored media sizes are recorded and reported (#94 follow-up). Migration `0125_media_file_sizes.sql` adds `media_attachments.file_size` / `preview_file_size`, `account_profiles.avatar_file_size` / `header_file_size` and `custom_emojis.image_file_size`: the byte size R2 reports for what tstodon stores in `MEDIA` (uploads after metadata stripping; the IMAGES preview). The upload path (`server/routes/media.ts`, `processUploadedImage`), `storeProfileImage` and the custom emoji create/replace write them; `StripStoredMediaMetadata` records the size of the object as now stored with each mark, so a rewrite keeps it true to the bucket; a removed avatar or header drops its size (`upsertAccountProfile`), and a deleted attachment, emoji or account takes its sizes with its row. Rows stored before are filled in by `BackfillStoredMediaSizes` (`StoredMediaSizes` in `packages/domain/src/stored-media-sizes.ts`, `server/media-sizes.ts`), queued by the six-hourly cron on `tstodon-outbox-process`: one `listUnsizedMedia` query per page across the four sources (partial indexes, so a finished run reads nothing), 50 rows a page, a HEAD per object (no body read), 0 bytes and a `MediaSizeNotRead` warning for a missing object, a failed HEAD left for the next run, `MediaSizeBackfillDone` logged once. `POST /api/v1/admin/dimensions` `space_usage` adds Mastodon's `media` entry ("Media storage": originals, previews, a legacy copy the rekey job keeps, avatars, headers, custom emoji images) beside `d1`; `POST /api/v1/admin/measures` `instance_media_attachments` totals this server's attachments (originals and previews) for its own domain, by upload day, and stays 0 for remote domains, whose media tstodon links rather than caches; `human_value` is Rails' `number_to_human_size` (`server/human-size.ts`, e.g. "1.18 MB"). `/settings/export` shows the account's "Media storage" (attachments, avatar, header). Nothing else lives in the bucket: link card images, remote avatars and remote media are not cached, and the `CUSTOM_EMOJIS` var's images are hosted elsewhere.
- The admin API has what Mastodon 4.5–4.7 serves and tstodon lacked. `/api/v1/admin/ip_blocks` (CRUD; `ip` as an IPv4 or IPv6 address or CIDR, stored as its network, `severity` `sign_up_requires_approval` / `sign_up_block` / `no_access`, `comment`, `expires_in`; migration `0122_ip_blocks.sql`; `IpRange` / `IpBlock` in `packages/domain/src/ip-block.ts`) is enforced from `CF-Connecting-IP`: `no_access` refuses every request, federation included, in `app/middleware/00.ip-block.ts`; `sign_up_block` refuses and `sign_up_requires_approval` holds an Access sign-up (`server/ip-access.ts`, per-isolate list cached a minute and dropped on change; see "IP blocks" in `docs/reference/configuration.md`). `/api/v1/admin/tags` (index, show, update `usable` / `listable` / `trendable` / `display_name`; migration `0123_tag_moderation.sql`) is enforced: an unusable tag's timeline is empty and it cannot be featured, an unusable or unlisted tag leaves hashtag search and trends, `trendable` is the trend review. `/api/v1/admin/trends/links/publishers` (index, approve, reject; a rejected publisher's links leave the link trends and the link timeline). `POST /api/v1/admin/measures`, `/dimensions` and `/retention` compute Mastodon's keys from D1 (`server/admin-metrics.ts`); `active_users` and the cohorts read `account_activity_days` and `Admin::Account#ip`/`#ips` read `account_ips` (migration `0124_account_activity.sql`, written at most once per account, address and day per isolate); `instance_media_attachments` and the `media` entry of `space_usage` read the stored media sizes recorded since (see the media storage accounting entry above). `GET /api/v2/admin/accounts` takes `origin`, `status` (all when absent), `permissions=staff`, `role_ids[]`, `invited_by`, `username`, `display_name`, `by_domain`, `email` and `ip` (address or CIDR over the recorded addresses); without `origin` it lists local accounts, as remote actors page by `offset` under `origin=remote`. `Admin::Account#role` is the account's role (it was always `User`), v1's `staff` filter works, and the account, report, domain block and allow, email domain and canonical email block, IP block and tag lists send Mastodon's `Link` headers (`origin=remote` accounts and the trends lists page by `offset` and send none). Schemas: `ApiAdminIpBlock`, `ApiAdminMeasure`, `ApiAdminDimension`, `ApiAdminCohort`, `ApiAdminPreviewCardProvider` (`ApiAdminTag#listable` is now always rendered). Scopes: `admin:*:ip_blocks`; tags, metrics and publishers under `admin:read` / `admin:write`; `/api/v2/admin/accounts` under `admin:*:accounts`.
- The Worker enforces the limits `/api/v2/instance` advertises, from the same `InstanceLimits` constants (`packages/core/src/instance-limits.ts`); #92 advertised Mastodon's account and media defaults without checking them, and the status limit ignored `characters_reserved_per_url`. Statuses: as Mastodon's `StatusLengthValidator` and the web UI's counter, the content warning and the body share `max_characters` and are counted by `countableText` (a URL is 23 characters, `@user@domain` counts as `@user`); there is no separate 500-character content-warning limit any more. Too long answers `Validation failed: Text character limit of 500 exceeded`, too many media `Cannot attach more than 4 files` (create, edit; `server/routes/statuses/status-limits.ts`). Polls: more than `max_options` options, an option over `max_characters_per_option` and an `expires_in` outside `min_expiration`..`max_expiration` are refused with Mastodon's `Validation failed: Poll ...` messages, on create and now on edit too (an edit checked nothing but the two-option minimum); a too short `expires_in` was clamped to 300 s, Mastodon's `PollExpirationValidator` refuses it. Accounts: `update_credentials` (and `PATCH /api/v1/profile`) refuses, before storing anything, a display name over `max_display_name_length`, a note over `max_note_length` (counted as a status), more than `max_profile_fields` fields (they were cut to four silently) and a field name or value over `profile_field_name_limit` / `profile_field_value_limit`, as `{ error: "Validation failed: ..." }` 422s (Rails' messages, e.g. `Display name is too long (maximum is 30 characters)`, `Note character limit of 500 exceeded`); it also reads Mastodon's `fields_attributes` (the web UI's profile editor sends a JSON array; forms send `fields_attributes[0][name]`), which it ignored. Media (`POST /api/v1/media`, `/api/v2/media`, `server/media-limits.ts`): a file over `image_size_limit` / `video_size_limit` is refused before it is read (`Validation failed: File file size must be less than 16 MB`, Paperclip's message), an image over `image_matrix_limit` (`7681x4320 images are not supported`) and a video over `video_matrix_limit` or `video_frame_rate_limit` (`121fps videos are not supported`) by the dimensions `MediaDimensions` (`packages/domain/src/media-dimensions.ts`) reads from the header without decoding, as FastImage and ffprobe: JPEG SOF, PNG IHDR, GIF, WebP VP8/VP8L/VP8X, AVIF `ispe`; an MP4 video track's `tkhd` size and `stsz` samples over its `mdhd` duration, a WebM track's `PixelWidth` / `PixelHeight` and `DefaultDuration`. Deviations: the frame rate is the track's average, and it is not checked when the container does not give one (a fragmented MP4, a WebM without `DefaultDuration`), nor are dimensions a header does not give; `max_avatar_description_length` / `max_header_description_length` are advertised but avatar and header descriptions are not stored (they render `""` and the request fields are ignored); `registrations.min_age` is not checked at sign-in (Access provisions accounts). Tests: `packages/core/src/post-text.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/media-dimensions.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/status-composition.test.ts`, `server/status-limits.test.ts`, `server/account-limits.test.ts`, `server/api-refusals.test.ts` (each limit at and just over it).
- The notification policy keeps Mastodon 4.6's `for_bots` (migration `0119_notification_policy_for_bots.sql`, `notification_policies.for_bots`, default `accept` as Mastodon). `GET /api/v2/notifications/policy` rendered a fixed `"accept"` and `PATCH` ignored the field. `PATCH` now takes `for_bots` (`accept` / `filter` / `drop`; no legacy `filter_bots` boolean, as Mastodon), it is stored and rendered, and `NotificationPolicy.disposition` (`packages/domain/src/notification-policy.ts`) applies it, with the other categories' most restrictive action, to a sender that is a bot: a local account marked `bot` (`account_profiles.bot`, set by `update_credentials`) or a remote actor whose `type` is `Service` or `Application` (`RemoteActorProfile.bot`). `filter` holds the notification as a notification request, `drop` never creates it. Tests: `packages/domain/src/notification-policy.test.ts`, `server/notification-policy.test.ts` (stored values with `expectContract(ApiNotificationPolicy, …)`, a local bot's and a remote `Service`'s mentions accepted, held and dropped, a human sender untouched).
- `requested_by` and `followed_by` cover remote accounts, as Mastodon's `RelationshipFilter`. `relationshipFlags` (`server/relationship-store.ts`) read the target's follow of the viewer only from `follows`, which holds local follows, so a remote actor whose `Follow` waited on a locked account's approval (`remote_follows`, `remote_request_kind = 'Queued'`) rendered `requested_by: false`, and an accepted remote follower `followed_by: false`. The same D1 batch now reads the viewer's `remote_follows` row for a remote (`r_…`) target (`findRemoteFollow`): `Queued` is `requested_by`, `Fulfilled` is `followed_by`. `GET /api/v1/accounts/relationships`, the account routes and `POST /api/v1/follow_requests/:id/authorize` / `reject` for a remote requester all render through it; the follow-request routes answered a remote requester with fixed flags (no block, mute, note or own follow), and `remove_from_followers` read a remote target's flags by its actor URI instead of its account id, so its own follow of the target came back `following: false`. Tests: `server/follow-requests.test.ts` (relationships before and after authorize / reject, checked with `expectContract(ApiRelationship, …)`).
- The Worker's own pages look like the web app and follow each account's appearance. Sign-in, OAuth consent, `/settings/*`, filters, account security, automated post deletion, severed relationships, import/export and `/admin/*` had a per-page `<style>` in system fonts; `page()` (`server/auth-pages.ts`) now links one hashed stylesheet built from the web app's theme tokens (`src/styles/entrypoints/pages.scss`: Linear-like palette, dark and light, contrast overrides, Inter), resolving `auto` with `prefers-color-scheme` / `prefers-contrast` since the pages load no script; form controls get a 3:1 boundary. A per-account colour scheme (`auto` / `light` / `dark`) and contrast (`auto` / `high`), Mastodon's `color_scheme` / `contrast` user settings, are stored in `account_appearance` (migration `0119`), edited on `/settings/preferences` (Appearance) and `/settings/preferences/appearance`, and rendered as `data-color-scheme` / `data-contrast` by the HTML shell and, through `app/middleware/06.page-appearance.ts` (an `HTMLRewriter` pass for the account `browserSession` resolved), by those pages. Signed-out visitors stay on `auto` (`server/appearance.test.ts`).
- The brand icons use the theme accent: the tstodon mark, favicons, Android and Apple icons are re-rendered at `#5e6ad2` (were `#4c5fd5`), and `/android-chrome-192x192.png`, the icon the service worker gives grouped notifications, is served from `public/`. The client's Vite manifests (`/.vite/manifest.json`, `/.vite/manifest-assets.json`, about 320 KB) are no longer uploaded or served: `public/.assetsignore` lists `.vite/`, while the Worker build still reads them from `dist/client`.
- Media stored before stripping on upload loses its metadata too (#87 / #88 follow-up). Originals, avatars and headers stored before `UploadMetadata.forUpload` kept their EXIF GPS position, XMP and video location atoms on the public media hostname (the two entries below listed "objects stored before this keep their metadata" as a deviation). The `StripStoredMediaMetadata` job on the `tstodon-outbox-process` queue (`StoredMediaMetadata.pass` in `packages/domain/src/stored-media-metadata.ts`, wired in `server/media-metadata-backfill.ts`) rewrites them in place with the same stripper, judged by the bytes: 10 rows and at most 64 MiB read per message, objects over 32 MiB passed over (`TooLarge`), the cursor (`source` attachments / avatars / headers, `afterId`) and the running counts in the message, the next page queued at once; the six-hourly cron queues the first page, so it needs no operator step and is safe to re-run. A clean object (already stripped, GIF) is marked without a write; a write goes back only if the etag still matches, and drops the Worker's own Cache API copy. Migration 0119 adds `metadata_stripped_at` / `metadata_strip_error` to `media_attachments` and the avatar / header pairs to `account_profiles`; uploads are marked when stored (`insertMedia`, and `upsertAccountProfile` marks a changed key), and an unreadable container keeps its bytes with the reason recorded and is not read again. Previews are not walked: they are WebPs encoded from already stripped originals. Not done: the CDN and browser caches keep the old bytes under the same URL (`immutable`, one year) until an operator purges the media hostname, and remote servers keep their copies; videos over 32 MiB keep their metadata. Operator steps: "Stripping metadata from stored media" in `docs/operations/cloudflare-deploy.md`. Tests: `server/media-metadata-backfill.test.ts` (a GPS JPEG and a located MP4 stripped and marked, the kept legacy copy, clean objects not written, an unreadable PNG left and counted and not read again, paging and the queued cursor, avatars and headers, uploads marked, cron), `packages/domain/src/stored-media-metadata.test.ts`.
- What #92 left untyped now has schemas, and the federation output too. REST: the admin API (`ApiAdminAccount`, `ApiAdminReport`, `ApiAdminDomainBlock`, `ApiAdminDomainAllow`, `ApiAdminEmailDomainBlock`, `ApiAdminCanonicalEmailBlock`, `ApiAdminTag`, tstodon's admin announcements and custom emoji; `packages/api/src/admin.ts`), `ApiV1Instance`, `ApiInstanceActivity`, `ApiApplication` / `ApiCredentialApplication`, and the profile builder (`ApiProfileDocument`: Mastodon's `ApiProfile`, which the web UI reads, with tstodon's `formatted_note` / `formatted_fields`); a local status's replaced revisions (`status_edits.snapshot_json`) are parsed at the D1 boundary with `StatusEditSnapshot` (`ApiStatusEdit` without its account), which also reads the whole-Status rows stored before. ActivityPub: a second entry, `@tstodon/api/activitypub` (`packages/api/src/activitypub/`), describes what tstodon sends and serves (actor, instance actor, `Note` / `Question`, poll vote, `Tombstone`, `QuoteAuthorization`, `Create` / `Update` / `Delete` / `Announce` / `Like` / `Follow` / `Accept` / `Reject` / `Undo` / `Block` / `Move` / `Flag` / `QuoteRequest`, the followers / following / outbox collections, WebFinger, NodeInfo); the builders return those types and the delivery queue takes an `ApActivityDocument` (a forwarded remote body goes through `forwardActivityToInboxes`). Corrections: Admin::Account nests the account as `account` (it spread its fields at the top level) with `ips`, `locale`, `confirmed` and a full `role`; Admin::DomainBlock has `digest`, Admin::EmailDomainBlock `allow_with_approval`, an admin report's rule `hint: ""`; `GET /api/v1/instance` lost tstodon's `kind` and has Mastodon's v1 shape (`urls.streaming_api`, v1 `configuration`, `rules`); local status history answers `StatusEdit` entities instead of whole statuses; `update_credentials` and `PATCH /api/v1/profile` read Mastodon's `fields_attributes` (only a non-Mastodon `fields` was read, so the web UI's profile editor could not change fields); an edited local note federates `updated` (Mastodon ignores a note `Update` without a newer `updated`, so tstodon's edits never showed there); NodeInfo's `openRegistrations` follows `registrations.enabled` (it was always `false`). Tests: `server/admin-api-contract.test.ts`, `server/activitypub-contract.test.ts` (served documents and every activity a run of actions queues), more endpoints in `api-contract.test.ts`, and `test/mastodon-preview-schemas.ts` is gone: the tests that used it parse with the contract schemas. Not done: tstodon sends no `OrderedCollectionPage` (collections are one page), notes carry no `Hashtag` tags, `contentMap` or `url`, and the admin endpoints Mastodon has but tstodon does not (IP blocks, measures, dimensions, retention) have no schemas.
- The REST API has one typed contract, `@tstodon/api` (`packages/api`): Zod 4 schemas of Mastodon's 4.5–4.7 wire entities with their inferred types, named as the web UI's `api_types` (`ApiStatusJSON`, `ApiAccountJSON`, `ApiNotificationGroupJSON`, ...), and the enum-like values (visibilities, quote policies and states, notification types, filter contexts, ...) in a Zod-free `@tstodon/api/values`. Wire entities have no `kind`; notifications and media discriminate on Mastodon's `type`, quotes on `state`; tstodon's `move` notification is an extension in the schema. The Worker's document builders (statuses local and remote, boosts, accounts and credential account, media, polls, cards, quotes, relationships, notifications v1 and grouped, reports, filters v1/v2, conversations, tags and featured tags, lists, markers, search, instance v2, suggestions, announcements, scheduled statuses, remote status edits, trending links) return these types instead of `Record<string, unknown>`; the domain's `Visibility.toMastodon` and quote audiences use the shared values. The web UI's `src/mastodon/api_types/*.ts` re-export the types (type-only; the client bundle has no Zod: 582 JS chunks, 15,148,061 → 15,148,231 bytes, gzip 4,654,188 → 4,654,163), and the client code the stricter nullability touched is listed in `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md`. Server corrections the types found: a poll that hides its totals still shows `votes_count` / `voters_count` (only option counts are hidden, as Mastodon's); a FeaturedTag's `statuses_count` is a string; a Tag always has its days of `history` (featured-tag suggestions had none); Relationship has `requested_by`; PreviewCard has `language`, `image_description`, `published_at` and `authors`, and a cached card's `type` is checked; `/api/v2/instance` has the account and media limits, `urls.status` and Mastodon 4.5's `timeline_access` (limits not enforced yet are Mastodon's defaults, in `InstanceLimits`), and a rule without a hint has `hint: ""`; v1 notifications carry `group_key` (it was only on the streaming payload), only the payload their type has (no `status: null` on a follow), and a status filter match on the status's `filtered` instead of the notification's; a grouped status notification always has `status_id`; a status shown inside a quote shows its own quote by id only (`quoted_status_id`), local statuses too (a local quote of a quote nested whole); v2 suggestions have `sources`; `POST /api/v1/reports` answers the stored Report with its target account (it sent `{ id }`); `POST /api/v1/conversations/:id/read` answers the Conversation; the endpoints that answer nothing send `{}` instead of an empty body (list delete and member add / remove, conversation delete, scheduled status delete, v1 filter delete); an announcement always has `published_at`. Tests: `server/api-contract.test.ts` parses the main endpoints' answers with the schemas (`expectContract`, `test/api-contract.ts`), and the poll, tag, notification and stream tests read the contract.
- The web frontend is the Void app's client ("SPA + API"). `packages/web` is gone: Mastodon's `app/javascript` is `packages/worker/src`, its Vite plugins `packages/worker/config/vite` (unmodified), its `public/` files `packages/worker/public`, and the Worker's shared code moved from `packages/worker/src` to `packages/worker/server` (`#src/*` became `#server/*`). One `vite build` with `voidPlugin()` builds the client (`packages/worker/web-build.ts`, upstream's `vite.config.mts` ported to Vite's `client` environment, URLs unchanged under `/packs`, the service worker at `/sw.js`) and then the Worker (`builder.buildApp`), which reads the client's Vite manifest through `virtual:tstodon/web-manifest` instead of the `packs/tstodon-web.json` that `scripts/build-mastodon-web.mjs` copied into `assets/`. The script, `pnpm web:build`, the up-to-date stamp and the `webBuilt` test skips are gone: the build fetches upstream's `public/emoji` / `public/ocr` itself, and every Worker test runs against a build with the UI (1555, none skipped). `pnpm dev` serves the frontend from source with hot reload (the shell links the source entries, `/@vite/client` and the React Refresh preamble). One Vite: the frontend builds on Vite+ core 1.3.0 (Vite 8.3) and its 4052 tests run on Vitest 5 (`pnpm test:web`, jsdom), typechecked with TypeScript 7 (`pnpm typecheck:web`); the scoped `@tstodon/web>vite` / `>vitest` overrides and Storybook, ESLint, Stylelint and oxfmt with their packages are removed. Two fixes the move needed: `src/package.json` without `type` keeps Rolldown's CommonJS interop as upstream's (under `"type": "module"` the web app failed to boot), and two `node_modules` globs lost one `../` (they silently dropped the plural-rules locale data and the emoji data). The client output has the same 17 entries, 331 `intl/` chunks, 3935 emoji and 4492 byte-identical files as the last `pnpm web:build`; 582 JS chunks instead of 641 (newer Rolldown merges small shared chunks). `scripts/import-mastodon-web-lock.mjs` now merges the frontend's entries into the `packages/worker` importer, resolving plugin peers to Vite+ core. Upstream path mapping and the 3-way update: `packages/worker/src/UPSTREAM.md`, `docs/operations/mastodon-web-upstream.md`.
- Mastodon's web UI is vendored in `packages/web` (`@tstodon/web`), so tstodon can change it directly. `pnpm web:build` used to clone a pinned release (`MASTODON_WEB_REF`, `v4.7.3`) into `.cache/mastodon-web/<ref>` and build it with Yarn; the frontend is now Mastodon `v4.7.5` (`24cd1c87`) in the repository: `app/javascript`, the Vite, Vitest, TypeScript, Storybook and lint configs, `config/vite`, `config/themes.yml`, the `public/` files the Worker serves, `LICENSE` and `AUTHORS.md`, imported unmodified in one commit (`packages/web/upstream.json` lists the paths, `UPSTREAM.md` the local changes). `public/emoji` (twemoji, about 21 MB) and `public/ocr` (Tesseract data, about 11 MB) are not committed; the build fetches them from the pinned commit (sparse, blob-filtered, shallow fetch of the tag, checked against the recorded commit) into `.cache/mastodon-web/upstream/<commit>/` and links them into `packages/web/public/`. Dependencies are part of the pnpm workspace: the lockfile entries come from upstream's `yarn.lock` through `pnpm import` (`scripts/import-mastodon-web-lock.mjs`), so 1219 of the 1250 packages in its tree are the versions upstream locks; the others are shared with the rest of the workspace (4) or optional peers Yarn did not install (`rollup`). Parent-scoped overrides (`@tstodon/web>vite`, `@tstodon/web>vitest`) keep Mastodon's Vite 8.2.1 and Vitest 4.1.10 for the package while the rest of the workspace keeps Vite+. Upstream relied on Yarn hoisting for `browserslist`, `tesseract.js-core`, `@types/history`, `jsdom` and `redux`; they are declared at upstream's versions. Root `vp check` skips the package. The up-to-date stamp is now a digest of every file under `packages/web` git tracks or does not ignore, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, the script and the manifest it wrote; `packs/tstodon-web.json` records `ref` as `v4.7.5+tstodon`. The output has the same files as the `v4.7.3` build (4822 files, same manifest keys and locales). How to move to a newer release: `docs/operations/mastodon-web-upstream.md`. CI now builds the web UI before the Worker build, so the web app tests that skip without a build run there, and runs Mastodon's own typecheck and Vitest suite (`legacy-tests`; Storybook's browser tests and Mastodon's ESLint/Stylelint are not run).
- Uploaded videos no longer publish their metadata (#87 follow-up). MP4 and WebM went to R2 byte for byte, so a phone video's GPS position (QuickTime `©xyz`, Apple's `com.apple.quicktime.location.ISO6709` key, an action camera's GPS track), device make and model, creation dates and XMP were public on the media hostname. Mastodon re-encodes with ffmpeg and `-map_metadata -1`; there is no ffmpeg in a Worker, so `VideoMetadata` (`packages/domain/src/video-metadata.ts`) rewrites the container in place, judged by the bytes: nothing moves and the length is unchanged, so `stco` / `co64`, fragment offsets, `iloc`, SeekHead and Cues stay valid. In ISO BMFF (MP4, MOV, M4V, 3GP; `ftyp` or a QuickTime first box) `udta`, `meta`, `meco`, `©` atoms, `XMP_`, `prft`, XMP / Canon / Sony metadata `uuid` boxes and every top-level `uuid` become `free` with a zeroed payload, under `moov`, `trak`, `mdia`, `minf`, `moof` and `traf`; a top-level `meta` goes too unless its handler is `pict`, whose `Exif` / XMP items are blanked with AVIF's code (`blankHeifMetadataItems`); timed metadata tracks (handler `meta` or `camm`: GoPro GPMF, DJI, Google CAMM, Apple `mebx`) are freed whole and their samples zeroed in `mdat`, found from `stsc` / `stsz` / `stz2` / `stco` / `co64`, or in fragments from `tfhd` / `trun` / `trex` with the implicit, `default-base-is-moof` and explicit bases; `mvhd` / `tkhd` / `mdhd` creation and modification times become 0, as ffmpeg's output has them. In EBML (WebM, Matroska; DocType checked) `Tags` and `Attachments`, the `Info` title, date, file names and segment UUIDs, track names, the SeekHead entries for what was voided and the CRC-32 of every changed element become `Void` elements of the same length; `MuxingApp` / `WritingApp` (mandatory, naming software) stay; a Segment and Clusters of unknown size, as MediaRecorder writes, are walked. Box nesting is bounded (8 levels), 64-bit and to-EOF sizes are handled, every box and element must fit its parent. `UploadMetadata.forUpload` (`packages/domain/src/upload-metadata.ts`) replaces `ImageMetadata.forUpload` for media, avatars and headers: an image or video container is stripped whatever the declared type, but an MP4 / WebM upload must be a video container and a JPEG / PNG / WebP / AVIF upload an image one, else 422; a malformed container is refused with 422 (`ValidationError`, logged `MediaVideoRejected`) and nothing is stored. As with the JPEG scan, an MP4 whose last box is an `mdat` cut short and a WebM cut inside a Cluster are kept (GPS samples are zeroed as far as the file goes). `sampleUploadFor` now gives real MP4 / WebM files. Checked locally (not in CI) with ffprobe and ffmpeg on ffmpeg-made MP4, MOV with `mdta` keys, fragmented MP4, WebM, live WebM and MKV with an attachment: no tags left, all frames decode. Deviations: no transcoding, so every track stays (subtitle telemetry included), and encoder strings in sample descriptions and bitstreams, chapter titles and `MuxingApp` / `WritingApp` are kept; objects stored before this keep their metadata. Tests: `server/video-metadata.test.ts` (fixtures in `test/video-fixtures.ts`: MP4 with user data, QuickTime keys, XMP `uuid` and a GoPro-style GPS track, moov after a 64-bit `mdat` with `co64` and version 1 headers, fragmented MP4, file-level `pict` / `mdir` `meta`, WebM with Tags, Attachments, title, date and CRC-32, live WebM; offsets still point at the same frames, truncations and corruptions never throw nor leak, uploads store the stripped bytes or answer 422 and store nothing).
- Uploaded images no longer publish their metadata. Originals, avatars and headers went to R2 byte for byte, so a phone photo's EXIF GPS position, camera and owner, its XMP and IPTC, and any comments were public on the media hostname (the #57 entry above lists this as a deviation). Mastodon strips them by re-encoding with libvips after auto-rotating. `ImageMetadata.forUpload` (`packages/domain/src/image-metadata.ts`) now rewrites the container losslessly before `POST /api/v1/media`, `POST /api/v2/media` and `update_credentials` store it, judged by the bytes, not the declared type: JPEG drops every APPn but JFIF, `ICC_PROFILE` and `Adobe`, plus comments and bytes after EOI (scans are walked, so segments between progressive scans go too); PNG drops `eXIf`, the text chunks, `tIME` and unknown ancillary chunks, keeping the colour and APNG chunks; WebP drops `EXIF`, `XMP ` and unknown chunks and rewrites the VP8X flags and RIFF size; AVIF has its `Exif` and XMP items overwritten in place (an empty IFD, spaces), as removing them would mean rewriting `iloc`. The ICC profile stays so colours do not shift. The EXIF orientation stays as a new EXIF block with only that tag, so a sideways-stored photo still displays upright; no pixels are rotated, so it works without the optional `IMAGES` binding, and `meta.original` keeps the displayed size (a test pins 800x600 with orientation 6 as `600x800`). A file whose container cannot be walked, or that is declared JPEG, PNG, WebP or AVIF but is none of them, is refused with 422 (`ValidationError`, logged `MediaImageRejected` / `ProfileImageRejected`) instead of being stored with its metadata, as Mastodon refuses a file it cannot process; a JPEG cut off inside its scan is kept. The walk is one linear pass that slices the input and copies once (the entropy scan uses `indexOf(0xff)`). Tests that uploaded placeholder bytes as `image/png` now upload real images (`sampleUploadFor` in `test/image-fixtures.ts`), which the `IMAGES` binding also analyses. Deviations: the original is not re-encoded, so it is neither rotated upright nor capped at 8,294,400 pixels; GIF, video (MP4 location atoms) and custom emoji images are stored as uploaded; objects stored before this keep their metadata (no backfill). Tests: `server/image-metadata.test.ts` (fixtures with GPS EXIF, XMP, IPTC, comments, PNG text chunks, WebP and AVIF metadata built in `test/image-fixtures.ts`; truncations and corruptions never throw nor leak), `server/media-image.test.ts`.
- Media is removed when nothing needs it any more, as Mastodon's `RemoveStatusService#remove_media` and `Vacuum::MediaAttachmentsVacuum` (#57, #86 follow-up). Deleting a status only cleared `media_attachments.status_id`, so the original and the preview stayed in R2 until the owner called `DELETE /api/v1/media/:id`, and an upload never posted stayed forever. `DELETE /api/v1/statuses/:id` now reads Mastodon 4.4's `delete_media` (query or body): set, the status's media rows go in the same D1 batch as the status (`deleteStatusMedia`, through `deleteStatus` in `server/status-store.ts` with `media: "Delete"`) and `deleteLocalStatus` (`server/local-status-delete.ts`) deletes their objects, original, preview and any legacy key, inline (a handful of keys, one R2 call; rows first, a failed bucket delete is logged as `MediaObjectsNotDeleted`). Left out, as the web UI's "Delete & redraft" does, the media stays unattached so the redraft can attach it again. The automated post deletion and moderation removal always delete the media; account deletion already removed every object of the account (`purgeAccount`). Mastodon keeps media an edit took off a status attached for its edit history; here the edit detaches it, so the status deletion also removes the owner's unattached media that the status's `status_edits` snapshots list, and the sweep keeps it while one of the owner's statuses has a snapshot listing it. The sweep is a new `OutboxJob` kind, `VacuumMediaAttachments` (`MediaVacuum` in `packages/domain/src/media-vacuum.ts` over the `DeleteUnattachedMedia` / `DeleteMediaObjects` contracts, wired in `server/media-vacuum.ts`), queued by the six-hourly cron: up to 100 of the oldest rows uploaded over a day ago (Mastodon's `TTL = 1.day`) that no status holds and no scheduled post names in its `media_ids` (Mastodon's `scheduled_status_id`), deleted rows first and then their objects; a full batch queues the next pass. Migration `0118_media_unattached_index.sql` adds a partial index on unattached rows' `created_at`. Remote media is not stored in R2 (`remote_statuses.media_json` keeps the remote URLs), so it is untouched. As in Mastodon the TTL counts from the upload, so media kept for a redraft of an old post goes at the next sweep if it is not posted again. Not done: Mastodon keeps a reported status's media (`preserve`); here a deleted status is gone from its report either way, so its media goes too. Tests: `server/media-cleanup.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/media-vacuum.test.ts`.
- Local actors publish their profile, as Mastodon 4.5's `ActivityPub::ActorSerializer`. `actorDocument` (`server/activitypub.ts`) emitted no avatar, header or bio, so remote servers showed none. `localActorDocument` (`server/local-actor.ts`, now taking `env`) loads the stored profile, standing and custom emoji, and the actor carries `icon` / `image` (`Image` with the stored content type as `mediaType` and the same `MEDIA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` URL the REST account returns; left out when unset), `summary` (the bio as the REST `note` renders it, `""` when empty), `attachment` (`PropertyValue` per profile field, value HTML-escaped), `tag` (`Emoji` for the custom emoji the name, bio and fields use; no emoji `id`, as there is no emoji URI here), `manuallyApprovesFollowers`, `discoverable`, `indexable`, `published` (the join day at midnight UTC), `type: "Service"` for a bot, and Mastodon's context terms (`toot:`, `schema:PropertyValue` / `value`, …). A suspended account shows `suspended: true`, its username as `name`, and none of its profile. `update_credentials` (and so `PATCH /api/v1/profile`) and deleting an avatar or header now queue an actor `Update` through `sendActorUpdate`, best effort, which carries the actor's context and `to: Public` as Mastodon's `UpdateSerializer`. Not published: `featured` / `featuredTags` (no collection routes), `memorial` and `attributionDomains` (not stored), linkified bio and field links. Tests: `server/actor-profile.test.ts`.
- Image uploads get dimensions, a `small` preview and a blurhash (#57, #61, #64 follow-up). `POST /api/v1/media` and `POST /api/v2/media` stored only the original, so the REST `MediaAttachment` had `meta` with at most `focus`, the original as `preview_url` and `blurhash: null`, and the federated `Document` had no `width` / `height`. For JPEG, PNG, WebP and AVIF the upload now asks the `IMAGES` binding (`imagesBindingAnalyzer` in `server/media-image.ts`, behind the `AnalyzeImage` contract in `packages/domain/src/media-image.ts`) for the original's dimensions, a WebP preview within Mastodon's `small` budget of 230,400 pixels (640x360, aspect kept, never enlarged; `ImageDimensions.fitWithin`), and a 100-pixel PNG thumbnail of that preview, which `decodePng` (`server/png-pixels.ts`, inflating with the runtime's `DecompressionStream`) turns into pixels for a 4x4-component blurhash (`encodeBlurhash` in `server/blurhash.ts`), as Mastodon's `BlurhashTranscoder` samples a 100-pixel thumbnail with `BLURHASH_OPTIONS` 4x4. The encoder gives the `blurhash` package's string (a test pins it; the package is a dev dependency only, nothing is added to the bundle) except that, as the C reference behind Mastodon's `blurhash` gem, it scales AC components by their largest magnitude, not the largest signed value; it reads linear light and cosines from tables and separates the sum, about 1 ms of CPU for a 100x100 thumbnail where the package takes about 47 ms. The preview is stored in R2 under its own `newAttachmentObjectKey`, so it is as unguessable as the original (#57) and served the same way; migration 0117 adds `width`, `height`, `preview_object_key`, `preview_width`, `preview_height` and `blurhash` to `media_attachments`. The REST document now has `meta.original` and `meta.small` (`width`, `height`, `size: "WxH"`, `aspect`), the preview's URL as `preview_url` and the `blurhash`; the note's `Document` carries the `blurhash` and the original's `width` and `height`, as Mastodon's `MediaAttachmentSerializer`. When the binding is missing or fails, the upload stores and answers exactly as before (logged as `MediaImageNotProcessed`), and a preview that fails to store keeps the dimensions and blurhash. GIF and video are not processed. `DELETE /api/v1/media/:id` and account purges also delete the preview. Deviations: the preview is WebP whatever the source format (Mastodon keeps it, JPEG for HEIC), and the original is stored as uploaded, not limited to Mastodon's 8,294,400 pixels (3840x2160) nor stripped of EXIF. Not done: deleting a status still only detaches its media (Mastodon's `RemoveStatusService` deletes it, and a vacuum drops unattached media after a day), so neither the original nor the preview of a deleted status's media is removed until the owner deletes the media. Tests: `server/media-image.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/media-image.test.ts`.
- Quote retries and refresh (#79, Q-4), on the existing `tstodon-outbox-process` queue as two new `OutboxJob` kinds; no new queue, binding or migration. A pending quote by a remote note was verified again only by the note's next `Update`, and an accepted quote's stamp was never checked again. As Mastodon's `fetch_and_verify_quote`, `syncRemoteQuote` (`server/remote-quote.ts`) now queues `RefetchAndVerifyQuote { quoteId, quotedUri, approvalUri, attempt }` (`enqueueQuoteRefetch`, `server/quote-retry-queue.ts`) when a quote of a remote status is left pending with its quoted note unresolved (a failed fetch, or past `MAX_QUOTE_DEPTH`, Mastodon's `RecursionLimitExceededError`) or with a stamp fetch that failed for now (5xx, network); the stamp is fetched in `syncRemoteQuoted` to tell that apart from a stamp that does not match, which queues nothing. The job (`processRefetchAndVerifyQuoteJob`, `server/quote-retry-jobs.ts`, Mastodon's `RefetchAndVerifyQuoteWorker`) is dropped when the quote is gone, no longer pending, names another quoted note, or its author is gone, suspended or outside the allow list; otherwise it runs the same verification under the same quote id (`reverifyRemoteQuote`), so a stamp that verifies accepts the quote, one that no longer resolves (4xx) rejects it, and a change is announced with `status.update` on the local timelines showing the note (`announceRemoteQuoteChange`, `server/remote-quote-announce.ts`, now shared with the inbox). A quote still left pending is queued again until four attempts (Sidekiq's `retry: 3` plus the first run); the delays come from `QuoteRetry` (`packages/domain/src/quote-retry.ts`): 30 to 600 seconds before the first (`perform_in(rand(30..600))`), then `ExponentialBackoff`'s `15 + 10n⁴ + rand(10n⁴)` seconds, sent with the queue's `delaySeconds`. Deviation: a quoted note that does not resolve is retried whatever the cause, since the fetch does not tell a 4xx from a 5xx at that layer; Mastodon retries only a raised (temporary) fetch error. As Mastodon's `schedule_refresh_if_stale!` from `Api::V1::StatusesController#show`, `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id` of a status whose accepted quote names a remote stamp and was last checked or changed over a week ago (`status_quotes.updated_at`, `BACKGROUND_REFRESH_INTERVAL`) claims it with a compare-and-set of `updated_at` (`findStaleQuoteRefresh`, `claimQuoteRefresh`; given back when the send fails) and queues `RefreshQuote { quoteId }` with a delay of up to six hours (`REFRESH_DEADLINE`). The job (`processRefreshQuoteJob`, Mastodon's `QuoteRefreshWorker`) fetches the stamp again: one that no longer resolves revokes the quote (`Quote.reject`), announced on the local note's streams (`announceNoteUpdate`, not federated) or the remote note's local timelines; a stamp that still resolves, does not match, or fails for now changes nothing, and the claim already marked it checked, as Mastodon's worker skips a quote it touched. A stamp on a suspended host or outside the allow list is not fetched. Our `QuoteRequest` needed nothing: it is a targeted outbox delivery whose `OutboxDeliveryWorkflow` retries a failed attempt with backoff (five attempts, 1 to 60 minutes apart), as Mastodon's `QuoteRequestWorker` is a `RawDistributionWorker` whose `DeliveryWorker` retries; neither asks again when no answer comes (Mastodon's `DeliveryWorker` retries longer, as for every outbox delivery). Tests: `server/quote-retries.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/quote-retry.test.ts`.
- No manual quote approval for local posts (#79, Q-3), confirmed as Mastodon 4.5. Its REST API takes `quote_approval_policy` `public` / `followers` / `nobody` only; `NoteSerializer#interaction_policy` advertises `automaticApproval` alone ("only automatic approval is supported" on outgoing posts, the author's own URI when nobody may quote); `ActivityPub::Activity::QuoteRequest#perform` answers at once, `Accept` when `StatusPolicy#quote?` allows and `Reject` otherwise; a local status renders `quote_approval.manual` as `[]`, so `current_user` is `manual` only for a remote note that advertised `manualApproval`; and there is no endpoint or UI to approve a pending quote. tstodon already did the same, so nothing changed beyond tests that pin it: a local note's `canQuote` carries no `manualApproval`, and a `QuoteRequest` outside a followers policy is rejected at once with no notification.
- Scheduled quotes and a remote quoting note's remote mentions (#79, Q-5, Q-14). `POST /api/v1/statuses` refused `quoted_status_id` with `scheduled_at`. As Mastodon 4.5, the quote is checked as for an immediate post and its id kept in the scheduled params (`quoted_status_id`), and the ScheduledStatus `params` echo `quoted_status_id` and `quote_approval_policy`. The publisher resolves it again through the create path's `resolveQuote` (moved to `server/quote-resolve.ts`), so a local quoted status is quoted per its policy and a remote one gets a `QuoteRequest`; a quoted status that is gone, no longer shown to the author or whose policy no longer lets them quote drops the post, as `PublishScheduledStatusWorker` drops one whose replay raises (`RecordNotFound` for a gone quoted status), and as a gone reply parent already did here; a failed read keeps it for the next run. Migration 0116 adds `remote_status_remote_mentions` (status, actor URI; no foreign key to `remote_actors`), written from a remote note's `Mention` tags off this server on `Create` and replaced on every `Update` (`replaceRemoteStatusRemoteMentions`); local accounts and their silent mentions stay in `remote_status_mentions`. `listStatusReachInboxes` reads it, so the stamp `Delete` of a remote quoting note reaches the remote actors it mentions, one per inbox with shared inboxes preferred. Mentioned actors are not fetched: only those cached by then are reached (Mastodon resolves them when the note arrives and reaches the accounts it has). Mastodon's `to` / `cc` silent mentions of known remote actors are not kept. Tests: `server/scheduled-quotes.test.ts`, `server/remote-update.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts`.
- Legacy, unresolved, inlined and nested quoted notes (#79, Q-12, Q-13). A remote note that named its quoted post only by `_misskey_quote`, `quoteUrl` or `quoteUri` kept no quote, so a `QuoteRequest` for it went unanswered; a quoted note that could not be fetched kept no quote; an inlined `quote` object or stamp `interactionTarget` was never imported; and quoted notes resolved one level deep. Migration 0114 rebuilds `status_quotes` (`-- void:allow-destructive`, as 0111) with `quoted_uri`, the quoted end when it is not resolved (exactly one of `quoted_status_id` / `quoted_remote_status_id` / `quoted_uri`, a remote quoting status only), and `legacy`. The domain `Quote` names a `QuotedTarget` (`LocalStatus` | `RemoteStatus` | `Unresolved{uri}`) and carries `legacy`. `syncRemoteQuote` (`server/remote-quote.ts`) stores a legacy quote as any other (the URI is the first of `quote`, `_misskey_quote`, `quoteUrl`, `quoteUri`, as `StatusParser#quote_uri`), with its state decided the same way (a legacy quote of a local status `Pending` until a `QuoteRequest` accepts it; of a remote status verified on its stamp, else `Pending`), and remote statuses render it only once accepted (`Quote.isShown`, Mastodon's `Quote#acceptable?`), so a `QuoteRequest` naming one is answered as for any quote. A quoted note that cannot be resolved keeps the quote `Unresolved` and `Pending` (`Rejected` when its stamp does not resolve, as `VerifyQuoteService`'s `reject! if @json.nil?`), rendered `{state: "pending", quoted_status: null}`; the note's next `Update` tries again and resolves it under the same quote id, then verifies it. Retrying sooner is `RefetchAndVerifyQuoteWorker`, under Q-4. An inlined `quote` object is stored without a fetch only when it is the quoting author's own note on the author's host (`persistVouchedQuoted` in `server/remote-status-persist.ts`, Mastodon's `safe_prefetched_embed`); any other inlined object, a `Tombstone` included, is fetched by its id, and one that does not resolve leaves the quote unresolved. When the quoted note does not resolve, a stamp fetched from its own URI that names this quote may inline the quoted note as `interactionTarget` (`stampQuotedObject`, `server/remote-quote-verify.ts`); it is stored only when it is the stamp author's own note on the stamp's host. Quoted notes are fetched up to three levels below the note that arrived (`MAX_QUOTE_DEPTH`, `PersistOptions.quoteDepth` replacing `fetchQuoted`), as Mastodon's `MAX_SYNCHRONOUS_DEPTH` of 2; a deeper note keeps an uncached quoted note unresolved and fetches no stamp for it. Tests: `server/remote-quotes-unresolved.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts`, `server/remote-quotes-remote.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/quote.test.ts`.
- Quote stamp reach and collection URIs (#79, Q-2, Q-5, Q-6). `remote_actors` now keeps the actor's `followers` and `following` URIs (migration 0114, `followers_uri` / `following_uri`), read from its document as Mastodon's `valid_collection_uri` (a URI, an object's `id`, or the first of a list, http(s)) and, stricter than Mastodon, only on the actor's own host. `QuoteInteractionPolicy.fromCanQuote` matches `canQuote` entries against them, as `quote_subpolicy` compares with `followers_url` / `following_url`, so a lookalike `/followers` path on the author's host is `unsupported_policy`; an actor cached before the migration (both NULL until its next fetch or `Update`) falls back to the path rule, which only affects how the policy renders. The stamp `Delete` now goes to the reach of both statuses as Mastodon's `RevokeQuoteService` (`quoteStampReach`, `server/status-reach.ts`, over `StatusReachFinder` with `unsafe: true`): the remote actors a local status mentions, those that boosted (`remote_announces`, `remote_boosts`) or favourited it, the authors of cached replies (`listStatusReachInboxes`), the replied-to remote author of a public or unlisted status, the remote followers of a local author whose status is not direct and of a local replied-to author, plus the remote quoting author, one activity per inbox, shared inboxes preferred. An inbound stamp `Delete` that carries a Linked Data `signature` is forwarded as received to the remote followers of the local accounts that boosted a public or unlisted quoting status and of the local author it replies to, minus the sender's inboxes, signed by that author or else the first booster (`forwardStampDelete`, Mastodon's `ActivityPub::Forwarder` in `Delete#revoke_quote`); nothing else in the inbox is forwarded yet. A remote `Update` that moves an accepted quote of a local status to another status, or drops it, sends the stamp `Delete` with that reach (`syncRemoteQuote`, `RevokeQuoteService` in `update_quote!`; Mastodon only destroys a dropped quote); a pending quote, or an edit that keeps the quote, sends nothing. Tests: `packages/domain/src/quote-approval-policy.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-policy.test.ts`, `server/quote-stamps.test.ts`, `server/remote-quotes.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts`.

- Quote follow-ups after Q-1 and Q-15 (#79, Q-7, Q-8, Q-9, Q-10, Q-11, Q-16). An inbound `Block` of a local account is stored in `remote_blocks` (`receiveRemoteBlock`) and lifted by `Undo(Block)`. A public or unlisted quote whose remote author blocked the viewer is `unauthorized` (`blocked_by` in `listQuoteFilterRelationsJson`, Mastodon's `author_blocking?`), the relationship reports `blocked_by`, and a notification from that actor is dropped. Accepting a `QuoteRequest` writes a silent mention of the quoted author (`ensureQuotedAccess`, `remote_status_mentions.silent`), so a followers-only or direct quoting note is shown to them and the `quote` notification is sent, without a `mention` notification; a later real mention of that account still notifies (`isActiveRemoteStatusMention`). An inbound `Delete` of a stamp revokes every pending or accepted quote that names it, local or remote (`revokeQuoteStamp`), and a local revoke publishes `status.update` for the quoting status (`publishQuoteRevoke`, Mastodon's `DistributionWorker`). A rejected or revoked local note keeps `quote` and drops `quoteAuthorization`. `GET /users/:username/quote_authorizations/:id` sends `Cache-Control: max-age=30, public` and answers 404 to a signed fetch from an actor, or a domain, the quoted author blocks. Tests: `server/quote-filter-states.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts`, `server/remote-quotes.test.ts`, `server/remote-quotes-remote.test.ts`, `server/quote-stamps.test.ts`, `server/quotes.test.ts`.

- Quote approval and filter states as Mastodon renders them (#79, Q-1 and Q-15). A local status's `quote_approval` answered `current_user: "unknown"` to an anonymous viewer and listed no audiences, and a quoted status was hidden only when the viewer could not see it by visibility. Local and remote statuses now share one rule: `QuoteInteractionPolicy.forViewer` (`packages/domain/src/quote-approval-policy.ts`, renamed from `RemoteQuotePolicy`, Mastodon's `quote_policy_for_account`) denies no viewer and a direct note, lets the author quote their own note (`viewerIsAuthor`), then reads the audiences. A local note's policy goes through `noteQuotePolicy` and `quoteInteractionPolicy` (`server/quote-policy-name.ts`): a note that is not public or unlisted is `nobody`, as `downgrade_quote_policy`, and `public` / `followers` render as `automatic: ["public"]` / `["followers"]`. Every `quote` (full and shallow, local and remote quoting statuses) now takes its accepted state from `quotedStatusAccess` (`server/quoted-status-access.ts`), Mastodon's `StatusFilter#filter_state_for_quote`: the quoted author sees it as is; a status the viewer may not see, or a public or unlisted one whose local author blocks the viewer, is `unauthorized` with no status; then the viewer's domain block, block and mute of the author give `blocked_domain`, `blocked_account` and `muted_account`, in that order, with the status still rendered (`quoted_status` / `quoted_status_id`), as `BaseQuoteSerializer`. The viewer's relations to every quoted author of a render are one statement (`listQuoteFilterRelationsJson`). A remote author's block of a local viewer is recorded later (Q-16). Tests: `server/local-quote-approval.test.ts`, `server/quote-filter-states.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/quote-approval-policy.test.ts`; `server/quote-visibility.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-policy.test.ts` and `server/quotes.test.ts` pass unchanged.

- Quote review fixes (#32). An accepted quote showed its quoted status to every viewer of the quoting one, so a public remote note self-quoting a followers-only note leaked it, even to anonymous viewers. Every `quote` (full, shallow, and so streams and notifications) now renders `unauthorized` with no status or id when the viewer may not see the quoted status (`viewableStatusIds` in `server/visibility-guard.ts`), as Mastodon's `StatusFilter#filter_state_for_quote`. A note fetched from one URI must name it as its id and sit on its author's host (`persistRemoteObject`), and `upsertRemoteStatus` never rewrites another actor's note, so a `QuoteRequest` instrument can no longer overwrite a neighbour's cached note. An accepted `QuoteRequest` notifies the quoted author only when they may see the quoting note (Q-11). A stamp `Delete` revokes the local quote among every quote naming that stamp, a `Reject` that changes nothing is not announced again, and a `QuoteRequest` reusing another quote's request id is dropped unanswered instead of failing the inbox. Tests: `server/quote-visibility.test.ts`, `server/remote-note-ownership.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts`, `server/remote-quotes.test.ts`.

- Quote policies on the wire, both ways (#32, FEP-044f commit 1 of 6). Mastodon 4.5 reads a note with no `interactionPolicy.canQuote` as quotable by nobody, so our notes were unquotable there, and remote notes rendered `quote_approval` as `{automatic:[],manual:[],current_user:"unknown"}` whatever their author allowed. `noteDocument` (`server/activitypub.ts`) now always emits `interactionPolicy.canQuote.automaticApproval` with the `gts:` context terms, as Mastodon's `NoteSerializer#interaction_policy`: `public` is the Public collection, `followers` the author's followers collection, and `nobody` the author's own actor URI. The policy is the status's stored one or the author's default, and a note that is not public or unlisted federates as `nobody` (Mastodon's `downgrade_quote_policy`); `loadNoteQuotePolicy` (`server/quote-policy-name.ts`) feeds all five `noteDocument` callers. Inbound, `noteFromUnknown` reads `canQuote` with `RemoteQuotePolicy.fromCanQuote` (`packages/domain/src/quote-approval-policy.ts`, Mastodon's `quote_subpolicy`): the Public collection is `public`, a URI on the author's host ending in `/followers` or `/following` is that collection, the author's own URI is dropped, anything else is `unsupported_policy`. It is stored in `remote_statuses.quote_policy_json` (migration 0110; NULL is no policy) and refreshed by every `Update`. `mastodonRemoteStatuses` renders `quote_approval.automatic` / `manual` from it and `current_user` with `RemoteQuotePolicy.forViewer` (Mastodon's `quote_policy_for_account`), reading in one batch which authors the viewer follows (`acceptedOutboundFollowActorUris`) and which follow the viewer (`remoteFollowerActorUris`, from `remote_follows`). An anonymous viewer and a direct note get `denied`, as Mastodon. What remains of #32 is listed under "Quote posts, FEP-044f (#32, follow-ups in #79)" above. Tests: `packages/domain/src/quote-approval-policy.test.ts`, `server/remote-quote-policy.test.ts`, `server/quotes.test.ts`.

- A quote names both ends as status targets (#32, FEP-044f commit 2 of 6). `status_quotes` could only say that a local status quotes a local status, so neither a local quote of a remote status nor a remote quote of a local one had a row to live in. Migration 0111 rebuilds the table (`-- void:allow-destructive`, as 0092) with the quoting status in exactly one of `status_id` / `remote_status_id` (both cascade) and the quoted one in exactly one of `quoted_status_id` / `quoted_remote_status_id` (no foreign key, as 0052, so a gone original still reports `deleted`), plus `approval_uri` (the remote quoted author's `QuoteAuthorization`, allowed only for a remote quoted status), `activity_uri` (the `QuoteRequest` id) and a `CHECK` on Mastodon's five states. Existing rows are copied as local-to-local. The domain `Quote` (`packages/domain/src/quote.ts`) holds both ends as `StatusTarget`s and the state as a `kind` union in which only `Accepted` carries its approval (`Implicit` or a `Stamp` URI); `accept`, `reject` and `revoke` are Mastodon's `accept_quote!`, `Quote#reject!` and the revoke route. `StatusQuoteTarget`'s `Quoted` names a `StatusTarget`. `server/quote-store.ts` reads and writes `Quote` values (`findQuoteByQuoting`, `findQuotesByQuoting`, `saveQuoteState`, `quoteCountsByQuoted`, `listQuotingTargets`); a row whose state is not one of the five is left out instead of being read as accepted. The revoke route now goes through `Quote.revoke` and `saveQuoteState`. `quotedStatusUri` became `noteQuote` (`server/quote-uri.ts`), and `noteDocument` takes `extras.quote` instead of `extras.quoteUri`. A pending quote also gets a `quote` URI, and a remote quoted status's `quote` is its object URI; no such rows exist yet, so the federated output is unchanged. Remote statuses now render `quotes_count` from the accepted local quotes of them. Tests: `packages/domain/src/quote.test.ts`, `server/quote-targets.test.ts` (each `CHECK` refuses a bad row; remote `quotes_count`); `server/quotes.test.ts` and `server/status-create-targets.test.ts` pass unchanged.

- Quote stamps for local statuses, withdrawn with `Delete` (#32, FEP-044f commit 3 of 6). Mastodon mints a `QuoteAuthorization` for every accepted quote of a local status (`approval_uri_for`), and a receiver checks a quote against it, so our accepted quotes carried no proof and a revoke never reached other servers. An accepted quote of a local status now names `${quoted author}/quote_authorizations/${quote id}` as `quoteAuthorization` in the quoting note (`noteQuote` in `server/quote-uri.ts`, the `Create` included; a `Stamp` approval names the remote stamp), with Mastodon's `quotes` context term. `GET /users/:username/quote_authorizations/:id` (`getUsersByUsernameQuoteAuthorizationsById` in `server/routes/activitypub.ts`) serves it as Mastodon's `QuoteAuthorizationSerializer` with the `quote_authorizations` context terms, and answers 404 unless the quote is accepted, the quoted status is local, public or unlisted and by `:username`, and the quoting status still exists. `server/quote-authorization-document.ts` holds the stamp and its `Delete` (`DeleteQuoteAuthorizationSerializer`: `id` is the stamp plus `#delete`, `to` the Public collection, the stamp inline). Revoking a quote sends that `Delete` signed by the quoted author. Mastodon's `RevokeQuoteService` delivers it to the `StatusReachFinder` inboxes of both statuses; tstodon sends it to the remote followers of each author whose status is not direct, as one activity (`enqueueActivityToInboxes` in `server/delivery.ts`). What remains of #32 is listed under "Quote posts, FEP-044f (#32, follow-ups in #79)" above. Tests: `server/quote-stamps.test.ts`; `server/quotes.test.ts` passes unchanged.

- Quotes between remote statuses (#32, FEP-044f commit 6 of 6). A remote note quoting another remote note kept no quote, so it rendered `quote: null` whatever the quoted author had allowed. `syncRemoteQuote` (`server/remote-quote.ts`) now stores it, as Mastodon's `update_quote!` and `VerifyQuoteService`. The quoted note is the cached one, or is fetched once as an announced object (`persistAnnouncedObject`: same id, author on the note's host, the author resolved first), through the SSRF-guarded `fetchActivityJson` and the inbox's allow and block lists (`remoteQuoteContext` in `server/remote-status-persist.ts`). A note stored that way does not fetch its own quoted note (`fetchQuoted: false`), so quotes resolve one level deep. A self-quote is `Accepted{Implicit}` without a fetch. Otherwise `verifyRemoteQuote` (`server/remote-quote-verify.ts`) fetches the `quoteAuthorization` and `stampAuthorizes` checks it: served under its own id, on the host of its `attributedTo`, typed `QuoteAuthorization`, `interactingObject` the quoting note, `interactionTarget` the quoted note, `attributedTo` the quoted author; it then is `Accepted{Stamp}`. As Mastodon, a stamp that does not match, no stamp, a stamp URI that is not http(s) or is ours, and a temporary fetch failure (5xx, network) leave the quote `Pending`; only a stamp that does not resolve (4xx, refused host; Mastodon's `reject! if @json.nil?`) makes it `Rejected`. An inlined `quoteAuthorization` is fetched by its id rather than trusted, since the quoting server is not its author; Mastodon reads the stamp URI the same way. An `Update` naming the same quoted note keeps a revoked quote, a self-quote, and a quote accepted on the stamp it still names, without fetching; any other state, or a changed stamp, is verified again under the same quote id; a changed quoted note starts over under a new id. A quoted note that cannot be resolved keeps no quote, since a row names its quoted status. The inline-stamp check of an `Accept` (`acceptedStamp`, `server/inbox/quote-answer.ts`) now uses `stampAuthorizes` too. Not checked: the stamp's JSON-LD context (Mastodon's `supported_context?`). What remains of #32 is listed under "Quote posts, FEP-044f (#32, follow-ups in #79)" above. Tests: `server/remote-quote-verify.test.ts`, `server/remote-quotes-remote.test.ts`; `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts` and `server/remote-quotes.test.ts` pass unchanged.

- Answering `QuoteRequest`s for local statuses (#32, FEP-044f commit 5 of 6). A remote server could not ask to quote a local status, and a remote note quoting one rendered `quote: null`. The inbox now takes `QuoteRequest` (`receiveQuoteRequest`, `server/inbox/quote-request.ts`), as Mastodon's `QuoteRequest#perform`. It is dropped without an answer when the request id is not on the signer's host, or `object` is not a public or unlisted local note (`distributable?`). The decision is `StatusPolicy#quote?`: the author is available and blocks neither the signer nor its domain, and the note's policy (`loadNoteQuotePolicy`: stored, else the account default) is `public`, or `followers` with an accepted `remote_follows` row of the signer; `nobody` refuses. On a refusal a `Reject` goes to the signer's inbox without fetching the quoting note; a quote already stored is rejected (revoked when it was accepted). On an approval the `instrument` is stored through `persistRemoteObject`: an embedded note on the signer's host as sent (`import_instrument`), anything else fetched, and only a note the signer wrote. Its quote must name this status (Mastodon's sanity check), or nothing is sent. The quote is then accepted with the request id as `activity_uri`, an `Accept` goes to the signer's inbox, the author gets a `quote` notification, and local timelines showing the note get `status.update`. A request for a quote already accepted is answered `Accept` again without a second notification; one for a quote the author revoked is answered `Reject`. The bodies are `AcceptQuoteRequestSerializer` and `RejectQuoteRequestSerializer` (`quoteRequestAnswerActivity` in `server/activitypub.ts`): ids `${author}#accepts/quote_requests/${quote id}` and `#rejects/...`, the `QuoteRequest` context term, the request echoed as `object` with the quoting note by URI as `instrument`, and the local stamp URI as `result`; as Mastodon, no `to`. A `Reject` with no stored quote is numbered by a fresh id, where Mastodon's is empty. Every stored remote note (inbox `Create` and `Update`, fetches, the instrument) reconciles its quote with `syncRemoteQuote` (`server/remote-quote.ts`), Mastodon's `update_quote!` for a quote of a local status: the quoted URI is the first of `quote`, `_misskey_quote`, `quoteUrl` and `quoteUri` (`noteQuoteRef`); a new quote of an existing local note is stored `Pending`; the same quoted status keeps its state, since our acceptance is authoritative and the note's `quoteAuthorization` is not read for a local quoted status; another quoted status starts over `Pending` under a new quote id; a note that drops the quote, or quotes a remote status, keeps none. Deviation: a legacy quote (no `quote` key) is not stored, where Mastodon stores it with `legacy` set and never shows it until accepted, so a `QuoteRequest` for a legacy-only quote goes unanswered; this spares a `legacy` column. Remote statuses now render `quote` through the same `BaseQuoteSerializer` states as local ones (`mastodonQuote`, `server/quote-document.ts`), the quoted local status rendered for the viewer; a status shown inside another's quote renders its own quote shallow (`{state, quoted_status_id}`, Mastodon's `ShallowQuoteSerializer`), so statuses quoting each other render once. `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id/quotes` takes a local or cached remote status the viewer may see (`StatusPolicy#show?`) and lists local and remote quoting statuses, each checked with `canViewStatus` or `canViewRemoteStatus`. Revoking a remote status's quote sends the stamp `Delete` to the quoted author's remote followers and to the quoting author's inbox. Remote quoting statuses get no `quoted_update`, since `listAcceptedQuoteEdges` joins local statuses only. What remains of #32 is listed under "Quote posts, FEP-044f (#32, follow-ups in #79)" above. Tests: `server/remote-quote-requests.test.ts`; `server/remote-quotes.test.ts`, `server/quotes.test.ts` and `server/quote-stamps.test.ts` pass unchanged.

- Quoting a remote status with a `QuoteRequest` (#32, FEP-044f commit 4 of 6). A local account could quote only local statuses. `POST /api/v1/statuses` with `quoted_status_id` now also takes a cached remote status the author may see (a local boost of one is unwrapped to it). As Mastodon's `Quote#validate_visibility`, only a public or unlisted remote status can be quoted (followers-only answers 422 `QuoteNotAllowed`, direct the existing 422); the remote author's `interactionPolicy.canQuote` read through `RemoteQuotePolicy.forViewer` decides who may ask, and `denied` answers the existing `quoteNotFound` 404, as `StatusPolicy#quote?`. `automatic`, `manual` and `unknown` all go ahead. A direct quote must mention the quoted author, as `safeguard_private_mention_quote!`. The quote is stored `Pending` with `activity_uri` `${actor}/quote_requests/${quote id}`; no `quote` notification is sent, since the quoted author is not local. After the `Create`, a `QuoteRequest` (`quoteRequestActivity` in `server/activitypub.ts`, Mastodon's `QuoteRequestSerializer` with `allow_post_inlining`: the `quote_requests` and `quotes` context terms, `object` the quoted note, the new note inline as `instrument`) goes once to the quoted author's personal inbox; Mastodon's `QuoteRequestWorker` retries, this does not. The note addresses the quoted author in `cc` (`NoteQuote.addressAuthor`, Mastodon's silent mention from `ensure_quoted_access`), without a `Mention` tag; the outbox expansion delivers to an actor named in `cc`, so the `Create` reaches the quoted author's inbox as well (asserted in the test), and a direct quote of a remote status federates even with no other remote mention. Inbound `Accept` and `Reject` first look up a quote by the request id (`receiveAnswer`, `server/inbox/answer.ts`) and only then fall back to the follow answer, as Mastodon's `Accept#perform`. `receiveQuoteAnswer` (`server/inbox/quote-answer.ts`) requires the quoted status to be the signer's and the quoting one a local note. An `Accept` of a pending quote needs `result` to be an https URI on the signer's host (an inline `result` must also be a `QuoteAuthorization` naming the signer, our note and the quoted note); the stamp is not fetched, as Mastodon. It stores `Accepted{Stamp}` and re-announces the note through `announceNoteUpdate`, so the `Update` carries `quoteAuthorization` and the author's streams and timelines get `status.update`. A `Reject` runs `Quote.reject` (pending to rejected, accepted to revoked) and federates nothing, as Mastodon; the streams still get `status.update`. A remote `Delete` of an object that is not local first looks for a pending or accepted quote with that `approval_uri` whose quoted author is the signer (`revokeQuoteStamp`): it is revoked and the note stays; otherwise the `Delete` removes a remote status as before. The local renderer shows a remote quoted status through `mastodonRemoteStatuses` (the same `findRemoteStatusesByIds` read as remote boost targets), `pending`, `rejected` and `revoked` with `quoted_status: null`, `deleted` when the quoted row is gone, and `unauthorized` when its author is not available. What remains of #32 is listed under "Quote posts, FEP-044f (#32, follow-ups in #79)" above. Tests: `server/remote-quotes.test.ts`; `server/outbound-follow.test.ts`, `server/outbound-follow-answer.test.ts`, `server/quotes.test.ts` and `server/quote-stamps.test.ts` pass unchanged.

- Grouped notification pages pick their groups in SQL (#75, #76). `GET /api/v2/notifications` folded one window of `limit * 4` rows (at most 200) in TypeScript. A long run of one group, such as 1000 favourites of one post, filled the window, so the web app, which fills the next gap from the last group's `page_max_id`, moved down one row per request; a `min_id` page stopped on a window of unavailable senders and answered empty with no `prev` link; and a group's `notifications_count` stopped at the window (160 in the list, 300 by key). As Mastodon's `paginate_groups`, `pageNotificationGroupsOlder` / `pageNotificationGroupsNewer` (`prisma/sql`) now walk the account's notifications with a recursive CTE, each step seeking the next notification whose group key is not met yet through `idx_notifications_account_id` (migration 0109), so a page costs one statement however long a group runs. Sender availability (`availableAccountIds`: a suspended local account; a suspended remote actor, a suspended domain on either host, or a host off the allow list) is a SQL condition there, so hidden senders never end a page. As `load_groups_data`, `summarizeNotificationGroups` then reads the page's groups in one statement: members found by id, by status (`idx_notifications_account_status`, migration 0109) or by sender, read once, with count, newest notification and three newest distinct senders up to the range's upper end and `page_min_id` from its lower end. `NotificationGroup.pageRange` is Mastodon's `load_grouped_notifications` range: oldest to newest representative, left open on the far side of a page with fewer than `limit` groups. Show (`/api/v2/notifications/:group_key`) is the same statement with one key and an open range, so the list and show agree on counts, and it no longer scans the account three times. Dismiss (`dismissNotificationGroup`) finds its rows by id, status or sender instead of scanning the account. `server/notification-group-plans.test.ts` fails if any of these statements reads the whole account. `Link` takes the oldest and newest representatives. This removes these deviations recorded under #59: counts covering only the row window, a short page spanning its window, the five-window bound, and show's 40-row sample. The envelope now carries the sample accounts only, as Mastodon. Still a deviation: the sample is three senders where Mastodon takes eight. Tests: `server/grouped-notifications.test.ts`, `server/account-unavailable.test.ts`, `server/notification-group-plans.test.ts`, `packages/domain/src/notification-group.test.ts`.

- Hashtag search finds stored tags by prefix (#74). `GET /api/v2/search` (and v1's `/api/v1/search`) with `type=hashtags` or no `type` answered only the queried tag itself, and only when `q` started with `#`, so it never found other tags and offered tags nobody had used. The `tags` table (migration 0108) now holds the tag names this server knows, as Mastodon's `tags`: a public or unlisted local note's tags (`extractHashtags`) are stored in the note's insert batch and in its edit's update batch, a public or unlisted remote note's in the batch that indexes its tags (`replaceRemoteStatusTags`, so the remote backfill cron also fills them), and a followed or featured tag in the follow's batch, as `Tag.find_or_create_by_names`; names are never removed, and no batch carries a tag statement without names. A direct or followers-only note's names are never stored (`searchableTagNames`, `server/hashtags.ts`), since search shows every name to anyone, anonymous included: before, a DM's tag or URL fragment (`https://app.example/invite#Kq8sZ2pX` stored `kq8sz2px`) became a name anyone could list. Both extractors take Mastodon's `HASHTAG_RE` lookbehind, so a `#` after a letter, digit, `=`, `/` or `)` starts no tag (`word#token`, a URL fragment); this also changes what trends, tag history and featured-tag suggestions count. A remote note's tags are read from its HTML text, where a link counts only when its text is a hashtag (`htmlHashtags`), so neither an `href` nor a URL Mastodon splits across spans yields one; `remote_status_tags` (the remote tag timelines) is indexed the same way. The migration backfills names with `INSERT OR IGNORE ... SELECT DISTINCT` from `remote_status_tags` (public and unlisted statuses only), `followed_tags` and `featured_tags`; local notes are backfilled by the `BackfillLocalNoteTags` queue job (`server/local-tag-backfill.ts`), queued by the hourly cron: it reads 200 local notes after the `local_note_tags` row of `backfill_cursors`, stores the names of the public and unlisted ones, and moves the cursor in the same batch only from where the page started (so of two runs on one page, one advances and chains, the other stops), chaining while pages are full; a short page completes the cursor, after which each cron run is one read. It covers notes the old worker wrote during the deploy. Nothing is deleted. Search (`searchTags` in `server/tag-store.ts`, SQL `searchTags`) is Mastodon's `Tag.search_for`: the term normalized as `HashtagNormalizer` (NFKC, lowercase, characters a hashtag cannot hold dropped, so `#Cat` is `cat`), names starting with it as a primary-key range, ordered by length and then name, paged by `limit` and `offset`; `offset` still needs sign-in (#72). Each result is a Mastodon tag document with its week of history and the viewer's `following` / `featuring` (read only when there are results); a page costs at most four D1 statements. Deviations from Mastodon: Mastodon stores the tags of notes of every visibility (from the ActivityPub `tag` array for remote notes) and hides names with its `listable` flag; this server stores only the names of public and unlisted notes and has no `listable` flag, and reads remote tags from the HTML because `remote_statuses` keeps no `tag` array. Names are lowercase ASCII (`[a-z0-9_]`), as the tag timelines already read them, so there is no first-seen `display_name` casing and a non-ASCII term matches nothing (no ASCII folding). As Mastodon, a name needs a letter (`#2024` is no tag), and a `#` after `&` starts none, so an undecoded numeric entity (`&#8217;`) in a remote note is no tag. A term with no tag characters (`#`) finds nothing, where Mastodon lists every tag. `exclude_unreviewed` is not read; names of equal length sort by SQLite's binary order, not PostgreSQL's collation; anonymous results still carry `following: false` and `featuring: false`, as every tag document here. Left as is: `remote_status_tags` rows indexed before this change keep the names the looser HTML reading found, including `href` fragments of public notes, and the migration copies those of public and unlisted statuses; local tag timelines still match `content_text` with `LIKE`, so `word#tag` still lists there. Tests: `server/tag-search.test.ts`, `server/tag-remote-timeline.test.ts`, `server/local-tag-backfill.test.ts`, `server/status-extract.test.ts`.

- `Link` header on the grouped notification list (#59). `GET /api/v2/notifications` now sends Mastodon's `rel="next"` (`max_id=` the oldest end of the page range) and `rel="prev"` (`min_id=` its newest end), keeping the other query parameters, as `Api::V2::NotificationsController#next_path` / `#prev_path`; an empty page sends no header. The cursors go through `setLinkHeader` (`server/http.ts`), which the v1 `setPaginationLinks` now calls. `NotificationGroup.fold` returns the groups with the page range (`oldestId` / `newestId`). A full page (`limit` groups) ends at the last group met, as Mastodon's pagination range: before, the range took the oldest notification of every group on the page, so paging from `page_min_id` (the web app) or `max_id` skipped groups whose notifications sat between. As Mastodon's `load_groups_data`, only `page_min_id` is bounded by that end; counts, samples and the most recent notification cover the group in the window up to the range's newest end.
