A `min_id` page now takes the groups next to `min_id` and lists them newest first with each group's newest notification as `most_recent_notification_id`, as `to_a_grouped_paginated_by_id`; before, it listed them oldest first with the oldest notification as most recent. `GET /api/v2/notifications/:group_key`, `/accounts` and `/dismiss` read the group by key (`NotificationGroup.target`), as Mastodon's `by_group_key`; before, they looked the group up in the request's page, so `limit` and the window cut them to part of the group. Every read by key is bounded: show counts in SQL (`summarizeNotificationGroup`), takes its sample from the 40 newest notifications and carries only the sample accounts; `/accounts` pages by notification id (40 by default, capped at 80, `Link` header); dismiss is one `INSERT ... SELECT` (`dismissNotificationGroup`), so a group past D1's per-invocation query limit (50 on the Free plan) is dismissed whole, a muted sender's notifications no longer come back after an unmute, and it answers 200 when nothing matched. As Mastodon gives held notifications no group key, a `{type}-{target}` key leaves them alone, and the list (`include_filtered=true`) keys each one `ungrouped-{id}`, the key that dismisses it. A read by key takes any notification type, whatever the request's `grouped_types`. Windows of hidden senders read past (#48) do not move the range. Deviations from Mastodon: the link keeps every query parameter except the cursors (Mastodon keeps `limit`, `types`, `exclude_types`, `grouped_types`, `include_filtered`; this server also filters the grouped list by `account_id`, so it carries over); counts cover the row window (at most 200 rows), not the whole group; a page with fewer than `limit` groups spans its window, so its `next` / `prev` are the window's oldest and newest notification where Mastodon takes the last and first group met; an empty page past the five-window bound still offers `next` from the last row read; show's count leaves out blocked and notification-muted senders (Mastodon stores none), and its sample is up to three distinct available senders where Mastodon takes the eight newest; show answers 404 when none of the 40 newest notifications has an available sender; `/accounts` also takes `min_id`, which Mastodon ignores.

- Browser e2e server start-up (#53). `pnpm e2e:web` could fail before any test ran: the web server had 180 s and took 88 to 145 s at load average 5 to 9, and more under heavier load. `e2e/web/serve.mjs` applied all 106 migrations on every run (36 to 89 s, one Wrangler step per migration) and deleted `.wrangler/e2e-web`, Vite's dependency cache included, so Vite also bundled the Worker's dependencies again and reloaded the Worker two or three times (about 45 s). `e2e/web/database.mjs` now migrates once per set of migrations and Wrangler version into `.cache/e2e-web/d1/<digest>`, and `pnpm e2e:web` runs it before Playwright. `serve.mjs` copies that database into `.wrangler/e2e-web/v3` and keeps Vite's cache. The server now answers in 17 to 33 s with a warm Vite cache (load average 6 to 11) and 45 to 86 s with a cold one (load average 8 to 16). The start-up budget went from 180 s to 150 s, since it no longer covers the migrations. `pnpm web:build` is not part of the e2e run, but it ran Yarn's install and Mastodon's build on every call (26 to 75 s); it now returns at once when `.cache/mastodon-web/assets.stamp` matches a digest of the ref, the script and `packs/tstodon-web.json`, and every file that manifest names, `emoji/`, `sounds/` and `sw.js` exist, which also shortens `pnpm run deploy`. The single `read ECONNRESET` on the local explorer KV lookup (`confirmationLink` in `e2e/web/fixtures.ts`) seen during #34 did not recur: 70 reads spaced around Node's 5 s keep-alive timeout at load average 13 to 16, and 305 reads every 200 ms across a cold start with its Worker reload at load average 11 to 14, all succeeded, so nothing was changed for it. With a warm cache no Worker reload happens during the run.

- Worker test timeouts on a busy machine (#50). Measured per call inside workerd (timers around `SELF.fetch`, the fixtures, `crypto.subtle.generateKey` and D1 statements): 75 to 85 % of each slow case is the API calls its scenario makes, not the fixtures. `POST /api/v1/statuses` runs about 52 D1 statements (it renders the status twice, once for the author's stream and once for the response) and took 100 to 400 ms; a boost took about 350 ms and a timeline page 50 to 200 ms. A new account's RSA key took 80 to 200 ms (10 to 20 % of a case); `authHeaders` for an existing account took under 15 ms, and none of these files signs up through the passkey browser fixture. So: the twenty-id cap case in `multiple-lookup.test.ts` stores its 21 statuses in one D1 batch instead of posting them (2.7 to 10 s down to 0.2 to 0.6 s); the two longest `list-rules.test.ts` cases are split into a local and a remote case each (the remote halves take 1 to 2.5 s; the local halves still cost 2.5 to 9.6 s because each posts through the API, so the split saves only 1 to 2 s and the per-file budget below is what stops the timeouts); and `list-rules`, `status-pins`, `status-engagement-accounts` and `account-domain-block` set `vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 45_000 })`: each of them timed out at 15 s in full CI runs under load, where single requests stall for seconds (a 3.5 s case once took 15.5 s). The global 15 s budget and the no-retry rule stay. Not done: rendering a new status once in `POST /api/v1/statuses` would drop about a third of its D1 statements, in production too; it changes `publishLocalNote`'s contract and is left for its own issue.

- A local poll's `Update(Question)` reaches its remote voters and boosters (#51). The expansion (`ExpandFollowers` in `server/outbox-job.ts`) read only the activity's addressing, followers and mentions, so a remote voter or booster that does not follow the author never got the new or final counts. `outboundAudience` now also reports the `Question` an `Update` carries (`updatedQuestion`). When that names a local status, the expansion adds the inboxes of the status's remote voters (`remote_poll_votes`) and boosters (`remote_announces`), read by `listPollInteractorInboxes` (`server/remote-interaction-store.ts`), once per shared inbox, as Mastodon's `DistributePollUpdateWorker`. Voters and boosters count only for a public or unlisted status: a followers-only or direct poll reaches only its addressing, so a voter who later lost access, or who voted before an edit narrowed the audience, hears nothing (Mastodon's poll worker still reaches such voters with counts). Boosters follow Mastodon's `StatusReachFinder`, and the inbox (`server/inbox/announce.ts`) now drops a remote Announce of a followers-only or direct local status without storing, counting or notifying it, as Mastodon's `announceable?`; before, a stranger who announced a private poll received every later `Update` with its content. Voters and boosters an admin suspended (`remote_actor_moderation`) or the author blocks are left out, as Mastodon's `Account.without_suspended`; Mastodon's poll worker does not screen the author's blocks, tstodon does. Suspended domains and the allow list still apply, and a failed recipient lookup is logged (`OutboxExpandLookupFailed`). Deviation: an edit of a local poll status also sends an `Update(Question)`, so it now reaches the poll's voters too, where Mastodon's `StatusReachFinder` sends an edit to boosters but not voters. Tests: `server/poll-expiry.test.ts`.
- Better Auth endpoints tstodon does not call are closed (#55). #31 closed the session revoke endpoints; the rest were still open to any session cookie, stale or stolen, because Better Auth checks only that a session exists (`/unlink-account` asks for one younger than a day, Better Auth's `freshAge`), and the admin plugin's endpoints only that its user is an admin. Through them a cookie could rename the user (`/update-user`), rename passkeys (`/passkey/update-passkey`), unlink accounts, read every session token of the user (`/list-sessions`), and, as an admin, end, ban, rename, remove, impersonate, or set the password or role of any user, or create a user without a confirmed sign-up link (`/admin/*`). The `before` hook in `app/auth.ts` (`securityPageGuards`) now refuses every endpoint outside `OPEN_ENDPOINTS` with 403: `/get-session` (Void's session lookup), `/sign-out` (the sign-in page's button), and the four passkey sign-in and registration endpoints. Nothing in tstodon called the others: the Password and security page and the admin pages work on D1 directly, the Mastodon web app signs out through `DELETE /auth/sign_out`. An endpoint a Better Auth upgrade or a new plugin adds is closed until it joins the list. Roles change in D1 (`docs/reference/configuration.md`). `/update-session` changed nothing even before, since no session field takes input, and is closed with the rest. Tests: `server/better-auth-endpoints.test.ts` asserts each endpoint answers 403 and that the user, session, passkey, and account rows are unchanged.
- Status and hashtag search page by `offset`; anonymous `resolve=true` answers 401 (#72, #73). `searchStatuses` (`server/status-timeline-store.ts`) takes the viewer and `offset`, and keeps in SQL, before `OFFSET`, only the notes the viewer may see: public and unlisted ones, the viewer's own, followers-only ones of accounts the viewer follows, and direct ones that mention the viewer, without blocked or muted authors. A page is one statement. Before, every `offset` answered the first page, and search served followers-only and direct notes, and blocked or muted authors' notes, to anyone (anonymous callers included). The hashtag result is still only the queried tag (no tag rows, see D1-3), so it comes on the first page only. As Mastodon's `Api::V2::SearchController#remote_resolve_error`, `GET /api/v2/search` (and v1's `/api/v1/search`) answer `resolve=true` without sign-in with 401 "Search queries that resolve remote resources are not supported without authentication" before any WebFinger or actor fetch; `/api/v1/accounts/search` already required sign-in. Deviations from Mastodon: Mastodon searches statuses only with Elasticsearch and only for a signed-in viewer, and then only the ones the viewer wrote, was mentioned in, favourited, boosted, bookmarked or voted on, plus public ones by `indexable` accounts (`Status::SearchConcern`); this server searches the text of every local note the viewer may see, for anonymous callers too; a direct reply the viewer may open only through its thread is not found; and `resolve` is truthy only for `true` or `1`, where Mastodon also takes values such as `yes`.

- Remote actors carry the account domain WebFinger confirms (#71). Every fetched actor document is cached through `cacheFetchedActor` (`server/remote-actor-fetch.ts`), which runs Mastodon's `check_webfinger!` round trip: WebFinger for `preferredUsername@<URI authority>` names a `subject`; a subject on the URI authority must link back to the actor, and a subject on another domain is taken only when WebFinger on that domain answers for the same account with the same actor URI as its `self` link. So `@alice@example.com` served from `https://social.example.com/users/alice` is stored as `alice@example.com`, while an actor still cannot claim a domain it does not control. The outcome is a `WebfingerConfirmation` (`packages/domain/src/webfinger-confirmation.ts`): `Confirmed` stores the handle, stamps `last_webfingered_at` and moves any other row cached under that handle back to its own URI authority (`demoteRemoteActorHandle`), the way Mastodon hands a verified handle's account to the new URI; `Mismatch` (an answer for another actor, a subject that does not loop back, or a 404 or 410, so a domain can stop vouching) stores the URI authority; `Unavailable` keeps the handle and its stamp as the row holds them at write time (`upsertRemoteActorKeepingHandle`, so a confirmation another actor made during the round trip stands), or the URI authority for an actor not cached yet. A failed lookup (a timeout, a 5xx, a redirect it does not follow, another 4xx, an unreadable answer, no `self`) is `Unavailable` only when it comes from the stored handle's own domain; when the URI host's lookups fail and the stored handle is on another domain, that handle is looked up at its own domain, as Mastodon's `ResolveAccountService` does, and kept only when that answer names it and links to the actor, so a former holder cannot keep a domain by failing its own WebFinger (or, on the cron, its whole server); `Refused` refuses the actor when the claimed account domain is suspended or off the allow list, as Mastodon's `domain_not_allowed?` (the inbox answers 403). A handle lookup's own WebFinger answer stands in only for the step it repeats (the first when the asked handle is on the URI authority, else the second), so a third server answering WebFinger with another server's actor as `self` cannot rename that actor. `findRemoteActorByHandle` prefers the row confirmed last, then the latest fetch, then the URI. An actor's own `Update` applies the document under its stored handle without WebFinger, as Mastodon v4.5.8's `Update` passes the account's username and domain to `ProcessAccountService`, which skips `check_webfinger!`; unlike Mastodon, the Update does not advance `last_webfingered_at`. A trailing dot names the same domain (`Hostname`, `parseAccountHandle`). Every domain rule matches both hosts (see [Remote Actor Domains](../architecture/tstodon-architecture.md#remote-actor-domains)): suspension, silence, `reject_media`, `reject_reports`, the allow list and a user's domain block apply when they name the account domain or the actor URI's host (`RemoteActor.hostnames` / `onAnyHost`; SQL `host` or the generated `uri_host`, migration 0107) in the inbox and authorized fetch, the timelines and thread context, account presentation and search, media rejection and its clean-up, report intake, suspension's and a user block's severance, notification screening and acceptances, follows both ways, poll votes, mentions, the backfills. A user's block thus matches more than Mastodon's `account.domain`, and a block made on an actor's URI host before its account domain was confirmed keeps working; this replaces #70's account-domain-only rule. Actor fetch refuses an id whose authority is not printable ASCII (so Unicode, non-punycode hosts are refused: SQLite's `lower` folds ASCII only, and the URL parser reads the Kelvin sign as `k`) or whose text reads otherwise than its parsed host and port. Rows cached before the round trip get the confirmed domain on their next re-fetch, and the hourly cron (only the `HOURLY_CRON` entry, `17 * * * *`, which the cron file's literal is type-checked against) re-fetches every actor whose last confirmation (its fetch, when never confirmed) and last refresh attempt are both over a day old, longest waiting first through the `idx_remote_actors_refresh_due` expression index, within a run budget of 200 actors, none started after 45 s, four at a time (under 2600 fetches a run since #77, up to 4800 actors a day); each is claimed in `refresh_attempted_at` just before it is fetched (`claimRemoteActorRefresh`), so an unreachable server waits a day instead of holding the head of the queue (`refreshStaleRemoteActors`, `server/remote-actor-refresh.ts`). A handle is also re-confirmed on use, as Mastodon's `possibly_stale?`: a mention or direct message, a `resolve=true` search or lookup, a CSV import and an alias or move resolve it through `resolveRemoteActorByHandle`, which uses a cached holder until it is due, then claims it and asks the handle's own WebFinger (at most seventeen subrequests per use since #77, and once a day for an unreachable actor). Follows, list memberships, blocks, mutes and notes stay with the actor URI when a handle moves to another actor; Mastodon keys an account by handle and hands it, with its relationships, to the new URI. Every other actor fetch runs the round trip (one WebFinger request, and at least three for a split domain that redirects (the URI host, the account domain and its redirect target)), as Mastodon v4.5.8's `FetchRemoteActorService` does unless `only_key`. Not done: each WebFinger answer is trusted for one hop only, so a host may still make this server look up one other domain per fetch; and `remote_actors` still has no unique index on the handle. Tests: `server/remote-actor-webfinger.test.ts`, `server/account-search.test.ts`, `server/remote-notifications.test.ts`.
- WebFinger follows redirects (#77). `fetchWebfinger` (`server/federated-fetch.ts`) refused every redirect, so a split-domain server (Mastodon's `LOCAL_DOMAIN` with `WEB_DOMAIN`, whose `example.com/.well-known/webfinger` redirects to `social.example.com`) read as a failed lookup and its actors kept their URI host. It now follows up to three redirects, as Mastodon v4.5.8's `Webfinger` through `Request` (`follow: { max_hops: 3 }`). Every hop passes `isFederatedHostRefused` and the `webfingerScheme` rule (HTTPS, or HTTP for a loopback host on `FEDERATION_ALLOW_HOSTS`), a URL already fetched is not fetched again, and one 5 s deadline covers the chain. A fourth redirect, a loop, a refused host or plain HTTP fails the lookup like a 5xx, so the round trip is `Unavailable` there (the stored handle stays only where #71 keeps it, and a first-seen actor takes its URI host). Only the final answer's `subject` and `self` count, compared with the handle first asked, never with the host a redirect points to. A lookup costs up to four requests: an actor refresh up to thirteen, a handle use up to seventeen, a cron run under 2600 fetches (the Workers Paid default is 10,000 subrequests; the 1000 D1 queries per invocation still bind first). Unlike Mastodon, a redirect to plain HTTP is refused, the 5 s deadline is for the whole chain rather than per request, and the `host-meta` LRDD fallback after a 404 is not tried (a 404 stays the domain's definite answer, which #71 relies on to release a handle). Tests: `server/remote-actor-webfinger.test.ts`.

- The federated fetch host guard reads hosts as a URL does; mention lookups are capped; an unknown actor's signature is checked before its round trip (#78). `isBlockedFederatedHost` (`server/federated-fetch.ts`) compared names, so `127.0.0.2`, `100.64.0.1`, `2130706433`, `0x7f.1`, IPv6 literals such as `[::ffff:7f00:1]`, `localhost.` and `metadata.google.internal` passed it. It now lower-cases the host, drops trailing dots and lets the URL parser write IPv4 in dotted decimal, then refuses 0/8, 10/8, 100.64/10, 127/8, 169.254/16, 172.16/12, 192.168/16 and 224/3, every IPv6 literal, `localhost`, `.internal` and `.local` names and this instance's own host (its port ignored), unless `FEDERATION_ALLOW_HOSTS` names the host. A name is then resolved over Cloudflare DNS-over-HTTPS (`server/remote-dns.ts`, ported from cfwdon's `url_guard.rs`; classification in `RemoteDnsPolicy`, `packages/domain/src/remote-dns-policy.ts`) before every `fetchActivityJson` and WebFinger hop and a link-card fetch, and is refused when any A/AAAA answer is loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT, multicast or unique-local (an IPv4-mapped IPv6 answer counts as its IPv4). It fails closed: a failed lookup is `VerificationUnavailable` (a link card is `Transient`) and an empty answer is refused. Verdicts are cached 60 s per isolate; there is no KV cache because the Worker has no KV binding. IP literals and `FEDERATION_ALLOW_HOSTS` hosts skip DNS. Not done: this is best-effort, not IP pinning, since `fetch` resolves the name again (Mastodon checks the resolved address at the socket); a link card's redirects are followed by hand (at most 5 hops) and every hop is checked like the first host, and a Web Push endpoint is checked, with the public-host rule and no allow list, both when `POST /api/v1/push/subscription` (or `/api/web/push_subscriptions`) stores it and when `push-delivery.ts` delivers (a private resolution drops the subscription, a failed lookup retries). `resolveRemoteMentions` (`server/mention-resolve.ts`) lets at most 20 handles per post go to the network (`mayLookUp` on `resolveRemoteActorByHandle`); the rest resolve from the cache alone, a cached holder even when due. Mastodon has no such cap. `resolveRemoteActor` takes `checkSignature`, which the inbox and signed GETs pass, so an actor fetched because it was not cached is checked against the request's signature before its WebFinger round trip and caching; a forged request reaches only the key URL. Mastodon runs the round trip first. The inbox is rate limited per address and per sending server (`inbox_ip`, `inbox_domain`; see the Rate limits entry below). Tests: `server/federated-fetch.test.ts`, `server/remote-dns.test.ts`, `server/remote-mentions.test.ts`, `server/remote-actor-webfinger.test.ts`.

- A user's domain block matches an actor's account domain everywhere (#70). Notification screening (`screenSender` in `server/notify.ts`) and the relationship's `domain_blocking` flag (`relationshipFlags` in `server/relationship-store.ts`) took the domain from the host of the actor URI, while the block's severance, its notification cleanup and the timelines match `remote_actors.host`, the hostname of the account's WebFinger domain. For an actor whose URI host differs from its account domain, such as a WebFinger domain `split-host.example` serving actors from `social.split-host.example`, a block on the account domain screened nothing and a block on the URI host screened what it did not clean up. Both now ask `isActorDomainBlocked` (`server/domain-block-store.ts`, SQL `findActorDomainBlock`), which compares the block with the actor's `remote_actors.host`, as Mastodon matches `account.domain`. Every other user domain-block check already took `Hostname.ofAuthority(actor.domain)`. An actor that is not cached has no account domain and is not screened by domain; a notification sender has always been cached by the inbox that verified it. Instance-level suspension and federation checks on inbox hosts are unchanged. Tests: `server/remote-notifications.test.ts`.

- Account search pages across local and remote results (#47). `searchAccountsPage` (`server/account-search.ts`) pages the non-exact results of `GET /api/v1/accounts/search` and of `GET /api/v2/search` (`type` given and not blank): the matching local accounts, then the matching cached remote actors, with `offset` counting only the accounts search lists. That is what Mastodon's web app sends as `offset`: the number of results it already holds. As Mastodon's search SQL, the queries leave out what the presenter hides (`searchAccounts`: pending or suspended accounts, and `following=true` in SQL; `searchRemoteActors`: suspended actors, suspended domains by `Hostname.ofAuthority`, and domains the allow list does not admit), so `OFFSET` counts listed rows and a page costs a fixed handful of D1 statements however many hidden rows match; a test pins the queries to `searchableLocalAccounts` / `searchableRemoteActors`. Before, v1's remote results ignored `offset` (page 2 repeated page 1's remote actors) and a page with hidden accounts came back short; v2 ignored `offset` and returned up to `limit` local accounts plus up to `limit` remote actors. As Mastodon's `SearchService` and `Api::V2::SearchController`, v2 answers 401 to `offset` without sign-in, and the `resolve=true` account joins only the first page, first and inside `limit` (v1's exact match counts inside `limit` too).

- A scheduled post's `Idempotency-Key` replays its ScheduledStatus (#69). `postV1Statuses` looked up a key's stored id with `findStatusById`, but a scheduled post stores a `scheduled_statuses` id, so a retry with the key answered 404. The key's row now records what it points at in `status_idempotency.kind` (migration 0106): `Pending` while the first request runs, then `Status` or `ScheduledStatus`; the migration labels existing rows from `status_id`. A retry renders that record again: a status through `mastodonStatus`, a scheduled status through `scheduledStatusDocument`, as Mastodon's `idempotency_duplicate` returns the ScheduledStatus. Mastodon picks the table from the retry's own `scheduled_at`; tstodon uses what the first request stored, so a retry that drops or adds `scheduled_at` still answers the first result. A scheduled status already published or deleted within the hour answers 404, as before. Test: `server/status-idempotency.test.ts`.

- Unavailable recipients and `blocked_mention?` (#66). `screenSender` (`server/notify.ts`) now opens with Mastodon's `@recipient.unavailable?`: a recipient whose `AccountPresentation` is not available (suspended) is notified of nothing, its own `poll` and the other self kinds included, before the self and staff checks. A mention that is not from staff is also dropped when the recipient blocks, or mutes notifications of, an account its post mentions or the author of the post it replies to (`FeedManager#filter_from_mentions?`). Each mention site loads that post context once (`MentionedPost` in `packages/domain/src/notification-store.ts`, built by `mentionedPost` and, for remote notes, `remoteMentionedPost` with `findThreadParent`) and passes it to `notifyAccount` / `notifyFromRemote`. The one query `silencesAnyOf` (`server/block-mute-store.ts`) checks every involved account per recipient. Mastodon v4.5's `filter_from_mentions?` has no silenced-sender check; a limited sender the recipient does not follow is the `for_limited_accounts` policy's call, as here. A remote note's involved accounts are its `Mention` tag hrefs and the local accounts it addresses; a reply parent this server has not stored is not checked, as Mastodon's unfetched `in_reply_to_account`.

- A domain block clears the domain's notifications and withdraws requests to it (#67). After #60, blocking a domain kept the blocker's existing notifications from the domain's actors in the list, kept the senders there whose notification requests it had accepted, and kept its own pending follow requests to the domain, so a later `Accept` made a follow the block forbids. Each `SeverBlockedDomainFollows` page now also covers the domain's actors that sent the account a notification (`listDomainPeers` in `server/severed-relationship-store.ts`) and deletes the account's notifications from the page's actors in the same batch as its follows (`severAccountPage`), as Mastodon's `clear_notifications!`; their held requests go with them by cascade. The first page forgets the account's accepted senders on the domain (`forgetDomainNotificationAcceptances` in `server/notification-policy-store.ts`), as `clear_notification_permissions!`. Deviation from Mastodon: the blocker's own pending requests to the domain are withdrawn with the page, with an `Undo(Follow)` and no severance record, where Mastodon keeps them. Withdrawing was chosen over refusing an inbound `Accept` from a blocked domain: it reuses the page's removal and its `Undo`, tells the remote server to drop the request, and leaves no request the user sees as pending but can never complete; a later `Accept` matches no follow, and a new follow of the domain is already refused (`DomainBlocked`). An `Accept` that lands between the block and the queued page that reaches its actor still makes the follow, which that page then severs and records. Not done: the acceptance sweep reads all of the account's accepted senders in one query, not in pages, since they are added only by the user accepting requests; dismissal rows of the deleted notifications stay, unread. Tests: `server/account-domain-block.test.ts`.

- Create-path targets are checked before anything is written (#68, #65). `media_ids` on `POST /api/v1/statuses`, a scheduled post and `PUT /api/v1/statuses/:id` may name only the author's own media that no status holds (on an edit, also media already on the edited status), as Mastodon's `PostStatusService#validate_media!` and `UpdateStatusService#validate_media!` check; anything else answers 422 `Media <ids> not found or already attached to another post` (`findUnattachableMediaIds` in `server/media-store.ts`, `checkAttachableMedia` in `server/routes/statuses/create.ts`). Before, the create answered 200 and its response and the author's `update` stream event carried another account's media with its random-key URL (#57), because they render the in-memory note whose `mediaIds` came from the request. The scheduled publisher checks the media again when a post comes due and drops the post when another status holds it by then, as Mastodon's worker fails it; a media read error keeps the row for the next run. Scheduled status documents list only the author's own media in `media_ids` order (`scheduledStatusMedia` in `server/routes/scheduled-statuses.ts`), so rows stored before the check do not show another account's media. `in_reply_to_id` and `quoted_status_id` now go through Mastodon's `StatusPolicy#show?` (`findShownStatusAnywhere` / `showsFoundStatus` in `server/found-status.ts`), so replying to or quoting a suspended or hidden author's status answers 404, also for a scheduled reply and when a scheduled reply comes due; the 404 bodies are Mastodon's `statuses.errors.in_reply_not_found` ("The post you are trying to reply to does not appear to exist.") and `quoted_status_not_found` ("The post you are trying to quote does not appear to exist."). Tests: `server/status-create-targets.test.ts`, `server/api-refusals.test.ts`, `server/note-attachments.test.ts`.

- A status keeps its media in the `media_ids` order (#63). D1 listed a status's attachments by `created_at`, so a status read back from storage showed them in upload order: `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id`, timelines, the fetched note, and an `Update` built from the reloaded note. `media_attachments` now has a `position` column (migration `0104_media_attachment_position.sql`), the counterpart of Mastodon's `ordered_media_attachment_ids`. `insertLocalNote` and `replaceStatusMedia` (`server/status-store.ts`) set it from the index in `mediaIds` through `attachMediaToStatus`, `detachStatusMedia` clears it, and `listMediaIdsForStatus` and `hydrateStatusRows` order by it. The migration fills it for attachments stored before it in `created_at` order, with `rowid` breaking ties, so older statuses keep the order they were shown in. Test: `server/note-attachments.test.ts` posts three media out of upload order, then edits them into another order, and reads each back through the API, the account timeline, the fetched note and the queued `Update`.

- Media uploads keep their alt text and focus (#64). `POST /api/v1/media` and `POST /api/v2/media` ignored the `description` and `focus` fields that Mastodon's `Api::V2::MediaController#create` accepts with the file, so a client that sent alt text at upload lost it. The upload now reads both through the same parser as `PUT /api/v1/media/:id` (`parseMediaMetadata` in `server/routes/media.ts`, with `mediaDescriptionFits` from `server/status-body-schema.ts` shared by `media_attributes`) and inserts them with the row. A too-long description or an invalid focus answers 422 before the R2 put and the row insert, so a refused upload stores nothing.

- Statuses of unavailable authors (#56, #62). The status renderers (`mastodonStatuses` in `server/local-status-documents.ts`, `mastodonRemoteStatuses` in `server/remote-status-documents.ts`, `remoteBoostDocuments` in `server/remote-boost-documents.ts`) now build the author and booster through `presentedAccounts` (`server/account-documents.ts`, the loaded-rows half of `presentedAccountsByIds`), one batched pass per render, and serve a status by its author's presentation as Mastodon does. A rule, `StatusAuthors` (`server/account-presentation.ts`), names whose statuses a render serves: `available` (the default, Mastodon's `StatusPolicy#show?`, which refuses an unavailable author) leaves out a suspended or hidden author's status, and a boost by a suspended booster, wherever statuses are served (by id, `?id[]=`, context, search, timelines, notifications, bookmarks, favourites); `existing` serves what Mastodon still keeps, every author but a hidden one, and is what a boost wraps, so a boost of a suspended author's status stays with the author stripped (`suspended: true`), and the quoting status a quote revoke answers; `any` is the admin report API (`GET /api/v1/admin/reports/:id`) and the admin trend review, which show a suspended author's status with the author stripped, and the status an interaction answers once the route has decided the viewer may act on it. A quote of an unavailable author's status renders `state: "unauthorized"` with `quoted_status: null`, as Mastodon's `StatusFilter#filter_state_for_quote`. The routes that act on one status take it from one lookup, `findShownStatusAnywhere` (`server/found-status.ts`), which is Mastodon's `StatusPolicy#show?` (`showsFoundStatus`): the viewer may see the status and its author is available. So favourite, bookmark, boost, mute and unmute, `favourited_by`, `reblogged_by`, `context` and the local and remote `history` answer 404 for an unavailable author's status before writing anything, as Mastodon's `Statuses::BaseController` does. As in Mastodon 4.5 (`FavouritesController#destroy`, `BookmarksController#destroy`, `ReblogsController#destroy`), unfavourite, unbookmark and unreblog still withdraw what the viewer did and answer the status with its author stripped, also for an author on a suspended domain; when there was nothing to withdraw they answer only a status the viewer may be shown, else 404. `deleteBookmark` returns the removed id for this. Pin, unpin and `source` stay owner-only; a suspended owner cannot sign in, so `show?` adds nothing there. Home and list timelines leave a suspended local author's statuses and boosts out in SQL before the `LIMIT` (`FEED_SUSPENDED_AUTHORS_SQL` in `server/followed-feed.ts`), as Mastodon unmerges them from followers' feeds, so a page is not cut short; remote feeds already did. Embedded local accounts now carry `moved`, as Mastodon's serializer renders it. `mastodonRemoteStatus` takes the presented author document instead of the actor. Mentions still name a suspended account by id, username and acct, as Mastodon's `REST::MentionSerializer` does. A home page of 20 statuses (local notes and boosts, remote notes and a remote boost) went from 58 to 70 D1 statements: each remote render loads the domain allow list, suspended domains and actor moderations once, and each local render loads standings and moves once. The renderers (`mastodonStatuses`, `mastodonStatus`, `mastodonRemoteStatuses`, `statusDocumentsByIds`, `remoteTimelineDocuments`) return a `Result`: a storage error loading the authors or their presentations fails the render, so reads answer 500 instead of 404 or a short list, writes answer 500 instead of an empty 200, an edit fails before it writes, and the author's own streams get no event instead of `{}`. The admin report document and the poll-expiry stream event still drop the statuses on a render error. A create whose render fails after the note is stored answers 500 but records its `Idempotency-Key` with the stored id (`postStatus` returns the id with the render result), as Mastodon records the key once the status exists, so a retry with the key answers that status rendered again instead of posting it twice; only a create that stored nothing releases the key (`server/status-idempotency.test.ts`). An edit whose render fails after the edit is stored and announced still answers 500: Mastodon's `PUT` has no idempotency key and carries the whole revision, so a retry leaves the note reading the same, with one more history entry.

- Outbound notes carry their media (#61). The `Note` / `Question` that `noteDocument` (`server/activitypub.ts`) builds had no `attachment`, so remote servers never saw a local status's images, video or audio. It now lists the note's media in the note's order as Mastodon's `ActivityPub::NoteSerializer::MediaAttachmentSerializer` does: `type: "Document"`, `mediaType`, `url` (the public media URL, from `mediaUrl` and the row's `object_key`), the description as `name` (or `null`), `blurhash: null`, and `focalPoint: [x, y]` when a focus is set, with the `toot:blurhash` and `toot:focalPoint` context terms. A note without media sends `attachment: []`, as Mastodon does. `media` is a required field of `noteDocument`'s extras, filled by `loadNoteMedia` (`server/note-attachments.ts`), so every `Create`, edit or moderation `Update`, poll `Update(Question)` and the fetched `/users/:name/statuses/:id` carry it; an `Update` without it would clear the media on servers that replace attachments on update, tstodon included. Scheduled posts publish through the same path, and the outbox serves the stored `Create`. Only rows the author attached to this note are sent, since an unsaved note's `mediaIds` are the request's `media_ids` and can name another account's media. A sensitized author's note with media is sent `sensitive: true`, as Mastodon's `NoteSerializer` and tstodon's API do. When the media cannot be read, nothing goes out without it: the `Create` or `Update` is not sent (`StatusNotFederated`, `StatusUpdateNotFederated`, `PollUpdateNotFederated` in the logs) and the fetched note answers 500, because receivers, tstodon and Mastodon's `ProcessStatusUpdateService` alike, replace a note's media with the incoming list. The post itself stays published locally, since its row and media are stored before the media is read; there is no retry of the skipped `Create`. Not sent: an `icon` thumbnail (`width`, `height` and the `blurhash` came with image processing, above). The federation e2e (`e2e/federation-follow-create.ts`) posts an image on A and checks that B's API shows it with A's URL, type and description. Tests: `server/note-attachments.test.ts`, including a D1 binding that fails the media read.

- Sender screening as Mastodon's `NotifyService::DropCondition` (#54). The single sender check that `deliverNotification` makes before the policy (`screenSender` in `server/notify.ts`, contract `ScreenSender` in `packages/domain/src/notification-store.ts`) now resolves to `Drop`, `Bypass`, or `Screen`, and follows Mastodon's order. A notification whose sender is its recipient is dropped (`from_self?`), except `poll`, `severed_relationships`, `moderation_warning`, and `annual_report`. A local mention from staff who outrank the recipient is `Bypass`: it reaches the recipient past blocks, notification mutes, domain blocks, the notification policy, and muted conversations, as Mastodon returns early for `message? && from_staff?` and its `FilterCondition` skips the same case. Staff follows `UserRole#bypass_block?` (a highlighted role with moderation permissions that outranks the recipient's): an admin, by the `user.role` column that `ADMIN_USERNAMES` seeds, mentioning a non-admin (`FediRole.bypassesBlock`). An admin does not outrank another admin. A domain block now drops a remote sender only when the recipient does not follow it (`domain_blocking? && not_following?`). Still different from Mastodon: no drop for an unavailable (suspended) recipient, and no `blocked_mention?` (`FeedManager#filter?(:mentions)`, which also drops a mention whose other mentioned accounts or replied-to author the recipient blocks).

- A user's domain block severs the follows with that domain (#60). `blockDomain` only wrote the `account_domain_blocks` row, so followers on the blocked domain kept receiving the blocker's posts and the blocker kept following its accounts. `blockAccountDomain` (`server/account-domain-block.ts`), used by `POST /api/v1/domain_blocks` and the CSV domain-block import, writes the row and then does what Mastodon's `AfterBlockDomainFromAccountService` does: it removes each accepted follow of an actor on the domain and each follower and pending request from it, and sends the `Undo(Follow)` or `Reject(Follow)` for each (`sendFollowUndo`, `sendFollowReject`). Each page records its accepted follows under one `user_domain_block` severance event, with the domain as `target_name`, and deletes the page's follows in the same D1 batch (`severAccountPage` in `server/severed-relationship-store.ts`), so a follow is recorded exactly when it is removed; the activities go out after the batch, best effort. Pending requests are rejected unrecorded. The account gets the `severed_relationships` notification (`finishAccountSeverance`, `notifySeverance` in `server/severance.ts`) and the event's CSV lists on `/severed_relationships`, as with an admin suspension. `finishAccountSeverance` answers the account event while it has no notification, so a retry after a failed notification notifies once. The request severs the first `SEVER_PAGE_SIZE` (20) actors, by actor URI. A larger domain continues in `SeverBlockedDomainFollows` queue jobs, each chaining the next page by an actor-URI cursor, and the last page closes and notifies the event. A failed first page is queued to run again; a queued page that fails, or whose next page cannot be queued, is retried. A queued page for a domain the account unblocked since severs nothing more and closes the event with what was removed. Blocking a domain that is already blocked does nothing, so a second block during a severance does not start a second event. Not done: unblocking and re-blocking while pages of the first block are still queued does split the severance across two events (and two notifications); no follow is recorded twice. An admin domain suspension (`severRelationships`, domains only since FED-6) moves a follow's record to its own event when one already exists, instead of failing its batch. The block row is written first and holds on its own. Unblocking restores nothing, as in Mastodon. The list triggers drop the severed accounts from the blocker's lists. The blocker's notifications from the domain, its accepted notification senders there, and its own pending requests to the domain go too (#67). Not done: an isolate that dies between a page's batch and its activities leaves those remote servers without the `Undo`/`Reject`; and if both the first page and queueing its retry fail, the severance stops until the domain is unblocked and blocked again. Tests: `server/account-domain-block.test.ts`.

- Unguessable attachment keys and a gated `/media/:id` (#57). An attachment was stored at `attachments/<accountId>/<mediaId>` and served without auth from the public media hostname and the Worker's `/attachments/*` route. The account id is public and a media id is its upload millisecond plus 16 random bits, so anyone who knew roughly when a direct post's picture was uploaded could find it in about 65,536 probes per millisecond. `GET /media/:id` served any attachment by id alone. New uploads get `newAttachmentObjectKey` (`server/media-keys.ts`): 128 bits from `crypto.getRandomValues` as hex, stored in the row's `object_key`, from which every API URL is built. Mastodon names its files with `SecureRandom.hex(8)` for the same reason. `GET /media/:id` now follows `MediaController#show`: media attached to a local status that the signed-in browser session may see (`canViewStatus`) redirects (302) to the file URL, and everything else answers 404. Existing objects: the `RekeyLegacyMedia` queue job (`server/media-rekey.ts`, queued by the hourly cron, chaining by an id cursor while a batch of 50 is full) finds rows still at the id-derived key by matching the key itself, so uploads by the previous Worker during the deploy are found too. It streams each object to a random key, then in one D1 batch points the row at the copy, keeps the old key in `legacy_object_key` (migration 0103), and rewrites the old key in every edit-history snapshot of the owner's statuses (also revisions of a status an edit later removed the media from). Deleting the old object is an operator opt-in, `MEDIA_REKEY_DELETE_LEGACY` (default off, since a deleted object cannot be restored). With it on, the job deletes the old object only after `head` confirms the row's copy is stored. When D1 fails after the update may have applied, the job keeps the copy rather than risk deleting the one the row points at. A row that fails is logged as `LegacyMediaNotRekeyed` and skipped. While an object is still at its old key, the Worker route serves it without the edge cache. `DELETE /api/v1/media/:id` and account purges also delete a legacy object that is still stored, and a purge deletes everything left under `attachments/<accountId>/` after the rows are gone, which catches a copy the job made in between. Until the operator turns the flag on and purges `/attachments/` from the media hostname's CDN cache, old guessable URLs stay reachable on the public R2 hostname (`docs/operations/cloudflare-deploy.md#moving-attachments-off-id-derived-keys`). Avatars and headers keep id-based keys, since they are public in Mastodon too. Not done: each hourly run scans all of `media_attachments` by primary key even after every row has moved (the key predicate is an expression no index covers), which D1 bills as rows read. `server/media-access.test.ts` pins the guessed key and `/media/:id` answering 404 for a direct post, the redirect for a public one, the move with the old object kept and then deleted under the flag, its resume, the edit history, a D1 failure after the update, a missing copy, an unreadable row, deletion of a legacy row, and the purge sweep.
- A follow's notification ends with the follow (#58). Ending a follow left the target's `follow` or `follow_request` notification in place, so "X followed you" stayed after an unfollow, a withdrawn or rejected request, a removed follower or a block. Mastodon destroys the notification with the `Follow` or `FollowRequest` (`has_one :notification, dependent: :destroy`), and `AuthorizeFollowService` creates no `follow` notification when it authorizes a request. Migration 0102 adds triggers on `follows` (sender by account id) and `remote_follows` (sender by actor URI) that delete the notification when the row is deleted, and the `follow_request` one when the row leaves `Pending` / `Queued`, so every path (including blocks, admin domain blocks and account deletion) drops it. A held notification leaves the notification requests through the `notification_requests` cascade. Notifications that follows ended before this change left behind are not deleted by the migration (an operator can run the cleanup SQL in the #58 closing comment). `Undo(Follow)` no longer deletes them itself, so `RemoteNotificationWithdrawal` always names a status. Tests: `server/follow-notification-ends.test.ts`.

- Distinct ids within an isolate (#52). Fifty-one parallel posts in a worker test could answer 500 with `UNIQUE constraint failed: outbox_deliveries.id`. An id is the millisecond shifted left 16 bits with 16 random low bits below it. workerd advances `Date.now()` only on I/O, so a burst shares milliseconds (510 parallel posts made 1530 ids in 456 milliseconds, up to 12 in one), and two ids in one millisecond matched with probability 1 in 65,536 per pair. `entityIdAt` (`server/ids.ts`) still draws the low bits uniformly at random, and now remembers which low values it handed out at each millisecond and draws again on a repeat. The low bits stay random because an id is the only secret guarding a media attachment: its R2 key is `attachments/<accountId>/<mediaId>`, served without auth, and `GET /media/:id` needs only the id. A sequential counter would let anyone who sees one id narrow the search for a neighbor's. The isolate remembers at most 65,536 low values and 256 milliseconds, forgetting the oldest milliseconds first and never the one being drawn. Once all 65,536 values at one millisecond are used, `entityIdAt` throws instead of moving to another millisecond, since a later one could sort after `Date.now()`. A backfill of posts published in the same second no longer collides with itself, and an id still never sorts after its instant, so `max_id` paging is unchanged. Ids from two isolates can still match at one instant (1 in 65,536 per pair), so the remote-status retry from #38 stays; `rerollEntityId` marks the collided low value used and draws a fresh random one. The poll backlog test creates its 51 polls in parallel again.

- Final poll counts at expiry and non-filterable notification types (#44). When the `ProcessExpiredPolls` run claims an ended local poll, it queues the poll's `DistributePollUpdate` (`scheduleFinalPollUpdate` in `server/poll-update.ts`) with no delay, as Mastodon's `PollExpirationNotifyWorker` does. Remote servers then get an `Update(Question)` with `closed`, `endTime`, the final `replies.totalItems`, and `votersCount`. A redelivered run claims nothing, so it queues nothing. The job takes the poll's `update_queued_at` slot, so when a vote's debounced job is still queued, that job runs after the end and sends the final counts instead. A poll that hides its totals now federates them once it has ended, as Mastodon's `show_totals_now?` (`expired? || !hide_totals?`). A run whose batch of 50 ended polls or 50 voters was full, and that claimed at least one, queues the next `ProcessExpiredPolls` at once instead of waiting up to an hour for the cron. A run that claims nothing never chains, so a batch that keeps failing waits for the cron. `POLICY_EXEMPT_KINDS` (`server/notify.ts`) is now the set that Mastodon 4.5 marks `filterable: false` in `Notification::PROPERTIES`: `status`, `poll`, `update`, `quoted_update`, `severed_relationships`, `moderation_warning`, `annual_report`, `admin.sign_up`, and `admin.report`. So an `update` to a booster whose policy drops strangers is delivered. The block, notification-mute, and domain-block check now runs inside `deliverNotification` for every notification of every kind, before the policy, as in Mastodon's `NotifyService`. Before, only `notifyFromLocal` and `notifyFromRemote` made it, so a local `mention`, `status`, `reblog`, `favourite`, `quote`, or `quoted_update` could reach a recipient who blocks the sender or mutes its notifications, and be streamed and pushed. The moderation kinds get the same check, as in Mastodon, so an admin who blocks a reporter gets no `admin.report` from them. `notifyFromLocal` is gone, and `notifyFromRemote` only fills in the remote sender.

- A block ends the follows both ways (#49). `blockAccount` only wrote the `blocks` row, so a blocked account kept following the blocker, stayed on the blocker's lists and in both follow counts. `blockAccountTarget` (`server/account-block.ts`) now does what Mastodon's `BlockService` does: it deletes the blocker's follow or request of the target (`unfollowAccount`, or `unfollowRemoteActor` with its `Undo(Follow)`) and the target's follow or request of the blocker (`unfollowAccount`, or `removeRemoteFollower` in `server/remote-follower.ts` with its `Reject(Follow)`, which `remove_from_followers` now uses too), after writing the block, and sends a remote target the `Block` (Mastodon's id, `{actor}#blocks/{id}`). The block is written first and each severing step is best effort (a failure logs `BlockFollowNotSevered`), so a storage failure cannot leave the follows half gone with no block. `unblockAccountTarget` sends the `Undo(Block)`. The API, the CSV block import and a move's carried blocks all go through it. The list trigger of migration 0101 removes the target from the blocker's lists, and a remote member leaves with its outbound follow. An inbound `Block` (`server/inbox/block.ts`) ends the follows with the local account the same way, as Mastodon's `ActivityPub::Activity::Block`, but tstodon does not record that a remote actor blocks a local account. An inbound `Follow` (`server/inbox/follow.ts`) from an actor the target blocks, by account or by account domain block, is answered with `Reject(Follow)` and stores nothing, as Mastodon's `ActivityPub::Activity::Follow`; a local signer the target blocks is refused the same way.

- Unavailable accounts on account lists and embedded documents (#48). The #43 rule (`AccountPresentation`) now applies wherever account documents are built, not only on search, lookup and by-id. `presentedAccountsByIds` (`server/account-documents.ts`) loads the presentations in the same batched pass as the documents and renders each id with its presentation: `Shown` in full, `Suspended` and `Hidden` stripped (as Mastodon's `REST::AccountSerializer`). A storage error while loading presentations fails the lookup (`Result`), so routes answer 500 and push delivery retries instead of leaving accounts out. Three views come off it: `accountDocumentsByIds` names every account another record points at and leaves `Hidden` out (followers and following, familiar followers, conversation participants), `embeddedAccountDocumentsByIds` keeps `Hidden` stripped for the account a record is about (report targets, and `move` targets in notifications), so an `admin.report` about an account on a suspended domain stays, and `availableAccountDocumentsByIds` keeps only available accounts, as Mastodon's `Account.without_suspended`, for lists of accounts (follow requests, list members, favourited/reblogged by, blocks, mutes, endorsements) and the notification requests list. Notifications whose sender is not available are left out of `GET /api/v1/notifications`, `/:id`, push and streaming (`mastodonNotifications`), and of `GET /api/v2/notifications`, whose rows are filtered by `availableAccountIds` before grouping. The v1 `Link` header pages by the rows read, not the notifications rendered, so left-out rows do not end the list; the grouped list reads up to five older row windows when a full window has nothing to show (the web app stops at an empty page), and still answers an empty page past that bound. An account's own lists are empty when it is not available, as Mastodon's `@account.unavailable?`: `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/statuses` (local and remote; remote checked the domain suspension only, not individual suspension or the allow list), `/followers`, `/following`, `/featured_tags`, `/endorsements`. Follow requests, favourited/reblogged by and endorsements rendered remote actors with `mastodonRemoteAccount` directly; they now pass `r_…` ids through the same builder. `AccountPresentation.searchable` is renamed `available`, and `accountRefPresentation` answers one account's presentation for these gates. Admin endpoints render with `mastodonAccountDocument` and keep full data. Deviations from Mastodon: familiar followers keeps an empty group for an unavailable requested account (Mastodon leaves the group out, as it does for an unknown id, which this server also answers with an empty group); `GET /api/v2/notifications/unread_count` still counts groups from unavailable senders; pages filtered after the SQL `LIMIT` come back short (their `Link` header still offers the next page); and a grouped page read with `since_id` or `min_id` does not read on past a window with nothing to show. Accounts embedded in statuses go through the rule since #56.

- Admin web e2e timeout (#34). "an admin hears of a sign-up and a report…" did not wait on a stream event: it opens the column with `page.goto("/notifications")`, which reads the notifications over REST, and every notification exists before then (`notifyAdmins` runs inside the sign-up and report requests). It ran out of its 60 s budget doing two kinds of slow work. Each full load of the web UI revalidated its ~180 packs, because the Worker passed on the platform's `max-age=0, must-revalidate`, and each revalidation is a Worker invocation (`run_worker_first`). That came to about 14 s per load on a loaded machine. And each sign-up spawned `wrangler kv key get` for the mailed link, 6 to 7 s per call. `notFound` now sends `private, max-age=31536000, immutable` for content-hashed packs (`packCacheControl` in `server/web-app.ts`). That also helps real users, whose browsers stop revalidating the app on every page load. `confirmationLink` in `e2e/web/fixtures.ts` now reads the mail through the dev server's local explorer API. With other test runs sharing the 4 CPUs (load average 14 to 22), the scenario failed 5 of 5 runs before and passed 15 of 15 after, in 28 to 46 s. With a `vp test run` loop added on top (load average about 26), it still runs past 60 s: each person's first, uncached load of the web UI takes 10 to 17 s, three times. Not done: serving `/packs/*` without the Worker (`run_worker_first` excluding it), which would cut that first load. The Vite dev server did not honor the exclusion, so its effect is unmeasured.

- One notification per new local follow (#40). A `Follow` signed by a local account and redelivered under a new activity id notified the target a second time, because `followAccount` reported the follow state whether or not it inserted the row. `followAccount` (`server/follow-store.ts`) now returns a `FollowWrite` (`Created{follow} | Existing`), read from the `INSERT OR IGNORE ... RETURNING id`, and `notifyNewFollow` (`server/notify.ts`) sends `follow` or `follow_request` only for `Created`. The inbox `Follow` handler, `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/follow`, and `followAccountRef` (moves, imports) all use it. The route and `followAccountRef` dropped their read-before-write checks; those checks read the same `follows` row, so their behavior is unchanged, and the insert also settles a race between two concurrent follows.

- Sign-up passkey names and Better Auth's session revoke endpoints (#31). The sign-up page now asks for a name for the passkey it creates, as `/auth/edit` does when it adds one, and sends it with the verification, so `/auth/edit` no longer lists that passkey as "Passkey". Better Auth's own `/api/auth/revoke-session`, `/revoke-sessions`, and `/revoke-other-sessions` only checked that a session existed, so a stale or stolen cookie could sign the owner out of every other browser. Nothing calls them (the page ends sessions through its own route with the recent sign-in rule), so a `before` hook in `app/auth.ts` refuses them with 403. The earlier attempt used an `after` hook: Better Auth runs `after` hooks once the endpoint has returned, so it answered 403 with the sessions already deleted.

- A remote vote refused because the poll ended is not counted (#46). `recordRemotePollVote` (`server/poll-store.ts`) checked expiry before its batch, but only the options update in the batch checked it again, so a remote vote that arrived as the poll ended answered `Expired` and still kept its `remote_poll_votes` row. That row counted toward `voters_count` and made a later vote from the same actor read as `Duplicate` or `AlreadyVoted`. `insertRemotePollVoteOrIgnore.sql` now writes the row only while the poll is open at the same `now` the options update uses, as #35 did for local votes in `insertPollVoteOrIgnore.sql`. `server/poll-expiry.test.ts` replays the race.

- List languages and followed-only list members (#45). A follow's `languages[]` now narrows the owner's lists as it narrows home: Mastodon's `FeedManager#filter` runs `filter_from_home` with the follow's languages for a list too. Home and the list query share `FEED_LANGUAGES_SQL` in `server/followed-feed.ts`; the `list` stream asks the same query, and remote statuses already shared one query (`listFollowedRemoteStatuses`). A list's local members now follow Mastodon's `ListAccount` rule (`validate_relationship`): `POST /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts` refuses a local account the owner neither follows nor has asked to follow with 422 `Validation failed: Account must be a followed account` (Mastodon's message; the remote refusal now uses it too), the owner may still add itself, and `findUnfollowedMember` in `server/list-store.ts` is the one check the API and the CSV list import use, so an import row naming an unfollowed local account fails as `NotFollowed`. Mastodon cascades `list_accounts` from `follows` and `follow_requests`, so migration 0101 adds a trigger that removes the followed account from the follower's lists when the follow row is deleted (unfollow, rejected request, removed follower, a move's unfollow). The list timeline keeps only posts by the owner and by members it follows (accepted), as Mastodon's `lists_for_local_distribution` feeds a list, so a requested member shows nothing until accepted and the list and its stream agree; this replaces #36's followers-only rule for unfollowed members. Memberships of local accounts the owner neither follows nor requested that were stored before this change stay in `account_list_members`; the list shows none of their posts. Deleting them is an operator decision (the SQL is in the #45 closing comment). The fan-out path was left as is: every member whose posts a list shows is an accepted follow, so `listHomeFanOutAccounts` already reaches its owner. `HIDDEN_ACTOR_URIS_SQL` in `server/remote-boost-store.ts`, and the same blocks and mutes subqueries inlined in `listFollowedRemoteStatuses`, now use `SUPPRESSED_ACTOR_URIS_SQL`. Not done: remote members still need an accepted follow where Mastodon also takes a pending request.

- Flaky forwarded-report test (#42). `vi.spyOn(globalThis, "fetch")` covers the whole isolate, so while `federated-reports.test.ts` and `instance-actor.test.ts` delivered a Flag through the spy, the `OutboxDeliveryWorkflow` that `enqueueInstanceActorActivity` had started for the same Flag could post through it too, from the workflow's own Durable Object. When that request came first, the test read its body from the test runner's Durable Object and workerd threw `Cannot perform I/O on behalf of a different Durable Object`. `deliverTarget` in `packages/worker/test/delivery-fixture.ts` now reads each body inside the spy, in the context that made the request, and returns a `Request` the caller owns. Production code was not at fault: the workflow reads its own response. The `uncaught exception ... jsg.Error: internal error` lines in test logs have another cause. workerd prints one for every `fetch` that fails DNS, even when the caller catches the error. They come from background deliveries and fetches to the made-up hosts that tests use, and they fail nothing.

- Account read paths agree on hidden and suspended accounts (#43). One rule, `AccountPresentation` (`packages/domain/src/account-presentation.ts`), decides how search, lookup, `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id` and `GET /api/v1/accounts?id[]=` treat an account: `Shown`, `Suspended` (the account's own standing), or `Hidden` (a remote actor on a suspended domain or outside the allow list). `server/account-presentation.ts` loads the inputs in one pass per list (`localAccountPresentations`, `remoteAccountPresentations`) and applies the rule (`searchableLocalAccounts`, `searchableRemoteActors`, `accountRefDocument`). Search (`/api/v1/accounts/search` and `/api/v2/search`, exact match, cached results and `resolve=true` alike) lists only `Shown` accounts. Before, cached actors on suspended or non-allow-listed domains and suspended local accounts were listed, and v1's `resolve=true` exact match kept an individually suspended actor. Lookup and by-id answer 404 for `Hidden` (the batch leaves it out) and render `Suspended` as Mastodon's `REST::AccountSerializer` renders an unavailable account: `suspended: true`, empty `display_name`, `note`, `emojis` and `fields`, default avatar and header, `locked`/`bot`/`discoverable`/`indexable` false, no `moved`, counts kept. Before, lookup and by-id rendered a suspended remote actor's full profile, lookup rendered a suspended local account in full, and by-id answered 410 for it; the batch left suspended local accounts out and rendered suspended remote actors in full. An account's `moved` target goes through the same rule wherever account documents are rendered (`movedToDocuments` in `server/account-documents.ts`, which loads the targets' presentations in the same batched pass): a suspended target is rendered stripped, as Mastodon's serializer renders it, and a hidden one leaves `moved` out. Before, a shown account that moved to a suspended account, or to one on a suspended or non-allow-listed domain, embedded that account's full profile. `findKnownAccountHandle` no longer checks domains itself. Deviation from Mastodon: by id, an actor on a suspended domain answers 404 (Mastodon renders it suspended, because a domain suspension suspends each account) and one outside the allow list answers 404 (Mastodon renders it, though limited federation purges such accounts). Search filters after the SQL `LIMIT`, so a page with hidden actors comes back short.

- Local boosts on list timelines (#36). `listListTimelineStatuses` now keeps `LocalReblog` rows by the list's local members, so the list timeline and the `list` stream (which asks the same query for one entry) show them, as Mastodon does. Home and lists share one rule for local boosts, `feedLocalReblogsSql` in `server/followed-feed.ts`, after Mastodon's `FeedManager`: the viewer's own boosts always pass; another's is dropped when the viewer's follow of the booster has `reblogs: false`, or when the viewer does not see the boosted status's author (blocks either way and mutes of a local author; blocks, mutes, and domain blocks or suspensions of a remote one). The author rule is new on home too: before, home showed a followed account's boost of a post by someone the viewer blocks or mutes. Remote boosts (`listFollowedRemoteBoosts`) already applied it. A list may hold a local account the owner does not follow, so the list query now also keeps that member's followers-only posts and boosts off the list, as home does by following only. Not done: a follow's `languages[]` still narrows only home, not the lists holding that account (Mastodon's `FeedManager` applies it to both).

- Status create and edit check the whole request before they write (#41). `editNote` (`server/routes/statuses/edit.ts`) now parses `quote_approval_policy` with the other fields, so an invalid value answers the same 422 (`Invalid quote_approval_policy`, `ValidationError`) before the edit history snapshot, the new text, quote notifications, the link-card job, or the poll change. Before, the local note changed while media, mentions, and the federated `Update` were skipped. The audit of `editNote` and `postStatus` found one more check that ran after writes: a `media_attributes` description over the limit was refused inside `applyMediaAttributes`, after the new note (create) or the whole edit was stored, and it answered 500 `RepositoryError`. Both flows now check it up front with `mediaDescriptionsFit` (`server/status-body-schema.ts`) and answer 422 `description is too long` / `ValidationError`, the body `PUT /api/v1/media/:id` already uses; a scheduled post is checked before it is stored, too. `applyMediaAttributes` lost its copy in `routes/statuses/create.ts`; edit uses the one in `server/local-status-publish.ts`. `server/api-refusals.test.ts` and `server/media-attributes.test.ts` assert that a refused edit leaves the source text and history unchanged and a refused create posts nothing.

- Declarative transforms and thinner status/account handlers (#30). Loops that only build an array, map, set, or string from values now use `map`/`filter`/`flatMap`/`reduce`/`Map.groupBy` (262 `for` loops in non-test source to 154). Two helpers carry the shapes that repeated: `okValues` in `server/d1.ts` (keep the results that succeeded) and `documentsById` in `server/documents-by-id.ts` (rendered documents keyed by id); `hashtagUses` replaces two copies of the hashtag count and `LocalStatus.isNote` names the note filter. Loops stay where they sequence awaits, exit early, feed several accumulators, perform effects (headers, sockets, `compileSchema`), fill a fixed-key record whose type needs every key (push alerts), or hash UTF-16 code units (`access-email`). `createStatus`, `updateStatus`, `update_credentials`, and the reblog handler now read as parse, use case, response: `postStatus`, `editNote`, `updateCredentials`, and `publishBoost` return `Result<_, ApiRefusal>` (`server/routes/api-refusal.ts`), which the handler maps to HTTP; checks and writes keep their order, and `server/api-refusals.test.ts` pins the error bodies (it passes against the handlers before the split). `oauth.ts` and `notifications.ts` were not split: each handler is already short. `poll-store.ts`, `remote-actor-store.ts`, and `status-store.ts` stay single files, as #27 decided (callers import 1.6 to 1.9 of their 18 to 23 functions). Typed fixtures whose `kind` or literal fields widened under an annotation now use `as const satisfies`. Behavior is unchanged.

- Remote account lookup by `acct` (#37). `GET /api/v1/accounts/lookup` reads the handle with `findKnownAccountHandle` (`server/account-ref.ts`), which parses it as `resolveAccountHandle` does (a leading `@` is dropped, the domain may carry a port). A bare username or one at this instance's domain is a local account; any other is a cached remote actor found by `username` and `host[:port]`, rendered as the remote account document. A domain outside the allow list or suspended answers 404. Mastodon's `Api::V1::Accounts::LookupController` calls `ResolveAccountService` with `skip_webfinger: true`, so an actor not yet cached is not fetched here and answers 404; the search and resolve endpoints still resolve by WebFinger.

- Local poll expiry notifications (#35), as Mastodon's `PollExpirationNotifyWorker`. When a local poll ends, the `ProcessExpiredPolls` job (`processExpiredPolls` in `server/poll-expiry.ts`, moved out of `server/outbox-job.ts`) sends a `poll` notification from the author to the author and to each local voter. Remote voters get nothing from here; their servers tell them. Each recipient is told once: the author through the `polls.expiry_notified_at` claim, which now comes before the `status.update` and the notification, and each voter through the new `poll_votes.expiry_notified_at` (migration 0100; every row of one voter on one poll is claimed together). Polls that had ended before the migration are not announced. An author who voted on their own poll is told once, as the author. Local and remote voters are one `EndedLocalPollVoter | EndedRemotePollVoter` union and go through the same claim-then-notify loop. A voter who blocks the author, or mutes the author's notifications, gets nothing: `notifyFromLocal` in `server/notify.ts` makes the block and mute check that `notifyFromRemote` already made. `poll` now bypasses the notification policy, as Mastodon's non-filterable types do, for local and remote polls. Each run handles 50 ended polls and 50 voters; a full batch queues the next run (#44, which also federates the final counts).
- Declarative transforms and thinner status/account handlers (#30). Loops that only build an array, map, set, or string from values now use `map`/`filter`/`flatMap`/`reduce`/`Map.groupBy` (262 `for` loops in non-test source to 154). Two helpers carry the shapes that repeated: `okValues` in `server/d1.ts` (keep the results that succeeded) and `documentsById` in `server/documents-by-id.ts` (rendered documents keyed by id); `hashtagUses` replaces two copies of the hashtag count and `LocalStatus.isNote` names the note filter. Loops stay where they sequence awaits, exit early, feed several accumulators, perform effects (headers, sockets, `compileSchema`), fill a fixed-key record whose type needs every key (push alerts), or hash UTF-16 code units (`access-email`). `createStatus`, `updateStatus`, `update_credentials`, and the reblog handler now read as parse, use case, response: `postStatus`, `editNote`, `updateCredentials`, and `publishBoost` return `Result<_, ApiRefusal>` (`server/routes/api-refusal.ts`), which the handler maps to HTTP; checks and writes keep their order, and `server/api-refusals.test.ts` pins the error bodies (it passes against the handlers before the split). `oauth.ts` and `notifications.ts` were not split: each handler is already short. `poll-store.ts`, `remote-actor-store.ts`, and `status-store.ts` stay single files, as #27 decided (callers import 1.6 to 1.9 of their 18 to 23 functions). Typed fixtures whose `kind` or literal fields widened under an annotation now use `as const satisfies`. Behavior is unchanged. Found, not fixed: an edit with an invalid `quote_approval_policy` answers 422 after the note, link card, and poll are already updated (media, mentions, and the federated `Update` are skipped).

- Lifecycle states as `kind` unions (#29). A report row parses into `ReportResolution` (`Open | Resolved{at, byAccountId}`, `packages/domain/src/report-resolution.ts`) and `ReportForwarding` (`NotForwarded | Forwarded`), and the row schema in `server/report-store.ts` rejects a row whose `action_taken_at` and `action_taken_by_account_id` disagree, whose reporter or target has both or neither column, or whose local target claims to be forwarded. A local account's moderation is one `LocalAccountModeration` (`packages/domain/src/local-account-moderation.ts`): an `AccountStanding` (`InGoodStanding | Silenced | Suspended`, shared with `RemoteActorModeration`), the `sensitized` mark, `login` (`Enabled | Disabled`) and `approval` (`Pending | Approved`). It is read from `account_moderation` and `account_flags` in one query (`listLocalAccountModerations`, also the access-token query) and written whole in one batch (`saveLocalAccountModeration`); the admin API, the admin pages and `takeAccountAction` all go through `LocalAccountModeration.apply` (`moderateLocalAccount` in `server/admin-actions.ts`), and `isBarred` replaces the suspended-or-disabled check that auth, the browser session, scheduled posts and statuses cleanup each spelled out. Standing stays one exclusive union: Mastodon keeps `silenced_at` and `suspended_at` apart, but a suspension hides everything a silence does, and the schema (one `moderation` column with a CHECK) already stores only the stronger one; login, approval and the sensitive mark are independent fields because Mastodon lets them combine with any standing. One fix landed with it: `silence` or `unsilence` on a suspended local account used to lift the suspension (the API overwrote or deleted the row); the shared transition keeps it, as it already did for remote actors. Wire format is unchanged, and there is no migration. Left as they are, because a union would not make them clearer: the poll `Update` claim and the outbox backfill claim (the state lives in a SQL compare-and-set; TypeScript only sees whether this caller won, as `boolean` / the claim timestamp), filter expiry (`expiresAt: string | null` is an instant or never, decided against the clock at match time, with no stored transitions), and the relationship flags (already derived per request from follow, block, mute and note rows; parsing the follow row's `kind` belongs to the follow stores in #27). A full-state write after a read can lose a concurrent admin change to the same account; before, each action wrote only its own column.

- Domain-owned read and write contracts (#27). `RepositoryError` moved to `packages/domain` (`server/d1.ts` still exports it as the error its runner produces, so the 175 worker files that name it did not change). Five use cases now take function-typed contracts from `packages/domain` as parameters, each with a unit test that needs no D1: `answerOutboundFollow` (`AcceptOutboundFollow`, `RejectOutboundFollow`, `FindLocalAccountByUsername`; `OutboundFollowAnswer` moved to the domain), `listThreadAncestors` (`FindThreadParent` over the domain's `ThreadStatus`, which replaced the worker's `ReplyTarget`), `deliverNotification` behind `notifyAccount` (`ResolveNotificationDisposition`, `FindMutedThreadStatusIds`, `InsertNotification` over `NewNotification`, `HoldNotification`, `PublishNotification`), `withdrawRemoteStatus` behind `removeRemoteStatus` (`DeleteRemoteStatus`, `PublishRemoteStatusRemoval`, `WithdrawRemoteNotifications`; `RemoteStatusMatch`, `RemovedRemoteStatus` and `RemovedBoost` moved to the domain), and `recordInboxFollow` (`HasRemoteFollow`, `RecordInboxFollow` over `InboxFollow`), which returns the notification and `Accept` to send so the inbox handler runs them. The convention is in `docs/architecture/tstodon-architecture.md#read-and-write-contracts`. Behavior is unchanged. Not done: reports (report store, forwarding, remote `Flag`, admin report routes) are left to #29, which remodels report and moderation state; local follows (`followAccount` in `server/follow-store.ts`) keep their D1 signature, because their three callers (the follow route, `followAccountRef`, the local-signer inbox `Follow`) apply different notify rules and a shared use case would change one of them; `findStatusAnywhere` and `listThreadDescendants` are not converted, as the first is a two-lookup adapter with no decision of its own and the second reads five stores for one breadth-first walk; and the store modules were not split by read and write, because callers already import single functions and the narrowing now happens in the contract a use case takes.

- Pure domain decisions in `packages/domain` (#26). These rules moved out of the worker, each with its own unit tests and no D1: `Hostname` (`ofAuthority`, `fromInput`, `ofUri`; the type domain blocks, suspensions and an account's blocked domains take, replacing `normalizeDomain`), `NotificationPolicy.disposition`, `NotificationGroup` (grouped types, group key, the fold of a page), `ContentFilter.match`, `StreamSubscription.authorize`, `OutboxBackfill.decide` / `onActorHost` (which outbox items a backfill stores, and the host checks for later pages and pinned notes), and `AccountActionType.remoteEffect` (what an admin action does to a remote actor). An open remote status is now `!Visibility.isRestricted` everywhere. The worker loads inputs and applies the decisions. One fix landed with it: a followers fan-out compared an inbox's `host[:port]` with the suspended hostnames, so a follower on a suspended host with a non-default port still got deliveries. Not done: `authorityHost` / `hostnameOf` stay in `server/domain-block-store.ts` as aliases of `Hostname.ofAuthority` / `ofUri` until the inbox rewrite (#28) stops importing them; move and alias verification belongs to #28; `visibility-guard.ts` and the link-card outcomes stay in the worker, because their decisions are interleaved with the lookups and fetches they decide on.

- Exhaustive inbox dispatch (#28). The inbox route (`server/routes/activitypub.ts`, 755 to 395 lines) now verifies the signature, parses, dedupes, and maps the result to a status; `dispatchInbox` in `server/inbox/dispatch.ts` switches on the signer (`Local | Remote`) and then on `activity.kind`, with `assertNever` in both switches, so a new `Activity` variant fails typecheck until both signers handle or decline it. Each kind has one module in `server/inbox/` with the shared `InboxHandler` signature (`Promise<Result<void, RepositoryError>>`); `Update`, `Move` and `Flag` call `applyRemoteUpdate`, `applyRemoteMove` and `receiveRemoteFlag` directly. Pure decisions sit beside their effects: `remoteDeleteEffect` (on `RemoteDeleteTarget.of`) and `remoteUndoTargets`. `server/inbox-local-signer.test.ts` pins the local-signer Follow, Undo, Like and Announce paths, which no suite covered. Behavior is unchanged, including the results that were already ignored: an `Update` storage failure still answers 202.

- PII and private keys as `Sensitive` (#25).
