The row schemas wrap `accounts.access_email` and `private_key_jwk`, `instance_actor_keys.private_key_jwk`, `signup_intents.email` and the Better Auth `session.ipAddress`; `VAPID_PRIVATE_JWK` is wrapped where `vapidKeys` parses it, a generated account key pair comes back wrapped, and the sign-up form and the canonical email block bodies wrap the submitted address. `Sensitive#unwrap` is now `reveal`, and the 18 calls outside tests (12 files) are listed with their reasons in `packages/core/src/sensitive-reveal-sites.test.ts`: the admin account page and API `email`, the mailer, the Better Auth `createUser` call, sign-up and canonical-hash block checks, request signing, and the SQL writes that store the values. Better Auth reads and writes its own `user` and `session` tables; our code wraps what it reads from them (the session IP) and reveals the address only where it hands it to `createUser`. The canonical email hash is not wrapped: Mastodon's admin API returns it, and it is a hash. Mastodon API output is unchanged. Workerd's `console.log` (with or without `nodejs_compat`) honors `util.inspect.custom`, so `Sensitive` needed no change for it; `server/sensitive-values.test.ts` checks `node:util` `inspect` / `format` in workerd and `packages/core/src/sensitive.test.ts` checks Node's console. Not done: the admin account search's `email` filter stays a plain string (it arrives in the GET query string, which request logs already hold), and `session.userAgent` is not wrapped.

- Parsed boundaries and no type assertions (#24). `queryTyped(db, sql, schema)` parses every TypedSQL row with a Zod schema and leaves out a row that does not match, as `parseRows` does; the hand-written row generics became module-level schemas. Every JSON column, JSON env var and queued payload is read through `parseJsonColumn` / `parseJsonText` (`server/json-column.ts`), so a corrupt filter keyword, profile field, link card or edit snapshot column reads as its fallback instead of throwing. Oxlint's `typescript/consistent-type-assertions` (`assertionStyle: "never"`) is on everywhere except Prisma's generated code; `schema-result.ts` keeps its two documented cache-read assertions. The `no-unsafe-*` rules fail an `any` reaching typed runtime code (tests, e2e specs and build scripts are exempt because test matchers are typed `any`). Behavior on valid data is unchanged; a corrupt row in a single lookup (account, access token, remote actor, remote status, local status, poll, list, outbound activity, OAuth code, signup intent) now reads as not found instead of a `RepositoryError`. Not done: raw `db.prepare(...).all<T>()` / `.first<T>()` generics (about 35 runtime sites) are still unchecked casts the lint cannot see.

- Severed relationships and the remote interaction helper (WEB-6, WEB-7). `/remote_interaction_helper` (`server/remote-interaction-helper.ts`) serves Mastodon's helper page: the `remote_interaction_helper` pack of the pinned release (an entry `scripts/build-mastodon-web.mjs` now records in `packs/tstodon-web.json`; a build from before has none and the page answers 404) with Mastodon's headers from `RemoteInteractionHelperController`: `Content-Security-Policy` (`default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; connect-src https:; form-action 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'self'`), `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and a five-minute public cache. The signed-out modal's WebFinger lookups run from that iframe. Before this change, a suspension removed no follows. A server suspension (`createDomainBlock` / `changeDomainBlock` ending at `suspend`, in `server/admin-actions.ts`) and a suspended remote account (`moderateRemoteActor` entering `Suspended`) now call `severAndNotify` (`server/severance.ts`), which, through `severRelationships` (`server/severed-relationship-store.ts`, migration 0099), writes one `relationship_severance_events` row (`domain_block` or `account_suspension`, with the domain or `user@host` as `target_name`), a `severed_relationships` row for each accepted follow it removes (`active` for a follow by a local account, `passive` for a follow of it, with the remote address kept as text so the list outlives the cached actor), and one `account_severance_events` row for each local account with its `followers_count` and `following_count`, then deletes the follows (`outbound_follows` and `remote_follows` of the matching actors) in the same D1 batch. Requests that were still pending are deleted unrecorded. Each account gets a `severed_relationships` notification from itself (`notifications.severance_event_id`; it bypasses the notification policy as the admin kinds do), carrying `event` (`id`, `type`, `purged`, `target_name`, `followers_count`, `following_count`, `created_at`) in `/api/v1/notifications` and in the groups of `/api/v2/notifications`. `/severed_relationships` (`server/routes/severed-relationships.ts`, `server/severed-pages.ts`; linked from the preferences index) lists the account's events newest first (at most `SEVERED_EVENTS_SHOWN` = 200) with `/severed_relationships/:id/following.csv` (`Account address,Show boosts,Notify on new posts,Languages`) and `…/followers.csv` (`Account address`), newest follow first, as Mastodon names and formats them; an id of another account's event is a 404. A user's own domain block records `user_domain_block` events (#60). Not done: `purged` is stored but nothing sets it (no domain purge exists); suspending a local account severs nothing, as in Mastodon, which hides and restores its follows; a remote `Delete` of an actor (`purgeRemoteActor`) cascades its follows away unrecorded, as Mastodon records no event for it; a domain suspension delivers no `Reject`/`Undo` to the suspended server (federation to it is refused), while a single actor's suspension does (FED-6, below); and an account that gains its first follow of the scope between the account list and the batch loses it without an event.

- D1 evidence instrumentation (#22). Queue passes of `FanOutHome`, `FanOutPublic`, `DistributePollUpdate`, `MoveFollows`, `BackfillRemoteOutbox` (per page), `BackfillRemoteThread`, `FanOutRemovedStatuses`, `ProcessCsvImport` and `CleanUpAccountStatuses` write `outbox.job.page` (size, elapsed ms, chained flag; kind, outcome, step) through `measureJobPage`; each fan-out recipient writes `stream.render`; the home, list, public and tag timelines write `timeline.<name>` through `writeTimelineMetric`. All best effort. `docs/architecture/d1-heavy-scenarios.md` documents every index and maps D1-1 to D1-5 to queries. Not done: D1 query counts per pass (D1 does not report them), a `MoveFollows` row count (the phase pages return only a cursor), and the D1-1..D1-5 decisions themselves, which need production data.

- Remote actor domains with ports. `remote_actors.domain` is the actor's `host[:port]` authority (`URL.host`), as Mastodon stores it, so a peer on a non-default port renders as `@bob@127.0.0.1:8792`, `@bob@127.0.0.1:8792` and `@bob@127.0.0.1:8793` are two accounts, and a handle with a port finds its cached actor (mentions accept `:port` too). Moderation names hostnames: a virtual generated column `remote_actors.host` (migration 0098, indexed) is the domain without its port, the timeline queries and `clearRemoteMediaForDomain` match blocks against it, TypeScript checks use `authorityHost()` (`server/domain-block-store.ts`), and `normalizeDomain` strips a typed port, so a block, suspension or media rule on `peer.test` covers every port. The allow list already matched by hostname. Migration 0097 recomputes `domain` from `actor_uri` for cached rows and rewrites existing block rows typed with a port to their hostname (dropping one that duplicates a bare-hostname block). Consequence: a port-specific block is not possible, and the instance peers list and admin filter name hostnames.

- Admin pages for per-post moderation, announcements, custom emojis, trends, email domain blocks, and a dashboard (WEB-5). `/admin/accounts/:id/statuses` (local or `r_…`) lists an account's own posts and, per selection, marks them sensitive or not sensitive or deletes them (a confirmation first); it runs `moderateStatuses` in `server/admin-status-actions.ts`, which deletes a local post through `deleteLocalStatus`, a remote one through the new `removeRemoteStatus` (`server/remote-status-delete.ts`, also the inbox's remote `Delete`), and tells followers and streams of a flag change through `announceNoteUpdate` (`server/local-status-update.ts`, also the edit route), which federates an `Update` for a local note except a direct one. The admin REST API has no such endpoint (Mastodon's is a web-only batch). `/admin/announcements` (list, create as a draft or published, edit, publish, unpublish, delete), `/admin/custom_emojis` (upload to `emoji/`, show or hide in the picker, delete; the server has no separate disabled flag, so "disable" is `visible_in_picker`), `/admin/trends/{tags,links,statuses}` (approve or reject, shown with the review state), and `/admin/email_domain_blocks` (list with the last five days' sign-ups, add, remove) call the same functions as their REST routes: `createAnnouncement` / `changeAnnouncement` (`server/admin-announcements.ts`), `uploadCustomEmoji` / `changeCustomEmoji` / `removeCustomEmoji` (`server/admin-custom-emojis.ts`), and `reviewTrend` (`server/admin-trends.ts`), extracted from the REST handlers, which now use them. `/admin` and `/admin/dashboard` show open reports, accounts awaiting approval, sign-ups in the last 7 days, and active users (`server/admin-dashboard-store.ts`; this server records no login time, so "active" means a refreshed browser session, a new access token, or a new post that week). One nav links all of them. Not done: editing an announcement's HTML keeps formatting only while its text is unchanged; saving changed text writes plain paragraphs. A direct note marked sensitive is not federated.

- Exclusive lists, list reply policies, and link timeline moderation. A list with `exclusive` keeps its members' posts and boosts off the owner's home: local members through `EXCLUSIVE_LIST_MEMBER_ACCOUNTS_SQL` in `listHomeStatuses`, remote ones through `feedFollowSql` for `Home`, which drops the follows on an exclusive list from both the notes and the boosts query. Home is defined once, so the home stream follows (`server/home-stream.test.ts`). A list's `replies_policy` is now one SQL rule for local and remote statuses (`feedLocalRepliesSql` and `feedRepliesSql` in `server/followed-feed.ts`; `FollowedFeed` `List` carries `localActorPrefix` to recognise a remote reply to a local post by its URI). As in Mastodon's `FeedManager`, a reply to its own author or to the list's owner always passes; otherwise `none` drops replies, `followed` keeps replies to accounts the owner follows (local or remote, including remote replies to local posts), and `list` keeps replies to the list's members (local or remote). Replies whose parent author is unknown (an uncached remote parent) are dropped under `followed` and `list`. The link timeline drops the viewer's blocked and muted authors, local and remote, in SQL before its `LIMIT` (`listLinkStatuses` takes the viewer; `listLinkRemoteStatuses` adds `SUPPRESSED_ACTOR_URIS_SQL`).
- Favourites, boosts, and bookmarks of remote statuses. A local account favourites, boosts, and bookmarks a cached remote status as it does a local one. The target is a `StatusTarget` (`LocalStatus` | `RemoteStatus`, `packages/domain/src/status-target.ts`), which `RemoteBoost` and `LocalReblog` now share. In SQL, `favourites` and `bookmarks` (rebuilt by migration 0092, each with an `id`) name the status in exactly one of `local_status_id` / `remote_status_id`, and a `LocalReblog` row names a remote status in `statuses.reblog_of_remote_id`. Each of these goes with its status. A favourite of a remote status federates a `Like` (id `<actor>#likes/<favourite id>`) to the author's inbox, and unfavouriting federates the `Undo`. A boost federates the `Announce` to the booster's followers and the author, and unboosting federates the `Undo`. The boost shows on local followers' home timelines and on the home stream. Only public and unlisted remote statuses can be boosted. Favouriting, boosting, and bookmarking a status answer 404 when the viewer may not see it; the undo actions do not check. Remote statuses now render `favourited`, `reblogged`, `bookmarked`, `favourites_count`, and `reblogs_count` on every surface (`mastodonRemoteStatuses` in `server/remote-status-documents.ts` replaces the per-caller rendering). The counts are the local accounts' favourites and boosts only, because remote totals are not stored. `favourited_by` / `reblogged_by` on a remote status list those local accounts. `GET /api/v1/favourites` and `GET /api/v1/bookmarks` list local and remote statuses together, paged by status id. Not done: quoting a remote status (FEP-044f `QuoteRequest` and authorization), the remote side's `likes` / `shares` totals, interacting through a remote boost's own id. A content-changing inbox `Update` of a remote status notifies each local account that boosts it, once per edit, with an `update` notification (through `notifyFromRemote`, so blocks and mutes apply; `update` is not filterable, so the policy does not); poll count refreshes and replays notify nobody. Quotes are not notified because local quotes of remote statuses do not exist yet.

- Moderating a single remote account. An admin can limit (`silence`), suspend, or mark the media of one remote actor sensitive, as Mastodon allows for a remote account (no disable, no approve). The state is `RemoteActorModeration` (`packages/domain/src/remote-actor-moderation.ts`: a `standing` of `InGoodStanding`, `Silenced` or `Suspended`, plus `sensitized`), kept in `remote_actor_moderation` (migration 0093) keyed by actor URI with no foreign key, so it survives a purge; the default state has no row (`server/remote-actor-moderation-store.ts`). `GET /api/v1/admin/accounts?origin=remote` (also the `remote` flag) lists cached actors by username with `status`, `username`, `display_name` and `by_domain` filters, paged by `offset` because `r_…` ids are not ordered; `/accounts/r_…`, `/action` (`none`, `sensitive`, `silence`, `suspend`; `disable` answers 422), and the silence, suspend and sensitize endpoints take a remote id, while `enable`, `approve`, `reject` and `DELETE` still answer 404 for one. The page `/admin/accounts/r_…` and the report page's action buttons run the same `moderateRemoteActor` / `takeAccountAction` in `server/admin-actions.ts`. A limited actor drops off the public, tag, and direct timelines, and its notifications count under `for_limited_accounts`; followers still see it on home and lists. A suspended actor is refused at the inbox (403, except the `Delete` of its own account), hidden from every feed and its profile's statuses, rendered with `suspended: true` and its profile stripped (#43), dropped from notifications, and left out of search. Sensitive adds `sensitive` to the actor's statuses that carry media, in the shared remote-status renderer (`mastodonRemoteStatuses`, `server/remote-status-documents.ts`), so every surface gets it. Not done: a limited remote actor's `Follow` still goes through without a request, and a suspended actor's statuses are still served by id (`GET /api/v1/statuses/:id`), as a suspended domain's are.

- Data export and import (WEB-3). `/settings/export` downloads Mastodon's CSV files (`/settings/exports/{follows,lists,blocks,mutes,domain_blocks,bookmarks}.csv`, same columns and headers: follows `Account address,Show boosts,Notify on new posts,Languages`, mutes `Account address,Hide notifications`, the rest headerless). Local accounts are `user@<domain>`, remote ones `user@host` from the cached actor; `server/csv-export.ts` streams a response from `listEntriesPage` (`server/csv-entries-store.ts`, keyset pages of `EXPORT_PAGE_SIZE` = 200). `/settings/import` (also `/settings/imports`) takes a multipart upload of one type (the domain `ImportType` union: `Following`, `Lists`, `Blocks`, `Mutes`, `DomainBlocks`, `Bookmarks`) in one `ImportMode` (`Merge`, `Overwrite`); `/settings/imports/:id` shows progress and the failed rows. Limits are named constants in `server/csv-import-file.ts`: `MAX_IMPORT_BYTES` (1 MiB, checked against `Content-Length` before the body is read, then against the file), `MAX_IMPORT_ROWS` (5000 data rows, a bigger file is refused whole), per-cell caps, a UTF-8 and RFC 4180 parse (`server/csv.ts`, at most `CSV_MAX_COLUMNS` columns). Mastodon allows 20 MB; the lower caps bound a Worker's memory and the D1 rows one file stores. A row is validated by the same handle pattern as account lookups (no `/`, `#`, `?`, second `@`), domain host names, and local status URLs only. The file is stored as `csv_imports` and `csv_import_rows` (migration 0095) with a per-row `Pending`/`Applied`/`Failed` outcome (domain `RowOutcome`, failures `Malformed`, `UnknownAccount`, `Refused`, `NotFollowed`, `Unsupported`). A `ProcessCsvImport` queue job applies `IMPORT_PAGE_SIZE` (10) rows per run, resolves each handle (local lookup, cached remote actor, else WebFinger through `resolveAccountHandle`) and calls the existing follow (`followAccountRef`, extracted from `MoveFollows`), block, mute, domain block, list member and bookmark functions, then queues the next page `IMPORT_PAGE_DELAY_SECONDS` (2) later; one import runs per account (a `Queued` import older than 6 hours, or one whose job the queue gave up on, is `Failed`, shown as stopped, and stops blocking), and the latest `IMPORTS_KEPT` (5) are kept. An overwrite then pages through the account's existing entries and removes the ones the file does not name (for lists, only in lists the file names); it is refused at upload (a 303 back to the page with the reason) when a row cannot be read, since that row's entry could not be matched. Each row that resolves records the account it resolved to (`csv_import_rows.resolved`), and the overwrite keeps entries by that, not by the handle text, so a handle whose WebFinger domain differs from its actor's host (`harasser@example.com` served by `social.example.com`) is not pruned; a row that did not resolve still protects entries with the same handle text. A first row is a header only when every cell is a known column name and it is not itself a valid row, so a list called `title` or a user `acct` is data. Not done: a followed account in a list import is not followed for the user (a list takes accounts already followed, as the list API does), requested (not yet accepted) follows are neither exported nor removed by an overwrite, bookmarks of posts on other servers cannot exist here so they fail as `Unsupported`, and cells starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab, or CR (list titles, remote usernames) are exported with a leading `'` (cells starting with `'` get a second one) which an import strips (`guardCell`/`unguardCell` in `server/csv.ts`). One import page is `IMPORT_PAGE_SIZE` rows, but the consumer takes up to 10 jobs per invocation, so the lookup rate is bounded per import, not per server.

- Favourites, boosts, and bookmarks of remote statuses. A local account favourites, boosts, and bookmarks a cached remote status as it does a local one. The target is a `StatusTarget` (`LocalStatus` | `RemoteStatus`, `packages/domain/src/status-target.ts`), which `RemoteBoost` and `LocalReblog` now share. In SQL, `favourites` and `bookmarks` (rebuilt by migration 0092, each with an `id`) name the status in exactly one of `local_status_id` / `remote_status_id`, and a `LocalReblog` row names a remote status in `statuses.reblog_of_remote_id`. Each of these goes with its status. A favourite of a remote status federates a `Like` (id `<actor>#likes/<favourite id>`) to the author's inbox, and unfavouriting federates the `Undo`. A boost federates the `Announce` to the booster's followers and the author, and unboosting federates the `Undo`. The boost shows on local followers' home timelines and on the home stream. Only public and unlisted remote statuses can be boosted. Favouriting, boosting, and bookmarking a status answer 404 when the viewer may not see it; the undo actions do not check. Remote statuses now render `favourited`, `reblogged`, `bookmarked`, `favourites_count`, and `reblogs_count` on every surface (`mastodonRemoteStatuses` in `server/remote-status-documents.ts` replaces the per-caller rendering). The counts are the local accounts' favourites and boosts only, because remote totals are not stored. `favourited_by` / `reblogged_by` on a remote status list those local accounts. `GET /api/v1/favourites` and `GET /api/v1/bookmarks` list local and remote statuses together, paged by status id. Not done: quoting a remote status (FEP-044f `QuoteRequest` and authorization), the remote side's `likes` / `shares` totals, interacting through a remote boost's own id, and `update` notifications to local boosters when a boosted remote status is edited.

- Password and security (`/auth/edit`), linked from the settings index. A signed-in user lists, adds, renames, and removes passkeys, lists and ends browser sessions, and revokes the apps that hold an OAuth token of the account (`server/account-security-store.ts`, `server/account-security-pages.ts`, `server/routes/account-security.ts`). Writes are form posts with the same-origin check, scoped by owner in the `WHERE` (Better Auth user id for passkeys and sessions, local account id for apps). Removing a passkey is one `DELETE` that refuses when it would leave none; ending a session, removing a passkey, adding a passkey, and revoking an app need a sign-in within `RECENT_SIGN_IN_WINDOW_MS` (10 minutes, shared with `/settings/migration`); a stale page offers a passkey sign-in and leaves those forms out. Adding a passkey calls Better Auth's registration endpoints from the page; `app/auth.ts` closes every Better Auth endpoint the pages do not call (#55) and applies the recent sign-in rule to registration by a signed-in user. The web app's own token is not an app: it belongs to its browser session, and ending the session ends it. Ending the current session signs the browser out.

- Automated post deletion (WEB-4). `/statuses_cleanup` (`server/routes/statuses-cleanup.ts`, linked from the preferences index) edits an account's policy as Mastodon's page does: on or off, minimum age (1 or 2 weeks, 1, 2 or 6 months, 1 or 2 years), keep pinned, direct, self-favourited, self-bookmarked, polls, media, and keep posts with at least N favourites or N boosts. The policy is the domain type `StatusesCleanupPolicy` (`Enabled` or `Disabled`, both keeping the rules) with the pure `shouldDelete(policy, facts, now)`; it lives in `statuses_cleanup_policies` (migration 0090, a missing row is the default: off, 2 weeks, Mastodon's keep defaults). The hourly cron queues `CleanUpStatuses`, which queues one `CleanUpAccountStatuses` per enabled account (`server/statuses-cleanup.ts`). A run looks at up to `CLEANUP_SCAN_BATCH` (100) of the account's notes older than the minimum age after `last_inspected_id`, and deletes up to `CLEANUP_DELETE_BATCH` (20) through `deleteLocalStatus` (`server/local-status-delete.ts`, extracted from `DELETE /api/v1/statuses/:id`, which now calls it), so streams, fan-out and the federated `Delete` fire as for a manual delete. The cursor keeps a long run of kept posts from blocking the older ones behind it and resets at the end or when the policy is saved. Favourite and boost counts are the local plus remote counts the status API shows. Not done: boosts are not deleted (the `Undo(Announce)` is built in the unreblog route, and `deleteLocalStatus` federates notes only). Suspended and disabled accounts are skipped.

- Admin notifications. Filing a report (`POST /api/v1/reports`) sends every admin except the reporter an `admin.report` notification, and every local sign-up, approval-required or not, sends the admins an `admin.sign_up` one (`server/admin-notify.ts`, through `notifyAccount`, so streaming and push follow). Admins are the local accounts whose Better Auth `user.role` holds `admin` (`listAdminAccountIds`); `ADMIN_USERNAMES` only decides who signs up as one. Both kinds skip the notification policy, as Mastodon's non-filterable types do. `notifications.report_id` points an `admin.report` at its report, which the v1 notification and the grouped v2 group render as Mastodon's `Report` (with `target_account`; its `id` is the one `/admin/reports/:id` takes). A report from another server notifies the admins too (see Federated reports).
- Federated reports. Either side of a report is a local account or a remote actor (`ReportParty`, `Local` or `Remote`; `reports.account_id`/`reporter_actor_uri` and `target_account_id`/`target_actor_uri`, one of each pair set). A signed `Flag` in the inbox (`server/remote-flag.ts`) files a report by the sending actor on the first local account it names, or on the author of the posts it names, with the listed posts that are that account's, category `other`, and the comment capped at 1000 characters; `reports.flag_uri` keeps a redelivered `Flag` from filing twice, and a domain block with `reject_reports` drops it. `POST /api/v1/reports` accepts a remote account (`r_…`) and its remote posts, keeps only the target's own posts, and answers 404 for an account it does not know; with `forward=true` on a remote account it sends a `Flag` (account URI, then the posts' object URIs, and the comment) from the instance actor to the account's inbox and sets `forwarded` (`server/report-forward.ts`). The instance actor is Mastodon's `Application` at `/actor` (inbox `/actor/inbox`, WebFinger `acct:<domain>@<domain>`); its key pair is generated on first use into `instance_actor_keys`, and an `outbound_activities` row with a NULL `account_id` is signed with it (`server/instance-actor.ts`). `forward_to_domains[]` also forwards the report, as the same `Flag`, to the shared inbox of the cached actor each requested domain names, but only for domains of accounts the reported posts reply to (Mastodon's `referenced_domains`, minus the target's own server, which `forward` covers); other domains are ignored, and a suspended domain or one outside the allow list is dropped by the delivery queue (`repliedToInboxes`, `forwardReportToDomains`). A report keeps the `user@domain` of each remote party (`reports.reporter_acct`, `target_acct`), so the admin views name an actor that was purged from `remote_actors`.
- Admin web pages. The web app's admin links land on server-rendered pages (`server/admin-pages.ts`, `server/routes/admin-web/`) for an admin's browser session; others get 403. `/admin/reports` lists open or resolved reports; `/admin/reports/:id` shows the comment, posts, and rules, resolves, reopens, and assigns, and runs the account action (none, sensitive, disable, silence, suspend) that resolves the report. `/admin/accounts` searches local accounts by status, username, and email; `/admin/accounts/:id` approves, rejects, limits, suspends, sensitizes, disables, or deletes, asking again before suspend, reject, and delete. `/admin/instances` and `/admin/instances/:domain` create, change, and lift domain blocks. The pages and `/api/v1/admin` share `server/admin-actions.ts`. `/admin` and `/admin/dashboard` redirect to the reports; the preferences index links the pages for admins.
- Settings from the web UI. The web app's Rails links land on server-rendered pages in the style of the sign-in pages (`server/settings-pages.ts`, `server/routes/preferences.ts`). `/settings/preferences` (and `/settings`, which redirects there) is a signed-in index of the settings the server has: profile (`/profile/edit` in the web app), privacy and reach, posting defaults, follow requests, mutes, blocks, domain blocks, followed hashtags, filters, and both account migration pages. `/settings/preferences/posting_defaults` writes `source[privacy]`, `source[quote_policy]`, `source[language]` and `source[sensitive]`; `/settings/privacy` writes `locked`, `discoverable`, `indexable` and `hide_collections`, with Mastodon's checkbox ids so `#account_unlocked` and `#account_discoverable` work. `/settings/profile` redirects to `/profile/edit`. Other Rails links get a 200 page that says the feature is not available and what to use instead (see WEB-2 to WEB-7); a 404 would be replaced by `index.html` through Void's SPA fallback on a browser navigation. Two fixes came out of it: `isSameOriginPost` now trusts `Sec-Fetch-Site` alone when present, because the pages' `no-referrer` policy makes a browser post their forms with `Origin: null` (every settings form and the OAuth consent form were refused in a real browser), and `source[sensitive]` sent on its own is saved. `/filters`, `/filters/new` and `/filters/:id/edit` (`server/routes/filter-settings.ts`, `server/filter-form.ts`, `server/filter-pages.ts`) list, create, edit and delete the filters of `/api/v2/filters`: title, expiry, contexts, warn or hide, keyword rows (three blank rows per page, a keyword is removed with its checkbox), and removing attached posts. A filter needs at least one keyword and one context. Not done: the `blur` action, and adding more than three keywords per save needs a second save (no client script). Not done: appearance and notification preferences (`/settings/preferences/appearance`, `/settings/preferences/notifications`) reach the generic settings notice.

- Remote timeline paging. Every remote timeline query leaves out the actors on domains the viewer blocks (`account_domain_blocks`) or the instance hides inside the SQL, before its `LIMIT`, so a page is no longer cut short or emptied by them (`server/domain-hidden-actors.ts`: `DOMAIN_RESTRICTED_ACTORS_SQL` for silenced and suspended domains on public surfaces, `DOMAIN_SUSPENDED_ACTORS_SQL` where followers still see silenced ones). That covers the public remote, tag, link, direct, home and list notes and boosts, a remote profile's notes, and its pinned notes. Domains compare case-insensitively. `remoteTimelineDocuments` no longer filters by domain. The public remote query (`listPublicRemoteStatuses`) now orders by `id` and takes `max_id`/`since_id`/`min_id` through `idCursorBounds` (`server/id-cursor.ts`), like the local public query; it used to order by `published_at` and ignore the cursor, so paging Federated repeated its first page. The merged local and remote public pages follow the same cursor.

- Remote profile backfill. A view of `GET /api/v1/accounts/r_…/statuses` claims the actor with one `UPDATE` on `remote_actors.outbox_fetched_at` (`OUTBOX_BACKFILL_TTL_MS`, 24h) and queues one `BackfillRemoteOutbox { actorUri }` job; the response shows what is cached. `remote_actors` now keeps `outbox_uri` and `featured_uri` from the actor document (migration 0082); an actor cached before that is refetched once by the job. The job reads the outbox's first page, at most `COLLECTION_PAGE_ITEM_LIMIT` (20) items (`server/remote-collection.ts`), and when the page has a `next` link on the actor's host it queues `BackfillRemoteOutbox { actorUri, from: { kind: "Page", pageUri, depth } }` for the following page, up to `OUTBOX_BACKFILL_PAGE_LIMIT` (5) pages (100 items), one page per queued job so a deep outbox never grows an invocation's subrequests. A page job reads no pins. Per page: a public or unlisted `Create` of the actor's own note from its host is stored through `persistRemoteObject`, an `Announce` through `persistAnnouncedObject` (third-party origin checks) and `recordRemoteBoost`; embedded objects from another host or attributed to someone else are not trusted, and cached items are no-ops. Backfilled notes and boosts take ids from their `published` time (`entityIdAt`, zero-padded to 18 digits so string order is time order before mid-2018), as Mastodon's Snowflake ids do, so they sort where they were written on the profile, home, and lists instead of looking new; nothing is fanned out and no mention is notified. The `featured` collection (same cap) fills `remote_actor_pins` (migration 0083), replaced on each backfill and kept when the collection cannot be read; `pinned=true` on a remote profile lists those notes. Opening a remote status (`GET /api/v1/statuses/:id/context` of a public or unlisted one) claims it with `remote_statuses.context_fetched_at` (migration 0096, `THREAD_BACKFILL_TTL_MS`, 1h) and queues `BackfillRemoteThread { statusId }` (`server/remote-thread-backfill.ts`). The job walks up `in_reply_to_uri` and stores up to `THREAD_BACKFILL_ANCESTOR_LIMIT` (5) missing ancestors through `persistAnnouncedObject` (its origin checks and author resolution), then reads the status's own `replies` collection (embedded or linked, first page) and stores up to `THREAD_BACKFILL_REPLY_LIMIT` (20) of them; nothing is fanned out, and the next view shows the result. Not done: account lookup triggering a backfill, paging a long `replies` collection, and fetching the descendants of fetched replies (opening a reply fetches its own).
- Remote accounts on profiles and lists. `GET /api/v1/accounts/r_…/statuses` lists the cached actor's notes and boosts, newest first, with `max_id`/`since_id`/`min_id`, `limit`, `exclude_replies`, `exclude_reblogs`, `only_media`, and `tagged` (the last three leave boosts out, as in Mastodon). Public and unlisted entries are open to everyone, followers-only ones to accepted followers, and notes that mention the viewer to that viewer. A boost needs a public or unlisted target whose author the viewer does not block or mute. The list is empty when the viewer blocks the actor or its domain or the instance suspends that domain, and for `pinned=true`. An uncached actor answers 404. A list member is now `{ kind: "LocalAccount", accountId } | { kind: "RemoteActor", actorUri }`. Remote members live in `account_list_remote_members` (migration 0081: `list_id`, `outbound_follow_id`, both `ON DELETE CASCADE`). Mastodon's `ListAccount` belongs to a `Follow` in the same way, so unfollowing, a `Reject`, a `Move`, or an actor purge drops the memberships. `POST /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts` takes `r_…` ids of accepted follows and answers 422 for any other remote account, adding nothing. `DELETE`, `GET /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts`, and `GET /api/v1/accounts/r_…/lists` handle both kinds. The list timeline uses the same pipeline as home (`feedDocuments` in `server/timeline-documents.ts`, with a `FollowedFeed` of `Home` or `List`), so blocks, mutes, muted conversations, domain blocks, filters, and the follow's `languages`/`reblogs` apply to remote members' notes and boosts. It now pages by all three id cursors. `replies_policy: none` drops remote replies, and `followed` keeps those that answer a followed remote actor. Not done: `exclusive` lists hiding their members from home (local members are not hidden either), `replies_policy: list` limiting replies to list members (local or remote), `followed` counting remote replies to local posts, carrying list memberships over on a `Move` as Mastodon does, and the `list` stream.

- Remote profile backfill. A view of `GET /api/v1/accounts/r_…/statuses` claims the actor with one `UPDATE` on `remote_actors.outbox_fetched_at` (`OUTBOX_BACKFILL_TTL_MS`, 24h) and queues one `BackfillRemoteOutbox { actorUri }` job; the response shows what is cached. `remote_actors` now keeps `outbox_uri` and `featured_uri` from the actor document (migration 0082); an actor cached before that is refetched once by the job. The job reads the outbox's first page, at most `OUTBOX_BACKFILL_ITEM_LIMIT` (20) items: a public or unlisted `Create` of the actor's own note from its host is stored through `persistRemoteObject`, an `Announce` through `persistAnnouncedObject` (third-party origin checks) and `recordRemoteBoost`; embedded objects from another host or attributed to someone else are not trusted, and cached items are no-ops. Backfilled notes and boosts take ids from their `published` time (`entityIdAt`, zero-padded to 18 digits so string order is time order before mid-2018), as Mastodon's Snowflake ids do, so they sort where they were written on the profile, home, and lists instead of looking new; nothing is fanned out and no mention is notified. The `featured` collection (same cap) fills `remote_actor_pins` (migration 0083), replaced on each backfill and kept when the collection cannot be read; `pinned=true` on a remote profile lists those notes. Not done: a second outbox page, account lookup triggering a backfill, and backfilling replies or threads.
- Remote accounts on profiles and lists. `GET /api/v1/accounts/r_…/statuses` lists the cached actor's notes and boosts, newest first, with `max_id`/`since_id`/`min_id`, `limit`, `exclude_replies`, `exclude_reblogs`, `only_media`, and `tagged` (the last three leave boosts out, as in Mastodon). Public and unlisted entries are open to everyone, followers-only ones to accepted followers, and notes that mention the viewer to that viewer. A boost needs a public or unlisted target whose author the viewer does not block or mute. The list is empty when the viewer blocks the actor or its domain or the instance suspends that domain, and for `pinned=true`. An uncached actor answers 404. A list member is now `{ kind: "LocalAccount", accountId } | { kind: "RemoteActor", actorUri }`. Remote members live in `account_list_remote_members` (migration 0081: `list_id`, `outbound_follow_id`, both `ON DELETE CASCADE`). Mastodon's `ListAccount` belongs to a `Follow` in the same way, so unfollowing, a `Reject`, a `Move`, or an actor purge drops the memberships. `POST /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts` takes `r_…` ids of accepted follows and answers 422 for any other remote account, adding nothing. `DELETE`, `GET /api/v1/lists/:id/accounts`, and `GET /api/v1/accounts/r_…/lists` handle both kinds. The list timeline uses the same pipeline as home (`feedDocuments` in `server/timeline-documents.ts`, with a `FollowedFeed` of `Home` or `List`), so blocks, mutes, muted conversations, domain blocks, filters, and the follow's `languages`/`reblogs` apply to remote members' notes and boosts. It now pages by all three id cursors. `replies_policy: none` drops remote replies, and `followed` keeps those that answer a followed remote actor. Not done: carrying list memberships over on a `Move` as Mastodon does, and the `list` stream.

- Public, hashtag, and list streaming. Each streaming socket keeps its own subscriptions in its WebSocket attachment (so hibernation keeps them): the `stream` query parameter (with `tag` / `list`) and later `subscribe` / `unsubscribe` messages, which is how the web UI multiplexes every column over one socket. The streams are `user`, `user:notification`, `direct`, `public`, `public:local`, `public:remote`, their `:media` variants, `hashtag`, `hashtag:local`, and `list`. Each event is tagged with the stream it was subscribed on (`["hashtag", "cats"]`, `["list", "<id>"]`); notifications now reach `user` sockets as `["user"]`, since the web UI ignored the old `["user", "user:notification"]`. Every stream needs a user's token (see the entry on tokenless streaming above), and a list its owner (`Not authorized to stream this list`). Scopes follow Mastodon's streaming server: every stream but `user:notification` needs `read` or `read:statuses`, `user:notification` needs `read` or `read:notifications`, and `user` carries notification events only when the token also has `read:notifications`; a refusal is the `{"error": "Access token does not have the required scopes", "status": 401}` frame on connect and on `subscribe`. The socket attachment keeps two flags derived from the scopes. The `direct` stream carries `conversation` events: a new local direct note (author and mentioned local accounts) or remote direct note mentioning a local account sends each of them the Conversation entity their conversation list would show (`publishConversationUpdate`), so a blocked or muted author, or a conversation the account removed, sends nothing. Marking a conversation read or unread (`POST /api/v1/conversations/:id/read` or `/unread`) sends the account's own `direct` streams the updated entity (`publishConversationMarked`); removing one sends nothing, as in Mastodon, where only the account conversation's update pushes. A `StreamHub` mirrors its sockets' subscriptions (all but `user:notification`) into `stream_subscriptions` (migration 0084), one row per distinct channel: inserted before the first socket takes it and deleted after the last lets go, with every change run in arrival order; a row left behind is removed when a fan-out finds no socket behind it. A hashtag stream needs a hashtag name (letters, digits, underscores, at least one letter, at most 100 UTF-16 units; otherwise `Invalid tag name parameter`). A socket holds at most 32 streams (`MAX_SOCKET_CHANNELS`) and a hub at most 32 distinct hashtag and list streams (`MAX_HUB_TAGGED_CHANNELS`, public streams not counted); past either the subscribe gets a 429 error frame and no row. A public local note, a public remote note delivered live to the inbox (never a backfilled one), and their edits and deletes queue `FanOutPublic { event, entryId, afterHub }`. It pages the hubs holding a public or hashtag subscription that may carry the status (20 per job) and asks `publicTimelineDocuments` / `tagTimelineDocuments` (the functions the REST timelines use) for that one entry for the hub's viewer, so blocks, mutes, domain blocks, and `filtered` apply as on the timeline; `delete` goes to every public and hashtag subscription. The home fan-out also looks up its page of followers' open list streams and asks `listTimelineDocuments` for the entry. Cost: one query per page of hubs, then one timeline query per matching subscription per hub (not per socket); hubs without such a subscription cost nothing. A page stops before the hub that would take it past 38 timeline passes and queues the rest. A hashtag matches when the status's text holds `#tag` as a plain substring (local) or its `remote_status_tags` row (remote); local statuses still have no tag table, so `#cats` also matches `#catsitting`, as the tag timeline does. The public and tag timelines now also leave out authors the viewer blocks or mutes, in their queries so a page is filled past them, and the public timeline takes `only_media`. Server-sent events: a GET without an upgrade on `/api/v1/streaming/user`, `/user/notification`, `/direct`, `/public[/local|/remote][/media]` (or `?only_media=true`), `/hashtag[/local]?tag=`, `/list?list=` answers a `text/event-stream` of `event:` / `data:` frames (the WebSocket payload without the `stream` tag), `:)` first and a `:thump` comment every 15 s (`SSE_HEARTBEAT_MS`); `/api/v1/streaming/health` answers `OK` to anyone. The token comes from `Authorization` or `access_token`, and scope checks, refusals (401 with Mastodon's `error` body, 400 for a missing or invalid tag, 404 for an unknown path), and the 32-tagged-stream hub cap are the WebSocket's (`requestStreamChannel`, `StreamHub.admit`). One SSE connection holds one stream. The streaming route holds the SSE response and its heartbeat (`eventStreamResponse` in `server/sse-bridge.ts`) and opens a WebSocket to the hub tagged `event-stream`, on which the hub sends ready SSE frames; the hub accepts it with the hibernation API like any socket, so an open SSE connection no longer keeps the Durable Object awake. A client disconnect closes that socket, and the hub drops the `stream_subscriptions` row once no other socket holds it; a hub restarted by a deploy closes the socket, which ends the SSE response. A client that stops reading is dropped after 256 queued frames. A hub takes at most 32 SSE connections (`MAX_EVENT_STREAMS`, 429 past it).
- Home streaming like Mastodon's `FanOutOnWriteService`: a local post, local boost, local edit, local delete, and unboost, and an inbox `Create`, `Announce`, `Undo{Announce}`, `Delete`, and note `Update` from a remote actor, queue a `FanOutHome { event, entryId, author, afterAccountId }` job. The job pages the author's local followers (accepted local `follows` and accepted `outbound_follows`), 20 per job, and chains a job for the next page, like `MoveFollows`. For `update` and `status.update`, each follower gets the entry only when `homeTimelineDocuments` (the function `GET /api/v1/timelines/home` uses) returns it for that follower under the `onlyId` cursor. So blocks, mutes, muted conversations, domain blocks, and follow `reblogs`/`languages` apply as on the timeline, and the document is rendered for that follower, with its `filtered` matches. `delete` goes to every follower. A redelivered job only re-sends the events. The author still hears its own post at once, and now also its boost, edit (as `status.update`), delete, and unboost. The `list` streams ride on this fan-out (see public, hashtag, and list streaming above). A deleted status, local or remote, takes the local boosts of it with it and tells the boosters and their followers (`announceRemovedBoosts`: `delete` on the booster's streams and a `FanOutHome` `delete` for each boost id). A local delete removes them in the same batch as the status (`deleteStatus`, since the foreign key would only null their target), a remote `Delete` collects them before the `reblog_of_remote_id` cascade (`deleteRemoteStatusByObjectUri`), and a purge of the actor does the same for all its statuses. A deleted actor's statuses are announced to its followers as described under the inbox account `Delete`.

- Remote boosts on home: a signed inbox `Announce` of a local note or of a remote note becomes a `remote_boosts` row (migration 0080: its own id from the status id space, the booster's `actor_uri`, the unique `activity_id`, exactly one of `local_status_id` / `remote_status_id`, the `Announce`'s visibility from its `to`/`cc`, `published_at`). As in Mastodon, only a public or unlisted status can be boosted, a direct `Announce` is dropped, and an actor keeps one boost per status. A remote note that is not cached yet is fetched from its URI: the booster's own note may come embedded, another actor's note must name that URI as its id and come from its author's host, and is stored under its author. A local note still gets its `remote_announces` row and the author's `reblog` notification. `Undo{Announce}` signed by the booster deletes the row by the Announce id; deleting the boosted status or purging the booster cascades. The home timeline merges the boosts of accepted remote follows with `reblogs` on, by the same id cursors, leaving out blocked or muted boosters and original authors and their blocked or suspended domains. A boost renders as a Mastodon status with the booster as `account`, the local or remote status as `reblog`, and the `Announce` id as `uri`, and `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id` serves it (a followers-only boost only to the booster's followers). Not done: remote boosts on public and tag timelines (Mastodon keeps boosts off them), `filtered` and muted threads on boosts, and Mastodon's reblog aggregation on home.

- Account migration, as Mastodon's: `/settings/aliases` and `/settings/migration` are server-rendered pages in the style of the sign-in pages (Mastodon keeps both in Rails views, outside the web app), driven by the Better Auth session cookie with the same-origin form check. An alias ("moving from") resolves a handle by WebFinger, or a bare or local-domain username to a local account, and is stored in `account_aliases` (migration 0085); the actor document lists the aliases as `alsoKnownAs`. A move ("moving to") resolves the new account, reads its `alsoKnownAs` fresh (a remote document is fetched, a local account's aliases are read from D1), and refuses when this account is missing. It needs a session under `RECENT_SIGN_IN_WINDOW_MS` (10 minutes, `server/recent-sign-in.ts`) old; the page signs in again with the passkey first when it is older, since there is no password to ask for. The move is recorded in `account_moves` (migration 0086, one row per account) by one conditional upsert that enforces `MOVE_COOLDOWN_MS` (30 days) before moving elsewhere; repeating the same move resends the `Move` and keeps the time. A `Move{actor, object: self, target}` goes through `ExpandFollowers`, so remote followers sharing an inbox get it once. Local followers move through the paged `MoveFollows` path the inbound `Move` uses (`moveFollowPage` takes `AccountRef`s for both ends): a local follow of the new account, or an outbound follow when it is remote, then the unfollow and a `move` notification. The account renders `moved` and the actor `movedTo`. An inbound `Move` to a local target now reads the target's aliases from D1 instead of fetching its own actor URL, and a remote account moved to a local one renders it under `moved`. The web UI reaches both pages through `/settings/preferences` (see "Settings from the web UI").
- Account migration follow-ups (#11). Federation: `sendActorUpdate` (`server/local-actor.ts`, which also builds the actor document the route serves) queues an `Update{object: <Person>}` through `ExpandFollowers` after a move and after a cancel, so remote servers that cached the actor see `movedTo` appear or go. An inbound actor `Update` now sets `remote_actors.moved_to` when its `movedTo` names an account whose document lists the signer in `alsoKnownAs` (same check as a `Move`), and clears it when the document names none; only a `Move` carries followers over. A server that has no follower of the account left (all moved) hears nothing from the cancel's `Update`, as in Mastodon. Cancel: `POST /settings/migration/cancel` ("Cancel redirect" on the page, no re-authentication as in Mastodon) sets `account_moves.cancelled_at` (migration 0091). The row stays, so the cooldown still counts from `moved_at` and applies to any new move, the cancelled target included; `findAccountMove` answers the standing move only (actor, `moved`, limits), `findAccountMoveRecord` answers `Moved | Cancelled` for the cooldown. Followers already moved stay moved, and a queued `MoveFollows` job stops at its next page because the move no longer stands. Carry-over (`server/move-carry.ts`, as `MoveWorker`): the `MoveFollows` job gained `phase` (`follows`, `notes`, `blocks`, `mutes`; a queued job without it is `follows`) and `moveCarryPage` walks the phases one page of `MOVE_PAGE_SIZE` each, so a request costs at most four pages and larger audiences chain jobs. Follows carry their list memberships (`replaceListMember`): `to` goes on the lists that held `from` before the follow of `from` is dropped, local or remote on either side; a remote `to` is on the list through the follow while it is still `Requested` (`account_list_remote_members` rows may point at a requested follow, `insertListRemoteMemberOfFollow`), and a follower whose follow is refused keeps `from` and its lists. Notes: copied to `to` with Mastodon's lead text, or merged in front of an existing note; a copy over 2000 characters is skipped (or saved bare when there was no note); redoing a page leaves a note that already leads with the text alone. Blocks and mutes: carried for local accounts that do not already block/mute or follow `to` (the old block/mute stays), mutes with the same notification choice and no expiry, an expired mute is dropped, and each new block or mute leaves a note ("which you had blocked/muted") when the account has none. Indexes on `blocks`/`mutes`/`account_notes` by `target_actor_uri` (migration 0091) back the pages. Fixed on the way: account notes on remote accounts were never shown in the relationship or cleared (read and delete used the `r_` id, not the actor URI). Limits of a moved account: Mastodon 4.4 refuses a moved user in `Api::BaseController#require_user!` (`User#functional?` is false once `moved_to_account_id` is set: 403 `Your login is currently disabled`), which every authenticated controller calls. `requireFunctionalUser` (`server/http.ts`) gives that answer for posting, editing, following, favouriting and boosting; the other write endpoints and reads still work. Not done: the remaining `require_user!` endpoints (unfollow, unfavourite, unboost, delete, bookmarks, lists, blocks and mutes, polls, media, settings) and Mastodon's redirect of the web UI to the settings pages for a moved login; a list membership of a local `from` for an account that does not follow it.

- A followed remote account moving: an inbox `Move{object: A, target: B}` signed by A counts only when `object` is the signer and B's actor document, fetched fresh through the federated fetch (allow list and suspended domains apply), lists A in `alsoKnownAs`; anything else is a 202 no-op. B is cached and A's `remote_actors.moved_to` (migration 0077, written only by a verified `Move`, untouched by actor refreshes) points at it, so `GET /api/v1/accounts/r_A` renders `moved` with B's account. Every local `outbound_follows` row of A (`Requested` or `Accepted`) moves as in Mastodon's `UnfollowFollowWorker`: follow B as `Requested` with the same reblogs/notify/languages and send `Follow` to B's inbox (a follow of B that already exists is kept untouched), then delete the follow of A and send it `Undo(Follow)`, then notify the follower with a `move` notification (`account` A, `target` B via `notifications.target_account_id`, migration 0078). A follower who blocks B, has a domain block for B's domain, or whose federation limits refuse B keeps the follow of A and gets nothing. The work is idempotent (a redelivered `Move` finds no rows left). Fan-out is paged: the `Move` itself moves the first `MOVE_PAGE_SIZE` (50) follows in id order (index in migration 0079) and a full page queues a `MoveFollows { fromActorUri, toActorUri, afterId }` job on `OUTBOX_PROCESS_QUEUE` for the next, so one request does bounded work and large followings need no inline cap. Followers of local accounts need nothing: B sends its own `Follow`, as Mastodon expects. Follow-ups: retrying a `Move` whose target fetch failed (the inbox records the activity id first, so a redelivery is dropped), `move` in grouped notifications' push alerts.

- Following remote accounts: `POST /api/v1/accounts/r_…/follow` records the follow in `outbound_follows` (migration 0076, one row per account and actor with its `follow_activity_id`, `state` `Requested` or `Accepted`, and the `reblogs`/`notify`/`languages` options) and sends `Follow` to the actor's inbox once; following again only updates the options. A suspended domain, one outside the allow list, a blocked actor, or a blocked domain answers 403. A signed `Accept` from the followed actor (naming our `Follow` id, or embedding a `Follow` of it by a local actor) makes it `Accepted`; a `Reject` deletes it; another actor's answer is ignored. `unfollow` deletes the row and sends `Undo(Follow)`. Accepted remote follows count in `following_count`, list in `/api/v1/accounts/:id/following` and the AP `following` collection, and show in the Relationship (`requested` until accepted). The home timeline merges the followed actors' public, unlisted, and followers-only notes (follow `languages[]`, blocks, mutes, domain blocks, suspended domains, and muted threads applied; silenced domains still shown to followers), and followers-only remote notes open for accepted followers. Remote notes now take their language from `contentMap`. The row id is the cursor and `actor_uri` can be re-pointed, so a `Move` can carry a follow over. Follow-ups: `notify` status notifications for remote posts, `followed_by` from `remote_follows`, severing follows when a domain is suspended later, `Undo(Follow)` when a local account is deleted, and `following=true` account search for remote accounts.

- Poll `Update(Question)` debounce, as Mastodon's `DistributePollUpdateWorker`: a vote (remote on a local poll, or local through `POST /api/v1/polls/:id/votes`) on a poll that shows its totals claims `polls.update_queued_at` (migration 0075, one `UPDATE ... WHERE update_queued_at IS NULL RETURNING id`) and only the claiming vote sends a `DistributePollUpdate { pollId }` job with `delaySeconds: 180` (`POLL_UPDATE_DELAY_SECONDS` in `server/poll-update.ts`); a failed send releases the claim. The job clears the claim first, so later votes queue a fresh job, then federates one `Update(Question)` with the counts as they are. Local votes sent no `Update` before; they do now, so remote audiences see them.

- Local polls federate as Mastodon polls: `noteDocument` serializes a note with a poll as a `Question` (`oneOf`/`anyOf` options with `replies.totalItems`, `endTime`, `closed` once expired, `votersCount` via the `toot:` context term; counts withheld while `hide_totals` is on) in the `Create`, the note document, the stored outbox `Create` and the edit `Update`. An inbox `Create` of a content-less `Note{name, inReplyTo: <local poll note>}` is a vote: counted in `remote_poll_votes` (migration 0073; single choice takes one option per actor, multiple choice one vote per option, expired polls, unknown options, repeats and followers-only polls from non-followers are ignored), never stored as a remote status and never a mention notification; counts and `voters_count` (now a real distinct-voter count for every poll) include remote votes, and the counted votes federate an `Update(Question)` to the note's audience (debounced, see below). Follow-ups: the outbox collection serves the `Create` as stored, so its poll counts are those at posting time; purge a deleted remote actor's poll votes, and decrement counts when a vote is retracted.

- Inbox account `Delete`: a remote actor deleting itself (signed with its cached key, object equal to its own URI, bare or embedded) purges its `remote_actors` row, which cascades to its statuses, follows, favourites and announces, plus its notifications and the blocks, mutes, endorsements and notes keyed by its URI. Another actor's URI is ignored, and a repeat from an already purged actor is a 202. The same batch also fails the queued `outbox_deliveries` to its inbox (state `Failed`/`Permanent`/410; a shared inbox stays while another cached actor uses it) and drops `status_mutes`, `conversation_reads` and `conversation_hides` rows keyed by its statuses. `updateOutboxTarget` only writes rows still `Queued`, so a delivery workflow that was mid-attempt or sleeping cannot revive a cancelled row and stops at its next `load` step. Before the purge `purgeRemoteActorAndAnnounce` (`server/remote-actor-purge.ts`) reads the actor's newest `PURGE_ANNOUNCED_ENTRY_LIMIT` (20) non-direct statuses and boosts and up to `PURGE_ANNOUNCED_FOLLOWER_LIMIT` (1000) local followers (the follows go with the purge, so a `FanOutHome` job could no longer find them), then queues `FanOutRemovedStatuses { accountIds, statusIds }` jobs of at most 20 x 20 that send each follower's `user` and list streams a `delete`. Older statuses and followers past the cap drop out on the next timeline fetch. Local boosts of its statuses go with them by cascade and their boosters' followers hear a `delete` (see below). Not done: `delete` events for the remote boosts of a deleted status (`remote_boosts` cascade), and thread marks for single remote `Delete`s (only a purge drops them)

- Voting on a remote poll: `POST /api/v1/polls/remote-<id>/votes` validates (visible, not ended, choices in range, one choice for a single-choice poll, one vote per account, author domain not suspended, blocked by the voter, or outside the allow list), stores the votes in `remote_poll_own_votes`, and federates one `Create(Note{name, inReplyTo, to: author})` per choice to the author's own inbox. The Poll entity carries `voted` and `own_votes` on every remote status render, and the votes are added to the displayed counts until the author's next changed `Update(Question)` tally replaces them. The vote Notes are not served at their `/users/:name/statuses/:id` URIs (Mastodon does not dereference them either).
- Remote polls, read-only: a remote `Question` (`oneOf` single, `anyOf` multiple choice, option counts, `endTime`/`closed`, `votersCount`) is stored in `remote_statuses.poll_json` and rendered as the Mastodon Poll entity (id `remote-<status id>`, also served by `GET /api/v1/polls/:id`); an author `Update(Question)` refreshes counts without setting `edited_at`. Custom emoji: a remote note's `Emoji` tags that its content, spoiler or option titles use are kept in `remote_statuses.emojis_json` (migration 0094) and rendered as the status `emojis`; the Poll entity carries those its option titles use. A local account that voted on a remote poll gets a `poll` notification from the author once it has ended (`server/poll-expiry.ts`, run by the `ProcessExpiredPolls` job; `remote_poll_own_votes.expiry_notified_at` makes it once per voter, polls that had ended before the migration are not announced). The author is remote, so nobody else is told.

- Inbox `Update`: a remote edit of a cached note (author-checked, `edited_at` rendered, newer edit wins, new mentions notified once) and a remote actor profile refresh. Edit history: each content-changing `Update` keeps the version it replaces in `remote_status_edits` (migration 0094), and `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id/history` renders them as StatusEdit entities, the original first and the current version last (a note edited before this change shows only its current version); `/source` answers 404 for a remote note and, now, for another account's local note. Local boosters of the edited note get an `update` notification; follow-up: the same for quotes once local quotes of remote statuses exist. Local polls send no `poll` notification at expiry either (only a `status.update` to the author's stream); Mastodon notifies the voters and the author.

- Cloudflare Access replaced by Void Better Auth (passkey-only sign-up/sign-in, admin plugin roles) and a first-party Mastodon OAuth 2 server (apps, consent, `authorization_code` + PKCE, `client_credentials`, revoke, RFC 8414 metadata) with Mastodon's per-endpoint granular scope enforcement

- Remote statuses render `in_reply_to_id` / `in_reply_to_account_id` from their stored `inReplyTo`

- Thread context spans local and cached remote statuses (either id; remote replies to local notes included)

- Local notes reply to cached remote statuses (`statuses.in_reply_to_uri`): rendered parent, `inReplyTo` on every federated note, direct replies grouped into the remote conversation

- Local notes mention remote accounts (`@user@domain`): Mention tags, Mastodon addressing, delivery by addressing (direct notes to mentioned inboxes only), `mentions` rendered

- Remote direct notes reach the addressed account's direct timeline and conversations (`remote_status_mentions`, replies grouped by `inReplyTo`), and open for it on `GET /api/v1/statuses/:id`

- Remote Follow/Like/Announce and mentions in new remote notes notify the local target (`r_…` senders, deduplicated, block/mute/domain-block aware); remote accounts can be blocked and muted

- Web Push: subscriptions per OAuth access token (WP-2; replaced by the token's next `POST`, removed with the token), VAPID key advertised, notifications delivered (`aes128gcm`/`aesgcm`, VAPID ES256)

- Toolchain on Vite+ (`vp check`/`test`/`dev`/`build`, Vitest 5 via `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`, Worker built by Void (`voidPlugin()`, wrapping `@cloudflare/vite-plugin`))
- `FEDERATION_ALLOW_HOSTS` matches `host:port` peers by hostname (inbox gate and follower fan-out)
- Link-card test no longer asserts an empty card right after posting (queue race)
- `GET /api/v1/accounts?id[]=` and `GET /api/v1/statuses?id[]=` batch lookups
- `DELETE /api/v1/profile/avatar` and `/header`
- Quote notifications (`quote`, `quoted_update`)
- `source[sensitive]`/`source[language]` defaults applied to new posts
- Outbound quote in the ActivityPub Note (`quote`/`quote_uri`/`_misskey_quote`)
- `indexable`/`hide_collections`/`noindex` and hidden follower collections
- Credential `source` (privacy/quote_policy defaults) and `update_credentials` support
- Quote posts (`quoted_status_id`, policy, quotes list, revoke, document fields)
- `favourited_by`/`reblogged_by` pagination on engagement row ids
- `Link` pagination headers on notifications, favourites, and bookmarks
- Follower/following pagination by follow row id with `Link` headers
- Stable notification-request ids (`notification_request_groups`) and id cursors
- Runtime `domain_allows` admin API and enforced limited federation
- Trend review records and admin approve/reject for tags, links, and statuses
- Row ids + `Link` pagination for blocks, mutes, endorsements, follow requests
- Scheduled statuses (create via `scheduled_at`, CRUD, cron publish)
- `GET /api/v1/preferences`
- Follow options: `reblogs`, `notify`, `languages[]` (and the relationship fields)
- `GET /api/v1/media/:id` and `DELETE /api/v1/conversations/:id`
- Remote tags indexed (`remote_status_tags`) with a cron backfill; exact tag paging
- `include_filtered` on the v1 and grouped v2 notification lists
- Canonical email + email domain blocks (enforced at sign-up)
- `GET /api/v1/admin/accounts/:id`
- `/action` resolves the named report (or all open reports for the account)
- Report `PUT` (category/rules), `assign_to_self`/`unassign`, `rule_ids` on create
- Admin reports list/single/resolve/reopen + report `category`
- Tag and public timelines honor `local`/`remote`; tag timelines include remote statuses
- `POST /admin/accounts/:id/action` + a real `disabled` state (`/enable` restores login only)
- Admin account/domain-block lists page by `max_id`/`since_id`/`min_id`
- Link-preview TTL test asserts the served title, not fetch counts (queue-flake free)
- `GET /api/v1/accounts/search` (exact handle, `offset`, `following`, resolve 503)
- `REGISTRATION_APPROVAL`, `approve`/`reject`, `approved` field, admin account filters
- `limit` caps match Mastodon per collection family (40 / 80 / 200); endorsements page
- `notifications/requests` accepts `limit` (40 default, 80 cap)
- Link-card test fetch stubs are host-scoped (no cross-file flake)
- Tag timeline accepts `any[]`/`all[]`/`none[]` (4-tag cap) and `only_media`
- Reblog honours `visibility`, refuses non-rebloggable statuses, and is idempotent
- `favourited_by` / `reblogged_by` list local and remote accounts behind a status's counts
- Poll edit accepts `hide_totals`
- `filter_action` is client-applied and unknown actions read as `warn` (documented + tested)
- `trends/links` supports `limit` + `offset` paging
- Link timeline (`/api/v1/timelines/link?url=`) for trending links
- Cron `RefreshLinkCards` job refreshes aged previews (20 per run)
- Single notification reports `filtered` like the list
- Link previews refresh after a week in the cache
- `ProcessLinkCard` retries transient failures (3 attempts) and reports `outbox.job` metrics
- `notification_requests.status_id` for an exact request `last_status`
- Trending links prefer the cached Open Graph preview
- `filtered` on remote statuses (HTML stripped to text for keyword matching)
- `notifications_merged` stream event when requests are accepted
- Notification cursors: `since_id` / `min_id` on the v1 and grouped v2 lists
- Link previews run off the request path (`ProcessLinkCard` queue job) and cover remote statuses
- `filtered` on the home/list/direct/public/tag/account-status timelines
- Link previews: bounded Open Graph unfurl cached in `link_cards`, stored on the status
- Profile fields capped at four
- Profile images (`avatar`/`header` uploads) and the `locked` toggle
- Notification policy + requests with `drop`/`filter`/`accept`, and `api_versions: {mastodon: 2}`
- Media uploads validate the advertised `supported_mime_types`
- Account profiles: bio (`note`), fields, bot/discoverable in every account document
- `GET /api/v1/notifications/:id`
- `filtered` attribute on search results and notifications (keyword + narrowed-status matching)
- Filter narrowing: `/api/v2/filters/:id/statuses` + `statuses` on the v2 document
- v2 filter verbs plus keyword sub-resources (`filters.keywords_json`)
- Filter paths and entities match Mastodon: flat `filters/keywords/:id` and `filters/statuses/:id`, FilterStatus `{id, status_id}`, v1 `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/filters/:id`
- Grouped notifications (`/api/v2/notifications` + group_key endpoints and unread count)
- Poll `hide_totals` (counts null until expiry) and a vote now answers the Poll entity
- `reject_media` enforced: remote attachments dropped on persist and cleared for existing rows
- Remote status attachments captured from ActivityPub and rendered (`remote_statuses.media_json`)
- `DELETE /api/v1/statuses/:id` returns the raw `text` for delete-and-redraft
- `Idempotency-Key` on POST /api/v1/statuses (`status_idempotency`, one hour, per account)
- `instance/peers` and `instance/activity` require a user token under limited federation
- `/api/v2/instance`: registrations detail, thumbnail description, policy URLs (icon and `api_versions` intentionally omitted)
- Instance policy documents from JSON vars (extended description, privacy policy, terms of service by date)
- Status translate endpoint (403 ineligible, 404 without a backend, as Mastodon) and empty `translation_languages`
- Enforced post limits from `InstanceLimits` (text/CW 500, media 4, poll 2-4 x 50 chars, expiry 5 min-1 month, description 1500) reflected in `/api/v2/instance`
- Public `GET /api/v1/instance/domain_blocks` (digest / obfuscate / public comment) and Monday-aligned `instance/activity`
- Trends tags carry the real seven-day history (shared with `/tags/:name`); search hashtags too
- Admin account sensitize/unsensitize (forces `sensitive`) and `DELETE /api/v1/admin/accounts/:id` purge
- Admin domain blocks: `/api/v1/admin/domain_blocks` CRUD (silence/suspend/noop) with fetch, inbox, outbound, and public-timeline enforcement
- Per-viewer domain blocks: `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/domain_blocks`, `Relationship.domain_blocking`, public timeline filtering
- `media_attributes` on status create/edit (JSON array or bracket form fields)
- Status context bounds: anonymous 40/60 depth 20 vs authenticated 4096, descendants in id order
- Admin accounts: `GET /api/v1/admin/accounts` + silence/suspend/enable, 403/410 and public-surface exclusion
- Media metadata: `PUT` / `DELETE /api/v1/media/:id` (alt text, focus, unattached-only)
- Remove from followers: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/remove_from_followers` (local + remote Reject)
- Timeline cursors: `max_id` / `since_id` / `min_id` on home, public, tag, bookmarks, favourites, account statuses
- Familiar followers: `GET /api/v1/accounts/familiar_followers?id[]=`
- Notification filters: `types[]` / `exclude_types[]` / `account_id` / `max_id`
- Admin announcements: `/api/v1/admin/announcements` CRUD + publish/unpublish, merged with `ANNOUNCEMENTS`
- Account notes: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/note` + `Relationship.note` (private per viewer)
- Endorsements (featured accounts) + list `exclusive`
- Account status filters: `exclude_replies` / `exclude_reblogs` / `only_media` / `tagged` / `max_id`; reblog now returns the reblog document
- Poll editing: re-option an unvoted, unexpired poll via `PUT /api/v1/statuses/:id`
- Admin custom emojis: `/api/v1/admin/custom_emojis` CRUD (bucket `emoji/` + `custom_emojis`), merged with `CUSTOM_EMOJIS`
- Tag actions: follow/unfollow, tag documents with 7-day history, featured tags (10 cap) + suggestions
- Conversation mutes: `status_mutes` by thread root, `muted` flag, timeline/notification suppression
- Custom emojis (`CUSTOM_EMOJIS`) and announcements (`ANNOUNCEMENTS`) replace the last placeholder routes
- Remote search: cached remote actors + `resolve=true` WebFinger/actor resolution
- Pinned statuses: pin/unpin, five-pin limit, `?pinned=true` account statuses
- Status edit: PUT/PATCH `/statuses/:id`, `/history`, `/source`, `status_edits` snapshots, `edited_at`, Update activity
- Instance rules served from `INSTANCE_RULES` (v1 rules endpoint + v2 instance)
- Suggestions (v1/v2, with per-viewer dismissal) and trends/links derived from local accounts and public notes
- Notifications: clear, dismiss, unread_count (marker-aware, honoring blocks/mutes)
- Path-safe remote account ids: `r_` + base64url actor URI; accounts/:id and follow request targets decode it
- Blocks + mutes: endpoints, relationship flags, home/list/direct timeline suppression, notification gating; `d1PrepareTyped` expands repeated `$N`
- Follow requests: list/authorize/reject for local + remote pending, Accept/Reject delivery, `remote_follows.follow_activity_id`
- json_each membership; TypedSQL fixed SQL; wrangler migrations SoT ADRs
- `runTypedBatch`; ExpandFollowers remote-only + chunked targets
- Create saga mentions-before-notify; `enqueueLocalActivity` Result
- Expired-poll bulk `mastodonStatuses`
- Analytics indexes: `outbox.expand`, `outbox.enqueue`, `search`, `timeline.tag`
- WP-1: notifications are pushed to Web Push subscriptions (`DeliverPush` queue job, RFC 8291/8292)
- DM-5: deleting a local note federates a `Delete` with a `Tombstone`, addressed like the note
- DM-7: the AP outbox filters to public Creates and Announces in SQL, before its `LIMIT`; boosts federate a Mastodon-shaped `Announce` (`id`, `published`, visibility-based `to`/`cc`, the note's own URI) and unreblog federates its `Undo`
- Query indexes (`0065_query_indexes.sql`): boost/favourite counts, favourited_by/reblogged_by, thread descendants, status media, status-delete cascades, the outbox, delivery fan-out rows, remote-actor handles, and per-account cascades no longer scan their tables
- DM-6: followers-only and direct notes answer a signed fetch (authorized fetch) from an actor they address, or from an accepted follower when they address followers, with `Cache-Control: private`
- HTTP signatures verify every signed header with its real value; signatures that also cover `content-type` or `accept` (as Mastodon's do) used to fail
- DM-4: a muted conversation covers remote replies too (their reply chain is walked back to the local root), and, as in Mastodon's NotifyService, no notification is created for a status in a muted conversation, so the v1 list, grouped v2 list, unread counts, and push agree (only the v1 list used to filter, and only local statuses)
- Batched account documents on account lists (followers, following, follow requests, endorsements, list members, favourited/reblogged by, search, familiar followers), remote actors on timelines, notification requests, and tag histories (one status scan for every tag on a page)
